# Dodam TODO

## Next: General-Purpose SQL Compatibility

Review follow-up:

- Remaining fast/special rule inventory to lower into common vectorized optimizer/execution rules:
  - `PricingSummary` / Q01 helpers: tiny-cardinality grouped decimal aggregate.
    - Lower to: generic typed small-group aggregate operator with decimal raw-block update, expression fusion, and cost gate by group cardinality estimate.
  - `ShippingPriorityRevenue`, `RegionalSupplierRevenue`, `BilateralShippingVolume`, `NationMarketShare`, `ProfitByNationYear`, `ReturnedCustomerRevenue`: multi-table lookup/join aggregate pipelines.
    - Lower to: cost-based join order + dynamic filter/semi-join reduction + vector aggregate sink over join output. Q-prefixed dense maps and lookup tables should become reusable `BuildLookup`, `DynamicFilter`, and `JoinAggregateSink` operators.
  - `OrderPriorityExistsCount`, `ShippingModePriorityCounts`, `SupplierWaitCountAntijoin`, `PrefixPartSupplierThreshold`: EXISTS/anti-join/counting patterns.
    - Lower to: correlated subquery rewrite rules that produce semi/anti join plans plus vectorized grouped aggregate. The current Q04/Q12/Q20/Q21 helpers should disappear behind a generic semi/anti join lowering.
  - `ImportantStockValue`, `JoinWithGroupedSumSemijoin`, `JoinWithCorrelatedAvgThreshold`, `DerivedPrefixAvgAntiJoinAggregate`: aggregate-subquery threshold and derived aggregate shapes.
    - Lower to: scalar/grouped aggregate subplan materialization with costed reuse, then dynamic-filtered scan or join. Keep late materialization as a physical decision, not a SQL-shape decision.
  - `DerivedJoin`, `DerivedLeftJoinCountDistribution`, `Derived`, `MaterializedJoinSubquery`: derived-table materialization and re-scan shapes.
    - Lower to: a logical plan node for materialized subplans with projection/filter pushdown, cardinality propagation, and spill-aware row-key fallback.
  - `MultiCommaJoin`: comma join parser/planner route.
    - Lower to: the same logical join graph as explicit JOIN syntax. Comma join should only be syntax; join order, projection pushdown, and residual filters should be shared.
    - Progress: split comma-specific edge extraction from the shared multi-input join graph/stat builder. `build_logical_multi_join_graph(...)` now accepts pre-extracted join edges and key columns, so explicit JOIN and derived multi-input plans can reuse the same NDV/range/row-width cost model instead of duplicating comma-specific planning. Added unit coverage for the shared stats builder.
    - Progress: explicit two-table JOIN now also emits a `LogicalJoinGraph` from Parquet row-count metadata, join keys, and projected row-width estimates. `DODAM_OPTIMIZER_TRACE=1` shows `rule=explicit_join_order`, so comma join and explicit join are now visible through the same join-order cost model surface.
    - Progress: explicit inner JOIN chains with more than two inputs now lower into the same multi-input join rule by converting ON predicates into the shared conjunct list. The CLI direct-join guard now detects both comma and explicit multi-input joins, so multi-join queries no longer fall into the two-table direct sink parser. Added DuckDB differential coverage for explicit 3-way JOIN aggregates.
    - Progress: optimizer trace labels for the graph path now use `multi_input_join_order` / `multi_input_join_output_projection` instead of comma-only names. With `DODAM_DISABLE_JOIN_AGGREGATE_LOOKUP_FUSION=1` (old `DODAM_DISABLE_MULTI_COMMA_LOOKUP_COUNT_SUM_FUSION=1` is kept as an alias), explicit 3-way JOIN visibly runs through the shared graph/tree executor.
    - Progress: the old `multi_comma_lookup_count_sum` execution helper has been renamed/lowered into a named `JoinAggregateLookupFusion` physical rule surface. Its profile output now reports `rule=join_aggregate_lookup_fusion`, and tuning envs gained `DODAM_JOIN_AGGREGATE_LOOKUP_*` names while preserving legacy aliases.
    - Progress: `JoinAggregateLookupFusion` is now planned as an eligible physical candidate, costed against a metadata-only `LogicalJoinGraph`, and logged with `rule=join_aggregate_lookup_fusion_graph` plus a candidate/graph cost line. The pre-scan graph uses table row-count metadata, projected row width, join edges, and an FK/PK-style `min(left_rows, right_rows)` NDV heuristic so the fusion no longer silently bypasses the optimizer trace.
    - Progress: the metadata-only graph now reads primitive Parquet row-group min/max for join keys, populates `ColumnRangeStats`, and caps key NDV by the observed range width. This gives the cost gate a real metadata-derived selectivity bound instead of only table row counts.
    - Progress: derived-table joins now build a `LogicalJoinGraph` from their materialized input batches, emit `rule=derived_join_order`, and choose hash build side from row-count x row-width cost. This is the first derived-input connection to the shared join optimizer surface.
    - Progress: CLI `query`/`query-file` sink execution now treats direct-join sink planner shape mismatches as "not a direct sink candidate" and falls back to engine SQL execution instead of surfacing parser errors. Derived joins now reach the engine rule path from CLI and show `derived_join_order` optimizer trace.
    - Remaining: persist/cache exact NDV and null-count metadata, then allow `JoinAggregateLookupFusion` itself to consume materialized/derived inputs instead of only base Parquet tables.
  - Set-op/direct distinct rules: already closer to common rules, but direct distinct still has primitive/string coverage gaps.
    - Lower to: shared `DistinctScan`/`SetOpScan` physical operators with nullable primitive, dictionary string, and mixed multi-column keys.
  - Current instrumentation:
    - `SqlRule` now reports a coarse `kind` such as `vector-aggregate`, `vector-join-aggregate`, `derived-aggregate`, or `subquery-rewrite` in `DODAM_SQL_RULE_PROFILE=1` output. Use this to track migration away from query-shaped names.
  - Latest maturity batch:
    - `sql.rs` decomposition: moved native filtered aggregate execution/state/mask logic into `src/sql/native_filtered.rs` and moved SQL rule registry/candidate/cost dispatch into `src/sql/rule_registry.rs`. This removes about `1.6K` lines from `sql.rs` without changing execution behavior, and makes two boundaries explicit: aggregate `FILTER` execution and registered optimizer-style SQL rule selection.
    - Follow-up decomposition: moved `IN`/`EXISTS` semijoin and correlated/uncorrelated subquery rewrite/execution into `src/sql/semijoin.rs` (`~5.4K` lines) and separated the window query entrypoint into `src/sql/window.rs`. `sql.rs` is now about `59.4K` lines, down from about `66.6K` before this decomposition pass. This is still large, but the most obvious rule/execution islands are no longer embedded directly in the top-level file.
    - Validation after the split: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `duckdb_differential_generic_performance_shapes`, `duckdb_differential_seeded_randomized_filtered_aggregate_smoke`, `duckdb_differential_tpch_q6_canonical_shape`, `duckdb_differential_in_subquery`, `duckdb_differential_exists_subquery`, and `duckdb_differential_correlated_in_and_scalar_subquery` passed.
    - Focused benchmark after release rebuild stayed in the same noise band: median-sum Dodam `0.185746s`, DuckDB `0.153635s` (`1.209x`) across aggregate-filter/group-expression/mixed-tuple/join/window cases. Per-case samples: `standard_aggregate_filter` `19.931ms`, `standard_group_by_all_expression` `30.636ms`, `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` `26.609ms`, `window_lag_lead_partition_order` `54.564ms`.
    - Expression aggregate decomposition: moved expression aggregate append/fold/later-materialized/fused-selected/scan-fold/row-group-map execution helpers plus expression group-key lowering into `src/sql/expression_aggregate.rs` (`~2.0K` lines). This makes the `GROUP BY ALL` / coalesce-expression aggregate path an explicit module while preserving the existing vectorized collector logic.
    - Validation after expression aggregate split: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `duckdb_differential_generic_performance_shapes`, `duckdb_differential_grouped_coalesce_expression_aggregates`, and `duckdb_differential_seeded_randomized_filtered_aggregate_smoke` passed. Focused release benchmark stayed in the same noise band: median-sum Dodam `0.184104s`, DuckDB `0.157724s` (`1.167x`); `standard_group_by_all_expression` `30.966ms`, `standard_aggregate_filter` `20.100ms`, `window_lag_lead_partition_order` `50.804ms`.
    - Negative decomposition result: attempted to move join aggregate lookup fusion by cutting from `JoinAggregateLookupFusionPlan` to the next comma-join helper. The range was too broad and pulled unrelated parser/table-ref/primitive helper definitions out of `sql.rs`, causing widespread missing-type/function errors. Reverted that move. Next attempt should first create a narrow marker boundary around only `JoinAggregateLookupFusionPlan`, its dimension structs, `choose/plan/execute`, dense update helper, group accumulator, and residual-edge checker; do not use `common_or_comma_join_equality_keys` as the end marker.
    - Next split candidates: a narrower join aggregate lookup fusion module, window helper implementation, and parser/projection structs. Those should move with focused differential/benchmark coverage because they still share many top-level parser/evaluator helpers.
    - Native filtered aggregate: tried a general sparse-mask blocked update path for integer group keys. It builds selected row lists per predicate mask and updates only matching rows instead of scanning every row/spec pair. This is now retained only behind `DODAM_NATIVE_FILTERED_SPARSE_MASKS=1` because the generic parquet benchmark regressed (`standard_aggregate_filter` opt-in sample `27.757ms` vs DuckDB `21.084ms`). Root cause: building selected row vectors requires an extra full mask scan, and the saved update work does not offset that for current medium/high-survival predicates.
    - Default native filtered aggregate remains on the previous row-major dense integer path. Focused default result (`--only standard_aggregate_filter,standard_group_by_all_expression,standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file,optimizer_four_way_comma_join,optimizer_selective_four_way_comma_join`, repeats 5, warmup 1, scale 1): median-sum Dodam `0.134494s`, DuckDB `0.103659s` (`1.297x`); `standard_aggregate_filter` `21.234ms` vs `16.352ms` (`1.299x`).
    - Correctness/robustness: added seeded randomized aggregate `FILTER` differential coverage (`count/sum/avg/min/max`, grouped and ungrouped, null/LIKE/IN predicates). This protects the native filtered aggregate path while future vectorized update work continues.
    - Spill safety: reran the existing partitioned spill join test as the current memory-pressure smoke. The next robustness step should add SQL-level low-memory differential coverage, but avoid global env mutation in parallel tests unless it is isolated.
    - Progress: added `SqlExecutionOptions::join_memory_limit_bytes` and `execute_sql_with_options(...)` so tests and future engine callers can force join memory strategy without mutating process-global env. Added DuckDB differential coverage for an explicit JOIN under a 1-byte memory limit. This is the first SQL-level low-memory correctness guard that does not rely on `DODAM_JOIN_MEMORY_LIMIT_BYTES`.
    - Progress: tuple semijoin pre-filter cleanup now strips redundant `IS NOT NULL` predicates on tuple key columns, including inside `AND`, before applying the semijoin pair mask. This avoids filtering the same key-null rows twice in dictionary-backed mixed tuple paths. Focused result (`standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file`, repeats 5, warmup 1, scale 1) was around `25-33ms` Dodam vs `19-27ms` DuckDB depending on sample; the combined focused set measured `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` `26.266ms` vs `20.085ms` (`1.308x`). This is a general semijoin-key-null rule, not a query-name fast path.
    - Correctness + performance: fixed native filtered aggregate input caching. The cache key previously used only input expression and nullability kind, so `count(value)` could cache a `NonNull` count-only sentinel that `sum(value)` / `avg(value)` then reused in later batches, silently skipping numeric updates. The key now includes aggregate kind. Added deterministic DuckDB differential coverage for shared filtered aggregate inputs across row groups, and kept the seeded randomized filtered aggregate smoke passing.
    - Native filtered aggregate performance: direct predicates now compare `i32`/`i64`/`Decimal128` primitives without the generic `Option<T>` comparison wrapper. Also made direct predicate detection return `Ok(None)` for non-column/non-literal comparisons such as `COALESCE(payload, 'missing') <> 'missing'`, so mixed grouped batches fall back to the generic predicate evaluator instead of raising a detector error. Focused result (`standard_aggregate_filter`, repeats 3, warmup 1, scale 1): Dodam `21.290ms`, DuckDB `19.402ms` (`1.097x`); combined focused set measured `standard_aggregate_filter` `21.111ms` vs `18.164ms` (`1.162x`).
    - Remaining high-value implementation target: replace `FILTER` aggregate's state dispatch loop with a real vector aggregate sink that evaluates predicate masks and typed inputs once, then updates contiguous group-state arrays. The selected-row-list experiment shows that simply changing loop order is not enough.
    - Join aggregate lookup fusion: added a query-plan-level combined dense lookup for shared fact keys in 3-dimension lookup aggregates. This lowers the 4-way shared-key hot loop from two shared dense probes plus multiply to one combined dense probe, while keeping it as a general physical rule for repeated fact-key dimensions. Focused profile after precompute showed the stable hot repeats around `~10ms` fact update for the 4-way case, with focused ratio around `~1.17x` vs DuckDB.
    - Failed experiment: changed the combined dense lookup representation from a simple `values + present` table to a `Full/Sparse` enum to remove the present-bit branch when both source dense lookups are full. The focused benchmark regressed (`optimizer_four_way_comma_join` sampled around `1.43x` vs DuckDB), likely due to noisier generated branch/code shape without a real data-access reduction. Reverted to the simpler present-bit representation.
    - Failed experiment: tried to split `single_key_count_sum_min_max` Utf8 into a key-null-free hot loop and to rewrite `two_key_count_sum_min_max` null-free batches over raw primitive slices. Focused aggregate benchmark regressed (`review_q1_alt_string_group` around `1.46x-1.60x`, two-key around `1.17x`), so both were reverted. This suggests the current cost is not primarily Arrow `value()` bounds/null checks; it is group lookup/update and batch partial bookkeeping.
    - Failed/default-off experiment: generalized `count/sum/min/max` typed grouped aggregate so `MAX` can be `Decimal128` as well as `Date32`. This made `review_q1_* max(amount)` take the typed path instead of generic fallback, but focused performance regressed (`review_q1_alt_string_group` around `1.43x`, one-key around `1.41x` vs DuckDB). The typed per-row group update is slower than the current generic Arrow aggregate for decimal min/max. Kept the code behind `DODAM_ENABLE_DECIMAL_MAX_COUNT_SUM_MIN_MAX_TYPED=1` for future work; default falls back to the previous generic path.
    - Progress: lowered the `count/sum/min/max` direct primitive fold from single-key only to a general two-key shape when the second key is `Date32` and also the filter date column. This is a planner/execution rule (`DirectPrimitiveFoldMode::TwoKeyCountSumMinMax`), not a query-name fast path. It scans projected primitive columns in stable order (`first key`, `date key`, `sum`, `decimal`) and updates a two-key vector state without RecordBatch aggregate fallback. Focused release result improved `review_q1_two_key_group` from about `1.20x` slower than DuckDB to `8.684ms` vs DuckDB `15.835ms` (`0.548x`).
    - Progress: changed direct UTF8 count/sum grouping state from `HashMap<Vec<u8>, (count,sum)>` to `HashMap<Vec<u8>, group_id> + Vec<(key,count,sum)>`, so dictionary/plain string grouping updates contiguous group state after one key lookup. This is a general string group-state layout improvement. Focused sample for `review_q1_alt_string_group` stayed slower but improved in ratio (`20.939ms` vs DuckDB `17.103ms`, `1.224x`).
    - Progress: added null-free block branches for mixed `(int,string)` tuple semijoin dictionary probe masks, removing per-row dictionary/null checks when the arrays are null-free. Focused `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` moved slightly from `24.318ms` to `23.985ms` vs DuckDB around `20.2ms` (`1.188x`). Remaining cost is still inner direct build and compact pair-set probe, not correctness/materialization.
    - Neutral/noisy: aggregate `FILTER` sparse/dense policy and direct predicate cleanup are correct, but focused results fluctuate around DuckDB (`standard_aggregate_filter` `1.001x`, sparse `1.035x`, dense `1.059x` in the latest run). The next real improvement still needs a vector aggregate sink that fuses mask evaluation, group id lookup, and typed update over contiguous state arrays; loop-order tweaks alone are not enough.
    - Progress: changed decimal `MAX` count/sum/min/max typed aggregate gating from env-only to a type/cost rule. Primitive keys can use the typed path by default, while plain `Utf8` keys still fall back unless explicitly enabled because that path previously regressed. Focused result: `review_q1_one_key_group` `8.142ms` vs DuckDB `15.241ms` (`0.534x`), `review_q1_two_key_group` `8.593ms` vs `15.636ms` (`0.550x`).
    - Neutral/small: widened late-materialized dictionary preservation and the single-key count/sum/min/max collector to accept `Dictionary<Int32, Utf8>` group keys with dictionary-id -> group-id caching. This is a general string-key aggregate improvement, but the current `review_q1_alt_string_group` gap is still mostly scan/decode/materialization: focused sample `21.028ms` vs DuckDB `16.931ms` (`1.242x`). Keep this as correctness-compatible groundwork; the bigger win likely needs a direct dictionary + decimal aggregate reader that avoids Arrow `RecordBatch` payload materialization entirely.
    - Mixed tuple semijoin profile after null-free probe cleanup: `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` still spends about `15ms` in inner direct build and `10-11ms` in outer early-empty scan. The selected-inner build remains opt-in because it regressed (`~35ms` vs DuckDB `~25ms`) by reading selected bytearray/dictionary ids more expensively than the full dictionary-id raw path.

Focused sample: Dodam `112.727ms`, DuckDB `104.519ms` (`1.079x`). `three_key_expression_group` was faster (`0.947x`) and `optimizer_three_way_comma_join` was near parity (`1.013x`). The remaining visible gaps were `review_q1_alt_string_group` (`1.206x`), mixed tuple semijoin (`1.162x`), and dense FILTER aggregate (`1.084x`).
    - Neutral/noisy mixed tuple experiment: generalized the early-empty probe cap for dense `(i32,dictionary-id)` pair sets with small string dictionaries (`DODAM_MIXED_TUPLE_EARLY_EMPTY_MAX_DENSE_PAIRS`, default `200k`). Profiling confirmed the rule selects `mixed-direct-empty`, but same-run A/B showed wall time stayed around `31.3ms` whether early-empty was enabled or disabled. This means the current gap is not the cap decision; it is dictionary-id build/read and the outer selected read cost.
    - Rechecked `review_q1_alt_string_group`: the UTF8 dictionary key path already uses dense dictionary-id -> group-id caching and previous buffer/cache experiments were neutral or negative. Avoid more small cache tweaks here; the next meaningful fix is a direct dictionary-key `count/sum/min/max` fold that bypasses Arrow RecordBatch materialization for `Dictionary<Int32, Utf8>` group keys.
    - Rechecked `standard_aggregate_filter_dense`: direct columnar FILTER aggregate remains better than disabling it (`~22.7ms` default vs `~23.3ms` disabled in the latest focused A/B), but update still dominates.     - Progress: added a direct UTF8 dictionary-key `count/sum/min/max` fold for Q1-like single-key string grouping. The planner now recognizes `Utf8` group keys as a `dictionary_i32_utf8` direct fold candidate, and execution reads row-group dictionary ids plus `date32/i64/decimal-i64` payloads directly without building Arrow `RecordBatch`es for the aggregate loop. The first sequential version regressed (`review_q1_alt_string_group` around `41ms`) because it scanned row groups serially; changing it to parallel row-group fold and merging UTF8 partial states reduced the aggregate profile to about `8.2ms`.
    - Focused result after the direct dictionary fold: `review_q1_alt_string_group` improved from the prior `~26ms` class to `12.659ms` vs DuckDB `21.159ms` (`0.598x`) in the 5-case focused run.     - Progress: parallelized mixed tuple semijoin inner raw dictionary-id build. The old direct build scanned row groups serially even though it already avoided `RecordBatch` materialization; the new path partitions row groups, uses per-worker pair vectors, shares only the prefix string-id map behind a short dictionary-refresh mutex, and merges into the same dense `(i32,dictionary-id)` pair representation. Profiled inner build dropped from about `15ms` to about `10ms`; focused repeated `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` improved to `23.961ms` vs DuckDB `26.686ms` (`0.898x`) in the isolated run.
    - Focused 5-case result after mixed tuple parallel build: Dodam `94.155ms`, DuckDB `105.704ms` (`0.891x`). `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` moved to `18.790ms` vs DuckDB `20.990ms` (`0.895x`). Remaining slower focused cases are now small/noisy: `optimizer_three_way_comma_join` around `1.11x` and `standard_aggregate_filter_dense` around `1.02x`.

Neither implementation was kept active; this is recorded to avoid repeating the same Arrow-bypass and batch-local slot experiments without first fixing the lower-level primitive decode/read path.
    - Progress: lowered batch-level tautological `IS NOT NULL` FILTER predicates into an `AlwaysTrue` direct predicate and added an all-selected columnar update path for native filtered aggregates. This removes per-row predicate checks for null-free batches without naming a query. Focused `standard_aggregate_filter_dense` profile improved update time from about `8.8ms` to `7.2-7.8ms`; isolated benchmark sampled `22.209ms` vs DuckDB `22.453ms` (`0.989x`).     - Progress: removed the remaining `tpch-rule-set` dispatcher from the SQL rule registry. Q02/Q14/Q15/Q16 shapes now register as individual costed rules (`minimum-cost-supplier`, `promo-revenue-ratio`, `top-supplier-revenue`, `parts-supplier-relationship`) with explicit rule kinds and required-column metadata, instead of hiding behind a legacy TPC-H dispatcher. SF=0.01 subset still ran faster than DuckDB for all four (`0.369x` median-sum in the one-run check).
    - Correctness/generalization: extended the global `SUM(left * right)` direct expression aggregate from integer-only to general numeric inputs (`Int32`, `Int64`, `Float64`, and `Decimal128`). This keeps Q6-like expression sums on a generic expression-shape rule after fast-path removal; `duckdb_differential_tpch_q6_canonical_shape` now passes without reviving the old Q6 query fast path.
    - Default-off experiment: batch-scanning direct FILTER predicates to simplify `id >= 0` / `amount >= 0` into `AlwaysTrue` reduced native filtered aggregate update time (`~7.5ms -> ~3.8-4.9ms`) but moved the work into predicate simplification/mask time (`~6-7ms`) and regressed/noised the focused set (`standard_aggregate_filter_dense` up to `~1.10-1.22x`). Kept the implementation only behind `DODAM_ENABLE_NATIVE_FILTERED_DIRECT_PREDICATE_SIMPLIFY=1`; a real default needs metadata/page-level predicate truth, not a full extra batch scan.
    - Default-off experiment: added a native filtered aggregate fused vector sink for dense `Int32` group keys so one key scan can update multiple aggregate states. This is the right architectural direction, but the first row-loop implementation was slower than the existing aggregate-columnar loops (`standard_aggregate_filter_dense` opt-in `23.721ms` vs DuckDB `21.328ms`, update around `8.8-9.4ms`). Kept behind `DODAM_ENABLE_NATIVE_FILTERED_FUSED_VECTOR_SINK=1`; a default-worthy version needs lower-level block predicate evaluation and per-state update specialization, not dynamic predicate/state dispatch inside the fused row loop.
    - Follow-up robustness/generalization pass:
      - Added an opt-in blocked direct-predicate mask path for native filtered aggregates (`DODAM_ENABLE_NATIVE_FILTERED_BLOCKED_DIRECT_PREDICATE_MASKS=1`). It feeds the existing dense row-mask updater and is intended for future page/block predicate evaluation experiments. Focused A/B was neutral/slightly worse (`standard_aggregate_filter_dense` opt-in `21.856ms` vs default `21.715ms`), so keep default-off until predicate masks can be produced from page/block metadata rather than another full predicate pass.
      - Renamed remaining Q02/Q14/Q15/Q16 profile labels to rule-shape names (`minimum-cost-supplier`, `promo-revenue-ratio`, `top-supplier-revenue`, `parts-supplier-relationship`) so external instrumentation no longer exposes those as query-number fast paths.
      - Added low-memory SQL differential coverage for `JOIN + GROUP BY + SUM` under `SqlExecutionOptions { join_memory_limit_bytes: Some(1) }`, extending the existing low-memory join correctness guard beyond plain join output.
      - Split public SQL output/options/sink types from `sql.rs` into `src/sql/types.rs`, shrinking the top-level SQL module and making the execution API boundary explicit.
      - Continued lowering the TPC-H-derived rule module naming: the minimum-cost supplier rule no longer uses Q02-prefixed helper/type names internally, and repeated comma-join table alias matching was replaced with a small `named_comma_join_tables(...)` resolver reused by multiple rule-shape implementations. External Q14/Q15/Q16 helper calls remain until those implementations are moved out of `sql.rs`; SF=0.01 Q02/Q14/Q15/Q16 subset stayed fast (`0.276x` median-sum), and the focused generic set stayed ahead (`0.909x`).

      - Replaced Q-number calls at the `tpch_rules.rs` boundary for Q14/Q15/Q16 with shape-named wrappers (`promo_*`, `top_supplier_*`, `parts_supplier_relationship_*`). The underlying implementations still live in `sql.rs`, but the rule surface no longer exposes those operations as query-number fast paths.
      - Extracted the Q15 typed revenue loop into a reusable `update_i64_grouped_discounted_revenue_by_date_view(...)` primitive. This keeps the block/vector path intact while making the operation reusable for other date-filtered discounted-revenue grouped aggregates.
      - Extended low-memory differential coverage from plain join and join aggregate to `JOIN + ORDER BY/LIMIT`, derived-table join, and 3-way join aggregate under `SqlExecutionOptions { join_memory_limit_bytes: Some(1) }`.

SF=0.01 Q14/Q15/Q16 focused comparison stayed faster than DuckDB (`0.020407s` vs `0.059984s`, `0.340x`). Generic focused set stayed close/faster overall (`0.110643s` vs `0.116485s`, `0.950x`), with remaining slower cases `standard_aggregate_filter_dense` (`1.105x`), `review_q6_expression_sum` (`1.186x`), and `optimizer_three_way_comma_join` (`1.058x`).
    - Projection/general execution rule:
      - Defaulted comma-join hash output projection to allow true zero-column side output when no downstream join, residual, aggregate, projection, HAVING, or ORDER BY expression needs columns from that side. This removes the previous forced key-column retention and keeps the old behavior available via `DODAM_DISABLE_ZERO_COLUMN_JOIN_OUTPUT=1`.
      - Added an opt-in dense `Int32` `count/sum/avg` native FILTER aggregate sink (`DODAM_ENABLE_NATIVE_FILTERED_COUNT_SUM_AVG_DENSE_SINK=1`). Same-run A/B showed only a small/noisy improvement for `standard_aggregate_filter_dense` (`24.238ms -> 23.229ms`, still slower than DuckDB in that sample), so it remains default-off. The next real FILTER aggregate win still needs predicate truth from metadata/page/block level, not another row-level predicate/update variant.
      - Focused validation after defaulting zero-column join output: `duckdb_differential_join_matrix`, `duckdb_differential_generic_performance_shapes`, and `duckdb_differential_join_low_memory_limit` passed. Focused generic set remained faster overall (`0.108668s` vs DuckDB `0.118555s`, `0.917x`); `optimizer_three_way_comma_join` was effectively parity/noise (`1.008x` in the 6-case set), while `standard_aggregate_filter_dense` remains the main small gap (`1.066x` in the same set).
    - Metadata predicate simplification:
      - Added a single-table aggregate pre-pass that rewrites native FILTER aggregate predicates to literal `true`/`false` when Parquet primitive row-group min/max and null-count statistics prove the predicate for all rows. This currently handles primitive column-literal comparisons and `IS NOT NULL`, and deliberately avoids Decimal metadata scaling until the row-group range API exposes the column data type.
      - `NativeFilteredDirectPredicate` now has an `AlwaysFalse` variant so metadata-proven empty filters do not need row masks or row predicate checks.
      - Focused A/B for `standard_aggregate_filter_dense` after keeping Decimal predicates out of the metadata proof path: default metadata simplification `21.301ms` vs disabled `22.291ms`; DuckDB in those samples was `20.894ms`/`21.682ms`. The 6-case generic focused set stayed faster overall (`0.107499s` vs DuckDB `0.118227s`, `0.909x`); `optimizer_three_way_comma_join` was parity (`1.000x`), while dense FILTER still moves around by sample and remains the next narrow target (`1.097x` in that 6-case run).
      - Extended the row-group range API with column `DataType`, then enabled Decimal128 literal scaling in the same metadata proof path. Focused `standard_aggregate_filter_dense` A/B: default `21.419ms` vs disabled `21.806ms`; DuckDB was `22.082ms`/`21.271ms` in those separate samples. The 6-case focused set improved to Dodam `0.101170s` vs DuckDB `0.115338s` (`0.877x`), with dense FILTER nearly parity (`1.010x`). The remaining dense FILTER gap is now mostly sample noise and the residual row-level update sink, not obvious predicate evaluation.
      - Structure cleanup: moved the Parquet metadata FILTER-aggregate predicate proof code out of `sql.rs` into `src/sql/metadata_predicate.rs`. This keeps the single-table aggregate dispatcher as a call site and makes the metadata-based rewrite a named optimizer component.
      - Structure cleanup: moved query/select feature rejection, DISTINCT parsing, and DISTINCT validation into `src/sql/query_features.rs`, separating parser feature gates from the main SQL dispatcher.

- TPC-H exact-shape fast paths are not a valid proxy for generic OLAP performance.
  - SF=1 review data showed Q1 exact `~33ms` but small GROUP BY changes falling to `~565-675ms`, while DuckDB stayed around `~40-47ms`.
  - Q6 exact stayed fast, but changing the aggregate expression to `sum(l_extendedprice * l_tax)` fell behind DuckDB.
  - The next optimizer work should keep lowering TPC-H-derived techniques into general rules instead of counting exact-query wins as engine maturity.
- Generic OLAP gaps to keep measuring:
  - high-cardinality GROUP BY
  - `count(DISTINCT ...)`
  - LIKE filter + grouped aggregate
  - filter + ORDER BY + LIMIT
  - low-cardinality GROUP BY variants outside exact TPC-H shapes
- Correctness fixes from review:
  - `sum(Decimal128)` now accumulates raw decimal values and returns `Decimal128(38, scale)` instead of silently using `f64`.
  - Added explicit JOIN and 2-table comma join decimal SUM coverage so semantically equivalent join syntaxes stay aligned.
  - Multi comma join HAVING now rewrites uncorrelated scalar subqueries before filter parsing, covering aggregate-expression HAVING subqueries.
- SQL compatibility expansion:
  - Added direct single-CTE execution for `WITH t AS (...) SELECT ... FROM t`, including filter, projection, distinct, grouped aggregate, HAVING, ORDER BY, LIMIT, and OFFSET over the materialized CTE output.
  - Added simple CASE (`CASE expr WHEN ... THEN ... ELSE ... END`) lowering in the scalar and JOIN expression evaluators.
  - Added `lag(expr[, offset])` and `lead(expr[, offset])` window functions through the generic window pipeline, reusing `EvaluatedScalar` so primitive, string, boolean, date/timestamp, and decimal outputs stay type-preserving.
  - Added DuckDB differential coverage across the requested 7 areas: nested subquery, lag/lead window, HAVING with simple CASE, direct CTE, expression set-op, scalar function/expression compatibility, and JOIN expression filter.
  - Added generic DuckDB benchmark cases for the same areas.
  - Latest focused generic benchmark (`--repeats 3 --warmup 1`, parquet output):
    - `standard_direct_cte_aggregate`: Dodam `8.2ms`, DuckDB `13.2ms` (`0.62x`)
    - `window_lag_lead_partition_order`: Dodam `73.7ms`, DuckDB `48.9ms` (`1.51x`)
    - `standard_join_simple_case_residual`: Dodam `34.1ms`, DuckDB `12.7ms` (`2.69x`)
    - `except_distinct_simple_case_rows`: Dodam `47.7ms`, DuckDB `18.2ms` (`2.62x`)
    - `standard_having_simple_case`: Dodam `123.7ms`, DuckDB `22.9ms` (`5.40x`)
    - `standard_nested_in_subquery` with distinct RHS: Dodam `253.6ms`, DuckDB `29.8ms` (`8.51x`)
    - Notes:
      - Direct CTE is structurally fine because it reuses the materialized-batch aggregate path.
    - Nested `IN (SELECT ...)` is no longer always correctness-first materialization. Positive uncorrelated single-key shapes such as `key IN (SELECT key FROM ... WHERE ...)` now lower to the shared semijoin key-set path before generic literal rewriting. The semijoin key-set path uses typed/block insertion and membership masks for `Int32`/`Int64`/`Utf8`, reducing row-at-a-time work in correlated EXISTS/IN and uncorrelated IN lowering.
      - Focused benchmark after lowering: `standard_nested_in_subquery` Dodam `13.196ms`, DuckDB `37.277ms` (`0.354x`) with parquet output, `--repeats 3 --warmup 1 --fixture-scale 1`.
      - Progress: `NOT IN` now uses the same semijoin key-set path with an explicit RHS-null flag, so SQL 3-valued logic is preserved: if the RHS contains NULL, anti-membership produces no matching WHERE rows. `ORDER BY` monotonic key + `LIMIT` shapes can stream the anti-membership filter and stop after enough rows instead of scanning/sorting the whole result.
      - Focused benchmark for `standard_not_in_subquery_null_safe`: initial semijoin lowering Dodam `95.566ms`, DuckDB `35.731ms` (`2.675x`); after monotonic early-limit branch Dodam `70.964ms`, DuckDB `28.294ms` (`2.508x`). This is correct but still slower than DuckDB because most rows survive the anti-filter and payload/order output cost dominates.
      - Remaining: multi-column tuple `IN` and expression-projected RHS still need generalized dynamic-filter lowering. A first tuple-IN attempt showed routing still falls through to the generic WHERE parser as `unsupported WHERE expression: (id, key) IN (...)`; the next fix is to make subquery detection/rule routing treat tuple-IN as an `InSubquery` candidate before adding the pair-set executor.
      - Fixed tuple `IN` routing by making the single-column same-source DISTINCT rewrite skip tuple expressions and by sharing `InSubquery`/`InList(Subquery)` extraction between single-key and pair-key semijoin lowering. Added `standard_tuple_in_subquery` to the DuckDB generic benchmark and differential coverage.
      - Generalized the monotonic `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` early-stop path from single-key semijoin to i64 pair semijoin. Focused benchmark (`--only standard_tuple_in_subquery,standard_not_in_subquery_null_safe`, repeats 3, scale 1): tuple `IN` improved from `116.880ms` to `72.523ms` (`2.446x -> 1.530x` vs DuckDB); `NOT IN` stayed around `75.087ms` vs DuckDB `30.441ms` because it is a high-survival anti-filter case and still pays output/materialization cost.
      - Fixed single-key uncorrelated `IN/NOT IN` owner inference for alias-less direct table subqueries. The previous strict owner check rejected unqualified columns like `key`, so generic materialization handled common SQL even though the semijoin rule existed. The helper now uses scoped default ownership for direct table subqueries and leaves derived subqueries to the existing fallback.
      - Added preserve-order row-filter scan and lowers small dynamic Int64 `IN/NOT IN` key sets into `Expr::InList` predicates. This moves high-survival anti-filter work into the scan pipeline instead of a separate semijoin mask loop. Focused benchmark (`--only standard_not_in_subquery_null_safe,standard_tuple_in_subquery`, repeats 5, scale 1): `standard_not_in_subquery_null_safe` improved to `15.515ms` vs DuckDB `29.921ms` (`0.519x`); `standard_tuple_in_subquery` was `75.415ms` vs DuckDB `50.992ms` (`1.479x`). Profile for `NOT IN ... LIMIT 100000`: total `10ms`, inner `3ms`, outer `7ms`, projection `0ms`, branch `row-filter`.
      - Added same-source tuple semijoin predicate transfer. For `(a,b) IN (SELECT a,b FROM same_table WHERE predicate)` where `predicate` references only `a,b`, the semijoin is lowered to the transferred predicate on the outer scan instead of building a pair key set. This is a general equivalence because every duplicate tuple shares the same predicate inputs. Focused benchmark (`--only standard_tuple_in_subquery,standard_not_in_subquery_null_safe`, repeats 5, scale 1): `standard_tuple_in_subquery` improved from `75.415ms` to `10.613ms` vs DuckDB `45.757ms` (`1.479x -> 0.232x`); `standard_not_in_subquery_null_safe` stayed fast at `14.021ms` vs DuckDB `28.100ms` (`0.499x`).
      - Added control coverage for the tuple predicate-transfer rule. Differential now includes duplicate tuple rows with a key-only predicate (transfer-safe) and a non-key predicate (`flag = true`, transfer-forbidden) to guard SQL semantics. Generic DuckDB benchmark controls (`repeats 5`, scale 1): transfer case `standard_tuple_in_subquery` `9.985ms` vs DuckDB `45.905ms`; direct equivalent filter `10.485ms` vs DuckDB `25.709ms`, confirming the rewrite behaves like direct filter; non-key predicate `48.448ms` vs DuckDB `27.337ms`; other-file tuple `IN` `78.977ms` vs DuckDB `35.024ms`. Remaining tuple semijoin gaps are therefore the non-transfer cases, not the same-source predicate-transfer rule.
      - Improved non-transfer tuple semijoin by streaming pair-set construction, replacing the default tuple `HashSet` with the project fast hasher, adding typed Int32/Int64 pair insert/probe loops, and packing Int32/Int32 pairs into a single `u64` key. Focused benchmark (`repeats 5`, scale 1): `standard_tuple_in_non_key_predicate` improved `48.448ms -> 32.198ms` vs DuckDB `27.282ms` (`1.772x -> 1.180x`); `standard_tuple_in_other_file` improved `78.977ms -> 36.169ms` vs DuckDB `35.299ms` (`2.255x -> 1.025x`). Same-source transfer cases stayed around direct-filter speed.
      - Reverted the unique-column extension of same-source tuple predicate transfer after differential coverage found a correctness hole with `(id, payload) IN (SELECT id, payload ... WHERE payload LIKE 'f')`. The active transfer rule is now intentionally narrower: same-source tuple transfer only applies when the inner predicate references tuple key columns and does not contain `LIKE`; LIKE/non-key cases use the pair-set path. This keeps correctness while preserving the simple key-only transfer win.
      - Added tuple `NOT IN` null-safe support on the pair semijoin path. RHS tuple rows with NULL still fall back because full SQL tuple 3VL is nuanced, but null-free tuple anti-semijoin now uses the same packed/vectorized pair-set path. Focused benchmark: `standard_tuple_not_in_null_safe` `16.501ms` vs DuckDB `29.684ms` (`0.556x`).
      - Added mixed `(int,string)` tuple semijoin support with a typed `(i64, String)` pair-set fallback before the generic literal fallback. This removes `array_value_to_string/sql_literal` from the hot path for common mixed keys. Focused benchmark: `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` improved `112.627ms -> 47.448ms` vs DuckDB `20.767ms` (`5.565x -> 2.285x`). Profile shows `inner=23ms`, `outer=25ms`, so the next structural target is dictionary/string-id pair sets to avoid per-row `String` allocation/probe.
      - Failed experiment: changing the mixed `(int,string)` pair-set from one `HashSet<(i64, String)>` to `i64 -> One/Many string set` removed outer probe string allocation but added a second hash lookup/branch per row. Focused benchmark regressed to `57.373ms` vs DuckDB `20.370ms` (`2.816x`), so the change was reverted. The next viable path is not a nested map; it is dictionary/string-id probing that keeps a single compact key.
      - Improved mixed `(int,string)` tuple semijoin by interning RHS strings to compact ids and probing `(i64, string_id)` instead of `(i64, String)`, with an additional numeric prefilter set so non-candidate numeric keys skip string-id lookup. Focused benchmark after reverting failed dictionary-scan/dense experiments: `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` Dodam `43.044ms`, DuckDB `19.405ms` (`2.218x`). This is a small improvement over the initial typed mixed path (`47.448ms`) but still leaves a large string/dictionary gap.
      - Failed experiment: forcing inner mixed tuple build through `scan_parquet_batches_dictionary_columns` plus dictionary-prefix LIKE fusion regressed to `57.914ms` vs DuckDB `25.540ms` (`2.268x`). The likely cause is losing the existing filtered scan path and paying extra dictionary schema/batch overhead; this was reverted. A deeper implementation would need filter pushdown and dictionary decode fused in the reader, not a post-scan dictionary batch rewrite.
      - Failed experiment: replacing the mixed tuple numeric prefilter `HashSet<i64>` with a `DenseI64BoolLookup` + hash fallback regressed to `47.069ms` vs DuckDB `20.227ms` (`2.327x`). For this path, the dense wrapper call/fallback branch cost outweighed the saved hash probe, so the plain hash prefilter remains.
      - Added direct mixed tuple readers for `Int32 + dictionary-id BYTE_ARRAY` and `Int32 + BYTE_ARRAY` prefix build. After rebuilding the release binary and fixing the stale-binary measurement, this is now enabled by default with `DODAM_DISABLE_MIXED_TUPLE_DIRECT_BUILD` as the escape hatch. Focused release benchmark: `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` improved to `33.562ms` vs DuckDB `21.466ms` (`1.564x`), while `standard_tuple_in_non_key_predicate` recovered to `19.059ms` vs DuckDB `27.881ms` after restoring unique-key non-key predicate transfer. The remaining mixed gap is mostly outer probe/output and byte-array materialization overhead.
      - Improved mixed tuple pair-set representation from `(i64, string_id)` tuple hashing to packed `(i32, u32) -> u64` when keys fit, with automatic fallback to tuple pairs for wider numeric keys. Focused release benchmark after packing: `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` `32.718ms` vs DuckDB `20.433ms` (`1.601x`), `standard_tuple_in_non_key_predicate` `18.289ms` vs DuckDB `28.525ms`, `standard_tuple_in_other_file` `35.011ms` vs DuckDB `33.875ms`.
      - Reworked the mixed tuple early-empty probe from byte-array string decode to dictionary-id key-only probing and enabled it by default for selective mixed semijoins, with `DODAM_DISABLE_MIXED_TUPLE_EARLY_EMPTY=1` as the escape hatch. This keeps the useful rule general: if a selective `(int, string)` tuple semijoin can prove no outer row matches by scanning only key columns, it avoids decoding payload/output columns. Focused release benchmark (`repeats 5`, warmup 1): tuple semijoin set median-sum `0.087199s` vs DuckDB `0.083124s` (`1.049x`); `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` `34.061ms` vs DuckDB `19.955ms` (`1.707x`). The mixed case is still slower than DuckDB, but the old byte-array early-empty route is no longer the blocker.
      - Added a dense i32 numeric prefilter representation for mixed tuple semijoins when the RHS numeric key range is compact enough. This is a general `I32Hash -> dense byte lookup` conversion with span and memory gates, not a query-name rule. It removes a hash lookup from the 60만-row key-only outer probe in dense-key cases. Focused release result: tuple semijoin set median-sum `0.093300s` vs DuckDB `0.091169s` (`1.023x`); `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` improved to `31.456ms` vs DuckDB `19.955ms` (`1.576x`). Profile confirms `numeric=i32-dense` and the mixed internal branch around `25-29ms`.
      - Added a numeric-first selected outer probe for mixed tuple semijoins. The rule scans the numeric key first, builds selected row runs with the dense/i32 prefilter, then reads only selected dictionary ids from the string key column. A first attempt exposed a real cache correctness risk: dictionary string-id cache keyed only by `pointer+len` can false-hit when row-group dictionary allocations reuse the same address. Fixed the cache with a full byte fingerprint before enabling the rule. Actual-match sanity with a copied Parquet file returned `95192` rows in both Dodam and DuckDB.
      - Result: selected outer probe is now default-on with `DODAM_DISABLE_MIXED_TUPLE_NUMERIC_SELECTED_OUTER=1` as the escape hatch. Focused release tuple semijoin set improved to Dodam `0.081347s`, DuckDB `0.081883s` (`0.993x`): `standard_tuple_in_non_key_predicate` `18.512ms` vs `28.169ms`, `standard_tuple_in_other_file` `34.461ms` vs `33.928ms`, `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` `28.374ms` vs `19.786ms` (`1.434x`). Profile sample for mixed: inner direct read still dominates (`~23-24ms` read, `~3ms` consume), while outer selected read drops to `~7-8ms` from the old full dictionary-id scan around `~21ms`.
      - Negative/neutral result: tried an opt-in selected inner build (`DODAM_MIXED_TUPLE_SELECTED_INNER_BUILD=1`) that reads the string dictionary ids first, builds prefix-selected row runs, then reads only selected numeric ids. The first version decoded the string column twice and regressed badly. Compacting selected dictionary ids from the already-decoded full string id buffer removed the second string read and reduced inner direct read from the full `i32-dictionary-id` path (`~23-24ms read`) to `~12-13ms read`, but end-to-end mixed runtime still stayed around `~30ms` because run construction, selected numeric reads, and set build consume cost offset the read savings. Keep it opt-in only; the useful next step would need page-level prefix run construction that avoids row-group-wide string id materialization in the first place.
      - Added direct `(i32, i32)` tuple semijoin build with nullable i32 alignment and simple filter support (`AND`, integer comparison, `IS [NOT] NULL`) so common pair semijoins no longer build the RHS pair set through Arrow `RecordBatch` materialization. The direct reader reduced Parquet read to `~4-5ms`, but initial packed-pair `HashSet<u64>` build still spent `~23ms` in consume.
      - Added a dense `i32 -> i32` pair representation when the RHS first key is compact and functionally maps to one second key. This is a general pair-semijoin representation with range/memory gates and duplicate-conflict fallback to packed pair hash, not a query-name rule. Profile for `standard_tuple_in_other_file`: direct consume dropped from `~23ms` to `~7-8ms`, inner from `~27-29ms` to `~14-16ms`; a focused sample was `28.615ms` vs DuckDB `34.972ms` (`0.818x`). With `DODAM_DISABLE_I32_PAIR_DENSE_MAP=1`, the same case sampled `49.921ms` vs DuckDB `43.791ms` (`1.140x`), confirming the dense representation is the win.
      - Latest focused tuple semijoin set (`repeats 5`, warmup 1): Dodam `0.090158s`, DuckDB `0.093139s` (`0.968x`). Per case: `standard_tuple_in_non_key_predicate` `25.266ms` vs `36.333ms`, `standard_tuple_in_other_file` `36.656ms` vs `36.343ms`, `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` `28.235ms` vs `20.463ms`.
      - Tuple semijoin profile now reports branch, inner build, outer scan/probe, projection, output rows, and negation for the uncorrelated tuple path. This makes future pair-set/string-intern experiments measurable without adding ad hoc timers.
      - Simple CASE group/set/join cases fall through generic expression materialization and row-key paths; these need expression-key vectorization and expression-aware set-op direct distinct rules.
      - JOIN `ON` expression residuals are not fully generalized yet; expression residuals are currently supported in `WHERE` after the join. ON residuals still need a plan shape that separates equi-join keys from expression residual filters.

Priority order:

1. Set operations
   - `UNION ALL` - implemented for compatible SELECT operands with top-level `ORDER BY`/`LIMIT`/`OFFSET`
     - Generic scale=10 parquet-output benchmark added:
       - Initial: `union_all_append` Dodam `72.4ms` vs DuckDB `53.9ms` (`1.34x`), `union_all_order_limit` Dodam `96.6ms` vs DuckDB `31.1ms` (`3.11x`)
       - After same-source shared scan, direct sink streaming, monotonic tail top-k, and reverse row-group suffix scan: `union_all_append` Dodam `~68.6ms` vs DuckDB `~55.8ms` (`1.23x`) in the latest run, `union_all_order_limit` Dodam `~6.7ms` vs DuckDB `~32.7ms` (`0.21x`)
       - Full-read/scrambled guard benchmarks added:
         - `union_all_order_limit_full_read`: initial Dodam `~141.0ms` vs DuckDB `~73.7ms` (`1.92x`); after ordered same-source direct sink, latest Dodam `~129.6ms` vs DuckDB `~75.6ms` (`1.71x`)
         - `union_all_order_limit_scrambled`: initial Dodam `~89.6ms` vs DuckDB `~33.3ms` (`2.69x`); after generic streaming primitive/date DESC top-k heap, latest Dodam `~54.4ms` vs DuckDB `~34.4ms` (`1.58x`)
     - Current implementation supports compatible SELECT operands and has a general same-source disjoint equality-filter shared scan rule plus monotonic `ORDER BY key DESC LIMIT N` reverse row-group suffix scan, non-monotonic primitive/date streaming top-k, and ordered same-source direct sink rules. Next performance step is reducing the remaining full-read ordered row-group sort/write gap and generalizing top-k beyond this narrow same-source set-op shape.
   - `UNION` / `UNION DISTINCT` - implemented for compatible SELECT operands through the materialized set-op path, then `DistinctExec`
     - Added DuckDB differential coverage for duplicate-eliminating `UNION` and explicit `UNION DISTINCT`
     - Current implementation is correctness-first and materialized; `UNION ALL` keeps its existing streaming/same-source fast paths
     - Added generic benchmark cases:
       - `union_distinct_low_cardinality`
       - `union_distinct_rows`
     - SF=10 initial benchmark showed the correctness-first path is much slower than DuckDB: Dodam `~436.7ms`, DuckDB `~65.0ms` across the two cases (`6.7x`). After pushing `DISTINCT` into each UNION DISTINCT operand before final distinct, the same sample improved modestly to Dodam `~408.1ms`, DuckDB `~66.0ms` (`6.2x`). This is still not acceptable.
     - Root cause: the current path executes each operand independently, scans the same Parquet file multiple times, materializes large intermediate batches, then uses Arrow row conversion for distinct. DuckDB is effectively doing the equivalent set operation in one tighter pipeline.
     - Next performance step: add a same-source `UNION DISTINCT` rule that combines compatible operand filters into one scan (`OR`/`IN`), then applies distinct once. For low-cardinality projected keys, push distinct/grouping into the scan/aggregate path instead of row-converting full intermediate batches.
     - Applied the same-source `UNION DISTINCT` rule:
       - Compatible operands over the same Parquet file/projection now combine positive literal filters into one `IN` scan.
       - Single projected `i32` filter columns use a direct primitive block scan that collects candidate presence during scan and emits only already-distinct values.
       - `(i32 filter column, i64 payload column)` projections use a direct primitive block scan that inserts `(i32,i64)` pairs into a per-worker set during scan, then merges/sorts the much smaller distinct pair set. This removes the row-at-a-time style final materialization for the benchmarked pair case and keeps the boundary as `column blocks -> local distinct set -> merged distinct batch`.
     - SF=10 focused after block/vector distinct:
       - `union_distinct_low_cardinality`: Dodam `~8.8ms`, DuckDB `~25.8ms` (`0.34x`)
       - `union_distinct_rows`: Dodam `~40.6ms`, DuckDB `~51.7ms` (`0.78x`)
       - Combined: Dodam `~49.4ms`, DuckDB `~77.5ms` (`0.64x`), down from `~408ms` before the block distinct rule.
     - Full SF=10 generic after this pass sampled Dodam `~491.8ms`, DuckDB `~571.7ms` (`0.86x`). The new `UNION DISTINCT` cases are no longer the bottleneck; remaining slower cases are still low-selectivity three-key aggregate and non-monotonic top-k scrambled.
     - Lowered the block/vector distinct rule from `UNION DISTINCT`-local code into a shared direct distinct scan rule used by both `UNION DISTINCT` and plain `SELECT DISTINCT`.
       - This treats distinct as a projection-level pushdown: compatible `SELECT DISTINCT ... WHERE key IN (...)` shapes now collect distinct values/pairs during scan rather than emitting all rows and deduplicating with `RowConverter` at the end.
       - Current shared direct distinct coverage:
         - single projected null-free `i32` filter column
         - `(i32 filter column, i64 payload column)` projection
       - SF=10 focused common distinct suite:
         - `union_distinct_low_cardinality`: Dodam `~8.7ms`, DuckDB `~25.9ms` (`0.34x`)
         - `union_distinct_rows`: Dodam `~35.1ms`, DuckDB `~50.4ms` (`0.70x`)
         - `select_distinct_low_cardinality`: Dodam `~7.5ms`, DuckDB `~18.2ms` (`0.41x`)
         - `select_distinct_rows`: Dodam `~29.4ms`, DuckDB `~35.4ms` (`0.83x`)
       - Full SF=10 generic with both UNION and SELECT distinct cases sampled Dodam `~527.7ms`, DuckDB `~652.9ms` (`0.81x`). Remaining slower cases were unrelated/noisy scan-heavy aggregate shapes.
       - Next generalization: widen direct distinct beyond i32/i64 pairs to nullable primitives, string dictionary ids, and multi-column mixed primitive keys using the same `column blocks -> local distinct set -> merged distinct batch` boundary.
       - Widened direct distinct primitive coverage:
         - single projected `i32/i64` with positive literal filter now shares one primitive presence rule
         - two-column primitive pairs now cover null-free `i32/i64` combinations instead of only `(i32 filter, i64 payload)`
         - single nullable `i32/i64` inputs can stay on the primitive path by skipping nulls when the positive literal filter excludes NULL
       - Added generic benchmark cases:
         - `union_distinct_i64_rows`
         - `union_distinct_i64_i32_rows`
       - SF=1 focused after widening:
         - `union_distinct_low_cardinality`: Dodam `~4.7ms`, DuckDB `~20.0ms` (`0.23x`)
         - `union_distinct_rows`: Dodam `~15.2ms`, DuckDB `~29.1ms` (`0.52x`)
         - `union_distinct_i64_rows`: Dodam `~6.9ms`, DuckDB `~19.0ms` (`0.36x`)
         - `union_distinct_i64_i32_rows`: Dodam `~8.0ms`, DuckDB `~19.6ms` (`0.41x`)
         - `select_distinct_low_cardinality`: Dodam `~4.9ms`, DuckDB `~18.5ms` (`0.27x`)
         - `select_distinct_rows`: Dodam `~14.9ms`, DuckDB `~21.8ms` (`0.68x`)
       - Remaining work:
         - nullable pair distinct still falls back because output null bitmap semantics must be preserved
         - string dictionary-id distinct is not yet lowered into this direct rule
         - generic set-op fallback still needs spill-aware row-key operators for very large operands
   - `INTERSECT` / `INTERSECT DISTINCT`
     - Implemented correctness-first materialized row-key fallback for `INTERSECT DISTINCT`/default `INTERSECT`, using the same Arrow row equality semantics as `DistinctExec` and DuckDB differential coverage over single-column and multi-column rows.
     - Added generic benchmark case `intersect_distinct_rows`.
     - Initial materialized fallback: Dodam `~50.6ms`, DuckDB `~29.2ms` (`1.73x`).
     - Lowered the same-source positive literal filter rule from `UNION DISTINCT` to `INTERSECT DISTINCT`: compatible operands over the same Parquet file/projection now turn filter predicates into a single literal-set intersection and reuse the shared direct distinct scan path. Focused result improved to Dodam `~9.9ms`, DuckDB `~29.9ms` (`0.33x`).
   - `EXCEPT` / `EXCEPT DISTINCT`
     - Implemented correctness-first materialized row-key fallback for `EXCEPT DISTINCT`/default `EXCEPT`, preserving distinct left-row order before final `ORDER BY`.
     - Added generic benchmark case `except_distinct_rows`.
     - Initial materialized fallback: Dodam `~44.6ms`, DuckDB `~22.5ms` (`1.98x`).
     - Lowered the same-source positive literal filter rule to `EXCEPT DISTINCT`: compatible operands over the same Parquet file/projection now turn filter predicates into a single literal-set difference and reuse the direct distinct scan path. Focused result improved to Dodam `~10.0ms`, DuckDB `~29.0ms` (`0.35x`).
   - `INTERSECT ALL` / `EXCEPT ALL`
     - Implemented correctness-first materialized row-count fallback for `INTERSECT ALL` and `EXCEPT ALL`.
       - `INTERSECT ALL` builds right-side row multiplicity counts and emits left rows while decrementing the available right count.
       - `EXCEPT ALL` builds right-side row multiplicity counts and drops only the matching number of left rows.
     - Added DuckDB differential coverage and generic benchmark cases:
       - `intersect_all_rows`
       - `except_all_rows`
     - SF=1 focused initial results:
       - `intersect_all_rows`: Dodam `~18.7ms`, DuckDB `~27.8ms` (`0.67x`)
       - `except_all_rows`: Dodam `~18.7ms`, DuckDB `~28.1ms` (`0.67x`)
     - Lowered same-source single-`i32` projection/filter `ALL` cases into a primitive block count rule:
       - `INTERSECT ALL` emits `min(left_count, right_count)` for overlapping literal values.
       - `EXCEPT ALL` emits `left_count - right_count` for left literal values.
       - This avoids two operand scans, Arrow row conversion, and full row materialization for the covered shape.
     - SF=1 focused after primitive count rule:
       - `intersect_all_rows`: Dodam `~4.8ms`, DuckDB `~27.8ms` (`0.17x`)
       - `except_all_rows`: Dodam `~5.2ms`, DuckDB `~29.2ms` (`0.18x`)
     - Widened the primitive count rule from `i32` only to null-free `i32/i64`.
       - Added DuckDB differential coverage for `INTERSECT ALL`/`EXCEPT ALL` over `id` (`Int64`).
       - Added generic benchmark cases `intersect_all_i64_rows` and `except_all_i64_rows`.
       - SF=1 focused after widening:
         - `intersect_all_rows`: Dodam `~4.0ms`, DuckDB `~22.8ms` (`0.17x`)
         - `except_all_rows`: Dodam `~4.1ms`, DuckDB `~24.2ms` (`0.17x`)
         - `intersect_all_i64_rows`: Dodam `~5.6ms`, DuckDB `~16.5ms` (`0.34x`)
         - `except_all_i64_rows`: Dodam `~6.2ms`, DuckDB `~14.7ms` (`0.42x`)
     - Next performance step: widen the primitive count rule to nullable primitives, string dictionary ids, and multi-column primitive keys. The generic fallback remains correct but still uses Arrow row conversion and full operand materialization.
2. JOIN generality
   - non-equi join fallback
     - Added an explicit SQL error test for pure non-equi `JOIN ... ON` without an equality key. Current behavior is intentionally rejected because the residual filter references both inputs; a future nested-loop/range join operator should replace this test with DuckDB differential correctness.
   - complex `ON` residual predicates
   - expression join keys
3. Window functions
   - aggregate window coverage beyond the current `count/sum/avg OVER (...)` one-table path
   - `lag`, `lead`, `first_value`, `last_value`
   - basic `ROWS BETWEEN` frames
4. Aggregate syntax
   - `FILTER (WHERE ...)`
   - broader aggregate/window integration for joins, grouped inputs, and explicit frames
   - `WITHIN GROUP` later
   - Large-data follow-up: grouped aggregate and set-op fallbacks still need spill-aware physical operators. The current `INTERSECT/EXCEPT ALL` generic fallback materializes both operands and uses Arrow row conversion; only the same-source primitive rule avoids that boundary.
5. Scalar expression/function coverage
   - `ILIKE`
   - `LIKE ANY`
   - simple `CASE x WHEN ...`
   - broader date/string/math functions
6. Subquery generality
   - `ANY` / `ALL` / `SOME`
   - SELECT-list scalar subqueries
   - deeper nested correlated subqueries
7. GROUP BY modifiers
   - `GROUP BY ALL` - implemented for single-table and JOIN aggregate SELECTs by expanding non-aggregate SELECT expressions into group keys, including scalar expression keys through the existing synthetic group-expression binding path. Added DuckDB differential coverage and a generic benchmark case.
     - Lowered the common expression-key shape `lower(coalesce(utf8_col, literal)), int_col + literal, count(*), sum(int)` into an expression-view aggregate collector. This bypasses full Arrow expression-column materialization and feeds original column blocks directly into the two-key count/sum group index.
     - Focused generic benchmark improved from roughly `~83ms` before vectorized lowering to `~31ms` after lowering (`standard_group_by_all_expression`), with profile showing `aggregate grouped expression-view` instead of `aggregate grouped append/fold`.
     - Follow-up experiment: preserved DictionaryArray ids through the `lower(coalesce(...))` expression reader and used a `(dictionary id, second key)` group-id cache for dictionary-first expression keys. Correctness checks passed, but the focused benchmark stayed around `~42ms` vs DuckDB `~28ms` on the current fixture. This suggests the measured fixture is mostly paying plain UTF8/hash lookup plus scan/decode cost, not dictionary-id lookup alone.
     - Follow-up: remaining gap is mostly plain UTF8 first-key lookup and scan/decode cost. Further work should add a transformed-key string-id cache for plain UTF8 expression keys and then move more expression aggregates to the same `column blocks -> expression view -> typed group update` boundary.
   - `ROLLUP`
   - `CUBE`
   - `GROUPING SETS`
8. Nested/list/struct SQL
   - `UNNEST`
   - list contains/overlap functions
   - broader struct/list expressions
9. DDL/DML with Iceberg
   - `CREATE TABLE`, `CREATE VIEW`
   - `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `MERGE`
10. Product-level SQL/database features
   - transactions
   - durable catalog evolution
   - permissions/resource management

## Done

### Core Execution

- Built a vectorized Arrow `RecordBatch` execution path for local Parquet scan, filter, projection, limit, sort, distinct, aggregation, and join.
- Added local Parquet file and directory planning with stable fragment order, schema/statistics discovery, metadata cache, projection pushdown, and row-group pruning.
- Added global and grouped aggregation for `COUNT(*)`, `COUNT(col)`, `SUM`, `AVG`, `MIN`, and `MAX`.
- Added faster grouped aggregation paths for common numeric and string keys.
- Added `INNER`, `LEFT OUTER`, `RIGHT OUTER`, `FULL OUTER`, and `LEFT SEMI` joins.
- Added hash join, sort-merge join, partitioned spill hash join, and chunked file-hash join execution.
- Added multi-column equality joins, build-side selection from estimates, partition count calculation, spill repartitioning, heavy-hitter separation, and join metrics.
- Added join memory safeguards: recursive repartitioning, bounded output chunks, bloom-style filtering metrics, skew handling, and nested-loop fallback for pathological spill partitions.
- Added output projection pushdown into hash join and partitioned/spill hash join where semantics allow it.
- Added SQL aggregate execution over streamed join output batches, including global aggregate, grouped aggregate, `HAVING`, aggregate aliases, `ORDER BY`, and `LIMIT`.
- Added dense `Int32` unique join key fast path and `Int64` single-key fast paths.
- Added dense `Int32` bitmap matched-build tracking for outer joins.

### SQL, CLI, COPY

- Added SQL parsing through `sqlparser`.
- Supported projection, column aliases, table aliases, qualified columns, filters, grouping, `HAVING`, `ORDER BY`, `LIMIT`, `DISTINCT`, joins, and `EXPLAIN`.
- Added streaming SQL execution for simple joins to avoid collecting all join output before CLI output.
- Added Dodam `COPY (SELECT ...) TO 'file' (FORMAT CSV)` for fair DuckDB-style benchmark comparison.
- Added Dodam `COPY (SELECT ...) TO 'file' (FORMAT PARQUET)`.
- Added configurable Parquet COPY options:
  - `COMPRESSION SNAPPY|UNCOMPRESSED`
  - `DICTIONARY true|false`
  - `ROW_GROUP_SIZE <rows>`
  - `WRITE_BATCH_SIZE <rows>`
  - `DATA_PAGE_ROW_COUNT_LIMIT <rows>`
- Added execution sink integration: `RecordBatchSink`, stream-to-sink drain helper, `PhysicalPlan::execute_to_sink`, and engine-level sink execution helpers.
- Routed CLI COPY and streaming SELECT through the engine sink boundary.
- Added `HashJoinExec::execute_to_sink` and SQL-level `try_execute_sql_to_sink`.
- Added configurable COPY output buffer:
  - CLI: `--copy-buffer-size <bytes>`
  - env: `DODAM_COPY_BUFFER_BYTES`
  - default: `8 MiB`
- Replaced `arrow-csv` writer path with a custom CSV sink using batch-level `Vec<u8>`, `BufWriter<File>`, `itoa`, and `ryu`.
- Added CSV serialization hot paths for single `Int32` and `Int32, Utf8` output shapes.
- Guarded direct sink/streaming join output so outer joins with SELECT-specific projection fall back to the correct materialized projection path.

### Storage And Catalog Layering

- Split major layers into catalog, storage, engine, logical execution, physical execution, optimizer, cost model, and SQL.
- Added `TableScanSource`, `StorageLocation`, `StorageFormat`, `ObjectStore`, and format-dispatching `ScanExec`.
- Added local filesystem object-store abstraction and Parquet table/source planning.
- Kept S3/object storage on hold until the local OLAP engine and execution model are stronger.
- Added a persistent local workspace catalog under `.dodam/catalog.json`.
- Added `dodam catalog register <name> <path>` and `dodam catalog list`.
- Added SQL resolution for registered table names such as `SELECT * FROM orders` while preserving direct path queries.
- Added catalog registration snapshots for schema fields, table statistics, and discovered partition columns.
- Added catalog registration snapshots for file fragments, fragment statistics, and fragment partition values.
- Changed registered table scans to use the catalog fragment snapshot instead of recursively rediscovering the directory on every query.
- Added `dodam catalog refresh <name>` to rebuild a registered table snapshot from its stored location.
- Added Hive-style directory partition discovery on file fragments.
- Added directory partition pruning for positive `=` and `IN` predicates before Parquet row-group planning.
- Added scan-time materialization of Hive partition columns so they can be selected, ordered, filtered after pruning when needed, and grouped.
- Added Parquet scan compatibility coverage for nullable and richer Arrow types:
  - nullable `Boolean`, `Float64`, and `Utf8`
  - `Decimal128(10, 2)`
  - `Timestamp(Millisecond)`
  - `List<Int32>`
  - `Struct<rank: Int32, label: Utf8>`
- Added filter execution support and SQL coverage for richer scalar Parquet types:
  - boolean literals and boolean comparison
  - decimal comparison against numeric/string literals
  - timestamp comparison against `YYYY-MM-DD` and `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` string literals
  - timezone-aware timestamp comparison using the same epoch semantics as Arrow
  - `Date32`/`Date64` comparison against `YYYY-MM-DD` string literals
- Added Parquet row-group pruning for richer scalar statistics:
  - boolean min/max statistics
  - `Float64` min/max statistics
  - `Date32`/`Date64` statistics
  - decimal statistics stored as `Int32`, `Int64`, `ByteArray`, or `FixedLenByteArray`
  - timezone-aware and timezone-less timestamp statistics stored as `Int64`

### Correctness And Compatibility

- Added a DuckDB differential test harness that skips cleanly when the `duckdb` CLI is unavailable.
- Added initial DuckDB differential coverage for:
  - scan/filter/null predicates, including `IS NULL`, `IS NOT NULL`, `NOT`, and `IN`
  - global and grouped aggregate matrix over count/sum/avg/min/max
  - inner/left/right/full/semi joins with duplicate build-side keys and unmatched rows
  - multi-key joins that combine integer and string keys
  - deterministic `ORDER BY` + `LIMIT`
  - scalar type filters over boolean, float, decimal, timestamp, and date columns
  - derived-table subqueries in `FROM`
  - uncorrelated `IN (SELECT ...)` / `NOT IN (SELECT ...)` subqueries
  - uncorrelated scalar subquery filters
  - uncorrelated top-level `EXISTS` / `NOT EXISTS` filters
- Expanded DuckDB differential coverage for NULL semantics:
  - `IN` / `NOT IN` with literal `NULL` inside `OR`
  - scalar subquery `NULL` comparisons
  - left join behavior for NULL join keys
- Expanded type compatibility coverage and fixes:
  - decimal literals with trailing fractional zeros beyond column scale
  - timezone offset timestamp literals such as `+09:00`, normalized to epoch semantics
  - matching filter and row-group pruning interpretation for decimal/timestamp literals
  - SQL projection coverage for nested `List` and `Struct` columns
- Added first-stage SQL scalar projection expressions for single-table non-aggregate SELECT queries:
  - numeric arithmetic with `+`, `-`, `*`, `/`
  - `CAST(... AS VARCHAR/INT/DOUBLE)`
  - `COALESCE(...)`
  - DuckDB differential coverage for arithmetic, cast, and coalesce projection output
- Extended the same scalar expression evaluator to single-table non-aggregate `WHERE` predicates:
  - arithmetic/cast/coalesce comparisons
  - expression `IS NULL` / `IS NOT NULL`
  - DuckDB differential coverage for computed `WHERE` output
- Added first-stage string functions and searched `CASE` support in scalar expressions:
  - `lower`, `upper`, and `length` in projection and computed `WHERE`
  - `CASE WHEN ... THEN ... ELSE ... END` for searched CASE expressions
  - DuckDB differential coverage for string functions and CASE output
- Added a TPC-H-lite DuckDB differential suite over lineitem/orders/customer-shaped Parquet fixtures:
  - Q1-style date-filtered grouped aggregate with multi-column `GROUP BY`
  - Q6-style date/discount/quantity filtered aggregate
  - Q3-style customer/orders join aggregate
- Added a full TPC-H 22-query support inventory harness:
  - records the current parser/planner support status for each canonical TPC-H query
  - turns unsupported reasons into a regression-tested baseline
  - current dominant blockers are `WITH`, `count(DISTINCT ...)`, and richer nested/correlated subquery shapes
- Improved TPC-H Q1/Q6 coverage:
  - added aggregate input expressions such as `sum(l_extendedprice * l_discount)` by materializing deterministic temporary expression columns before aggregation
  - added constant folding for numeric literal arithmetic in predicates
  - added `DATE 'YYYY-MM-DD' +/- INTERVAL` folding for day/month/year intervals
  - added `BETWEEN` / `NOT BETWEEN` lowering to comparison predicates
  - TPC-H inventory now advances Q1 and Q6 to `needs-data:lineitem`
  - TPC-H-lite Q6 now executes expression aggregate + `BETWEEN` + date interval arithmetic under DuckDB differential comparison
- Added a canonical-shape TPC-H Q6 DuckDB differential fixture:
  - uses ratio-style `l_discount` values such as `0.06`
  - compares `sum(l_extendedprice * l_discount)` with decimal literal bounds `0.06 +/- 0.01`
  - fixed literal folding so numeric `0.06 + 0.01` preserves decimal scale before converting to `Float64`; naive f64 folding produced `0.069999...` and incorrectly excluded the `0.07` boundary row
- Added first-stage SQL `LIKE` / `NOT LIKE` filters for `Utf8` columns:
  - supports `%`, `_`, and single-character `ESCAPE`
  - keeps `NULL LIKE pattern` as SQL unknown/null in filter masks
  - added DuckDB differential coverage for `LIKE`, `NOT LIKE`, `_`, `%`, `ESCAPE`, and null inputs
- Advanced TPC-H Q13 planning:
  - explicit table aliases are no longer required for JOIN inputs; unaliased joins use the table/file stem as the execution prefix
  - unqualified equality columns in JOIN `ON` are accepted left-to-right, covering canonical `c_custkey = o_custkey` style predicates
  - right-side-only residual predicates in `INNER`/`LEFT`/`SEMI` JOIN `ON` clauses are pushed into the right input filter, covering `AND o_comment NOT LIKE ...`
  - unqualified join projection, `GROUP BY`, and aggregate arguments can now be inferred from TPC-H-style column prefixes such as `c_` and `o_`
  - added `UInt64` group key support so outer grouping by `count(...)` aliases such as Q13's `c_count` preserves the count result type
  - TPC-H inventory now advances Q13 to `needs-data:customer`
- Added first-stage SQL `SUBSTRING(expr FROM start FOR length)` scalar expression support:
  - works in projection and computed `WHERE` expression filters, including expression `IN (...)`
  - uses SQL 1-based character indexing and preserves NULL propagation
  - added DuckDB differential coverage for projection and WHERE `IN`
  - TPC-H Q22 advanced past SELECT expression parsing; later expression-filter/subquery work advances it to `needs-data:customer`
- Added first-stage 2-table comma join planning:
  - lowers `FROM a, b WHERE a_key = b_key AND ...` to the existing inner hash join execution path
  - extracts one or more equality join keys from top-level `AND` predicates and keeps the remaining predicates as post-join filters
  - supports explicit aliases and TPC-H-style inferred unqualified columns through the existing join column resolver
  - added SQL coverage comparing comma join output with the existing join path
  - TPC-H inventory now advances Q12/Q14/Q19 from FROM-list parsing to join aggregate expression blockers, while 3+ table queries report the explicit 2-table comma join limitation
- Added correctness-first multi-table comma join planning:
  - supports 3+ table `FROM a, b, c...` inner joins by materializing left-deep hash joins over equality predicates from top-level `AND` filters
  - preserves residual filters, grouped aggregates, aggregate input expressions, `ORDER BY`, `LIMIT`, aliases, and `DISTINCT` over the final joined batches
  - added SQL coverage for a 3-table TPC-H-shaped `customer/orders/lineitem` grouped aggregate
  - TPC-H inventory now advances Q2/Q3/Q5/Q7/Q8/Q9/Q10/Q11/Q18/Q21 past the previous multi-table `FROM` parser/planner blockers to `needs-data:*`
- Added join aggregate input expression support:
  - materializes deterministic temporary expression columns over join output before aggregation, matching the existing single-table aggregate expression path
  - supports arithmetic and searched `CASE` inside join aggregate inputs
  - disables join projection narrowing for aggregate-expression joins to keep correctness simple
  - Q19 now advances to join equality extraction under OR branches
- Added column-to-column comparison filters:
  - supports `column op column` predicates in single-table filters and join residual filters
  - compares Arrow arrays directly and keeps row-group/partition pruning conservative for these predicates
  - added SQL coverage for single-table and comma-join residual column comparisons
  - TPC-H inventory now advances Q12 to `needs-data:orders`
- Added aggregate-output scalar projection for join aggregates:
  - supports expressions such as `100.00 * sum(...) / sum(...)` by evaluating scalar expressions over the aggregate result batch
  - reuses aggregate input expression materialization for nested `sum(l_extendedprice * (1 - l_discount))` and searched `CASE` inputs
  - added scalar predicate `LIKE` support inside expression `CASE` evaluation for join aggregate inputs
  - TPC-H inventory now advances Q14 to `needs-data:lineitem`
- Added comma join key extraction from OR branches:
  - detects equality join keys that are common to every top-level OR branch, then keeps the original OR predicate as the residual filter
  - added `BETWEEN` lowering for join residual filters
  - added SQL coverage for comma joins whose equality predicate appears inside OR branches
  - TPC-H inventory now advances Q19 to `needs-data:lineitem`
- Added expression-filter aggregate and derived projection coverage:
  - supports aggregate queries with scalar expression predicates such as `avg(c_acctbal) WHERE substring(c_phone...) IN (...)`
  - supports scalar expression projection over materialized derived-table rows
  - expression and subquery predicates can be split across top-level `AND` conjuncts so each side uses the appropriate evaluator
  - TPC-H inventory now advances Q22 to `needs-data:customer`
- Added aggregate support to the row-bound correlated subquery fallback:
  - supports grouped aggregate outer queries filtered by correlated `EXISTS` / `NOT EXISTS`
  - binds both explicit alias references and TPC-H-style unqualified outer columns such as `o_orderkey`
  - added SQL coverage for a Q4-shaped grouped aggregate with correlated `EXISTS`
  - TPC-H inventory now advances Q4 to `needs-data:orders`
- Added first-stage `count(DISTINCT column)` aggregate support:
  - supports global and grouped distinct counts through the generic aggregate accumulator
  - keeps fast aggregate paths on non-distinct aggregates and falls back to generic for distinct counts
  - added SQL coverage for grouped `count(DISTINCT id)`
  - TPC-H inventory now advances Q16 to the join residual `NOT IN (subquery)` blocker
  - TPC-H Q17/Q20 also advance past earlier FROM-list routing blockers to their scalar/nested subquery blockers
- The TPC-H-lite suite caught missing `Date32` `min`/`max` aggregate support; added `Date32` aggregate state and `Date32Array` result materialization.
- The TPC-H-lite join aggregate case caught an aggregate join output projection correctness issue around qualified column names when the cost model chooses the left side as the hash build input.
- Fixed build-side-aware join output projection mapping for hash/materialized join output schemas and restored aggregate join output projection pushdown.
- Extended temporal `min`/`max` aggregate support beyond `Date32`:
  - `Date64`
  - `Timestamp(Millisecond)` with timezone metadata preserved in SQL result batches
  - Added SQL tests that validate output Arrow types and epoch values directly; DuckDB CSV differential is intentionally avoided for these output values because DuckDB CLI formats `Date64`/timestamp results differently.
- The first differential grouped-aggregate case caught a real correctness bug: grouped `min(Utf8)` / `max(Utf8)` on the single-key fast path produced `NULL` because empty min/max state defaulted to `Int64(NULL)`.
- Fixed the grouped aggregate fast path so min/max states keep the expected output null type (`Int64`, `Float64`, or `Utf8`) even when a group starts with nulls.
- Added first-stage subquery support for `FROM (SELECT ...) AS alias` by materializing the inner query to in-memory `RecordBatch`es and applying the outer projection/filter/group/order/limit over those batches.
- Added `DISTINCT` support over derived tables.
- Added first-stage derived table join support by materializing join inputs and reusing `HashJoinExec`.
- Added aggregate and `DISTINCT` support over derived table joins.
