      - Normal aggregate scans and late-materialized aggregate attempts now use the same helper, so future optimizer rules can decide between full scan and late payload reads from one place instead of duplicating projection logic.
      - `DODAM_COLUMN_READ_PROFILE=1` prints this decision; `DODAM_PARQUET_COLUMN_PROFILE=1` also enables it so column role decisions and Parquet bytes/encoding attribution can be read together.
    - Tried and rejected preserving Parquet dictionary arrays for Q01's tiny string group keys:
      - Added a local opt-in experiment that forced `l_returnflag/l_linestatus` to Arrow `Dictionary<Int32, Utf8>` and consumed dictionary keys directly in the Q01 aggregate loop.
      - Correctness matched Q01, and Q01-only warm samples improved only from roughly `0.068-0.085s` to `0.065-0.070s`.
      - Full SF=1 TPC-H `query-file` Parquet COPY was unchanged (`~0.68-0.70s` warm), and a global column-name env broke other queries that expect `StringArray` for `l_returnflag` unless projection-aware guards were added.
      - Conclusion: this is not a meaningful general optimization. The remaining Q01 gap is not dominated by decoding one-byte string group keys; it is still mostly numeric column decode/materialization and scan/operator dispatch.
    - Tried and rejected forcing Q21 ordered-lineitem counting through preserve-order scan:
      - The ordered path did become active, but Q21 warmed around `0.086-0.090s` with `Q21 ordered lineitem counts` at `~75-78ms`.
      - The existing parallel row-group fallback was faster in the same run range (`~0.057-0.064s` Q21). Preserving physical order loses too much scan parallelism for SF=1 lineitem.
      - Conclusion: ordered-run aggregation is only worth revisiting if the scan can preserve row-group order while still decoding row groups in parallel and merging boundary states deterministically.
    - Added Q21 parallel ordered-lineitem boundary merge:
      - Reworked the ordered Q21 lineitem path to use `parquet_row_group_map`, preserving parallel row-group decode while returning chunk outputs in row-group order.
      - Each chunk counts fully internal orders and returns first/last order-state boundaries. The final merge stitches adjacent boundaries deterministically before counting qualifying suppliers.
      - Same-binary comparison: fallback Q21 was `~0.056-0.063s`; the new ordered partial merge was `~0.017-0.018s`, with identical Q21 Parquet output. Full SF=1 `query-file` warmed around `0.664-0.676s`.
      - Generalized the boundary stitching into `merge_ordered_row_group_chunks` plus reusable ordered boundary/chunk types in the engine layer. Q21 now supplies only its selected-order state merge and emit logic. Post-generalization Q21 stayed around `~0.018-0.020s` with identical output.
    - Rechecked Q09 lookup/layout options:
      - `DODAM_Q09_SUPPLYCOST_FANOUT=1` was not stable enough to become the default. One run set favored it slightly, but the next same-binary comparison had packed supply-cost lookup faster (`packed ~0.047-0.054s`, fanout/default attempt ~0.052-0.053s` for Q09 warm samples). Kept packed as default and retained fanout as opt-in.
      - Added Q09 supply-cost layout profiling (`layout`, `entries`, `part_keys`, `max_fanout`) under `DODAM_TPCH_PROFILE=1`; SF=1 uses `packed_u64`, `42656` entries, `10664` part keys.
      - Added a packed/fallback accumulator for Q09 `(nationkey, year)` profit groups to avoid tuple hash keys on the hot group update. Q09 output matched prior Parquet output; Q09 samples were mixed (`~0.046-0.059s`), while full `query-file` warm samples were `~0.641-0.671s`. Retain as a small general packed-key cleanup, not a major speed claim.
    - Continued the latest fast-path generalization pass:
      - Tried converting Q10's `orderkey -> custkey` returned-order map to `AdaptiveI64Map`; it regressed focused Q10 from the retained `~45ms` range to about `~75-79ms`, likely from dense-range/cache cost and conversion/materialization overhead. Reverted it.
      - Reordered Q12 shipping-mode predicates so cheap Date32 predicates run before UTF-8 shipmode slicing/comparison in both the typed aggregate loop and the late-materialized selector. Focused Q12 samples were small/neutral-positive around `~46-50ms`, and output matched prior results.
      - Extended `merge_ordered_row_group_chunks` to emit the full boundary (`key + state`) instead of state only, then rewired the engine's ordered `i64 + Decimal128 SUM > threshold` merge onto the same helper used by Q21. This removes the separate Q18-style carry merge logic and makes ordered row-group boundary stitching a common engine rule. Full SF=1 in-process checks stayed `22/22`; warm samples showed Q18 around `~17-19ms`, Q21 around `~17-20ms`, and Q12 around `~45-48ms`.
    - Added `--only` to the in-process TPC-H runner so focused experiments can run a single query or comma-separated query-name filters without creating temporary query inventories.
    - Rechecked the current Q12 opt-in paths with focused repeats:
      - `DODAM_Q12_ENABLE_ORDER_ROW_FILTER=1` and late materialization remained neutral-to-noisy for the warm single-query shape. Even though cold profile can show less `orders` payload decode, selected keys are spread enough that row-selection overhead usually offsets the saved narrow `o_orderpriority` read. Kept both non-default.
      - Q12 row-group chunk `4` plus order row-filter was also only a sub-millisecond/noise-level change, so the default remains unchanged.
    - Retained Q03 sorted integer lookup by default:
      - The Q03 lineitem revenue scan now defaults to `SortedI64Lookup` for the built order-key payload, with `DODAM_Q03_DISABLE_SORTED_ORDER_LOOKUP=1` as the rollback knob.
      - Focused SF=1 warm Q03 repeats moved from about `~39-41ms` with the hash lookup to about `~32-34ms` with the sorted contiguous lookup. This is a general probe-layout rule for large fact scans probing an immutable integer build-key set: fewer random hash-table touches beat the extra binary-search comparisons in this shape.
      - Full SF=1 in-process TPC-H still passed `22/22`; warm Q03 samples were around `~31-34ms`, with full warm totals around `~0.52-0.55s` in the local run.
    - Investigated DuckDB's Q12 advantage:
      - DuckDB `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` shows a hash-join dynamic filter pushed into the `orders` Parquet scan: `o_orderkey` min/max plus `o_orderkey IN BF(l_orderkey)`. The scan outputs about `34,850` orders rows instead of the full `1.5M`.
      - Added an opt-in Q12 orders late-materialized payload path (`DODAM_Q12_ENABLE_ORDER_LATE_MATERIALIZE=1`) that scans `o_orderkey`, builds a row selection from the pending lineitem keys, then reads only selected `o_orderpriority` rows and aggregates directly.
      - Rejected it as the default: focused warm Q12 regressed from roughly `~46-48ms` to `~54-56ms`. The extra reader setup, row-selection construction, and sparse selector application cost more than the saved priority payload decode. This confirms the next useful direction is a cheaper scan-integrated dynamic filter, not a second-pass late payload path.
      - Tried a lower-level opt-in direct Parquet column-reader path (`DODAM_Q12_ENABLE_ORDER_DIRECT_COLUMN_READER=1`) that reads `o_orderkey` first and conditionally decodes/skips `o_orderpriority` with `ColumnReader::skip_records`.
      - The first selective version reduced cached/read bytes but regressed warm Q12 (`~64-77ms` versus the default `~60-70ms`) because Q12's selected orders are sparse and randomly distributed: about `29K` hits across `1.5M` orders produced about `28.5K` payload runs.
      - Added an adaptive guard that only uses selective payload decode when the per-batch run count is low; Q12 correctly falls back to full payload batch reads (`selective_batches=0`, `full_batches=96`), but the direct reader still remains slower (`~65-85ms`) than the Arrow dictionary-preserved default because it bypasses the existing Arrow reader/cache pipeline and does not eliminate the dominant lineitem-side work.
      - Conclusion: page/column-level fused payload decode is only useful when selectors are clustered enough to skip whole pages or large runs. For Q12's random dynamic-filter shape, the better general direction is scan-integrated Bloom/dynamic filtering plus cheaper pipeline/materialization, not per-hit payload skipping.
      - Added a default Q12 lineitem dictionary-preserved `l_shipmode` row-group map path with `DODAM_Q12_DISABLE_LINEITEM_DICTIONARY_SHIPMODE=1` as the rollback knob.
      - Initial implementation exposed an important engine contract bug: dictionary schema projection can return projected batches in schema/physical order (`l_orderkey,l_commitdate,l_receiptdate,l_shipdate,l_shipmode`) instead of requested projection order. The Q12 dictionary path now resolves columns by name before entering the typed loop.
      - Focused Q12 on/off results were effectively neutral (`~0.57s` total over 8 repeats in both cases), but profile shows the dictionary path can reduce warm lineitem chunk consume/read costs slightly by comparing integer dictionary keys instead of UTF-8 bytes. Kept it as a small general low-cardinality string cleanup, not a major speed claim.
      - Fixed the projection identity issue at the storage boundary: `ParquetBatchReader` now preserves requested `Projection::Columns` order for every returned `RecordBatch`, including dictionary-preserved scans and row-selection scans.
      - Added a regression test that scans `payload,id` with dictionary-preserved `payload` and asserts the returned batch schema remains `payload,id`. This keeps query/execution code from having to defensively rediscover columns whenever the Arrow Parquet reader changes projected output order.
      - Validation after the storage-level fix: `cargo test -q` passed; focused Q12 passed; SF=1 TPC-H in-process `66/66 ok` with warm totals in the existing range (`~0.51-0.55s`).
      - Started closing the fast-path instrumentation gap: generic `ScanExec` already had an aggregate scan profile, and now row-group-map plus dictionary row-group-map return common per-chunk scan metrics and print one operator-level summary line. This makes Q12 lineitem-style fast paths easier to compare against normal scan profiles. Late-materialized and direct-column paths still need the same treatment.
      - Tightened the fused scan/aggregate primitive:
        - `parquet_row_group_map` now has an internal metrics-aware result form, and `parquet_scan_fold_chunks` logs scan `read_next`, consumer, and final merge time in one scan-fold line instead of reporting only the near-zero final merge as `fused_total`.
        - Added `DodamEngine::parquet_scan_accumulate_chunks`, a reusable variant where each decoded batch mutates the worker-local partial state directly. Q01 now uses this path, avoiding `Map(batch) -> Q01GroupSlots -> merge` for every batch.
        - Effect is useful but limited: focused SF=1 Q01 warm samples moved from roughly `~0.026s` to `~0.020-0.024s`, while summed consumer time stayed around `~64-68ms`. This confirms the dominant remaining Q01 cost is still Parquet/Arrow reader decode and RecordBatch materialization, not small-group aggregate state merging.
        - Validation: `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q`, and SF=1 in-process TPC-H `44/44 ok` over two repeats (`0.760s` cold-ish, `0.545s` warm in the local run).
      - Extended the accumulate experiment carefully:
        - Applied `parquet_scan_accumulate_chunks` to Q06's default scalar aggregate and Q15's supplier revenue aggregate. Q06 is effectively neutral, while Q15 focused warm samples stayed in the `~14-18ms` range and avoids one batch-local HashMap allocation/merge layer. Kept both because the change is structurally cleaner and not a measured regression.
        - Tried the same worker-local mutation idea for Q20 and Q12 lineitem. Both were rejected: Q20 stayed around `~23-25ms` focused and did not beat the retained `~19-20ms` full-run samples; Q12 lineitem `into` increased focused/full samples instead of reducing them. Likely cause is worse worker-local HashMap growth/cache behavior versus smaller batch-local maps that merge cheaply.
        - Tried a Q20 dense membership/raw-decimal inner loop for `AdaptiveI64Set` + Decimal128 quantity. It did not move Q20, so the experiment was reverted with the Q20 accumulate change.
      - Rechecked Q12 against DuckDB after the current scan changes:
        - Focused DuckDB CLI Parquet output samples: Q12 about `~36ms`; Dodam still above that.
        - Q12 lineitem row-group chunk sweep showed chunk `1` is consistently better than chunk `2/4/8` for Q12-only warm runs (`~43-45ms` versus `~46ms+` and `~50ms+`). Default Q12 lineitem chunk changed from `2` to `1`.
        - Re-tested Q12 orders row-filter/Bloom/late paths. Bloom and row-filter can look faster in one run, but same-binary repeats showed Bloom-on regressing to about `~56ms` while Bloom-off stayed around `~43-48ms`; Bloom remains opt-in. Full-run totals remain noisy, so this is a focused Q12 improvement rather than a claimed total TPC-H win.
        - Re-tested the existing Q12 orders fused row-group-map path. It looked promising in one run, but same-binary repeats with the default-on change regressed focused Q12 to about `~60-62ms`; kept it opt-in.
        - Replaced Q12 orders' one-task-per-batch aggregate with chunked batch fold and set the default orders chunk to `2`. This is a small task-granularity cleanup, not a scan breakthrough: chunk `1/2` focused samples were around `~44-45ms`, chunk `4+` was worse, and the latest full SF=1 repeat4 sample was Q12 `0.0447s`, total `0.536s`.
        - Added a reusable dictionary row-group-map API that also accepts pruning predicates, then wired Q12 lineitem's `l_receiptdate` range into it. On the current SF=1 layout it prunes `0/53` row groups, so it is structurally useful but not a Q12 speed win.
        - Tried an opt-in Q12 lineitem Arrow RowFilter path on the same `l_receiptdate` range. It regressed focused Q12 from about `~45ms` to `~52-54ms`; the row-selection/payload-filter cost is larger than the saved downstream checks for this layout. Kept it opt-in only for diagnostics.
        - Re-tested Q12 lineitem late materialization after the current changes. It can look faster in one run and has very low selected ratio (`~0.5%`), but same-binary on/off repeats and full-run samples were not stable enough to default it; it remains opt-in.
        - Found a real Q12 regression source: forcing `o_orderpriority` to dictionary arrays in the orders scan is now slower than the normal StringArray scan. Same-binary focused repeats showed dictionary priority around `~60-62ms` versus normal priority around `~44-47ms`. Changed Q12 orders dictionary priority from default-on to opt-in (`DODAM_Q12_ENABLE_ORDER_DICTIONARY_PRIORITY=1`). Latest focused Q12 warmed around `~43-45ms`; latest full SF=1 repeat4 sample was total `0.510s`.
        - Revisited Q12 orders scan/operator fusion after disabling dictionary priority. Fused row-group-map can win focused Q12 samples, and row-group chunk `2` is better than `1/4/8` for the opt-in fused path. However, same-binary full-run comparison still favored the retained stream/chunked-fold path (`fused_on` total `~0.537s`, `fused_off` total `~0.520s` in the checked run). Conclusion: scan/operator fusion needs a cost rule; keep Q12 orders fused scan opt-in with improved chunk `2`, not default.
        - Added a generic late-materialization policy gate for selector run density (`selector_runs / selected_rows`). This captures sparse selectors that have low selected ratio but many tiny runs, which are bad for second-pass row-selection payload reads.
        - Applied the gate to Q12 late materialization with `DODAM_Q12_LATE_MAX_RUNS_PER_SELECTED` default `1.25`. The policy correctly models why Q12's selector is unattractive (`~60K` runs for `~31K` selected rows), but the sample pass itself costs enough that opt-in `late_policy` is slower than default (`~53-58ms` vs `~43-45ms` in the focused run). Keep Q12 late opt-in only; the new policy is retained for future late-materialization decisions where sampling overhead is amortized by wider payloads.
        - Re-tested Q12 pending-order partial map initialization. A direct `FastHashMap` swap was rejected again because focused samples were neutral/worse and the dominant cost remains scan/decode, but giving each selective lineitem row-group partial a modest initial capacity helped stability. Sequential focused SF=1 comparison showed cap `0` over 12 repeats totaling about `0.706s`, while cap `2048` totaled about `0.569s` with warm repeats mostly `~43-45ms`. Default Q12 pending-map initial capacity is now `2048` with `DODAM_Q12_PENDING_MAP_INITIAL_CAPACITY` as the tuning knob. Full SF=1 in-process TPC-H stayed `88/88 ok`, repeat 4 total about `0.548s`, Q12 about `46ms`.
        - Pushed already-derived build-side key/date ranges into more lineitem row-group scan paths:
          - Q03 now passes both `l_orderkey` range and `l_shipdate > cutoff` to the pruning-aware row-group map.
          - Q05/Q07 now use `parquet_row_group_map_pruned` and pruned stream fallbacks; Q05 passes the candidate `l_orderkey` range and Q07 passes candidate `l_orderkey` plus `l_shipdate BETWEEN`.
          - Q08's row-filter-disabled path now uses `l_orderkey`/`l_partkey` range pruning, while the default `l_partkey` row-filter remains enabled because focused samples still favor it over pruning-only.
          - On the current SF=1 layout these predicates did not skip lineitem row groups (`53/53` still scanned), so this is retained as a general planner/execution cleanup rather than a claimed current-data speed win. Full SF=1 in-process TPC-H stayed correct (`88/88 ok`) with a warm repeat around `0.509s`; Q03/Q05/Q07/Q08 warm samples were around `32ms/25ms/28ms/35ms`.
        - Added a reusable DuckDB-vs-Dodam TPC-H comparison script:
          - `scripts/compare_tpch_duckdb.py` extracts the TPC-H SQL fixtures, runs Dodam's in-process Parquet-output benchmark, runs DuckDB CLI with matching Parquet COPY output options, and reports per-query medians, ratios, absolute gaps, median-sum ratio, and optional JSON output.
          - Smoke test on Q06/Q13 passed; full SF=1 CLI-per-query DuckDB comparison with repeats `3`, warmup `1` showed Dodam median-sum about `0.527s` vs DuckDB about `1.073s` in this harness, with Q12 the only slower Dodam query (`~47.5ms` vs DuckDB `~35.6ms`). Important caveat: this harness intentionally runs DuckDB as a fresh CLI process per query, so it is repeatable and output-equivalent but not a same-process DuckDB comparison.
        - Generalized the integer set RowFilter gate:
          - Q08/Q09/Q10 now share a key-range density policy instead of query-local switches. The policy uses `DODAM_I64_SET_ROW_FILTER_MAX_DENSITY` (default `0.35`) and `DODAM_I64_SET_ROW_FILTER_ALWAYS_ALLOW_KEYS` (default `4096`) to avoid dense/wide key sets where Arrow RowFilter spends time scanning many mostly-kept values.
          - Focused Q08/Q09/Q10 validation stayed correct; timings were noisy but did not show a correctness or obvious regression signal.
        - Tightened Parquet column attribution for the scan/decode bottleneck work:
          - `DODAM_SCAN_PROFILE=1` and `DODAM_TPCH_PROFILE=1` now also enable projected column compressed/uncompressed byte attribution, so the normal scan profile shows which decoded columns dominate without needing a second flag.
          - Row-group chunk-level column attribution is now opt-in via `DODAM_PARQUET_COLUMN_CHUNK_PROFILE=1`; the default profile stays at logical scan/plan granularity to avoid huge logs.
          - Focused Q01/Q03/Q08/Q12 profile confirms the next meaningful decode work should target large numeric/date payload columns rather than tiny low-cardinality strings. Q01 is dominated by `l_extendedprice` compressed bytes, Q03 by `l_extendedprice` plus `l_orderkey/l_shipdate`, and Q12 by `l_orderkey` plus date columns.
        - Revisited Q12 orders probe after the scan attribution work:
          - Re-tested defaulting the Q12 orders `o_orderkey` RowFilter using the shared i64 set-filter cost gate. It looked good in one focused run, but same-binary and DuckDB comparison runs regressed Q12 (`~60ms` with RowFilter default versus `~46ms` with the retained full orders scan in the checked comparison). Reverted the default; exact RowFilter remains opt-in because Arrow RowFilter setup and sparse row-selection overhead are still noisy for this narrow two-column orders scan.
          - Tried a sorted immutable pending-order lookup for the orders probe, reusing the same `SortedI64Lookup` layout that helped Q03. This avoids converting the sparse `~29K` pending order keys into a large dense vector keyed by max TPC-H orderkey, and it keeps the probe layout compact.
          - This was a large win: focused Q12 warm samples moved from roughly `~46-49ms` to `~14-16ms`. Profile shows the orders side wall time drops to about `6-8ms` after warmup, while lineitem remains the larger decode component.
        - Made sorted pending lookup the default for Q12 orders with `DODAM_Q12_DISABLE_SORTED_PENDING_LOOKUP=1` as rollback.
        - Full SF=1 TPC-H in-process stayed `22/22 ok`, with Q12 about `~15ms` and warm totals around `~0.49-0.51s`. The DuckDB CLI-per-query comparison now shows no Dodam-slower TPC-H query in this harness: median-sum Dodam `~0.503s` vs DuckDB `~1.079s`, Q12 `~15.4ms` vs DuckDB `~36.2ms`. Caveat remains that DuckDB is measured as a fresh CLI process per query.
        - Generalized the late-materialized integer-key selection fast path:
          - Added a reusable `I64SetLateSelectionState` and `i64_set_late_build_selection_batch_into` helper for the common pattern: scan one Int64 predicate/key column, build a `RowSelection`, retain selected keys, and consume a narrower payload batch by selected-key offset.
          - The helper keeps the dense-set membership shortcut, so moving Q09/Q10 onto the common rule does not throw away the previously useful dense `AdaptiveI64Set` path.
          - Rewired Q10 customer late materialization and Q09 late partkey selection to the shared helper. This is mostly a structure/generalization change, not a query-specific performance claim; it makes the rule reusable for future small-key-set + wide-payload late materialization candidates.
          - Validation: `cargo test -q` passed. Focused Q09/Q10 DuckDB single-process comparison over SF=1, repeats `8`, warmup `2`: median-sum Dodam `0.079521s` vs DuckDB `0.094000s`; Q09 `43.382ms` vs `57.500ms`, Q10 `36.140ms` vs `36.500ms`. Full SF=1 repeats `4`, warmup `1`: median-sum Dodam `0.493245s` vs DuckDB `0.562000s`; remaining slower queries by largest gap were Q03, Q08, Q16, Q07, Q22.
- First TPC-H coverage implementation target:
  - Q6 support, because it is single-table and mainly needs aggregate input expressions, `BETWEEN`, and date interval arithmetic.
  - Initial Q6 parser/execution blockers are cleared for single-table Parquet inputs, including a canonical-shape Q6 fixture; next step is real TPC-H table registration and then multi-table `FROM` planning.
- Add SQL tests for more alias and expression combinations.
- Extend richer Parquet type support beyond residual filtering:
  - explicit behavior for nested/list/struct projection in SQL and CLI output
  - casts and richer date/time literal forms
- Add more expression parser support only after parser structure remains clean.

### 7. Storage/Catalog Next Steps

- Add catalog table removal/rename commands.
- Expand directory partition pruning beyond positive `=` and `IN`:
  - range predicates for ordered partition values
  - `IS NULL`/`IS NOT NULL`
  - safe handling of `OR`
- Avoid reading a physical Parquet column when a query projects only partition columns.
- Add a real CSV scan implementation behind `ScanExec`.
- Add JSON and Arrow IPC scan implementations after CSV.
- Improve statistics:
  - column-level compressed bytes
  - null counts
  - row-group min/max exposure through catalog APIs
  - approximate NDV later
- Add Iceberg metadata/snapshot planning from manifests instead of local directory scans.
- Revisit S3/object storage later with:
  - range-read capable object store
  - remote metadata cache
  - listing abstraction
  - retry/backoff and request metrics

## Validation Checklist

- Run before considering the current branch healthy:
  - `cargo fmt`
  - `cargo test`
  - `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`
- Run before claiming performance:
  - Run benchmark variants one at a time. Do not run Dodam/DuckDB comparison jobs or env-var A/B variants in parallel, especially on SF=10+, because Parquet IO, OS page cache, and Dodam file-cache activity interfere with each other and make the numbers unusable.
  - release build
  - Dodam CLI benchmark
  - DuckDB CLI benchmark
  - engine-level criterion benchmark
  - profile with `DODAM_PROFILE_COPY=1` for COPY-heavy queries

### Benchmark Notes

- SF=10 Q04/Q12 tuning note:
  - A parallel A/B run of Q04 i64-set row-group chunk variants was discarded. Running the two benchmark processes concurrently caused IO/cache interference and produced worse, non-comparable numbers.
  - Benchmark policy going forward: every performance variant must be measured as a separate process run, with no other benchmark process active.
  - Q04 lineitem row-filter is now adaptive rather than always-on:
    - SF=1 benefits from the row-filter path (`~19-20ms` focused), but SF=10 regresses because the candidate key set is large enough that row-filter setup/selection overhead dominates.
    - Default rule: use Q04 lineitem row-filter only when candidate order keys are at or below `DODAM_Q04_LINEITEM_ROW_FILTER_MAX_KEYS` (default `100000`). `DODAM_Q04_ENABLE_LINEITEM_ROW_FILTER=1` and `DODAM_Q04_DISABLE_LINEITEM_ROW_FILTER=1` remain force knobs.
  - Q04 lineitem probe was parallelized with row-group map:
    - The old probe mutated the dense candidate-priority marker while scanning, which made the lineitem probe effectively sequential. The new default path collects matched orderkeys per row-group worker, then sort/dedups and counts priorities once at the end.
    - Chunk sweep on SF=10 Q04: chunk `1` around `~228ms`, chunk `2` around `~223ms`, chunk `4` around `~222ms`, chunk `8` around `~227ms`; default chunk remains `4` via `DODAM_Q04_LINEITEM_ROW_GROUP_MAP_CHUNK`.
    - SF=10 Q04 focused improved from current-default around `~294ms` to `~223ms`; SF=1 Q04 stayed around `~20ms`, so the row-group probe is now default with `DODAM_Q04_DISABLE_LINEITEM_ROW_GROUP_MAP=1` as rollback.
    - Full SF=10 median-sum after the Q04 row-group probe: Dodam `6.072s` vs DuckDB `5.798s` (`1.047x`), with Q04 gap down to about `~71ms`.
  - Q03 SF=10 retest:
    - Rechecked old opt-in knobs under SF=10. `DODAM_Q03_ENABLE_ORDER_ROW_GROUP_MAP=1`, `DODAM_Q03_ENABLE_REVENUE_VECTOR=1`, and row-group chunk `1/4/8` were all neutral or slower than the retained chunk `2` default.
    - Tried an opt-in orders `o_custkey` i64-set RowFilter (`DODAM_Q03_ENABLE_ORDER_ROW_FILTER=1`). It regressed Q03 focused from about `~402ms` to about `~419ms`; customer segment selectivity is not low enough to pay for Arrow RowFilter/setup overhead. Kept it opt-in only for diagnostics.
    - Switched Q03 order/revenue partial maps to `FastHashMap`. This made the hash lookup path better than the old sorted binary-search lookup on SF=10:
      - Q03 focused improved from about `~402ms` to `~366ms` with fast maps.
      - Disabling sorted lookup after fast-map conversion improved further to about `~335ms`; made hash lookup the default and left `DODAM_Q03_ENABLE_SORTED_ORDER_LOOKUP=1` as a diagnostic knob.
      - Latest Q03 focused default after the change is about `~326ms`; full SF=10 median-sum moved to Dodam `5.833s` vs DuckDB `5.778s` (`1.010x`), with Q03 gap about `~101ms`.
    - Re-tested Q03 order row-group map after fast maps; still neutral/slower (`~330ms` focused), so it remains opt-in only.
    - Tried a Q03 order stream-accumulate path to remove partial-map merge overhead. It regressed badly (`~731ms`) because it removed useful scan/batch parallelism. Kept it opt-in only for diagnostics via `DODAM_Q03_ENABLE_ORDER_STREAM_ACCUMULATE=1`.
  - Q14 dense dimension-key lookup:
    - Q14 was spending substantial CPU in the part dimension scan and lineitem promo lookup using `HashMap<i64,bool>` for dense TPC-H part keys.
    - Added reusable `DenseI64BoolLookup` and rewired Q14 SQL plus engine late-materialization path to use dense present/value arrays.
    - SF=10 Q14 focused improved from about `~344ms` in the previous full run to `~183ms`, now faster than DuckDB in the focused run (`~0.76x`). SF=1 Q14 also improved to about `~19ms` vs DuckDB `~27.5ms`.
    - Full SF=10 median-sum after Q04 row-group probe and Q14 dense lookup: Dodam `5.919s` vs DuckDB `5.787s` (`1.023x`). Remaining largest slower gaps: Q03, Q08, Q13, Q07, Q04.
  - Local Parquet file cache was a major SF=10 bottleneck:
    - With the old default 512MB cache and 4MB chunks, Q12 projected about `~470MB` compressed lineitem columns but the file cache read several GB due chunk over-read. Q04 showed the same pattern.
    - Q12 SF=10 focused: old default around `~500ms`, `DODAM_FILE_CACHE_CHUNK_BYTES=1048576` around `~323ms`, `524288` around `~242ms`, and `DODAM_FILE_CACHE_BYTES=0` around `~193ms`.
    - Q04 SF=10 focused improved from around `~611ms` after adaptive row-filter to around `~321ms` with file cache off.
    - Full SF=10 comparison improved from Dodam median-sum `12.206s` vs DuckDB `5.802s` (`2.104x`) to Dodam `6.185s` vs DuckDB `5.829s` (`1.061x`) with file cache disabled by default.
    - 512KB cache was better for small SF=1 Q04/Q12 (`~39ms` combined vs `~45ms` cache-off), but SF=10 full regressed to `~8.098s`; keep local default cache disabled and allow opt-in via `DODAM_FILE_CACHE_BYTES`.
  - SF=10 follow-up improvements after switching the comparison target to one benchmark process at a time:
    - Q08: `DODAM_Q08_PARTKEY_ROW_FILTER_ROW_GROUP_CHUNK=8` was better than the global chunk setting without hurting Q04, so Q08 now has its own default chunk. More importantly, the existing Q08 late-materialized lineitem path was much better at SF=10: focused Q08 moved from about `~507ms` to `~413ms`; made it default with `DODAM_Q08_DISABLE_LATE=1` as rollback.
    - Q13: moved LIKE-only dense count filtering to a hotter typed loop. Finder construction is now once per scan, not once per batch, and null-free Int64/StringArray batches avoid per-row schema/null checks. Focused Q13 improved from about `~382ms` to `~362ms`. Projection-index hints were retained but only moved another `~1ms`. Changing the dense count vector from `u64` to `u32` was neutral/noisy, not a clear win.
    - Q07: row-group chunk sweep (`1/4/8`) did not help; larger chunks were worse. The real win was replacing integer-key default `HashMap` usage in the order/customer and volume group maps with the reusable `FastHashMap` integer hasher. Focused Q07 improved from about `~403ms` to `~366ms`.
    - Q10: applied the same integer-key `FastHashMap` rule to orders and returned-revenue maps. Focused Q10 improved from about `~390ms` to `~370ms`.
    - Q16: profile showed `part` group build, not partsupp distinct counting, was the dominant Rust-side cost. Reordered the part-group typed loop so numeric `p_size IN (...)` is checked before touching `p_brand`/`p_type`, and used fast maps for part/group intern state. Focused Q16 improved from about `~148ms` to `~112ms`; byte-finder supplier comment matching added a smaller follow-up win to about `~108ms`.
    - Latest full SF=10 single-process DuckDB comparison after these changes: Dodam median-sum `5.583s`, DuckDB `5.845s`, ratio `0.955x`. Remaining slower gaps are led by Q04 (`~70ms`), Q13 (`~65ms`), Q03 (`~57ms`), Q07 (`~37ms`), Q05 (`~34ms`), Q12 (`~30ms`), and Q10 (`~25ms`). Q16 is no longer slower in the full run.
  - Additional SF=10 follow-up:
    - Q04: replaced the row-group probe's matched-orderkey Vec + global sort/dedup with a dense atomic marker consume path. This is a general dense-marker probe rule: each matching lineitem atomically swaps the candidate marker to zero and increments a worker-local priority count, so duplicates are suppressed without a final sort. Focused Q04 improved modestly to about `~200ms`; with the atomic path, row-group chunk `2` beat `1/4/8/16`, so Q04 default chunk changed from `4` to `2`. `DODAM_Q04_DISABLE_ATOMIC_LINEITEM_PROBE=1` remains a rollback.
    - Q13: added a two-substring byte matcher for `LIKE '%x%y%'` / `NOT LIKE '%x%y%'`, avoiding the generic parts loop in the hot path. Focused Q13 improved to about `~354ms`, but full-run samples remain noisy and Q13 is still the largest positive gap.
    - Q03: tried a dense order membership sidecar before probing the order `FastHashMap`. It regressed focused Q03 (`~340ms` in the checked run), likely because the dense sidecar's cache footprint outweighed saved hash misses. Kept it opt-in only with `DODAM_Q03_ENABLE_DENSE_ORDER_PROBE=1`.
    - Latest full SF=10 single-process DuckDB comparison: Dodam median-sum `5.525s`, DuckDB `5.820s`, ratio `0.949x`. Remaining slower gaps: Q13 (`~73ms`), Q04 (`~64ms`), Q03 (`~42ms`), Q05 (`~38ms`), Q07 (`~29ms`), Q12 (`~21ms`), Q10 (`~19ms`).
  - Q13/Q05 follow-up after the previous commit:
    - Q13 dense right-count build is now parallelized with row-group map for LIKE-only right filters. This removes the sequential consumer loop over `orders[o_custkey,o_comment]` and merges dense `u32` count vectors afterward.
    - Q13 row-group chunk sweep: chunk `2` improved Q13 to around `~290ms`, chunk `4/8` to around `~232-234ms`, chunk `16` to around `~212-214ms`, while chunk `32` regressed to around `~326ms`. Default `DODAM_DENSE_RIGHT_COUNT_ROW_GROUP_CHUNK` is now `16`.
    - Q13 focused now beats DuckDB in this harness (`~214ms` vs `~292ms`); full SF=10 sample showed Q13 `~243ms` vs DuckDB `~296ms`.
    - Q05 received the same integer-key `FastHashMap` cleanup that helped Q07/Q10: order-customer maps and revenue group maps moved off default `HashMap`. Focused Q05 improved to about `~323ms` vs DuckDB `~299ms`, reducing but not eliminating the gap.
    - Q03 order row-group map was re-tested and made default-on with `DODAM_Q03_DISABLE_ORDER_ROW_GROUP_MAP=1` as rollback. The improvement is modest/noisy (`~9ms` in one focused run), while revenue-vector/sorted lookup remains slower.
    - Latest full SF=10 single-process DuckDB comparison: Dodam median-sum `5.493s`, DuckDB `5.835s`, ratio `0.941x`. Remaining slower gaps are Q03 (`~67ms`), Q04 (`~48ms`), Q07 (`~41ms`), Q12 (`~34ms`), Q05 (`~31ms`), Q10 (`~20ms`), and Q09 (`~19ms`).
  - Q03/Q04 follow-up:
    - Q04 candidate build no longer materializes a `HashSet<i64>` unless the lineitem RowFilter path is actually selected. The default dense-priority probe only needs the dense priority vector and candidate count.
    - SF=10 Q04 focused improved from about `~212.8ms` to `~191.9ms`; lineitem row-group chunk `2` still beat chunk `1/4`, so the default remains `2`.
    - Q03 customer segment filtering now builds an `AdaptiveI64Set` directly with a null-free typed/string-byte loop. This removes the default `HashSet` build and clone before the dense membership probe.
    - SF=10 Q03 focused improved from about `~335.5ms` to `~317.4ms`.
    - Tried converting Q12 pending-order map from default `HashMap` to `FastHashMap`; it regressed focused Q12 to about `~180ms`, so the change was reverted. In this path the later dense `AdaptiveI64Map` probe dominates and the faster integer hasher is not a clear win.
    - Latest full SF=10 single-process DuckDB comparison after Q03/Q04 changes: Dodam median-sum `5.436s`, DuckDB `5.798s`, ratio `0.938x`. Remaining slower gaps are Q03 (`~56ms`), Q04 (`~45ms`), Q05 (`~31ms`), Q12 (`~30ms`), Q10 (`~25ms`), Q07 (`~25ms`), Q08 (`~16ms`), and Q11 (`~15ms`).
  - Additional Q03/Q05/Q08 follow-up:
    - Added a metadata-backed constant integer column rule for Parquet `Int32`/`Int64` columns. Q03 uses it to avoid decoding `orders.o_shippriority` when every row group has exact `min=max` and the column is non-nullable, or has explicit zero null count. This is a general constant-column projection rule with `DODAM_Q03_DISABLE_CONSTANT_SHIPPRIORITY=1` as rollback for the Q03 path.
    - Q03 orders projection dropped from `[o_orderkey,o_custkey,o_orderdate,o_shippriority]` to `[o_orderkey,o_custkey,o_orderdate]`; focused Q03 improved to about `~299ms`, and full-run Q03 sample improved to about `~284ms`.
    - Tried changing Q04 atomic consume from unconditional `swap(0)` to `load` plus `compare_exchange`; focused Q04 was neutral/slightly worse (`~193ms`), so the change was reverted.
    - Q05/Q08 customer/supplier nation dimension builds now use a shared typed integer-key path with `AdaptiveI64Set` for the small nation filter and `FastHashMap` for the output map.
    - SF=10 focused Q05 improved from about `~341ms` to `~287ms`, now faster than DuckDB in this harness. Q08 also benefited from the shared customer dimension path, improving to about `~374ms`, also faster than DuckDB in focused comparison.
    - Latest full SF=10 single-process DuckDB comparison: Dodam median-sum `5.263s`, DuckDB `5.770s`, ratio `0.912x`. Remaining slower gaps are Q04 (`~52ms`), Q12 (`~34ms`), Q03 (`~26ms`), Q10 (`~24ms`), Q09 (`~22ms`), Q07 (`~16ms`), Q11 (`~13ms`), and Q16 (`~4ms`).
  - Q10/Q12/Q04 follow-up:
    - Added a pruned late-materialization engine helper. Existing late-materialization calls still use empty pruning predicates; Q04 candidate scan now passes `o_orderdate` range predicates. On the current SF=10 data this is neutral because all orders row groups overlap the date range, but it is a general correctness-preserving capability for clustered datasets.
    - Q10 returned revenue now uses late materialization: first scan `l_orderkey,l_returnflag`, select rows with `l_returnflag='R'` whose orderkey is in the Q10 order-customer map, then decode `l_extendedprice,l_discount` only for selected rows.
    - SF=10 focused Q10 improved from about `~354ms` to `~256ms`, now faster than DuckDB in this harness. Full-run Q10 sample improved to about `~243ms` vs DuckDB `~328ms`.
    - Re-tested Q12 sorted pending lookup and lineitem late-materialization toggles. Disabling sorted lookup regressed Q12 to about `~190ms`; enabling lineitem late materialization regressed to about `~275ms`. Keep the current default dictionary row-group map plus sorted pending lookup path.
    - Latest full SF=10 single-process DuckDB comparison: Dodam median-sum `5.146s`, DuckDB `5.777s`, ratio `0.891x`. Remaining slower gaps are Q04 (`~49ms`), Q12 (`~28ms`), Q03 (`~20ms`), Q17 (`~17ms`), Q07 (`~12ms`), Q11 (`~11ms`), and Q16 (`~7ms`).
  - Q04/Q12 follow-up:
    - Added an opt-in Q04 candidate row-group map path and then made it default after A/B. It reads `orders[o_orderkey,o_orderdate,o_orderpriority]` in parallel instead of using late materialization for the tiny `o_orderpriority` payload. This lowers candidate build time in profile from about `~90ms` to about `~76-78ms`. `DODAM_Q04_DISABLE_CANDIDATE_ROW_GROUP_MAP=1` is the rollback knob.
    - Q04 candidate row-group chunk sweep: chunk `2` was best in the focused run (`~189ms` total); chunk `4` was similar/noisy (`~192ms`), chunk `8` was worse (`~196ms`). Default `DODAM_Q04_CANDIDATE_ROW_GROUP_MAP_CHUNK` is now `2`.
    - Re-tested Q04 sequential mutable lineitem probe by disabling row-group map; it regressed to about `~290ms`, so the parallel atomic probe remains the default.
    - Re-tested Q12 dictionary row-group chunk under the current code. Chunk `2` improved focused Q12 to about `~174ms`, while chunk `4` regressed to about `~188ms`; default `DODAM_Q12_ROW_GROUP_MAP_CHUNK` is now `2`.
    - Latest full SF=10 single-process DuckDB comparison: Dodam median-sum `5.058s`, DuckDB `5.811s`, ratio `0.871x`. Remaining slower gaps are Q12 (`~25ms`), Q04 (`~24ms`), Q03 (`~13ms`), Q11 (`~9ms`), Q07 (`~9ms`), and Q16 (`~5ms`).
  - Q11 follow-up:
    - Converted Q11 supplier membership from default `HashSet<i64>` to `AdaptiveI64Set`, and Q11 grouped part values from default `HashMap` to the reusable integer `FastHashMap`. The partsupp typed loop now uses Decimal128 raw values directly instead of `DecimalInput::value(row)`.
    - Added a Q11 late-materialized partsupp path: scan/select only `ps_suppkey`, then decode `ps_partkey,ps_supplycost,ps_availqty` for matching supplier rows. On SF=10 the selected ratio is about `323,920 / 8,000,000` (`~4.05%`), so it avoids most payload decode despite random selector runs.
    - Focused SF=10 Q11 after release rebuild: default late path `~44.25ms` vs late disabled `~66.50ms`; DuckDB same-process sample was around `~29ms`.
    - Q11 late row-group chunk sweep: chunk `1` was best (`~40.34ms`), chunk `2` default sample was `~44.25ms`, chunk `4` was `~45.16ms`, and chunk `8` was `~50.85ms`. Default `DODAM_Q11_LATE_ROW_GROUP_CHUNK` is now `1`.
    - After setting chunk `1` as the default and rebuilding release, focused SF=10 Q11 sampled at `~41.70ms` vs DuckDB `~29.50ms`.
    - Latest full SF=10 single-process DuckDB comparison after Q11 late default: Dodam median-sum `5.129s`, DuckDB `5.817s`, ratio `0.882x`. Q11 sampled faster than DuckDB in the full run (`~37.8ms` vs `~40.5ms`). Remaining slower gaps are Q04 (`~37.7ms`), Q09 (`~26.5ms`), Q03 (`~17.8ms`), Q12 (`~16.9ms`), Q07 (`~5.4ms`), Q16 (`~4.7ms`), and Q17 (`~1.3ms`).
    - Important failure/limit: Q11's selected rows are sparse and produce many selector runs (`~621K` runs in one profile), so this is still not a low-level page-skip win. The retained improvement comes from avoiding most payload column materialization; a deeper fused Parquet decoder would be needed to close the remaining gap to DuckDB.
  - Q04 follow-up:
    - Reordered the atomic lineitem probe hot loop to check the dense candidate marker before reading/comparing `l_commitdate,l_receiptdate`. This is a general sparse-marker probe rule: when the candidate marker is selective, defer payload/date checks until after membership. Profile showed lineitem consume sum dropping sharply, but wall-clock improvement is modest because Parquet decode/read remains dominant.
    - Added a dictionary-preserved candidate priority path for `orders.o_orderpriority` using the existing dictionary row-group-map API. This is a general low-cardinality string payload rule candidate, not a hard-coded priority-value shortcut. Focused SF=10 Q04 improved modestly: dictionary on `~169.8ms`, dictionary off `~174.3ms` in one same-binary check. `DODAM_Q04_DISABLE_CANDIDATE_DICTIONARY_PRIORITY=1` is the rollback knob.
    - Re-tested Q04 lineitem chunk after marker-first. Chunk `1` had one fast sample (`~160.0ms`) but was not stable on rerun (`~171-177ms`). Chunk `2` remained more stable (`~172.2ms` in the checked same-binary rerun), so `DODAM_Q04_LINEITEM_ROW_GROUP_MAP_CHUNK` default remains `2`.
    - Candidate chunk stayed best at `2`; candidate chunk `1`/`4` with lineitem chunk `1` regressed to about `~170.7ms`/`~172.8ms` in the checked runs.
    - Latest full SF=10 single-process DuckDB comparison after Q04 marker-first and dictionary candidate: Dodam median-sum `5.161s`, DuckDB `5.792s`, ratio `0.891x`. Remaining slower gaps are Q12 (`~27.0ms`), Q04 (`~24.6ms`), Q03 (`~19.4ms`), Q09 (`~12.3ms`), Q07 (`~9.9ms`), Q16 (`~8.2ms`), and Q17 (`~2.8ms`).

  - Q03 order-build follow-up:
    - Committed the previous late-materialization batch before this round as `c17cfdf`.
    - Q03 profile showed `orders` row-group-map consume sum around `~235ms` before order-build cleanup. Changed the Q03 orders row-group-map consumer to insert directly into the task-local accumulator instead of building a temporary per-batch `FastHashMap` and merging it. Profile consume sum dropped to about `~214ms`, though focused wall-clock movement was noisy.
    - Added capacity-aware final merge for Q03 order partial maps. This was only a small/noisy focused improvement.

  - Q09/Q04 follow-up:
    - Re-tested Q09's existing late materialization and matched-index paths on SF=10. Late materialization is still a clear failure (`~821.9ms` focused, and worse with matched-index) because the selected rows are too sparse and row-selection/second-reader overhead dominates.
    - Q09 matched-index is a clear win. It first collects matching row indexes from dense part-key membership, then performs order/supplier/supply/amount work only for matched rows. Focused Q09 moved from about `~606.4ms` default to `~596.5-598.6ms`, while DuckDB was around `~616ms`; made it default with rollback `DODAM_Q09_DISABLE_MATCHED_INDEX=1`.
    - Q09 profile with matched-index showed lineitem consume sum dropping from about `~4.97s` to about `~2.00s` across row-group tasks, and Q09 focused remained faster than DuckDB in the same-process harness.
    - Latest full SF=10 single-process DuckDB comparison after Q09 matched-index default: Dodam median-sum `5.028s`, DuckDB `5.803s`, ratio `0.867x`. Remaining slower queries in that sample were only Q04 (`~12.4ms`) and Q12 (`~3.0ms`).
    - Re-tested Q04 chunking under the current binary. Candidate chunk `2` beat `4/8` in focused runs (`~147.2ms` vs `~151.5ms`/`~156.7ms`), so `DODAM_Q04_CANDIDATE_ROW_GROUP_MAP_CHUNK` default was restored to `2`. Lineitem chunk `2` remains best among `1/2/4`.
    - Tried lower-level direct Parquet column readers to bypass Arrow `RecordBatch` materialization:
      - Q04 lineitem direct reader scanned `l_orderkey` first and selectively read `l_commitdate/l_receiptdate`; it regressed badly (`~192.0ms` vs default `~147.9ms`) because sparse skip/read runs cost more than the Arrow row-group-map path.
      - Q12 lineitem direct full-column reader decoded `l_orderkey,l_shipmode,l_commitdate,l_receiptdate,l_shipdate` directly and accumulated pending orders without RecordBatch creation; it also regressed badly (`~260.5ms` vs default `~148.4ms`).
    - Conclusion: with the current `parquet` crate low-level `ColumnReader` API, direct row/record readers are not the structural fix. The Arrow reader path is still faster because it uses optimized batch/page decode. Closing Q04/Q12 further likely requires a fused Arrow/Parquet decoder or DataFusion/arrow-level vector pipeline integration, not ad-hoc per-column `read_records` loops.
    - Applied a structural Q17 lineitem aggregation change: process stream batches in chunks and reuse one chunk-local partial state instead of allocating/merging one `HashMap`/candidate vector per `RecordBatch`. This is a general batch-dispatch/partial-state reduction pattern for small build-set scan aggregates.
      - SF=10 focused Q17, individual runs: chunk `1` `~306.8ms`, `4` `~302.1ms`, `8` `~299.7ms`, `16` `~305.0ms`, `32` `~294.2ms`, `64` `~289.7ms`, `128` `~297.6ms`; DuckDB in the same harness was `~313-317ms`.
      - Default `DODAM_Q17_LINEITEM_CHUNK_SIZE` is now `64`. This reduced dispatch/merge overhead without query-value specialization and made focused Q17 faster than DuckDB in the current SF=10 single-process comparison (`~0.920x`).
    - Re-profiled Q04 under the same-binary SF=10 harness. The lineitem stage is dominated by Arrow/Parquet `next()`/decode, not row consume (`row_group_map_summary` lineitem read-next sum around `~1000ms` across parallel tasks, consume around `~47ms`, wall stage around `~123ms` with profiling enabled).
      - Re-tested lineitem row-group chunk individually: chunk `1` `~149.0ms`, `2` `~151.1ms`, `4` `~150.2ms`, `8` `~155.7ms`; changed default `DODAM_Q04_LINEITEM_ROW_GROUP_MAP_CHUNK` to `1`.
      - Tried general predicate ordering in Q04 atomic probe by checking `l_commitdate < l_receiptdate` before random candidate-marker load. It did not improve (`~151.3ms` focused) because the date predicate does not filter enough on this data; reverted to marker-first ordering.
    - Applied a structural Q09 order-year build change: move `orders[o_orderkey,o_orderdate]` from one sequential stream consumer into the existing row-group-map pipeline, using compact chunk-local `(orderkey, year)` partials and building the final dense `orderkey -> year` map once. This is a general dense-key map-build parallelization rule with a bounded final dense range, not a query-value shortcut. Rollback: `DODAM_Q09_DISABLE_ORDER_YEAR_ROW_GROUP_MAP=1`.
      - Q09 profile before this showed `orders` scan `next_wait` around `~2ms` but `consumer_gap` around `~204ms`, so the bottleneck was the single dense-map fill loop rather than Parquet decode.
      - SF=10 focused Q09 individual runs: new row-group order-year path `~577.6ms`; old sequential path with rollback env `~606.2ms`; DuckDB in the same harness was around `~615ms`.
      - Chunk sweep: `DODAM_Q09_ORDER_YEAR_ROW_GROUP_MAP_CHUNK=2` `~591.6ms`, `4` `~577.6ms`, `8` `~577.0ms`, `16` `~594.8ms`; default is now `8`.

Q09 is now faster than DuckDB in that full sample (`~580.3ms` vs `~641.5ms`). Remaining slower queries are Q12 (`~12.3ms`), Q16 (`~9.0ms`), Q04 (`~6.0ms`), Q17 (`~1.4ms`), and Q11 (`~0.1ms`).

This failed badly and was interrupted: each row-group partial grew a large dense key range, causing memory/merge cost explosion. Reverted. Lesson: dense maps are good for the final shared lookup, but not for many parallel sparse partials unless the dense storage is sharded or built only once.

Changed `DODAM_Q12_ORDER_FUSED_ROW_GROUP_CHUNK` default from `2` to `4`; later same-binary reruns were noisy (`4` `~151.0ms`, `2` `~151.8ms`) but not a regression.

It was not stable: one focused run sampled `~92.0ms` vs sequential rollback `~102.3ms`, but repeat default was `~103.5ms` while rollback was `~99.1ms`, and profiling showed extra partial merge/string remap cost.     - Q16 dense/dictionary follow-up:
      - Added a dense direct-probe path inside Q16 packed distinct supplier counting. When `part_to_group` and bad supplier membership are both dense, the typed null-free `partsupp` loop now reads dense slices directly instead of calling `AdaptiveI64Map::get()` and `AdaptiveI64Set::contains()` for every row. Focused SF=10 Q16 improved to about `~86.4ms`; chunk `4/16` regressed, so supplier chunk default remains `8`.

      - Focused SF=10 Q16 improved to about `~78.6ms`; disabling the dictionary part path in the same binary regressed to about `~101.7ms`.
      - Tried precomputing Q12 order-priority dictionary high/low flags per batch. It did not improve (`~151.7ms` focused) because the extra per-batch Vec allocation outweighed the tiny saved byte comparisons, so it was reverted.
      - Latest full SF=10 single-process DuckDB comparison after Q16 dense/dictionary work: Dodam median-sum `5.015s`, DuckDB `5.828s`, ratio `0.860x`. Q16 is effectively parity in that sample (`~79.4ms` vs `~78.5ms`). Remaining slower queries are Q12 (`~17.3ms`), Q04 (`~9.6ms`), Q17 (`~2.0ms`), and Q16 (`~0.9ms`).
    - Scan/vector pipeline follow-up:
      - Applied a small stage-1 RecordBatch-after-cost reduction to Q12 lineitem: the projected hot path now uses fixed projected column positions for `l_orderkey,l_shipmode,l_commitdate,l_receiptdate,l_shipdate` instead of repeated schema/name lookup in the dictionary path. Focused Q12 sampled around `~149.6ms`, modest/noisy but retained as a general projected-column rule.
      - Tried stage-2 predicate ordering in Q12 dictionary lineitem by checking shipmode dictionary key before date predicates. It regressed (`~152.8ms` focused), so date-first ordering was restored.
      - Latest full SF=10 comparison after the retained Q12 direct-column and Q16 dictionary/dense changes sampled Dodam `4.950s` vs DuckDB `5.810s` (`0.852x`). Remaining slower queries in that sample were Q04 (`~30.5ms`), Q12 (`~2.6ms`), and Q03 (`~2.1ms`).
      - Re-tested Q04 row-filter forcing under the current binary (`DODAM_Q04_ENABLE_LINEITEM_ROW_FILTER=1`); it regressed to about `~156.7ms` focused. Stage-4 lower-level Parquet `ColumnReader`/direct decoder experiments remain rejected for default use because earlier Q04/Q12 direct column readers were slower than Arrow's optimized batch reader; a true win likely needs a fused page/decoder layer rather than ad-hoc `read_records` loops.
      - Started the real vector pipeline refactor with a shared `BatchView` typed-column boundary, a `BatchConsumer` handoff, and a reusable `SelectionVector` skeleton. Q12 projected lineitem now consumes a `BatchView` through a consumer object, and Q16 projected part-group hot paths use fixed projection positions through `BatchView` instead of doing local `RecordBatch`/schema/name plumbing. This is stage 1 only: it removes duplicated glue and establishes the common vector handoff, but it does not yet bypass Arrow `RecordBatch` materialization or implement a fused Parquet page decoder.
      - Validation after the stage-1 vector boundary: `cargo test -q` passed. SF=10 focused Q12 sampled Dodam `~146.4ms` vs DuckDB `~126.0ms`; focused Q16 sampled Dodam `~94.1ms` vs DuckDB `~64.0ms`, both noisy and not a claimed speed win. Full SF=10 single-process comparison sampled Dodam `5.044s`, DuckDB `5.819s`, ratio `0.867x`; slower queries were Q04 (`~19.4ms` gap), Q12 (`~14.6ms`), Q16 (`~5.0ms`), Q17 (`~4.3ms`), and Q11 (`~0.9ms`).
      - Extended the vector boundary into the engine row-group-map layer with `parquet_row_group_map_pruned_view` and `parquet_row_group_map_dictionary_columns_pruned_view`. This is a structural scan-consumer split, not a page-decoder bypass.

Full SF=10 single-process comparison sampled Dodam `5.052s`, DuckDB `5.869s`, ratio `0.861x`; slower queries were Q04 (`~22.0ms` gap), Q12 (`~15.6ms`), Q17 (`~4.1ms`), and Q16 (`~1.8ms`).
      - Added lazy projection-order caching inside `ParquetBatchReader`: the first output batch computes whether projection reorder is needed, and later batches skip schema/name matching or use cached column indices.
