      - Selection-vector experiment for Q04/Q12:
        - Added an opt-in `SelectionVector` path for Q04 lineitem date predicate before atomic candidate marker lookup and for Q12 lineitem date predicate before shipmode/hash pending updates. It is disabled by default behind `DODAM_Q04_ENABLE_LINEITEM_SELECTION_VECTOR=1` and `DODAM_Q12_ENABLE_LINEITEM_SELECTION_VECTOR=1`.
        - Focused SF=10 A/B did not show a default-worthy win. Q04 default-off sampled `~168.5ms`, opt-in `~166.6ms` in one noisy run; earlier same-binary samples were slightly worse with selection vectors. Q12 default-off sampled `~149.0ms`, opt-in `~149.2ms`. Lesson: compact row-id vectors after Arrow batch materialization do not fix the main decode/materialization cost; real improvement still needs a lower-level fused Parquet payload decoder or page-level selection.
        - Q01 small-group aggregate is already mostly in the intended shape: fixed small group slots plus scan-aggregate `BatchView` fusion. Further Q01 work should target Parquet decode/page fusion rather than another hash/group loop rewrite.
      - Q04 direct ColumnReader experiment:
        - Added an opt-in `DODAM_Q04_ENABLE_LINEITEM_DIRECT_COLUMN_READER=1` path that reads `l_orderkey`, `l_commitdate`, and `l_receiptdate` through parquet-rs `ColumnReader` and fuses predicate/probe/count without creating Arrow `RecordBatch`.
        - Focused SF=10 A/B regressed: default Arrow row-group-map path sampled `~164.3ms`, direct column reader sampled `~197.0ms`. Profile showed Q04 lineitem direct path total around `~175ms` with parallel-sum read `~805ms` and consume `~248ms`, so bypassing `RecordBatch` at this API level is slower than Arrow's optimized reader.
        - Lesson: a useful fused decoder likely needs a lower-level page/vector decoder integrated with buffering and projection, not ad-hoc `ColumnReader::read_records` loops per column.
      - Direct column scan boundary pass:
        - Moved the null-free `i64/i32/i32` direct Parquet column loop into `storage` as `scan_parquet_i64_i32_i32_columns`, with reusable row-group, read, and consume metrics. Q04's opt-in direct lineitem path now supplies only the fused predicate/probe/count closure.
        - This is a structural generalization step for page/vector decoder work, not a speed win. Focused individual SF=10 Q04 default path sampled Dodam `~162.6ms` vs DuckDB `~127.0ms`; the opt-in direct path sampled Dodam `~191.9ms` vs DuckDB `~123.0ms`.
        - The important result is negative: query-local direct `ColumnReader` loops should not be promoted as a fast path. The next useful version needs to sit below Arrow `RecordBatch` materialization but above query-specific code: a buffered page/vector decoder that can expose typed vectors, selection, and payload decode policy through a common storage API.
      - Q12 selective payload boundary pass:
        - Added `scan_parquet_i64_byte_array_payload_columns` and `DirectByteArrayPayloadReader` in `storage`. Q12's opt-in direct orders path no longer opens parquet column readers or resolves column indexes in SQL code; it receives key vectors and a payload skip/read wrapper from storage, while keeping its selective payload policy in the query closure.
        - Validation: `cargo test -q` and release build passed. Individual SF=10 Q12, run sequentially, sampled default Dodam `~147.8ms` vs DuckDB `~125.0ms`; direct opt-in sampled Dodam `~162.6ms` vs DuckDB `~124.0ms`.
        - A simultaneous default/opt-in run produced much worse numbers due I/O/cache interference and is not used as a benchmark record. Keep the "individual benchmark only" rule for future measurements.
        - Lesson is unchanged: public `ColumnReader` can now be experimented with through a common boundary, but the performance gap still requires a buffered page/vector decoder or tighter Arrow reader integration rather than more query-local direct-reader code.
      - Direct scan engine/cache boundary pass:
        - Routed Q04/Q12 opt-in direct scans through `DodamEngine` wrappers so direct experiments use the same object-store/file-cache boundary as normal Parquet scans instead of SQL opening `SerializedFileReader<File>` directly.
        - Validation: `cargo test -q` and release build passed. Individual SF=10 direct opt-in samples did not improve: Q12 direct `~170.0ms` vs DuckDB `~123.0ms`, Q04 direct `~194.8ms` vs DuckDB `~123.0ms`. Enabling `DODAM_FILE_CACHE_BYTES=512MiB` for Q12 direct worsened to `~226.8ms`, so cache lookup/copy overhead is not useful for this public `ColumnReader` path.
        - Default-path individual recheck after the engine-boundary change: Q04 `~154.2ms` vs DuckDB `~123.0ms`, Q12 `~153.7ms` vs DuckDB `~124.0ms`. A bundled Q04/Q12 run was discarded as a record because it violates the individual-benchmark rule.
        - Lesson: keeping direct scan I/O under engine ownership is the right architecture, but it confirms again that the next real performance work must target fused Arrow/page decode rather than caching around `ColumnReader::read_records`.
      - Stream fold vectorization pass:
        - Moved additional fallback stream folds from `parallel_batch_fold_chunks` to `parallel_batch_fold_view_chunks`: Q01 projected aggregate fallback, Q12 lineitem stream fallback, and Q03 orders stream fallback now consume `BatchView` directly and keep per-chunk state instead of mapping owned `Vec<RecordBatch>` first.
        - Removed the now-unused Q01 projected `RecordBatch` wrapper. This is mostly structural: it makes the fallback path match the row-group-map/vector consumer shape and reduces query-local batch ownership, but it does not bypass Arrow `RecordBatch` materialization.
        - Validation: `cargo test -q` and release build passed. Individual SF=10 samples: Q01 Dodam `~169.3ms` vs DuckDB `~186.0ms`, Q03 Dodam `~258.1ms` vs DuckDB `~247.0ms`, Q12 Dodam `~156.5ms` vs DuckDB `~126.0ms`.
      - Stream fold vectorization pass 2:
        - Moved Q05 order build, Q05 revenue stream fallback, Q07 order build, Q07 volume stream fallback, and Q08 market-share stream fallback to `parallel_batch_fold_view_chunks`.
        - Added small `BatchView` wrappers for Q05/Q07 order maps and Q08 market-share so fallback stream execution now follows the same vector consumer shape as row-group-map paths. The fallback still clones `RecordBatch` only when the typed/projected layout does not match.
        - Validation: `cargo test -q` and release build passed. Individual SF=10 samples: Q05 Dodam `~278.3ms` vs DuckDB `~296.0ms`, Q07 Dodam `~307.6ms` vs DuckDB `~314.0ms`, Q08 Dodam `~371.6ms` vs DuckDB `~399.0ms`.
      - Parquet page-index metadata pass:
        - Changed the shared Parquet metadata cache to load Arrow reader metadata with `PageIndexPolicy::Optional` by default, with `DODAM_PARQUET_PAGE_INDEX=0` as a rollback knob. The cache key now includes the page-index mode so on/off metadata is not mixed, and dictionary-preserved metadata rebuilds keep the same reader option.
        - Individual SF=10 focused samples before making it default: Q04 improved from `~154.6ms` to `~144.7ms`; Q12 improved from `~151.9ms` to `~146.1ms`.
        - After making page index default, individual SF=10 samples were Q04 `~143.5ms` vs DuckDB `~126.0ms`, and Q12 `~138.2ms` vs DuckDB `~124.0ms`. Disabling it with `DODAM_PARQUET_PAGE_INDEX=0` moved Q12 back to `~150.1ms`, confirming the rollback and effect.
        - Full SF=10 single-process comparison improved in the same-binary A/B area from Dodam `4.920s` vs DuckDB `5.868s` (`0.838x`) with page index disabled/default-old to Dodam `4.737s` vs DuckDB `5.847s` (`0.810x`) with page index enabled. Only Q04 remained slower in that enabled full run.
        - Failed follow-up: forcing Arrow RowFilter on top of page index was still worse for Q04 (`~147.7ms`, slower than page-index default) and much worse for Q12 lineitem (`~236.5ms`). Page index is useful as general scan metadata, but Arrow RowFilter predicate/reconstruction overhead is still not a default-worthy path for these distributions.
      - Deeper vector pipeline boundary pass:
        - Collapsed the engine's non-view late materialization implementation into the `BatchView` implementation. Existing public batch-callback callers now adapt through `BatchView`, so predicate selection and payload selection have one shared execution path.
        - Made `BatchView` an internal enum-backed boundary instead of a direct `RecordBatch` field. It still only has a `RecordBatch` variant today, but the type is now ready for a future page/vector decoded view without changing consumer signatures.
        - Removed the old `parallel_batch_fold_chunks` helper after converting the remaining Q03 fallback and Q16 stream fallback chunk folds to `parallel_batch_fold_view_chunks`. A direct search for `parallel_batch_fold_chunks(` now returns no matches.
        - Moved Q09 and Q11 late-materialized predicate/payload callbacks to `BatchView`; Q09 now uses projected-position typed access in both selection and payload consume, and Q11 does the same for supplier selection and partsupp payload.
        - Validation: `cargo test -q` and release build passed. Individual SF=10 samples: Q03 `~249.4ms` vs DuckDB `~246.0ms`, Q09 `~591.4ms` vs `~611.0ms`, Q11 `~40.1ms` vs `~28.0ms`, Q16 noisy at `~81.6-93.9ms` vs `~61-62ms`. Disabling Q11 late materialization was worse (`~62.5ms`), so Q11's late path remains useful but still not DuckDB parity in isolated runs.
        - Full SF=10 single-process comparison after the pass sampled Dodam `4.721s`, DuckDB `5.880s`, ratio `0.803x`; only Q04 was slower (`~9.1ms` gap). Q11/Q16 were faster than DuckDB in the full run, so their individual slow samples are noisy/context-sensitive rather than a clear retained regression.
      - Q04 vector pipeline follow-up:
        - Re-profiled Q04. Lineitem still dominates; profile summary for `lineitem[l_orderkey,l_commitdate,l_receiptdate]` showed parallel-sum `read_next` around `~784ms` and consume around `~279ms`, so Arrow/Parquet decode remains the larger cost but the atomic consume loop is non-trivial.
        - Re-tested Q04 lineitem row-group chunk sequentially after page-index work. Default chunk `2` remained best/stable enough: default sampled `~142.3ms`, chunk `1` `~148.1ms`, chunk `4` `~144.2ms`, chunk `8` `~150.6ms`. A simultaneous chunk sweep was discarded because it violated the individual-benchmark rule and showed obvious I/O interference.
        - Tried splitting the atomic marker into dense immutable priority bytes plus a separate atomic live marker so non-candidate rows would avoid atomic loads. It regressed clearly (`~171-175ms` focused), likely due worse cache locality/two dense arrays offsetting cheaper non-candidate checks. Reverted the experiment.
        - Moved Q04 lineitem late-materialized opt-in callbacks to the direct `BatchView` late-materialization API. This keeps the page/selection-aware boundary consistent, but it is not a default speed win: focused default samples in this run were noisy around `~156-157ms`, and late opt-in sampled `~153.6ms`, still slower than the best default area.
        - Tried a compact candidate-domain bitset probe: build a dense `orderkey -> candidate-id` map, let each row-group worker set local candidate bits, then OR partial bitsets and count priorities. It regressed badly (`~261.2ms`) because the large `u32` candidate map build/access cost and extra merge work outweighed removing atomic marker writes. Reverted; the single dense `AtomicU8` marker remains the better Q04 duplicate-elimination representation so far.
      - Late-materialization view convergence:
        - Converted the remaining non-view late materialization call sites to `BatchView`: Q07 volume shipping, Q08 market share, and Q19 discounted revenue. A direct search for `late_materialized_parquet_map_with_policy(` and `late_materialized_parquet_map_pruned_with_policy(` in `src/sql.rs` now returns no matches.
        - Q08/Q07/Q19 predicate and payload callbacks now use projected-position typed access where possible, and Q07/Q08 payload decimal loops were split into reusable typed helpers shared by the view paths.
        - Validation: `cargo test -q` and release build passed. Individual SF=10 samples: Q07 `~298.1ms` vs DuckDB `~318.5ms`, Q08 `~365.0ms` vs `~400.5ms`, Q19 `~227.4ms` vs `~261.0ms`.
        - Full SF=10 single-process comparison after this pass sampled Dodam `4.720s`, DuckDB `5.881s`, ratio `0.803x`; no TPC-H query was slower in that run. Q04 sampled `~144.6ms` vs DuckDB `~147.0ms`, but Q04 remains noisy and should still be treated as the primary close-gap query.
      - Scan-aggregate view convergence:
        - Moved the remaining SQL-side `parquet_scan_fold_chunks` users, Q19 fallback lineitem revenue and Q20 lineitem quantity sums, to `parquet_scan_accumulate_chunks_view`.
        - Q19/Q20 now consume fixed-projection `BatchView` typed columns first and keep the older `RecordBatch` name-based logic only as a layout fallback. A direct search for `parquet_scan_fold_chunks(` in `src/sql.rs` and `src/engine.rs` returns no matches.
        - This is a structural vector-pipeline step, not yet a real page/vector decoder fusion: the underlying source is still Arrow `RecordBatch` materialization, but query execution no longer requires owned batch map/fold closures on these scan aggregates.
        - Validation: `cargo test -q` and release build passed. Individual SF=10 samples: Q19 `~428.3ms` vs DuckDB `~483.5ms`; Q20 first sampled cold/noisy at `~480.2ms` vs DuckDB `~367.5ms`, then immediate individual recheck sampled `~206.9ms` vs DuckDB `~236.0ms`.
        - Full SF=10 single-process comparison after this pass sampled Dodam `4.685s`, DuckDB `5.868s`, ratio `0.798x`; only Q04 was slower (`~12.8ms` gap). Treat Q04 as the remaining close-gap target, while the larger structural gap remains Arrow `RecordBatch` materialization rather than true page/vector decoder fusion.
      - Raw-vector BatchView boundary pass:
        - Added the first non-`RecordBatch` `BatchView` variant for direct `i64/i32/i32` vectors and an engine wrapper that exposes direct Parquet column scans as `BatchView` consumers.
        - Rewired the opt-in Q04 direct column reader to reuse the same `q04_count_late_candidate_priorities_atomic_view` consumer as the Arrow row-group-map path. This proves the vectorized consumer boundary can accept non-Arrow batches without changing query logic.
        - This is not a default performance win yet: individual SF=10 Q04 default sampled `~154.4ms` vs DuckDB `~124.5ms`, while the opt-in direct raw-vector path sampled `~189.0ms` vs DuckDB `~122.5ms`. Keep direct column reader disabled by default.
        - Lesson: the useful next step is not public `ColumnReader` loops, but plugging a buffered page/vector decoder into this `BatchView` raw-vector boundary so decode, selection, and payload policy are fused below Arrow `RecordBatch` materialization.
        - Follow-up cleanup removed per-batch count summing from the Q04 direct raw-vector consumer by returning raw-loop hit counts directly. It did not improve the direct path: Q04 direct opt-in still sampled `~191.6ms` vs DuckDB `~124.0ms`, while default sampled `~142.1ms` vs DuckDB `~123.5ms`. This confirms the direct path gap is not SQL consumer accounting but the public `ColumnReader` scan/decode path.
      - Q04 sparse candidate Bloom front-filter experiment:
        - Profiling Q04 showed lineitem row-group-map summary around `read_next ~802ms` and `consume ~214ms` parallel-sum, with wall `Q04 late lineitem probe ~138ms`; read/decode is dominant, but random atomic probe is still visible.
        - Checked row-group pruning potential with DuckDB metadata: all 524 lineitem row groups have at least one Q04 candidate orderkey in their min/max range, so row-group range skip cannot help this dataset.
        - Added an opt-in `DODAM_Q04_ENABLE_CANDIDATE_BLOOM=1` front-filter before atomic marker probes. It regressed badly: default-off Q04 sampled `~145.8ms` vs DuckDB `~123.5ms`, while Bloom opt-in sampled `~192.4ms` vs DuckDB `~124.5ms`.
        - Lesson: for Q04/SF=10, two hash probes plus bit checks cost more than the avoided relaxed atomic loads. The remaining gap still points back to Arrow/Parquet read/decode and the need for page/vector-level fusion rather than another row-loop prefilter.
      - Direct ColumnReader required-level experiment:
        - Added opt-in `DODAM_DIRECT_SKIP_REQUIRED_DEF_LEVELS=1` for the direct `i64/i32/i32` vector scan. When parquet schema says `max_def_level == 0`, it passes `None` for definition-level buffers instead of decoding/filling them.
        - It regressed Q04 direct opt-in: normal direct path sampled `~191.1ms` vs DuckDB `~124.5ms`, while skip-required-levels sampled `~244.7ms` vs DuckDB `~130.0ms`.
        - Lesson: parquet-rs public `ColumnReader::read_records` is not faster on this path when def-level buffers are omitted. Keep this disabled by default; it reinforces that useful decode fusion needs a different buffered page/vector path, not just toggling public ColumnReader arguments.
      - Arrow scan batch-size and p95 profiling pass:
        - Re-tested larger Arrow batch sizes because scan-heavy paths still pay `RecordBatch` materialization/dispatch costs. Q04 worsened at 32K (`~152.5ms`) and 64K (`~163.0ms`) vs the 16K area. Q01 also worsened at 32K (`~196.5ms`) vs default (`~151.5ms`). Keep 16K as the default; larger batches hurt cache/locality more than they reduce dispatch overhead.
        - Added profile-only `reader_next_p95` samples to `ParquetBatchReader` and row-group-map logs. Sampling is enabled only under `DODAM_SCAN_PROFILE`/`DODAM_TPCH_PROFILE`, so normal execution does not store per-batch timings.
        - Q04 profile after this change showed lineitem summary around `reader_next_avg ~0.192ms`, `reader_next_p95_max ~0.847ms`, `reader_next_max ~1.385ms`, `read_next ~765ms`, and `consume ~211ms` parallel-sum. The issue is aggregate decode volume, not a few extreme slow batches.
      - Raw-column BatchView generalization pass:
        - Replaced the query-shaped raw `i64/i32/i32` `BatchView` variant with a generic `RawColumnView` slice. The engine direct scan wrapper now constructs raw column views by projected position and calls the same `BatchView::from_raw_columns` entrypoint that future primitive/page-vector decoders can reuse.
        - Removed the now-unused query-specific constructor so the raw-vector boundary is no longer tied to Q04's exact column layout. This is intentionally infrastructure, not another TPC-H fast path.
        - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q`, and release build passed. Individual SF=10 Q04 default sampled Dodam `~144.7ms` vs DuckDB `~126.5ms`; direct raw-column opt-in sampled Dodam `~196.2ms` vs DuckDB `~121.5ms`.
        - Lesson: the generic raw-column boundary is the right shape for broader vectorized pipeline work, but the public parquet-rs `ColumnReader` path is still too slow. The next real win should come from feeding this boundary from a buffered page/vector decoder or Arrow reader extension, not from more query-local direct-reader special cases.
      - Direct primitive scanner generalization pass:
        - Replaced the storage-level `i64/i32/i32` direct scan loop with a generic `DirectPrimitiveColumnSpec` scanner for primitive `I64`/`I32` columns. The engine now exposes `scan_parquet_primitive_columns_view`, and the old Q04-shaped wrapper is only a thin compatibility helper over that generic API.
        - Added raw `BatchView` debug validation that all raw columns in a batch have the same row count. This is a guardrail for future page/vector decoders feeding the same execution boundary.
        - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q`, and release build passed. Individual SF=10 Q04 default sampled Dodam `~154.4ms` vs DuckDB `~124.0ms`; direct primitive opt-in sampled Dodam `~193.1ms` vs DuckDB `~128.0ms`.
        - Lesson: the generic scanner did not create a speed win, which is expected. Its value is architectural: direct primitive decoding is no longer tied to Q04's exact three-column shape, and future decoder work can target one storage/engine boundary instead of adding query-specific fast paths.
      - Direct primitive logical-type cleanup:
        - Added a logical `Date32` raw primitive view and changed the Q04 direct wrapper to mark `l_commitdate`/`l_receiptdate` as `Date32` instead of generic `I32`. The compatibility consumer still sees physical `i32` slices where needed, but the storage/engine boundary now preserves logical intent for future vectorized date predicates.
        - Avoided per-batch heap allocation for common direct primitive view shapes by using stack arrays for 0-4 projected columns and keeping a Vec fallback only for wider scans.
        - Checked parquet-rs 59.0.0 lower-level APIs. `SerializedPageReader`, `PageReader`, and `GenericColumnReader` are public, but the current public `ColumnReaderImpl` already uses that decoder core. So simply rebuilding the current direct path with page readers is unlikely to close the gap; a useful version needs a real fused page/vector reader with predicate/payload policy, not just another wrapper over `read_records`.
        - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q`, and release build passed. Individual SF=10 Q04 direct opt-in sampled Dodam `~192.1ms` vs DuckDB `~126.0ms`, effectively unchanged from the previous `~193.1ms` area.
      - Q12 pending hash-map pass:
        - Switched Q12's pending order accumulator from the default `HashMap` hasher to the project-wide `FastHashMap`. This is a general hash-heavy accumulation improvement, not a TPC-H-only branch: Q12 lineitem creates many small per-chunk pending maps and then merges them.
        - Retuned `DODAM_Q12_PENDING_MAP_INITIAL_CAPACITY` default from `2048` to `4096`. Individual SF=10 checks: default fast map at `2048` sampled `~141.8ms`; `4096` sampled `~137.1ms` in the env sweep and `~133.6ms` after making it default; `8192` was `~138.4ms`, `16384` `~139.3ms`, and `262144` regressed badly to `~261.1ms` because every parallel partial over-allocated.
        - Also factored Q04 dense atomic marker probing into a single helper with one range check before marker access. Q04 did not improve in the individual sample (`~156.6ms` vs DuckDB `~124.5ms`), so treat that as structural cleanup, not a speed win.
        - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q`, and release build passed. Q12 individual SF=10 improved from the pre-change sample `~147.8ms` vs DuckDB `~124.5ms` to `~133.6ms` vs DuckDB `~128.0ms`, reducing the gap from about `23.3ms` to `5.6ms`.
      - Q12 vector predicate cleanup:
        - Centralized Q12's lineitem date predicate (`commitdate < receiptdate`, `shipdate < commitdate`, and receipt-date range) into an inline predicate kernel used by dictionary, string, fallback, and late-materialization selection loops.
        - Centralized Q12 pending updates behind `q12_pending_increment`/`q12_pending_add_order`, leaving the internal implementation on `entry` because an attempted `get_mut`+`insert` variant double-hashed new keys and was slower (`~135.2ms` vs the prior `~133.6ms` area).
        - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q`, and release build passed. Individual SF=10 Q12 after the date predicate cleanup sampled Dodam `~128.5ms` vs DuckDB `~125.0ms`, reducing the remaining gap to about `3.5ms`.
      - Dictionary vector view boundary pass:
        - Added `DictionaryI32View` and a raw `RawColumnView::DictionaryI32 { keys, values }` shape so Arrow dictionary arrays and future raw dictionary-key vectors can use the same match-flag helper.
        - Moved Q12 lineitem shipmode and Q12 orders priority dictionary paths to `dictionary_i32_view_match_flags`; added a small `Q12LineitemDictionaryView` layout to bind projected columns once before the dictionary row loop.
        - This is structural vector-pipeline work, not a speed win yet. Individual SF=10 Q12 samples after the view abstraction were around `~129.2-131.8ms` vs DuckDB `~123-124ms`, slightly noisier/slower than the best prior `~128.5ms` sample. Keep it because it creates the common boundary needed for raw dictionary/page decoder work; do not count it as a performance improvement.
        - Broadened the same dictionary view boundary to Q16 part brand/type dictionaries and Q04 candidate order-priority dictionaries, removing the remaining SQL-side direct calls to `dictionary_i32_string_values`. Individual SF=10 samples after broadening: Q16 `~76.0ms` vs DuckDB `~62.5ms`, Q04 `~145.7ms` vs DuckDB `~124.5ms`, Q12 `~132.2ms` vs DuckDB `~126.0ms`. Treat this as infrastructure for raw dictionary/page vectors rather than a measured win.
        - Added a `Q16PartDictionaryView` layout that prebinds `p_partkey`, dictionary `p_brand`/`p_type`, and `p_size` once per batch before the Q16 part-group loop. This removes repeated SQL-local downcast/layout branching from the hot loop and follows the same vector boundary style as Q12.
        - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q`, and release build passed. Individual SF=10 Q16 samples were noisy: first run Dodam `~85.9ms` vs DuckDB `~70.5ms`, immediate rerun Dodam `~74.1ms` vs DuckDB `~66.5ms`. Treat this as structural cleanup only, not a proven speed win.
        - Added a shared `Q16SupplierKeyView` for the supplier-count side so packed distinct, bitset, and hash fallback paths all consume prebound `Int64` part/supplier key arrays from the `BatchView` boundary instead of each helper downcasting `ArrayRef`s.
        - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q`, and release build passed. Individual SF=10 Q16 reruns sampled Dodam `~79.0ms` vs DuckDB `~69.5-70.0ms`. This is neutral/noisy versus the prior Q16 range, so count it as vector-pipeline convergence, not a speed improvement.
        - Added common `BatchView::try_record_batch` and `BatchView::i32/required_i32` accessors, then changed Q11 late-materialized callbacks to avoid raw-view `record_batch()` fallback panics. Q11 now returns a normal fallback signal if a future raw/page vector view cannot satisfy the typed layout.
        - Extended Q16 supplier key views from Arrow-only `Int64Array` references to an Arrow-or-raw `i64` vector wrapper. The same packed distinct/bitset/hash supplier loops can now consume future raw primitive vectors without changing query code.
        - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q`, and release build passed. Individual SF=10 samples: Q11 `~38.3ms` vs DuckDB `~28.0ms`, Q16 `~79.2ms` vs DuckDB `~66.0ms`. This is vector-pipeline infrastructure and safety work; current Arrow-backed performance is effectively unchanged.
        - Moved the Arrow-or-raw `i64` wrapper from Q16-local SQL code into `vector::I64VectorView`, with `BatchView::i64_vector(index)` as the common accessor. Q16 supplier counting now uses this shared primitive vector boundary instead of a query-local equivalent.
        - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q`, and release build passed. Individual SF=10 Q16 sampled Dodam `~79.1ms` vs DuckDB `~69.5ms`, matching the prior range. This is a generalization step, not a speed win.
        - Added `vector::I32VectorView` and `vector::Date32VectorView`, plus `BatchView::i32_vector`/`date32_vector`, so raw primitive/date vectors and Arrow arrays share the same hot-loop boundary.
        - Rewired Q04 late lineitem payload date comparison to consume `Date32VectorView`, and Q16 dictionary part-size layout to consume `I32VectorView`/`I64VectorView` instead of SQL-local downcasts.
        - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q`, and release build passed. Individual SF=10 samples: Q04 `~152.5ms` vs DuckDB `~123.5ms`, Q16 `~80.2ms` vs DuckDB `~66.0ms`. This is infrastructure for raw/page-vector execution; no current Arrow-backed speed win.
        - Added `vector::Decimal128VectorView` and raw `RawColumnView::Decimal128`, then moved Q06 view execution from Arrow-only `DecimalInput`/`Date32Array` downcasts to `Decimal128VectorView` + `Date32VectorView`.
        - Removed the now-unused Q06 Arrow-array helper variants; Q06 view execution now has one vector-view kernel that can consume Arrow or future raw decimal/date vectors.
        - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q`, and release build passed. Individual SF=10 Q06 sampled Dodam `~94.3ms` vs DuckDB `~123.0ms`.         - Moved engine-owned Q06 late-materialized selection/payload callbacks and Q14 late-materialized date/payload callbacks onto `BatchView` primitive/decimal vector accessors, avoiding `record_batch().clone()` fallback in their normal projected layouts.
        - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q`, and release build passed. Individual SF=10 samples: Q06 `~91.4ms` vs DuckDB `~123.0ms`, Q14 `~161.4ms` vs DuckDB `~238.5ms`. This is a real vector-boundary cleanup with small positive/no-regression performance signal.
        - Moved Q03 late-materialized order/date selection and revenue payload callbacks to `I64VectorView`/`Date32VectorView`/`Decimal128VectorView`, and changed Q12 late i64 selection/payload fallbacks to use `I64VectorView` plus safe `try_record_batch` fallback.
        - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q`, and release build passed. Individual SF=10 samples: Q03 `~247.5ms` vs DuckDB `~248.5ms`, Q12 `~130.3ms` vs DuckDB `~124.0ms`. No regression; Q03 is parity in this run and Q12 remains a small close-gap target.
        - Moved Q10 returned-revenue late selection/payload and Q19 late payload onto vector views where their projected layouts allow it, and converted remaining Q10/Q19 `record_batch()` fallbacks to safe `try_record_batch` fallbacks.
        - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q`, and release build passed. Individual SF=10 samples: Q10 `~229.4ms` vs DuckDB `~316.5ms`, Q19 `~222.5ms` vs DuckDB `~262.0ms`. No regression; both remain faster than DuckDB in this harness.
        - Extended the generic direct primitive scanner to preserve Decimal128 logical intent for physical INT64 decimal columns, and added an opt-in Q06 direct primitive experiment that feeds raw `Date32`/`Decimal128` vectors into the same vector-view Q06 kernel.
        - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q`, and release build passed. Individual SF=10 Q06 default sampled Dodam `~92.1ms` vs DuckDB `~120.5ms`; opt-in `DODAM_Q06_ENABLE_DIRECT_PRIMITIVE=1` sampled Dodam `~143.1ms` vs DuckDB `~122.0ms`.
        - Lesson: the Decimal128 raw-vector boundary is now general enough for future fused/page-vector sources, but the parquet-rs public `ColumnReader` path is still not a speed path. Keep the Q06 direct primitive route disabled by default; real improvement still needs a lower-level buffered decoder or Arrow reader integration that avoids `RecordBatch` materialization without paying public-column-reader overhead.
        - Added a shared `Utf8VectorView` boundary and moved Q10 returned-revenue returnflag checks plus Q12 lineitem/order priority string paths onto `BatchView` string vectors. Q12's projected typed/profile/sorted order aggregators now consume `I64VectorView + Utf8VectorView` instead of pulling `ArrayRef/StringArray` directly from the `RecordBatch`.
        - This is part of the fast-path removal direction: the TPC-H query handlers still exist, but their hot loops depend less on Arrow-specific `RecordBatch`/downcast shapes and more on reusable vector inputs that planner/execution rules or future page-vector decoders can feed.
        - Validation: `cargo test -q` and release build passed. Individual SF=10 samples: Q10 Dodam `~230.7ms` vs DuckDB `~318.0ms`; Q12 Dodam `~131.3ms` vs DuckDB `~124.5ms`. Q10 remained stable/faster; Q12 remains a close-gap query with no structural regression relative to the recent `~128-132ms` range.

        - Validation: `cargo test -q` and release build passed. Individual SF=10 Q10 sampled Dodam `~231.7ms` vs DuckDB `~318.0ms`, matching the previous `~230.7ms` range.

        - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q`, and release build passed. Individual SF=10 Q15 sampled Dodam `~185.4ms` vs DuckDB `~213.5ms`, so the structural move kept Q15 faster in this harness.
        - Moved Q20 lineitem quantity aggregation to `I64VectorView + Decimal128VectorView + Date32VectorView`, with a null-free raw-slice loop for quantity sums. The view path no longer needs `view.column()`/Arrow `ArrayRef` access for the projected lineitem layout.
        - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q`, and release build passed. Individual SF=10 Q20 sampled Dodam `~200.7ms` vs DuckDB `~237.5ms`, keeping Q20 faster while reducing Arrow-specific hot-loop dependency.

        - Moved Q03 projected lineitem revenue paths to `I64VectorView + Date32VectorView + Decimal128VectorView`, covering both hash-map revenue accumulation and sorted-order lookup accumulation.         - Moved Q04 candidate-order priority view paths further onto vector views: late candidate selection now uses `I64VectorView + Date32VectorView`, late priority payload uses `Utf8VectorView`, and the row-group candidate UTF8 path no longer requires `view.column()` for the projected primitive/string layout.

        - Moved Q01 projected pricing-summary aggregation to `Utf8VectorView + Date32VectorView + Decimal128VectorView`, preserving the existing contiguous single-byte and raw-complement hot loops through the vector boundary.

Individual SF=10 Q11 improved to Dodam `~23.2ms` vs DuckDB `~27.0ms`.
        - Short full individual SF=10 recheck after the Q11 fix showed Q11 faster; longer confirmation left Q02 and Q04 as the only slower queries checked: Q02 `~57.7ms` vs DuckDB `~52.5ms`, Q04 `~127.7ms` vs DuckDB `~123.5ms`; Q12 reconfirmed faster at `~123.7ms` vs `~125.5ms`.
        - Improved Q02 supplier scan by moving supplier payload strings (`s_name`, `s_address`, `s_phone`, `s_comment`) behind a late-materialized selection on `s_suppkey`, `s_acctbal`, and `s_nationkey`. The selected supplier ratio is about `20.0%`, so this avoids decoding most wide supplier strings before the partsupp min-cost join. Individual SF=10 Q02 improved from about `~57.7-58.3ms` to `~51.4ms` vs DuckDB `~51.0ms`; disabling the new path with `DODAM_Q02_DISABLE_SUPPLIER_LATE_MATERIALIZE=1` returns to `~58.3ms`.
        - Q02 profile after the change: supplier late materialization selected `20033/100000` rows, supplier stage sampled `~21.1ms`, parts stage `~10.8ms`, partsupp min-cost stage `~35.3ms`. Q04 remains the main small gap query in focused checks, sampling `~125.6ms` vs DuckDB `~123.0ms`.
        - Post-Q02 full sequential short snapshot had only tiny/noisy slower blips. Longer individual confirmations showed those were noise: Q12 `~123.1ms` vs DuckDB `~125.5ms`, Q16 `~67.5ms` vs `~70.5ms`, Q11 `~20.1ms` vs `~27.0ms`, and Q04 `~126.4ms` vs `~126.5ms`.
        - Correctness/robustness pass after the SF=10 performance work:
          - Added DuckDB differential coverage for decimal scale/boundary filters, timestamp/timezone boundary filters, all-null aggregate groups, correlated subquery boolean combinations with `AND`/`OR`/`NOT EXISTS`, and `COPY CSV` header/escaping.
          - The new `OR NOT EXISTS` case exposed a real correlated fallback bug: outer-row binding can turn predicates such as `f.key IS NULL` into literal predicates like `1 IS NULL`, and the subquery-aware filter parser did not fold literal `IS NULL`/`IS NOT NULL`.
          - Fixed `parse_filter_with_subqueries` to handle `NOT` around subquery-aware predicates and to fold literal null predicates to boolean filter expressions. This keeps row-bound correlated subquery fallback compatible with SQL boolean combinations.
          - Validation: `cargo test -q --test duckdb_differential` passed with `26 passed, 1 ignored`; full `cargo check -q && cargo test -q` passed.
        - Extended the robustness pass further:
          - Added a scalar subquery edge matrix covering 0-row scalar subqueries, scalar `NULL` comparison behavior, `ORDER BY ... LIMIT 1` scalar subqueries, and `NOT IN (SELECT ...)` with RHS `NULL`.
          - Added nested/list/struct Parquet COPY roundtrip coverage: Dodam writes `List<Int32>` and `Struct<rank,label>` output to Parquet, reads it back, compares canonical rows, and asserts nested output schema types are preserved.
          - Validation: `cargo test -q --test duckdb_differential` passed with `28 passed, 1 ignored`; full `cargo check -q && cargo test -q` passed.
        - Added the first seeded randomized DuckDB differential smoke test:
          - Seed is fixed at `0xD0DA_2026_0001` and currently generates `96` deterministic cases over the small `facts` and `types` fixtures.
          - The grammar mixes single-table projection expressions, NULL/boolean predicates, decimal/date/timestamp/string filters, grouped aggregates, `IN` subqueries, scalar subqueries, and alias-qualified `EXISTS` predicates. It intentionally stays inside the SQL surface already expected to work, so failures are actionable regressions rather than unsupported random SQL.
          - First run exposed an ambiguity trap: an unqualified correlated-looking `EXISTS` predicate can be bound differently by the two engines. The generator was tightened to always alias the outer table and qualify the outer key in generated `EXISTS` cases.
          - Validation: randomized smoke passed; `cargo test -q --test duckdb_differential` passed with `29 passed, 1 ignored`; full `cargo check -q && cargo test -q` passed.
        - Expanded the correctness safety net across the next five gaps:
          - Added a seeded randomized join differential smoke (`0xD0DA_2026_0002`, `72` cases) covering single-key joins, multi-key joins, NULL join keys, `INNER`/`LEFT`/`FULL OUTER` shapes, join filters, and grouped join aggregates. The comparison uses unordered row multiset equality so it can test SQL result equivalence without relying on accidental output order.
          - Added a negative/error semantics matrix for unknown projection columns, scalar subqueries returning multiple rows, and ambiguous unqualified join columns. These are asserted as "both engines fail" rather than comparing exact error text.
          - Extended the type differential matrix for decimal scale comparisons, decimal NULL/inequality logic, timestamp/date filtering, date grouping, and boolean `CASE`. Timestamp display itself still differs (`T` vs space formatting), so timestamp filter cases compare stable IDs rather than raw display text.
          - Added Parquet COPY interoperability coverage across `snappy`, `uncompressed`, and `zstd`, with dictionary and row-group/write-batch/page-row-count options. Dodam-written Parquet is read back by both Dodam and DuckDB, and a DuckDB-written ZSTD Parquet file is read by Dodam.
          - Added an explicit unordered-result comparison helper and test for `GROUP BY` without `ORDER BY`, documenting the result comparison policy for randomized/differential cases.
          - COPY Parquet compression parsing now accepts `ZSTD` via Arrow parquet's default `ZstdLevel`.
          - Limitation exposed while building the join generator: JOIN projection still does not share the full scalar projection path (`CASE`, `COALESCE`, arithmetic projection over joined rows). The randomized join grammar intentionally avoids those expressions for now; this should be a future SQL compatibility/generalization item.
          - Validation: `cargo test -q --test duckdb_differential` passed with `34 passed, 1 ignored`; full `cargo check -q && cargo test -q` passed.
        - Added the next correctness/robustness layer across JOIN projection, randomized generation, type/cast, nested, COPY, and long-run differential:
          - Generalized JOIN scalar projection parsing so joined rows can use the same scalar projection evaluator for `COALESCE`, arithmetic expressions, and searched `CASE` expressions. The join randomized generator now includes those expressions again.
          - Added a deterministic DuckDB differential case for `LEFT JOIN` scalar projection expressions, compared as an unordered multiset because expression-output alias ordering is still a separate compatibility item.
          - Tightened CAST semantics: invalid string-to-integer/double projection casts now return an error instead of silently producing `NULL`, matching DuckDB's fail-fast behavior. Added valid bool/int/string cast comparison and invalid cast both-error coverage.
          - Added struct field projection support for `attrs.rank` / `attrs.label` over Arrow `StructArray`, plus DuckDB differential coverage. List operations are still not implemented beyond projection/roundtrip.
          - Added an env-gated long randomized differential test: `DODAM_LONG_DIFF=1 cargo test -q --test duckdb_differential duckdb_differential_long_run_seeded_randomized -- --nocapture`. It runs four fixed seeds and 768 generated cases across single-table and join generators.
          - The long run exposed a current unsupported shape: aggregate query with subquery predicate (`GROUP BY ... WHERE id IN (SELECT ...)`). The generator now keeps aggregate random cases inside supported non-subquery predicates; the unsupported shape remains a future SQL compatibility item.
          - Validation: `cargo test -q --test duckdb_differential` passed with `36 passed, 1 ignored`; the env-gated long randomized test passed; full `cargo check -q && cargo test -q` passed.
        - Closed the next six correctness gaps:
          - Expression-output alias `ORDER BY` now works for JOIN scalar projection aliases such as `COALESCE(...) AS dim_name ORDER BY dim_name`. The fix distinguishes real aliases from unresolved identifiers even when the alias target is the alias name itself, and avoids pushing expression-output sort columns into join input projection planning.
          - Aggregate queries with materialized subquery predicates now run through the same aggregate path after rewriting `IN (SELECT ...)` filters, so `GROUP BY ... WHERE id IN (SELECT ...)` is supported and back in the randomized generator.
          - Added list scalar operations for `array_length(list_col)` / `list_length(list_col)` and 1-based `list_col[index]` projection/filter over primitive/string Arrow `ListArray` children, with child NULL semantics matching DuckDB in the differential fixture.
          - Struct/list expressions now work in filters for the covered cases, e.g. `attrs.rank > 1 OR tags[1] = 10`, by collecting the base nested column into scan projection and evaluating the predicate through the scalar expression path.
          - `CAST(Date32 AS VARCHAR)` and `CAST(Timestamp(Millisecond) AS VARCHAR)` now preserve typed scalar information long enough to format DuckDB-compatible `YYYY-MM-DD` and `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` strings.
          - Long randomized differential remains env-gated but now exercises aggregate+subquery predicates again: `DODAM_LONG_DIFF=1 cargo test -q --test duckdb_differential duckdb_differential_long_run_seeded_randomized -- --nocapture`.
          - Validation: `cargo test -q --test duckdb_differential` passed with `37 passed, 1 ignored`; the env-gated long randomized test passed; full `cargo check -q && cargo test -q` passed.
        - Expanded the next correctness/robustness layer across decimal, temporal, nested, subquery, and diagnostics:
          - Mixed-scale `Decimal128` comparisons and `+`/`-` scalar expressions now align raw integer scales instead of falling back to `f64`; overflow is surfaced as an error rather than silently producing `NULL`.
          - SQL typed string literals such as `DATE '2024-01-03'` and `TIMESTAMP '2024-01-03 04:05:06.789'` are lowered through the scalar cast evaluator, and timestamp parsing accepts timezone-offset forms for epoch normalization.
          - `array_length` / `list_length` now accepts struct list fields such as `attrs.more_tags`, extending nested/list coverage beyond top-level list columns.
          - Correlated subquery differential coverage now includes a correlated `IN` predicate inside an outer boolean combination, and the row-bound fallback treats unknown outer qualifiers from the new resolver error type as bind-later correlated references.
          - SQL diagnostics now has dedicated `AmbiguousColumn`, `UnknownTableQualifier`, `InvalidCast`, and `TypeMismatch` error variants; existing user-facing ambiguous-column text was preserved for compatibility.
          - Validation: full `cargo test -q` passed.
        - Continued the decimal/nested correctness pass:
          - General `Decimal128 * Decimal128` scalar expressions now preserve decimal output instead of being intercepted by the old generic `f64` product fast path; `Decimal128 / Decimal128` intentionally remains `DOUBLE` to match DuckDB.
          - Single-table `WHERE amount = amount3 OR amount3 > amount` now works for mixed-scale Decimal128 column-to-column predicates by aligning raw decimal scales in the physical filter path.
          - Nested list indexing now supports struct list fields such as `attrs.more_tags[1]`, not only top-level `tags[1]`.
          - Validation: full `cargo fmt && cargo check -q && cargo test -q` passed.
        - Implemented the next 1-4 correctness/generalization pass:
          - Added `CAST(... AS DECIMAL(p,s))` scalar support with DuckDB-compatible scale rounding and precision overflow errors.
          - Extended nested/list scalar projection into JOIN output expressions for alias-qualified struct fields and struct-list operations such as `n.attrs.rank`, `n.attrs.more_tags[1]`, and `array_length(n.attrs.more_tags)`.
          - Added a narrow optimized correlated `IN` rewrite for integer/date pair keys of the shape `outer_value IN (SELECT inner_value WHERE inner_corr = outer_corr AND ...)`; unsupported key types fall back to the correctness-first row-bound path.
          - Routed arithmetic type mismatches through the dedicated `TypeMismatch` error variant instead of generic `UnsupportedSql`.
          - Expanded randomized DuckDB differential generation with correlated pair-key `IN`, mixed-scale decimal column comparisons, Decimal CAST, and Decimal product projection.
          - Added a JOIN expression-filter fallback for nested/list predicates, so alias-qualified predicates such as `n.attrs.more_tags[1] = 9 OR array_length(n.attrs.more_tags) > 1` are evaluated after join materialization with DuckDB differential coverage.
          - Generalized the JOIN expression-filter path further: input scan projection now adds only the base columns referenced by the expression predicate instead of falling back to `Projection::All`, while the joined output still stays wide until the filter is applied.
          - Removed duplicate scalar predicate evaluator logic by routing single-table and JOIN expression predicates through one evaluator with parser-specific scalar binding.
          - Extended JOIN nested/list predicate differential coverage to `COALESCE`/`LIKE`, `CAST`/`IN`/`IS NULL`, and searched `CASE` expression predicates.
          - Added JOIN nested/list error-contract coverage for unknown nested fields, invalid list-index expression types, unknown nested qualifiers, and unsupported nested scalar functions.
          - Tightened JOIN output binding: qualified projected columns now register implicit output aliases, `ORDER BY f.id` can resolve after scalar projection, and output projection/scalar evaluation can resolve qualified names against the materialized batch schema.
          - Reduced JOIN output `Projection::All` for scalar projection/expression-filter cases by pushing only the base columns required by final expressions and expression predicates, while keeping wider output for filters/order/outer join shapes that still need it.
          - Expanded seeded and long randomized JOIN differential generation with facts-to-nested JOIN cases covering nested/list predicates and qualified join-key projections.
          - Reduced remaining inner/semi JOIN output `Projection::All` cases by building a working projection from final output, filter columns, order columns, and expression-filter columns; final output projection is applied only when the working projection carries extra columns.
          - Added `ORDER BY` ordinal support for single-table and JOIN queries, including aggregate ordinal ordering such as `ORDER BY 1`; expression-output ordinal ordering is partially covered when the ordinal resolves to a physical/output-available column.
          - Extended correctness coverage with `count(DISTINCT ...)` aggregate combinations, JOIN aggregate `count(DISTINCT ...)`, and nested LEFT JOIN randomized differential cases.
          - Split `ORDER BY` ordinal metadata out of output aliases so positional ordering no longer participates in output renaming/direct-sink alias checks.
          - Added generic grouped expression materialization for single-table and JOIN aggregates: `GROUP BY lower(...)`, `GROUP BY CASE ...`, alias/ordinal ordering over grouped expressions, and `count(DISTINCT expression)` now share the aggregate input-expression path.
          - Added a first minimal window-function path for `row_number() OVER (ORDER BY ...)` on one-table non-aggregate scans, with DuckDB differential coverage.
          - Extended SQL compatibility with `ORDER BY ... NULLS FIRST/LAST`, `LIMIT ... OFFSET ...`, JOIN `DISTINCT`, `GROUP BY` alias/ordinal references, JOIN qualified wildcard expansion (`alias.*`), and partitioned `row_number`/`rank`/`dense_rank` window functions.
          - Validation: full `cargo fmt && cargo check -q && cargo test -q` passed.
        - Followed up the SQL compatibility pass with a correctness/planner hardening layer:
          - Generalized the one-window projection path into a multi-window projection path for multiple `row_number`/`rank`/`dense_rank` outputs that share the same `OVER` specification. This keeps the implementation safe by using one window sort and appending multiple vector outputs; different `OVER` specifications remain explicitly unsupported.
          - Fixed JOIN `DISTINCT` semantics so non-aggregate joins apply SELECT projection before `DISTINCT`, and apply `ORDER BY`/`LIMIT`/`OFFSET` after `DISTINCT`. The earlier join path could accidentally distinct internal working columns or apply distinct after limit.
          - Added JOIN-level `DISTINCT` validation through the same common `validate_distinct` helper used by single-table SELECT.
          - Added DuckDB differential coverage for multiple window projections with `LIMIT/OFFSET`, JOIN `DISTINCT + ORDER BY + LIMIT/OFFSET`, and error-contract coverage for mixed window specs, `QUALIFY`, and unsupported aggregate window functions.
          - Validation: `cargo test -q --test duckdb_differential` passed with `41 passed`; full `cargo check -q && cargo test -q` passed.
          - Short SF=10 single-process TPC-H regression snapshot after the compatibility changes: median-sum Dodam `~4.269s` vs DuckDB `~5.790s` (`~0.737x`). Slower queries in this run were Q03 `~259.8ms` vs `~250.5ms` (gap `~9.3ms`) and Q04 `~140.5ms` vs `~140.5ms` (effectively parity, gap `~0.013ms`). No broad TPC-H regression from the SQL compatibility hardening was observed.

          - Applied the existing sparse late-selection API to Q03's lineitem late-materialized selector paths. The selected ratio is about `0.5%`, so null-free vector paths now append selected row offsets instead of pushing one selector state per input row.

Sequential SF=10 sweep showed chunk `8` slightly better (`~243.3ms` in the short sweep; longer confirmation `~244.4ms`) than chunk `2` (`~244.9-246.7ms`). Final default run sampled Dodam `~245.7ms` vs DuckDB `~248.0ms` (`~0.991x`).
          - Full SF=10 single-process recheck after the Q03 sparse-selection/chunk change sampled Dodam `~4.237s` vs DuckDB `~5.785s` (`~0.732x`) with no slower query. Q03 sampled Dodam `~247.3ms` vs DuckDB `~257.0ms` (`~0.962x`), and Q04 sampled `~139.0ms` vs `~143.5ms`.
        - Generalized the Q03/Q04 sparse late-selection pattern one layer deeper:
          - Added `LateSelectionBuilder::push_selected_rows(row_count, predicate)` so sparse late materialization can build selector runs directly from a vectorized predicate loop without allocating a query-local `Vec<usize>` of selected offsets.
          - Rewired Q03 lineitem late selection and Q04 lineitem late selection to use this engine-level primitive. This keeps the TPC-H fast path behavior but moves the reusable part into the execution boundary.

          - Individual SF=10 checks after the change: Q03 Dodam `~243.8ms` vs DuckDB `~251.5ms` (`~0.969x`); Q04 longer confirmation Dodam `~124.4ms` vs DuckDB `~125.0ms` (`~0.995x`).
        - Retuned Q12 defaults: `DODAM_Q12_ROW_GROUP_MAP_CHUNK` from `2` to `4`, and `DODAM_Q12_ORDER_FUSED_ROW_GROUP_CHUNK` from `4` to `1`. Sequential sweeps: lineitem chunk `4` was best in the sampled run (`~129.0ms` vs default area `~129.8-133.5ms`), while order fused chunk `1` was clearly best (`~125.3ms` vs `~132-139ms` for larger chunks). Combined explicit setting sampled Dodam `~123.0ms` vs DuckDB `~125.5ms`.

        - Continued moving TPC-H fast-path behavior into reusable vector/execution rules:
          - Extended the engine-level sparse late-selection primitive `LateSelectionBuilder::push_selected_rows(row_count, predicate)` from Q03/Q04 to Q12 order late materialization and Q19 lineitem late materialization. This keeps sparse selected-row construction at the late-materialization boundary instead of each query manually pushing one selector bit per input row.
          - Added `dictionary_i32_view_match_index(keys, flags, row)` in the vector layer and rewired Q12 dictionary shipmode/order-priority paths to use it. This is a small but useful low-cardinality dictionary rule: query code now asks for a target index, while dictionary key conversion/null-safe flag lookup stays in the vector helper.
          - Added `AdaptiveI64Map::get_cached(dense_slices, key)`, mirroring the existing `AdaptiveI64Set::contains_cached` rule, so hot vector loops can bind the dense/hash lookup strategy once per batch and avoid a per-row enum dispatch. Q12 and Q19 late-selection map probes now use this common adaptive lookup helper.
          - Benchmark note: running Q12 and Q19 comparisons concurrently produced misleading slow numbers because both jobs competed for I/O/cache. TPC-H comparisons should be recorded as individual per-query runs unless the experiment is explicitly about multi-query concurrency.

          - Re-ran SF=10 TPC-H as individual per-query comparisons, not one concurrent/mixed benchmark. Median-sum was Dodam `~4.228s` vs DuckDB `~5.598s` (`~0.755x`). Q04 was the only slower query in this snapshot: Dodam `~127.0ms` vs DuckDB `~124.0ms` (`~1.024x`, gap `~3.0ms`). All other queries sampled faster than DuckDB.

The next structural step is not another Q-specific wrapper, but lowering this into a first-class physical operator (`Scan -> LocalFold -> FinalMerge`) so generic aggregate/join planning can select it declaratively.
          - Remaining `Projection::All` work should focus on subquery/CTE/JOIN fallback boundaries. JOIN scalar/expression filters are mostly narrowed now, but CTE/subquery materialization and some distinct/order/outer-join safety paths still force wide batches.

Keep candidate late materialization as fallback/experiment only.

        - Started fixing the generic benchmark gaps:
          - Added vector predicate fast paths for scalar SQL predicates of the form `array_length(list_or_struct_list) <op> integer_literal` and integer column/struct-field `IN (literal, ...)`. This is column-name independent and applies before the generic `EvaluatedScalar` path.
          - The generic nested/list benchmark improved from about `~192ms` to about `~5.5-5.8ms`, beating DuckDB's CLI comparison around `~12.9ms`. This confirms the prior `~15.7x` nested/list gap was mostly scalar predicate materialization overhead, not Parquet nested decode itself.

          - Removed an unnecessary `collect_batches(stream) -> append expressions -> MemoryExec` materialization in the parsed JOIN aggregate path by reusing `append_aggregate_expression_stream`. This keeps aggregate expression materialization streaming after join output.
          - Generic benchmark after these changes (`repeats=7`, warmup `2`) sampled Dodam `~58.5ms` vs DuckDB `~45.4ms` (`~1.29x`). Remaining gaps are now decimal/date aggregate (`~21.2ms` vs `~14.4ms`) and join expression aggregate (`~31.7ms` vs `~18.2ms`); nested/list is no longer the blocker.
          - Validation: `scripts/check_correctness.sh quick` passed. SF=10 TPC-H individual regression check sampled Dodam `~4.305s` vs DuckDB `~5.854s` (`~0.735x`), no slower query across all 22.

        - Applied the requested 1-4 generic aggregate follow-up:

        - Added a retained aggregate-order cleanup:
          - Aggregate group results are already emitted in group-key order by the aggregate executor. SQL now detects `ORDER BY` clauses that are an ascending prefix of `GROUP BY` and, when runtime group keys in that prefix contain no NULLs or explicitly request `NULLS FIRST`, skips the redundant output sort and only applies `LIMIT/OFFSET`.
          - This is a general execution property rule, not query-name specific. It is intentionally conservative for default `NULLS LAST` ordering because the aggregate comparator sorts `None` before `Some`; in that case it skips only when the relevant group keys have no NULL values.

        - Added a direct primitive/vector fused aggregate path for the generic `one Int32 group key + count(*) + sum(Int64) + min(Decimal128 backed by INT64) + max(Date32)` shape with conjunctive decimal/date filters:
          - The planner recognizes the aggregate/filter shape from column names and schema, preserves existing row-group pruning through `PredicateSet`/`plan_parquet_scan_tasks`, and falls back to the normal path when the physical/logical types, nullability behavior, or predicates are unsupported.
          - The first sequential direct-primitive attempt was not kept as a speed win: it avoided Arrow `RecordBatch` materialization but lost the normal parallel Parquet scan benefit, so `filter_group_decimal_date` regressed to about `~24.4ms`.
          - The retained version scans pruned row-group chunks in parallel, feeds raw `BatchView` primitive vectors directly into a typed partial aggregate state, then merges partial states. This removes `RecordBatch -> MemoryExec -> aggregate` redispatch for this common fixed-width aggregate shape.

          - Added an integration test over a small Parquet file proving `DirectPrimitiveFoldExec` can execute `GROUP BY bucket, count(*), sum(value)` through the physical plan builder and produce `[bucket,count,sum] = [(1,2,30),(2,3,45)]`.
          - Generic comparison rerun after warmup sampled Dodam `~128.6ms` vs DuckDB `~160.7ms` (`~0.80x`). An earlier generic sample was cold/noisy (`~1.10x`) and did not reproduce.

          - Added an integration test over a two-row-group Parquet fixture proving the physical operator executes filtered `count/sum/min/max` correctly: expected groups `(bucket=1,count=2,sum=30,min=10.00,max_date=20)` and `(bucket=2,count=2,sum=20,min=25.00,max_date=25)`.
          - Generic comparison rerun sampled Dodam `~133.2ms` vs DuckDB `~161.2ms` (`~0.83x`). The decimal/date direct primitive queries stayed fast: `filter_group_decimal_date` `~10.1ms` vs DuckDB `~18.6ms`, `decimal_date_group_no_order` `~8.0ms` vs `~14.3ms`.

        - Connected the executable direct primitive operator back into aggregate fast-path selection:
          - Changed `DirectPrimitiveFoldExec` from a sequential scanner to a parallel row-group fold, using the same raw `BatchView` state/merge contract as the prior engine helper.
          - Added `DirectPrimitiveFoldExec::try_execute_metrics()` so planner/engine auto-selection can gracefully return `None` and fall back when a nullable or unsupported Parquet layout violates the non-null primitive contract. This fixed a regression caught by `duckdb_differential_unordered_result_policy` on nullable `GROUP BY key`.
          - Rewired the engine direct primitive `count/sum` and `count/sum/min/max` paths to execute through `DirectPrimitiveFoldExec` instead of duplicating the default parallel fold logic in `engine.rs`. The opt-in selected-payload experiment remains separate because it has different skip/read semantics.
          - Row-group pruning is preserved: the existing `plan_parquet_scan_tasks(...)` output row-group list is now passed into `DirectPrimitiveFoldExec` config before execution.
          - Generic comparison stayed noise-sensitive in per-query CLI process runs, but rerun median-sum sampled Dodam `~210.5ms` vs DuckDB `~277.1ms` (`~0.76x`). Full SF=10 individual TPC-H sampled Dodam `~4.276s` vs DuckDB `~6.900s` (`~0.620x`) with no slower query.

        - Continued lowering TPC-H/generic fast-path behavior into common planner/execution rules:
          - `AggregatePlan` now carries an optional `DirectPrimitiveFoldExec` physical node. `to_plan_node()`/`EXPLAIN` can show the direct primitive fold decision instead of always rendering `FinalMergeExec(LocalFoldExec(ScanExec))` for shapes that will execute through the direct vector path.
          - Extracted direct primitive fold config construction into one planner-side builder. The builder owns shape recognition, Parquet schema checks, row-group pruning via `plan_parquet_scan_tasks(...)`, primitive column descriptors, and execution mode selection for both `count/sum` and `count/sum/min/max`.
          - `execute_aggregate_plan()` now tries the planned direct primitive physical node first and falls back to the normal scan/fold path if the raw primitive non-null contract is not satisfied. This keeps the fast path as a general optimizer/execution rule instead of a query-local shortcut.
          - The selected-payload experiment remains opt-in (`DODAM_DIRECT_SELECTION_FOLD=1`) but is separated as a distinct execution candidate. It is still default-off because previous measurements showed fragmented `skip_records` work can outweigh saved payload decode on the generic fixture.
          - Added `--dodam-mode query-file` to `scripts/compare_generic_duckdb.py`. This runs warmup+repeat Dodam COPY statements in one process and parses `copy_profile total=...us`, reducing per-sample CLI startup noise while preserving per-query/per-repeat samples.
          - Short validation of the new mode (`--repeats 3 --warmup 1 --dodam-mode query-file`) sampled Dodam `~95.2ms` median-sum vs DuckDB CLI `~185.2ms` (`~0.51x`). This is useful for Dodam internal stability, but it is not a fully symmetric process-model comparison because DuckDB is still launched per sample.
          - Remaining deep structural target: true page/vector decoder fusion. The current direct primitive path still uses parquet-rs `ColumnReader::read_records`; replacing or specializing that decoder boundary is the next place where DuckDB-style scan/operator fusion would matter.
        - Applied the next benchmark/rule/trace pass:
          - Added `--duckdb-mode query-file` to the generic benchmark. DuckDB can now run each query's repeat set in one process through `.timer on`, while Dodam can use `query-file` and `copy_profile` in the same benchmark script. This makes process model explicit instead of accidentally comparing Dodam one-process samples to DuckDB per-query CLI startup.
          - One short symmetric process-model check (`--repeats 3 --warmup 1 --dodam-mode query-file --duckdb-mode query-file`) sampled Dodam `~97.7ms` vs DuckDB `~51.0ms` (`~1.92x`). This exposed that prior DuckDB CLI-per-sample numbers were overstating Dodam's relative position on the generic fixture. DuckDB `.timer` output is coarse on these very small queries, so longer repeat counts are needed for final numbers.
          - Extended the direct primitive `count/sum` shape from `count(*) + sum(i64)` to also allow `count(col) + sum(i64)` when `col` is already one of the non-null primitive columns read by the rule (`group_by` or `sum` column). Unsupported/nullable layouts still fall back through the existing raw primitive contract.
          - `SingleKeyCountSumVectorState` and the shared count/sum finish path now preserve the count expression instead of always emitting `count(*)`. Added a direct physical execution test for `count(value), sum(value)`.
          - Added optimizer trace logging behind `DODAM_OPTIMIZER_TRACE=1`, including direct primitive fold accept/reject reasons and `rule=direct_primitive_fold` in the physical node attributes. A smoke run confirmed `count(value), sum(value)` emits `decision=accept`.
          - Added selected-payload gate trace behind `DODAM_DIRECT_SELECTION_TRACE=1`, logging selected ratio, run count, average run length, accept/reject, and reason. This keeps the opt-in selected-payload experiment measurable without enabling it by default.
          - Page/vector decoder fusion remains partially prepared, not fully implemented: the direct primitive path has clearer operator/rule boundaries and selection gate telemetry, but it still calls parquet-rs `ColumnReader::read_records`. The next real structural step is replacing that boundary for the primitive required-column subset.
        - Continued generic benchmark improvements after switching to symmetric query-file comparison:
          - Added optimizer trace for join input pushdown (`rule=join_input_pushdown`) so JOIN projection/filter pushdown can be verified directly. For `join_expression_projection`, the left filter is pushed (`left_filter=true`) and side projections are narrow (`facts[key,bucket,value]`, `dim[key,class]`).
          - Generalized the coalesce expression aggregate fast path from only dense 3-key leading ranges to also support dense 2-key ranges. This targets `GROUP BY int_key, coalesce(utf8,literal)` generally, not a TPC-H-specific query.
          - Short symmetric generic smoke after the dense 2-key change sampled `join_expression_projection` around `~18.8ms` vs the previous `~21.7ms` area, though query-file timings remain noisy and DuckDB `.timer` is coarse for these small queries.
          - Tried a more structural `JOIN + coalesce group count/sum` fusion that builds a unique right-key map and aggregates during the left scan without materializing join output. It was correct on the smoke query but did not improve performance (`join_expression_projection` stayed around `~19-20ms` and a focused traced run was slower). The likely reason is that the second explicit left scan plus Rust row-loop aggregation loses to the existing vectorized scan/hash-join/expression aggregate path at this scale.
          - The join fusion is retained only behind `DODAM_ENABLE_JOIN_COALESCE_COUNT_SUM_FUSION=1` as an experiment and is default-off. It needs a lower-level fused scan/operator implementation, not a SQL-layer batch loop, before it is worth enabling.
          - Final default-on smoke for this pass (`--repeats 7 --warmup 2 --dodam-mode query-file --duckdb-mode query-file`) sampled Dodam `~83.6ms` vs DuckDB `~42.0ms` (`~1.99x`). This is better than the prior default symmetric sample around `~89.2ms`, but the remaining gap is still dominated by `join_expression_projection`, nullable/string grouping, and three-key expression grouping.
        - Continued direct primitive nullable/generalization work:
          - Extended raw direct primitive scans to expose nullable Int32/Int64 vectors using Parquet definition levels, allowing the direct `single key + count/sum` physical operator to handle nullable group keys instead of falling back to the Arrow RecordBatch aggregate path.
          - Fixed an important compatibility detail: optional Parquet columns that are null-free in the current batch are still exposed as non-null raw slices. Without that, optional-but-null-free columns such as the generic `bucket` key were incorrectly routed through nullable raw views and broke the existing `count/sum/min/max` direct path.
          - Removed the per-batch `Vec<Option<i64>>` materialization for nullable raw sum inputs. The direct count/sum state now reads sum values through a small sequential cursor over raw values + definition levels, so nullable direct aggregation no longer allocates an Option vector per batch.
          - Symmetric generic query-file comparison after the retained changes sampled Dodam `~77.5-87.3ms` vs DuckDB `~43.5-48.0ms` (`~1.78-1.82x`). `nullable_key_group` now runs through the direct primitive path and sampled around `~4.7-4.8ms` vs DuckDB `~3.0ms`; this is a large improvement over the earlier nullable-key Arrow path, but still not a DuckDB-level decode/pipeline boundary.
          - A focused `string_low_cardinality_group` profile showed row-group pruning is already effective (`2/5` row groups, `200k` output rows). The remaining time is mostly Parquet decode + RecordBatch materialization for `id/value/label`, not the Utf8 hash loop alone. This argues against more SQL-layer string fast paths and toward deeper scan/vector decoder fusion.
          - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, targeted direct primitive tests, `scripts/check_correctness.sh quick`, release build, and symmetric generic query-file comparisons passed.
        - Followed up on the remaining string low-cardinality gap:
          - Added a reusable Parquet reader constructor for `row_selection + dictionary_columns`, and added `Dictionary<Int32, Utf8>` support to the single-key `count/sum` aggregate collector. This prepares the normal aggregate path to consume dictionary-preserved string keys instead of always requiring decoded `StringArray` keys.
          - Also wired a dictionary-column candidate into the late-materialized aggregate helper for `GROUP BY one key + count/sum`, but current SQL COPY/default aggregate planning does not route through that helper, so this is infrastructure rather than a default performance win.
          - Tried an adaptive linear `Utf8` group index for low-cardinality string keys. It was clearly worse: `string_low_cardinality_group` regressed to about `~15.5ms` in the symmetric generic run and a focused profile showed aggregate time around `~24.8ms`. Reverted the adaptive index; the normal `HashMap<String, group_id>` remains better even at ~65 groups.
          - Final default symmetric generic comparison after reverting the failed adaptive index sampled Dodam `~80.0ms` vs DuckDB `~42.0ms` (`~1.91x`). `string_low_cardinality_group` returned to `~5.9ms` vs DuckDB `~3.0ms`; focused profile still shows scan/decode plus aggregate materialization around `~10-12ms` with `2/5` row groups scanned.
          - Conclusion: the next meaningful improvement is not a SQL-layer low-cardinality string index. The useful direction is to connect dictionary-preserved payload scans into the actual SQL aggregate plan, or go deeper and aggregate directly from dictionary/vector batches without `RecordBatch -> aggregate` redispatch.
        - Connected and measured the dictionary-preserved filtered aggregate candidate:
          - Added an opt-in `DODAM_ENABLE_FILTERED_DICTIONARY_AGGREGATE=1` candidate for filtered `GROUP BY one key + count/sum`. It preserves Parquet dictionary output for the group key, applies the filter through `FilterExec`, and then runs the existing grouped aggregate collector that now understands `Dictionary<Int32, Utf8>`.
          - The candidate works but is default-off because it regressed the target case: `string_low_cardinality_group` sampled around `~7.1-7.4ms` vs the default `~5.9-6.2ms`. The dictionary aggregate loop itself was lower, but the dictionary reader + `MemoryExec -> FilterExec -> aggregate` redispatch cost outweighed it.
          - Default-off comparison after the guard sampled Dodam `~75.9ms` vs DuckDB `~40.0ms` (`~1.90x`); opt-in sampled Dodam `~80.1ms` vs DuckDB `~47.8ms` (`~1.68x`) with `string_low_cardinality_group` still worse. The ratio difference is mostly DuckDB timer noise; per-query Dodam time confirms the candidate is not a default win.

The direct accumulator candidate sampled `string_low_cardinality_group` around `~7.7ms`; focused profile showed dictionary row-group read around `~8.0ms` plus direct consume around `~5.3ms`. The default path stayed around `~6.2ms` for the query and `~75.0ms` total generic median-sum.
          - Conclusion updated: for this fixture, preserving dictionary through Arrow's reader does not help enough because the dictionary reader path and mask consume cost exceed the saved string materialization/grouping work. A real win likely requires lower-level page/vector filtering or a specialized reader that can apply the simple `id < constant` predicate while decoding only selected value/key vectors, not just another Arrow `RecordBatch`-level dictionary path.

It reads the left filter/group column first, builds a `RowSelection` and stores the selected group key, then reads only selected `key/value` payload rows before performing the right lookup and aggregate.           - 3 / coalesce projection: inspected the projection path; meaningful `ArrayRef` reuse requires a larger projection-output rewrite because `EvaluatedScalar` currently owns vectors. Deferred to avoid adding another query-shaped fast path.
          - 4 / decimal/date aggregate: focused profile shows the generic decimal/date cases already use `direct_primitive_count_sum_min_max` with aggregate around `~4.1ms` over `600k` rows; the remaining gap is scan/decode/materialize rather than bound literal conversion.
          - 5 / vector reader fusion: confirmed the next structural boundary is still below `ParquetBatchReader`/Arrow `RecordBatch`. Current `BatchView` consumers reduce SQL-layer coupling, but they do not yet avoid Arrow materialization.
          - Latest generic comparison sampled Dodam `~54.9ms` vs DuckDB `~40.0ms` (`~1.37x`). `join_expression_projection` was close (`~6.7ms` vs `~6.0ms`), while the largest remaining gap stayed `three_key_expression_group_no_order`.

          - This is a general projection rule, not tied to the generic benchmark query. It also establishes the representation needed for later `BatchView`/aggregate consumers to avoid scalar-vector materialization.
          - Validation passed (`cargo fmt --all`, `cargo check -q`, `scripts/check_correctness.sh quick`, release build).         - Continued collector-loop improvement attempts:
          - Added an opt-in `DODAM_ENABLE_COALESCE_RUN_AGGREGATE=1` path for three-key coalesce aggregates with null-free sum input. It collapses adjacent rows with the same `(first, second, string_id)` into one group update using `(count, sum)` instead of updating per row.
          - Focused no-order three-key profile looked promising in one run: update moved from `~6.30ms` to `~6.02ms`, and COPY total from `~20.2ms` to `~14.9ms`.
          - Full generic comparison with the run path enabled was worse: Dodam `~63.4ms` vs DuckDB `~46.0ms` (`~1.38x`), with `three_key_expression_group` regressing to `~13.4ms`. This means adjacent-run aggregation is data-order sensitive and cannot be a default rule.
          - Kept run aggregation opt-in only. This is another useful negative result: row-level update reduction helps only when input is already clustered by the full group key; without a clustering estimate it is unsafe.
          - Default generic comparison after keeping experiments opt-in sampled Dodam `~58.4ms` vs DuckDB `~44.0ms` (`~1.33x`). Remaining durable path is still a lower-level vector/page consumer or a costed clustered-run detector before enabling run aggregation.
        - Added and tested an adaptive clustered-run gate:
          - Implemented `DODAM_ENABLE_COALESCE_ADAPTIVE_RUN_AGGREGATE=1`, which samples adjacent full group-key equality before using the run aggregation path. The forced `DODAM_ENABLE_COALESCE_RUN_AGGREGATE=1` override remains available.
          - Focused no-order runs were inconclusive/noisy: adaptive reduced measured update time in one run (`~6.0ms` vs off `~7.8ms`) but did not reliably reduce total COPY time.
          - Generic comparison showed adaptive default-on would regress: adaptive sampled Dodam `~64.9ms` vs DuckDB `~47.0ms`, while adaptive disabled sampled Dodam `~61.6ms` vs DuckDB `~45.0ms`. Therefore adaptive run aggregation is also opt-in, not default.
          - Final default generic comparison after disabling adaptive by default sampled Dodam `~53.4ms` vs DuckDB `~42.0ms` (`~1.27x`). This confirms the safe default path is still the non-run collector loop.
          - Conclusion: clustered-run aggregation needs a stronger cost model than adjacent sampling, or it should only be considered when storage/order metadata says the input is clustered by the aggregate key. The next real improvement should target lower-level scan/filter/decode fusion.

          - Dense ordered finish now stops after producing the requested number of groups instead of finishing every group and letting the outer output layer slice later. Non-dense ordered finish still sorts and then truncates, preserving correctness.
          - This is a general `ORDER BY group keys LIMIT N` rule, not a query-name fast path. It benefits the generic `three_key_expression_group` case, where the collector has about `10017` groups but the query only needs `2000` rows.
          - Focused profile improved finish from about `~2.9ms` to `~0.6ms`; full generic comparison sampled `three_key_expression_group ~9.28ms` vs the prior `~12.25ms` sample, and total median-sum improved from `~62.5ms` to `~58.5ms` in the same noisy environment.
