This is a general reader cleanup, but scale-10 impact was mostly neutral; the main primitive gap remains the generic `value` column decode path used by `nullable_key_group`.
          - 2 / fused aggregate candidates: rechecked direct join left aggregate, expression aggregate view payload, run aggregation, adaptive run aggregation, and small string cache after the reader cleanup. They still regressed or stayed noisy, so none were promoted. The remaining high-value work is a new reader that evaluates the decimal predicate and updates `CoalesceKeyCountSumGroups` directly from decoded page/vector data.
          - 3 / ORDER BY LIMIT early stop: added row-group min/max monotonicity detection for a single order column and an opt-in `DODAM_ENABLE_EXPRESSION_FILTER_MONOTONIC_STREAM_LIMIT=1` mode. Combined with `DODAM_ENABLE_EXPRESSION_FILTER_STREAM_LIMIT=1`, scale-10 `nested_projection_filter` improved from about `~11-19ms` to `~3-4ms` in focused runs. This remains opt-in because row-group monotonicity does not prove row ordering inside each row group; a future default rule needs stronger order metadata or an intra-row-group proof.
          - Validation passed: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `python3 -m py_compile scripts/compare_generic_duckdb.py`, release build, and `scripts/check_correctness.sh quick`. Scale-1 generic sampled Dodam `~61.2ms` vs DuckDB `~42.5ms` (`~1.44x`) in a noisy run. Default-safe changes are modest; opt-in monotonic LIMIT is a strong targeted win when the file is known physically ordered.
        - Continued the requested 1/2/3 structural pass:
          - 1 / three-key expression aggregate fusion boundary: removed one remaining materialization point in the opt-in dictionary scan-fold path. Instead of building a filtered `RecordBatch` after evaluating the predicate mask, `CoalesceKeyCountSumCollector::consume_projected_view_masked(...)` now consumes the `BatchView` plus `BooleanArray` mask directly and updates selected rows only. This is a general filter-mask aggregate fold rule, not a query-name fast path.
          - Scale-10 focused comparison with defaults remained around Dodam `~87.7ms` vs DuckDB `~47.5ms` for `nested_projection_filter`, ordered/no-order three-key, and `nullable_key_group`. The new masked dictionary fold is not default-enabled.
          - With `DODAM_ENABLE_EXPRESSION_AGG_DICTIONARY_SCAN_FOLD=1`, scale-10 ordered three-key regressed (`~35.5ms` vs default `~29.5ms`) while no-order improved slightly/noisily (`~29.8ms` vs default `~32.2ms`). Because ordered output is worse and the signal is mixed, dictionary scan-fold remains opt-in.
          - 2 / primitive value decode: no new default was promoted. The nullable primitive aggregate already uses raw nullable keys and a cursor-style nullable sum input; the scale-10 gap is still dominated by `ColumnReaderImpl::read_records` value decode for the `value` column, not by aggregate-loop null lookup.
          - 3 / monotonic ORDER BY LIMIT: kept the existing row-group monotonic streaming path opt-in only. It is a large win when the file is physically ordered, but row-group min/max monotonicity alone still cannot prove intra-row-group ordering, so defaulting it would be a correctness risk.
        - Disk-I/O reduction follow-up:
          - Added an opt-in byte-aware late-materialization cost gate. When enabled for unordered expression aggregates with `DODAM_ENABLE_EXPRESSION_AGG_LATE_IO_COST_GATE=1`, the late path estimates predicate and payload compressed bytes from Parquet metadata and requires at least `DODAM_LATE_MIN_ESTIMATED_IO_SAVING_RATIO` estimated I/O saving before doing the payload second pass. The helper can also override a pure selected-ratio rejection when byte savings are high.
          - This is intentionally opt-in. A default experiment on the scale-10 focused set was mixed/noisy: no-order three-key sometimes improved, but ordered three-key and full focused totals regressed in the same run series. The underlying issue is that reducing payload bytes is not enough when RowSelection setup, second-pass reader setup, and Arrow RecordBatch materialization dominate.
          - Current conclusion: yes, unordered aggregates should reduce disk I/O more aggressively, but the default rule needs a lower-level fused predicate+payload reader or page/vector-level skip. A metadata-only cost gate is useful instrumentation/policy infrastructure, not yet a retained speed win.
        - Implemented the requested 1/2/3/4 fused reader experiment:
          - Added raw Parquet dictionary values to `BatchView` via `DictionaryStringValues::Bytes`, so execution can aggregate dictionary-id string keys without first constructing Arrow `StringArray` values.
          - Added an opt-in fused selected reader for the common expression aggregate shape: `Int32` key + `Date32` key + dictionary string key + `Int64` sum, filtered by a `Decimal128(Int64 physical)` predicate column. Null dictionary rows are remapped to the `COALESCE` fallback id before reaching the aggregate collector.
          - Added a shape-based SQL rule behind `DODAM_ENABLE_FUSED_DICTIONARY_SELECTED_AGG=1`. It is not query-name specific: it matches `GROUP BY column, column, coalesce(dictionary_column, utf8_literal)` plus `count(*)`/`sum(int64)` and a decimal range predicate.
          - SF=10 focused result was worse, so it remains opt-in: with `DODAM_ENABLE_FUSED_DICTIONARY_SELECTED_AGG=1`, `three_key_expression_group` sampled about `~51.0ms` and no-order about `~43.4ms`, versus default samples around `~35.4ms` and `~29.6ms` in the same run series.
          - Root cause: this implementation still decodes dictionary ids for the whole row group up front, and the fixture has about 50% selected rows. It avoids Arrow string materialization, but adds dictionary page decode/compaction work without enough payload I/O reduction. To win, the next version must push selection into page/vector decode itself or use Parquet page index/page-level skipping, not just compact after row-group dictionary decode.
          - Follow-up: moved dictionary decode after the selected-payload gate so high-selectivity inputs can fallback after reading only the predicate batch instead of decoding row-group dictionary ids first. This reduced wasted work conceptually but still did not make the opt-in path competitive.
          - Added `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` to the generic benchmark to test the intended low-selectivity shape. Default SF=10 sampled about `~25.6-29.7ms` vs DuckDB `~9.5-11ms`; fused dictionary selected stayed worse (`~57.5ms`) because it still decodes dictionary ids for the whole row group.
          - Byte-aware late I/O gate (`DODAM_ENABLE_EXPRESSION_AGG_LATE_IO_COST_GATE=1`) did help the low-selectivity three-key sample in one run (`~25.6ms -> ~19.5ms`), but regressed/noised high-selectivity no-order and the full suite. It remains opt-in. A lower selected-ratio threshold was rejected because it sent high-selectivity cases to the much slower scan-fold fallback.
          - Follow-up page/vector selection work: changed the opt-in fused dictionary selected reader so it no longer decodes row-group dictionary ids and compacts afterward. It now builds predicate-selected row runs first, skips non-overlapping dictionary pages, and applies selection inside the dictionary id decode step. For non-null pages, selected-run gaps are skipped directly in the RLE/bit-packed decoder. For nullable pages, def-levels are scanned to count present values and only selected rows append dictionary ids or the COALESCE fallback sentinel.
          - SF=10 focused result improved materially but is still not default-worthy: `DODAM_ENABLE_FUSED_DICTIONARY_SELECTED_AGG=1 low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` sampled `~27.9ms` vs DuckDB `~12.5ms`, better than the prior fused `~36-57ms` samples but still worse than the best late-materialization/io-gate experiment. High-selectivity three-key remained slower (`~39.2ms` ordered, `~33.7ms` no-order), so the fused path stays opt-in. This confirms page/vector selection is the right structural direction, but current custom decoder overhead and selected payload reader setup still outweigh the saved Arrow string materialization on these fixtures.
        - Started a thin custom primitive page reader experiment:
          - Added an opt-in `DODAM_ENABLE_PRIMITIVE_PAGE_READER=1` path for `DirectPrimitiveFold` scans. It reuses the `parquet` crate file/page reader but bypasses `ColumnReaderImpl::read_records` for flat `INT32`/`INT64` columns with repetition level 0, definition level 0/1, and PLAIN encoding. Unsupported encodings/shapes safely reopen and fall back to the existing reader.
          - The first version emits one raw `BatchView` per row group and supports compact nullable values plus def-levels, so it is a structural step toward a Dodam page/vector pipeline without rewriting footer/schema/compression handling.
          - Added little-endian byte-copy decode for PLAIN `i32/i64`, avoiding per-value `from_le_bytes` loops. Also removed an intermediate `Vec<i32>` from custom RLE def-level decode by decoding directly to `i16`.
          - SF=10 focused comparison is mixed, so this stays opt-in: default `nullable_key_group + non_null_bucket_count_sum + low_selectivity_decimal_date_group` sampled Dodam `~41.45ms` vs DuckDB `~20.0ms`; with `DODAM_ENABLE_PRIMITIVE_PAGE_READER=1` sampled Dodam `~41.20ms` vs DuckDB `~19.5ms`. `non_null_bucket_count_sum` and low-selectivity decimal/date were slightly better, while `nullable_key_group` was slightly worse/noisy.
          - Conclusion: a thin page reader alone is not enough. The next real gap-closing step is chunked page/vector streaming or direct page-to-aggregate fusion, because row-group materialization still leaves a large pipeline boundary and nullable def-level handling cost.
          - Added a second required-only page streaming path under the same opt-in gate. For required flat primitive columns, the reader now first tries a cursor that keeps one Parquet page at a time and emits normal query-sized batches, avoiding the previous full row-group materialization. Optional columns still use the row-group page reader or fall back.
          - SF=10 focused result remained mostly neutral: `DODAM_ENABLE_PRIMITIVE_PAGE_READER=1 nullable_key_group + non_null_bucket_count_sum + low_selectivity_decimal_date_group` sampled Dodam `~40.85ms` vs DuckDB `~20.0ms`. Required-only `non_null_bucket_count_sum` alone was effectively unchanged (`~11.37ms` default vs `~11.36ms` opt-in in one 7-repeat run).
          - Updated conclusion: the 1.37x-style gap is not closed by replacing `read_records` with a thin page cursor. The durable next step must fuse page decode with aggregate update so values are not first copied into `Vec`s and then scanned again by `SingleKeyCountSumVectorState`.
          - Pushed the required-only cursor one step closer to page-to-aggregate fusion:
            - Added byte-backed primitive `RawColumnView` variants (`I32Bytes`, `I64Bytes`, `Date32Bytes`, `Decimal128I64Bytes`) so a required PLAIN page slice can flow through `BatchView` without first copying into a typed `Vec`.
            - Added byte-backed `I32VectorView`/`I64VectorView` support plus count/sum aggregate loops that directly read key/sum bytes with little-endian unaligned loads when both inputs are byte-backed.
            - Validation passed with `DODAM_ENABLE_PRIMITIVE_PAGE_READER=1`.
          - SF=10 focused result still stayed near neutral: `DODAM_ENABLE_PRIMITIVE_PAGE_READER=1 non_null_bucket_count_sum` sampled `~11.78ms` vs DuckDB `~6.5ms`; the 3-query focused set sampled Dodam `~41.03ms` vs DuckDB `~18.5ms`. This means the remaining gap is not just copy avoidance or enum dispatch. The next useful step is a real aggregate-specific operator that updates `CountSumGroupIndex` directly from page bytes and can specialize group lookup/cache policy, rather than routing through the generic `BatchView`/vector abstraction.
          - Tried the first aggregate-specific page operator:

            - Added `SingleKeyCountSumVectorState::consume_i32_i64_page_bytes(...)` and `consume_i64_i64_page_bytes(...)`, then wired `DirectPrimitiveFoldExec` behind `DODAM_ENABLE_PRIMITIVE_PAGE_AGG_OPERATOR=1`.
            - Result was worse on SF=10 `non_null_bucket_count_sum`: page aggregate operator sampled `~12.2-12.6ms` vs DuckDB `~6.0ms`, worse than the previous `~11.7-11.9ms` area. Enabling the existing i32 batch group cache did not help (`~12.6ms`).

To win here, the operator needs a more specialized group-index update strategy, e.g. direct dense-slot update for known narrow integer ranges, not just a different input transport.
          - Tried direct dense-slot update for i32 page bytes:
            - Added an opt-in dense range preparation path that converts `DenseI32GroupIndex` to a concrete dense slot array for narrow batch key ranges, then updates slots directly instead of calling `get/insert` per row.
            - Inside the failed page aggregate operator it improved the failed path slightly (`~12.68ms -> ~11.98ms` in one SF=10 focused run), but still did not beat the existing page reader path.
            - Applying the same dense update to the byte-backed `BatchView` path regressed (`~11.83ms` with dense vs `~11.41ms` disabled in one run). The extra min/max key scan per batch costs more than the saved lookup on this fixture.
            - Kept dense page aggregate behind `DODAM_ENABLE_PRIMITIVE_PAGE_DENSE_AGG=1`; default is off. Current conclusion: dense-slot update only helps if the key range is known cheaply from metadata/planner or carried from a prior pass. Sampling min/max inside every batch is too expensive.

        - Continued the three-key expression aggregate work:
          - Added `DirectI32I32DictionaryI64SelectedBatch` and changed the opt-in fused dictionary selected aggregate to pass typed `i32/date32/dictionary-id/i64` slices into execution.
          - Added `CoalesceKeyCountSumCollector::consume_i32_date_dictionary_i64_slices(...)` so this fused path can update groups directly without building a `BatchView` or re-binding projected columns. The method preserves the existing dense-leading-index policy by deriving the same small dense range from the selected first/date slices.
          - Result: this improved the previously failed fused path a bit but still did not beat the default late-materialized path. SF=10 low-selectivity three-key no-order sampled `~27.0ms` with `DODAM_ENABLE_FUSED_DICTIONARY_SELECTED_AGG=1` vs default `~25.5ms` in the same run series. Profiling showed the real fused-path cost is still selected payload/dictionary read (`value` and `label` column reads), not `BatchView` construction.
          - Added direct primitive profile logging to the fused dictionary fold so future experiments expose read/consume/selected-run metrics.
          - Added a default-on small-payload late aggregate rule: when late-materialized expression aggregate payload batches are small (`DODAM_EXPRESSION_AGG_LATE_VIEW_PAYLOAD_MAX_ROWS`, default `8192`), the collector consumes the `BatchView` directly instead of converting back through `RecordBatch`.           - Effect was modest: SF=10 low-selectivity three-key no-order improved around `~20.5ms -> ~19.5ms` in focused runs, while the full generic suite was effectively neutral/noisy (`~189.6ms` with the rule vs `~189.5ms` disabled in one 5-repeat comparison). Keep it because it is policy-gated to small payload batches, but it does not close the structural gap.

The default Arrow RowSelection late path is already competitive with the custom selected reader. To beat it, Dodam needs lower-level page/vector payload decode that avoids repeated selected-run reader setup and updates aggregate groups while decoding, or a better way to use Parquet page indexes/dictionary pages for sparse selected payloads.
        - Tried balanced direct primitive row-group parallel partitioning:
          - The previous direct primitive fold used `chunks(ceil(row_groups / workers))`. With SF=10 generic facts (`49` row groups on a `32` CPU machine), this produced only `25` tasks, leaving available CPU parallelism unused for scan-heavy primitive aggregates.
          - Added balanced partitioning so direct primitive folds can create up to `min(row_groups, available_parallelism)` partitions with sizes differing by at most one. The experiment covers both engine and physical direct primitive folds and is gated by `DODAM_ENABLE_BALANCED_DIRECT_PRIMITIVE_PARTITIONS=1`.
          - Focused profiling showed the intended effect on primitive aggregates: `non_null_bucket_count_sum` materialize time dropped from a prior profile around `~33.6ms` to `~17.3ms`, and `nullable_key_group` from around `~19.8ms` to `~17.0ms`. Worker-summed `value` column read also dropped materially in the profile.
          - Query-file benchmark comparison did not confirm it as a safe default. With balanced partitions enabled, SF=10 `nullable_key_group + non_null_bucket_count_sum` sampled about `~31.5ms`; with the old chunking behavior in the same binary it sampled about `~28.4ms`. The extra tasks/merge/scheduler overhead can outweigh the better CPU utilization.
          - Therefore the change remains opt-in only. Conclusion: row-group scheduling can matter in profiles, but it is not the current durable bottleneck. The deeper gap is still `value` decode throughput and page/vector-to-aggregate fusion.
        - Tried merging i32 dense index `get`/`insert` into one get-or-insert path:
          - The idea was to avoid duplicate dense offset work when a group is first seen.
          - SF=10 primitive focused benchmark regressed/noised (`nullable_key_group + non_null_bucket_count_sum` around `~30.6ms`), likely due to larger branchy code in the hot path.
          - Reverted to the simpler existing `get` then `insert` structure. This confirms the primitive gap is not primarily first-seen group insertion; it is dominated by scan/decode and steady-state per-row update.
        - Tried dense i32 direct update for already-dense primitive aggregates:
          - Added `DODAM_ENABLE_DENSE_I32_DIRECT_UPDATE=1`, which bypasses `count_sum_group_id_for_i32_cached(...)` in the hot loop when the state already has a dense i32 slot range from metadata/statistics. It updates the dense slot directly for non-null key/sum and nullable-key/non-null-sum paths.
          - Focused SF=10 primitive benchmark showed only a small/noisy improvement: opt-in sampled `~28.7ms` vs default `~29.0ms` for `nullable_key_group + non_null_bucket_count_sum`. `nullable_key_group` improved slightly in that run (`~17.2ms -> ~16.9ms`), while non-null bucket was effectively unchanged.
          - Profile showed consume time can drop, but read/decode variance still dominates wall time. Kept the path opt-in; it is a useful building block but not enough to default.
        - Added selected payload reader instrumentation:
          - `DirectPrimitiveColumnScanMetrics` now tracks `selected_skip_calls`, `selected_skipped_rows`, `selected_read_calls`, and `selected_read_rows`, and the direct primitive profile prints them.
          - This explains why the opt-in fused dictionary selected aggregate remains slower than the default Arrow RowSelection late path. SF=10 low-selectivity three-key selected `60k` rows, but the custom selected payload reader performed `1,821` read calls and `1,944` skip calls across payload columns, reading `180k` payload rows and skipping `17.82M` row positions. The run-level `skip_records/read_records` loop is too chatty.
          - Conclusion: selected payload improvement must happen at page/vector decode level or by coalescing selected runs into page/window reads. More collector-side changes will not close this gap.
        - Added exact monotonic proof for expression-filter stream LIMIT:
          - Existing `DODAM_ENABLE_EXPRESSION_FILTER_MONOTONIC_STREAM_LIMIT=1` used row-group min/max monotonicity, which is fast but does not prove row ordering inside a row group.
          - Added `DODAM_ENABLE_EXPRESSION_FILTER_EXACT_MONOTONIC_STREAM_LIMIT=1`. When enabled, Dodam scans only the ORDER BY column first and verifies it is globally non-null ascending before using stream-limit execution. This is still opt-in because it adds an extra proof scan, but it removes the correctness concern from the previous row-group-stats-only mode.
          - SF=10 `nested_projection_filter` with exact proof sampled Dodam `~4.8ms` vs DuckDB `~7.0ms`, still much faster than the default full expression-filter scan. This is a good candidate for a future cost-based default when the order column is narrow and projected/filter payload is expensive.
        - Audited the current vectorized pipeline boundary:
          - The project has moved many TPC-H/generic hot paths from Arrow-only `RecordBatch` downcasts to `BatchView` + typed vector views, but many operators are still vector-shaped row loops. This is expected for grouped aggregation and selection because the hot operation is usually branchy group lookup/update or nullable predicate output, not pure arithmetic.
          - Added a small general cleanup for projected-view scalar predicates: `compare_*_view` and `IN` list view evaluators now build boolean masks with `BooleanBuilder` instead of `Vec<Option<bool>>`, and null-free raw/Arrow slice cases use `BooleanBufferBuilder` to build the values bitmap directly.
          - Added small-literal `IN` matching for `i32/i64` view predicates and selection pushdown, avoiding repeated generic `slice.contains` calls for common short literal lists.
          - Correctness quick passed. SF=10 `nested_projection_filter` individual benchmark stayed effectively flat/noisy (`~27.53ms -> ~27.37ms`, DuckDB `~21.6-22.1ms`). This confirms that this path's remaining default gap is not mask allocation alone; it is still mostly Parquet/Arrow decode/materialization plus the lack of a default monotonic/order proof.
          - SIMD assessment: there is no current `std::simd`/SIMD dependency, and stable Rust 1.89 does not make portable SIMD a low-risk default. The best SIMD candidates are lower than the current SQL mask layer: page/vector decoder predicates, null-free primitive comparisons that can emit Arrow bitmaps directly, and dense aggregate updates when the planner/storage provides range facts. Applying SIMD around current `BatchView` row loops is unlikely to close the DuckDB gap because scan/decode and scatter-style group updates dominate.
        - Improved the byte-backed vector pipeline:
          - Added `Date32VectorView::raw_bytes()` and extended projected-view predicate mask builders to consume `RawBytes` `i32/i64/date32` vectors directly with `read_i32_le_unaligned` / `read_i64_le_unaligned`, emitting null-free Arrow boolean bitmaps without routing through enum `value(row)` per element.
          - Split the direct primitive scanner entrypoint so execution can force the page/byte-backed reader only for `SingleKeyCountSum` direct primitive folds. The decimal/date count/sum/min/max path keeps the existing reader by default because prior opt-in page-reader measurements were mixed or regressed low-selectivity selected/filter payload cases.
          - Correctness quick passed with the new default. SF=10 primitive focused benchmark improved materially in the default path: `nullable_key_group + non_null_bucket_count_sum + decimal_date_group_no_order` sampled Dodam `~69.5ms` vs DuckDB `~76.7ms`; the previous default sample in this pass was Dodam `~91.2ms` vs DuckDB `~78.2ms`.
          - The largest focused win was `non_null_bucket_count_sum`, moving from the previous default sample around `~41.4ms` to `~20.8ms`, now faster than DuckDB's sampled `~24.8ms`.
          - SF=10 full generic 3-repeat sample after the change was Dodam `~312.2ms`, DuckDB `~309.7ms`, ratio `~1.01x`. Remaining slower generic cases are mostly expression/nested projection and three-key expression aggregates, not the direct primitive count/sum scan.
        - Defaulted a narrow exact-monotonic expression-filter LIMIT rule:
          - For expression-filter scans with `ORDER BY <single ascending column> LIMIT`, Dodam now tries a safe automatic stream-limit path. It first checks row-group min/max monotonicity, then scans only the order column to prove global non-null ascending order. Only if both proofs pass does it filter batches and stop at LIMIT before materializing/sorting the full result.
          - Added `DODAM_DISABLE_EXPRESSION_FILTER_EXACT_MONOTONIC_AUTO=1` as the escape hatch. The old explicit env flags still work for forced stream-limit experiments.
          - Correctness quick passed. SF=10 `nested_projection_filter` A/B improved from `~28.1ms` with auto disabled to `~11.2ms` with the automatic proof, versus DuckDB `~21.4ms`.
          - Full SF=10 generic 3-repeat after this and the direct primitive page-reader change sampled around Dodam `~322ms`, DuckDB `~315ms`, ratio `~1.02x`; `nested_projection_filter` is now faster than DuckDB in the default path.
        - Retuned expression aggregate late payload routing:
          - Raised `DODAM_EXPRESSION_AGG_LATE_VIEW_PAYLOAD_MAX_ROWS` default from `8192` to `131072` so high-selected 3-key expression aggregates can stay on `BatchView` instead of falling back through `RecordBatch` reconstruction.
          - Focused SF=10 three-key set remained modestly better/noisy: `three_key_expression_group_no_order` sampled around `~45.6ms` vs DuckDB `~37.0ms`, and `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` around `~32.3ms` vs DuckDB `~28.4ms`. The ordered variant is still slower around `~53ms` in focused runs.
          - Profiling shows the remaining 3-key gap is `CoalesceKeyCountSumCollector` update work, not scan proof or output. The next useful work is a more specialized composite group index/update path for `(i32/date32, coalesce dictionary/plain utf8, i64 sum)`, not another late-materialization policy tweak.
        - Retuned expression aggregate late row-group chunking:
          - Swept `DODAM_EXPRESSION_AGG_LATE_ROW_GROUP_CHUNK` for SF=10 `three_key_expression_group_no_order`. In short runs, chunk `1` was around `~65.8ms`, chunk `2` around `~51.2ms`, chunk `4` around `~39.5ms`, and chunk `8` around `~40.4ms`.
          - Changed the default late expression aggregate row-group chunk from `2` to `4`. This is a general task-granularity rule for late expression aggregate map/fold, not a query-name rule.
          - Rechecked chunk `5` because one short sweep looked slightly better, but the same-binary 5-repeat comparison favored chunk `4` again (`chunk=4` three-key set around `~114.6ms` vs DuckDB `~108.1ms`; default chunk `5` sample around `~134.3ms`). Kept default at `4`; chunk tuning is noisy and should remain env-overridable.
          - Focused SF=10 three-key set after the default change sampled Dodam `~121.8ms` vs DuckDB `~101.5ms`, better than the recent default `~136.9ms` sample but still noisy.
          - Full SF=10 generic 3-repeat after the default change sampled Dodam `~291.1ms`, DuckDB `~309.5ms`, ratio `~0.94x`. Remaining slower cases are still the 3-key expression aggregates and occasional decimal/date noise.
          - Failed experiments in this pass:
            - Forcing `DODAM_ENABLE_DENSE_U32_EAGER=1` for the third string-id group index regressed the focused three-key set (`~141.0ms` vs better defaults), so eager dense third-index remains opt-in/off.
            - Forcing `DODAM_ENABLE_COALESCE_RUN_AGGREGATE=1` did not improve the focused set enough and sometimes regressed no-order cases; run aggregation remains conditional.
            - A direct dense-leading-slot update helper for 3-key non-null sum loops regressed the focused three-key set (`~144.0ms`). It was removed. The likely reason is that the existing dense leading index is already cheap; adding another branch/helper path enlarged the hot loop without reducing the dominant third-index/string-id work enough.
            - A dictionary-specific 3-key loop that bypassed `CoalesceStringIdViewCache::string_id(row)` and read dictionary key slices directly also regressed the focused three-key set (`~135.2ms` in one 5-repeat sample). It was removed. The likely reason is either that the generic fixture is not dominated by dictionary-key dispatch, or that the extra branch/specialized loop does not reduce the remaining third group lookup cost enough.
            - Added an opt-in batch-local dense accumulator (`DODAM_ENABLE_COALESCE_LOCAL_DENSE_ACCUMULATE=1`) for 3-key non-null count/sum loops. It accumulates `(dense leading slot, string_id)` locally and flushes once per touched group. The focused three-key set regressed (`~144.7ms` opt-in vs `~131.6ms` default in the same binary), especially low-selectivity (`~44.7ms` vs `~32.2ms`), because local array allocation/initialization and flush overhead outweighed fewer global group updates. Kept opt-in only as a documented negative result.
            - Added an opt-in dictionary group-id cache (`DODAM_ENABLE_COALESCE_GROUP_ID_CACHE=1`) that caches `(dense leading slot, dictionary id) -> group id` and updates groups directly. It helped some high-selected samples slightly but regressed low-selectivity badly without a row-count gate. With `DODAM_COALESCE_GROUP_ID_CACHE_MIN_ROWS=32768`, the focused three-key set was still effectively neutral/worse (`~132.5ms` opt-in vs `~132.1ms` default in one same-binary comparison). Kept opt-in only; default remains off.
            - Added an opt-in AVX2 decimal-only range predicate experiment (`DODAM_ENABLE_DECIMAL_DATE_SIMD_FILTER=1`) for the direct decimal/date aggregate loop. The implementation keeps the normal group update path and only vectorizes the `i64` decimal predicate test when there is no date bound. It did not produce a durable improvement: SF=10 `low_selectivity_decimal_date_group` sampled `~24.7ms` default vs `~24.8ms` with SIMD opt-in, both faster than DuckDB in that run. Date-bound cases stay on the scalar path. Conclusion: SIMD around the current row-update loop is too shallow; useful SIMD needs to sit lower in page/vector decode or feed a fused aggregate operator that avoids per-row callback/update overhead.
            - Extended the dictionary group-id cache experiment to the typed fused selected dictionary aggregate path. The path now can cache `(dense leading slot, dictionary id) -> group id` for `DirectI32I32DictionaryI64SelectedBatch`, but it is also kept behind `DODAM_ENABLE_COALESCE_GROUP_ID_CACHE=1`. A/B on SF=10 low-selectivity three-key no-order showed no improvement (`~42.1ms` with the cache vs `~41.6ms` with the cache disabled in one run). The better fused selected sample after reverting the cache to opt-in was around `~41.0ms`, still slower than DuckDB `~33.9ms`. Conclusion: the remaining gap is not repeated group-id lookup alone; selected payload read/skip and scattered group updates still dominate.
            - Structural selected-path cleanup:
              - Direct decimal selected-run building now merges adjacent runs across predicate decode chunks. This is generally correct for `RowSelection`-style payload reads and prevents artificial run fragmentation at batch boundaries, although the SF=10 low-selectivity three-key case did not materially move because its natural selected runs are already long and sparse.
              - Added `--show-stderr` to `scripts/compare_generic_duckdb.py` so direct primitive/coalesce profile lines are visible during benchmark runs. This exposed the current low-selectivity fused selected shape: `6M` scanned rows, `60k` selected rows, about `600` selected runs, and roughly `1800` selected payload read calls across payload columns.
              - Added Decimal128 Parquet min/max support to the i128 statistics helper and introduced an automatic fused dictionary selected aggregate gate. The rule estimates decimal range selectivity from Parquet min/max and tries the fused selected path when the estimated ratio is at most `DODAM_FUSED_DICT_SELECTED_AUTO_MAX_ESTIMATED_RATIO` (default `0.05`). It can be disabled with `DODAM_DISABLE_FUSED_DICTIONARY_SELECTED_AUTO=1`; the old `DODAM_ENABLE_FUSED_DICTIONARY_SELECTED_AGG=1` still forces the path.
              - Same-binary SF=10 A/B: `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` improved from `~46.4ms` with auto disabled to `~40.7ms` with auto enabled, vs DuckDB `~32.8-33.7ms`. High-selectivity `three_key_expression_group_no_order` showed a small stats-gate overhead in one sample (`~59.3ms` auto vs `~57.9ms` disabled), so the next refinement is caching or cheaper selectivity estimation before making this broader.
              - Refined the auto gate overhead:
                - Changed decimal type and Decimal128 min/max checks to use cached `ArrowReaderMetadata` directly instead of opening the file and constructing a `ParquetRecordBatchReaderBuilder` just to inspect schema/statistics.
                - Added `DODAM_FUSED_DICT_SELECTED_AUTO_WORKERS` for the automatic low-selectivity fused path and defaulted it to `8`. A real connected-worker sweep showed that `1` worker is a severe regression (`~120.7ms`) because it serializes row-group scans; `8` workers sampled best around `~39.0ms`, and `16` was slightly worse around `~39.8ms`.
                - Final same-binary samples in this pass: low-selectivity three-key no-order default auto `~39.0ms` vs DuckDB `~33.5ms`; high-selectivity no-order default auto `~60.2ms` vs auto disabled `~59.2ms`. The remaining high-selectivity overhead is about `~1ms`; the next step is a cheaper pre-gate or query-level stats cache so high-selectivity queries avoid even the min/max decision cost.
                - Added an engine-level Decimal128/i128 column min/max cache keyed by path, file length, modified timestamp, and column. This keeps the selectivity decision from rescanning row-group metadata within the same engine process and avoids stale results when files change. SF=10 high-selectivity no-order improved to around `~57.8ms` with auto enabled vs `~56.9ms` with auto disabled in one same-binary comparison; low-selectivity remained around `~37.8-40.1ms` depending on run noise.
              - Failed follow-up experiments:
                - Hybrid selected payload (`DODAM_FUSED_DICT_SELECTED_FULL_PRIMITIVE_PAYLOAD=1`) reads primitive payload columns (`bucket/event_date/value`) as full row-group vectors and compacts in memory while still using dictionary selected page decode. It reduced consume/update time (`~17ms -> ~13ms` in profile) but increased read time materially (`~113ms -> ~157ms`), regressing low-selectivity three-key no-order to around `~51-52ms`. Keep opt-in only.
                - Cross-batch dictionary-to-string-id cache (`DODAM_ENABLE_COALESCE_DICTIONARY_STRING_ID_CACHE=1`) avoids remapping the same dictionary values to global string IDs in each selected batch. The dictionary is tiny in the generic fixture, so hashing/cloning the dictionary key costs more than it saves: low-selectivity three-key no-order sampled `~40.8ms` with the cache vs `~40.1ms` default. Keep opt-in only.
                - Window/coalesced primitive selected payload (`DODAM_FUSED_DICT_SELECTED_WINDOW_PAYLOAD=1`) reads wider primitive windows around selected runs and compacts selected rows in memory. With conservative defaults it was neutral because the natural selected gaps are too large to merge. With aggressive `DODAM_FUSED_DICT_SELECTED_WINDOW_MAX_GAP=12000` and amplification `100`, read calls dropped dramatically (`~1800 -> 147`) but selected read rows exploded (`~180k -> 16.5M`), regressing low-selectivity three-key no-order to around `~48ms`. This confirms the current selected-run reader is already the better side of the I/O tradeoff for sparse periodic predicates; the real next step is lower-level page/vector predicate fusion rather than coalescing logical runs.
                - Primitive page-slice selected payload (`DODAM_FUSED_DICT_SELECTED_PRIMITIVE_PAGE_SLICE=1`) decodes required/non-null PLAIN int32/int64 payload columns by slicing selected byte ranges directly from Parquet page buffers, falling back to the selected-run reader when unsupported. It successfully stayed in the fused selected path, and profile showed lower consume/update time in one run, but 5-repeat SF=10 low-selectivity three-key no-order regressed (`~42.7ms` opt-in vs `~40.8ms` default, DuckDB `~33.4ms`). The likely reason is that page-reader setup and per-page/range slicing overhead outweighs avoiding `ColumnReader::skip_records/read_records` for this fixture. Keep opt-in only.
                - Generalized selected-mask plumbing for the fused dictionary aggregate path:
                  - Added common `SelectionRuns` / `SelectionRunsBuilder` types in `vector.rs` and moved decimal predicate selected-run construction onto that builder. This makes predicate pushdown output a reusable mask/run boundary instead of ad hoc `(start, len)` vectors plus manual selected-row counters.
                  - Extended `DirectI32I32DictionaryI64SelectedBatch` into compact and masked variants. The default path still sends compact selected arrays, but execution can now consume full vectors with a `SelectionRuns` mask through `CoalesceKeyCountSumCollector::consume_i32_date_dictionary_i64_masked(...)`.
                  - Added an opt-in masked full-payload experiment (`DODAM_FUSED_DICT_SELECTED_FULL_PRIMITIVE_PAYLOAD=1 DODAM_FUSED_DICT_SELECTED_MASKED_FULL_PAYLOAD=1`). It validates the new structural boundary but is slower: SF=10 low-selectivity three-key no-order 5-repeat sampled Dodam `~47.5ms` vs DuckDB `~33.4ms`, while the default compact path sampled `~41.4ms`. The regression is expected because it full-decodes primitive payload and dictionary ids before applying the mask; this is not yet true page/vector decoder fusion.
                  - Added a default-on AVX2 decimal selected-run builder for closed `i64` range predicates. It can be disabled with `DODAM_DISABLE_DECIMAL_SELECTED_RUNS_SIMD=1`. Same-binary SF=10 low-selectivity three-key no-order 5-repeat sampled default SIMD `~41.4ms` vs SIMD disabled `~42.5ms`; the gain is small but positive and sits at the storage selection boundary rather than the aggregate row-update loop.
                  - Conclusion: selection/mask is now a first-class execution boundary, but the big gap still requires decoding payload pages directly into aggregate-specific vector/update operators. Full-payload masked execution is a useful scaffold, not the final fast path.

                  - Added selected-path sub-metrics to `DirectPrimitiveColumnScanMetrics`: selected predicate, selected payload, and selected dictionary time. `DODAM_DIRECT_PRIMITIVE_PROFILE=1` now prints these separately, so the low-selectivity selected aggregate can distinguish decimal predicate scan, payload decode, dictionary decode, and consume/update.
                  - Profile on SF=10 `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` showed the remaining gap is still read/decode, not aggregate consume: selected predicate around `~30ms`, selected payload around `~76-82ms`, selected dictionary around `~31-33ms`, consume around `~9-13ms` in worker-summed profile time. The `value` payload column dominates selected payload time.
                  - Added an opt-in low-cardinality dictionary precompute rule (`DODAM_ENABLE_COALESCE_PRECOMPUTE_DICTIONARY_STRING_IDS=1`) that converts a row-group dictionary to global string ids before the row update loop. It keeps row updates to integer lookup only. Focused 5-repeat samples were noisy/near-neutral (`~40.0ms` opt-in vs `~40.3ms` default in one same-binary pass), so this remains opt-in.
                  - Generalized primitive page-slice selected payload to work per column instead of requiring all payload columns to support page-slice. Also extended the i64 PLAIN page-slice reader to nullable schema pages when def levels prove all rows are present.
                  - This made page-slice actually affect the `value` column, but it regressed: selected skip rows dropped (`~17.8M -> ~11.9M`), yet `value` column read rose from about `~67-70ms` to `~103-106ms`, and wall time regressed to around `~44.4ms` vs default around `~40.3ms`. The likely reason is extra page-reader/def-level validation overhead; the current `ColumnReader::skip_records/read_records` path is still better for this sparse periodic pattern.

                    - Added `DODAM_ENABLE_FUSED_SELECTED_I64_PAGE_DECODER=1` as a clearer opt-in alias for the selected i64 page decoder experiment.
                    - Replaced the selected PLAIN i64 page compact path's per-run `Bytes::slice -> decode_plain_i64_values(...)` calls with direct `copy_nonoverlapping` bulk copies into the output vector. This is the right primitive for the current shape because selected runs are contiguous and moderately long; AVX2 gather would be a poor fit for this access pattern.
                    - The result still regressed: SF=10 low-selectivity three-key no-order 5-repeat sampled default `~40.0ms` vs opt-in i64 page decoder `~45.4ms`. Profile still showed `value` column read around `~104-106ms`, much worse than the default selected reader's `~67-70ms`.
                    - Follow-up removed two obvious overheads in the page-decoder experiment: pages that do not overlap selected runs are skipped before def-level proof, and DataPageV2 with `num_nulls == 0` no longer decodes def levels just to prove all-present. This still did not make the path competitive: 5-repeat sampled default `~40.9ms` vs opt-in `~45.1ms`.
                    - Split the selected i64 decoder into a dedicated `storage/selected_i64_decoder.rs` module and made it bypass `ColumnReader::read_records/skip_records` entirely. The module consumes Parquet `Page` values directly, supports `INT64 + PLAIN + max_def_level 0/1`, skips non-overlapping pages with a run cursor, copies required/all-present pages by byte range, and supports nullable pages when selected rows are present by mapping def-level positions to value offsets. Unsupported shapes still fall back to the normal selected reader.
                    - This is the first real lower-level decoder boundary in Dodam, but it still sits on parquet crate's public `PageReader`, which has already performed page-level processing/decompression. It did not improve the target case: SF=10 low-selectivity three-key no-order sampled default `~41.3ms` vs `DODAM_ENABLE_FUSED_SELECTED_I64_PAGE_DECODER=1` `~45.2ms`. Profile showed `value` column read still around `~108-112ms`.

                      - The selected i64 decoder can now stream page chunks as `(selected_offset, &[i64])` directly into the existing aggregate consume closure, avoiding one row-group-sized `selected_sums` Vec.
                      - This regressed: SF=10 low-selectivity three-key no-order sampled default `~39.7ms` vs sink fusion `~43.8ms`. Profile showed worker-summed selected payload/read time much worse because the decoder now calls aggregate consume inside the sum decode timing path, and page/chunk-level consume dispatch adds overhead.
                      - Conclusion: eliminating the `selected_sums` Vec is not enough and can be worse when it increases consume dispatch frequency. The missing piece is not just streaming into the existing batch consume API; it would need a true aggregate-state sink with a hot loop over decoded values, not repeated `DirectI32I32DictionaryI64SelectedBatch::compact(...)` callbacks.
                    - Refined the sink experiment into a single-dispatch SumChunks batch:
                      - Added a `DirectI32I32DictionaryI64SelectedBatch::SumChunks` variant. The storage path collects selected i64 page chunks as `(selected_offset, Vec<i64>)`, then calls the collector once. The collector prepares group/dictionary state once and updates chunks directly, avoiding repeated batch consume callbacks.
                      - This still regressed: SF=10 low-selectivity three-key no-order sampled default `~40.2ms` vs `DODAM_ENABLE_FUSED_SELECTED_I64_AGG_SINK=1` `~45.2ms`. Profile showed consume dropped to around `~7-10ms`, but selected payload/read stayed very high (`~159-165ms` worker-summed). The sum chunk vectors also reintroduce materialization, just in fragmented form.
                      - Conclusion: batching the sink fixes callback frequency but not the core issue. The page decoder path remains slower than the default selected reader. A useful sink must be paired with a lower-cost decoder below the current public `PageReader`, not just a different handoff shape above it.

                      - It did not improve: page decoder without SIMD sampled `~45.7ms`; page decoder with SIMD sampled `~46.0ms`; default selected reader sampled `~42.2ms` in the same pass.
                      - Conclusion: for selected contiguous i64 ranges, the final byte copy is not the bottleneck. `copy_nonoverlapping` is already good enough, and SIMD load/store cannot compensate for the public page-reader/decode overhead.
                    - Tried moving SIMD/predicate work to a more appropriate layer: dictionary-encoded decimal predicate selection (`DODAM_ENABLE_DICTIONARY_DECIMAL_SELECTED_RUNS=1`).
                      - The target generic `amount` column is `INT64 + PLAIN_DICTIONARY`, so the right conceptual location is dictionary predicate evaluation: decode the dictionary values once, mark selected dictionary ids, then build selected runs from dictionary id pages instead of materializing every decimal value and comparing i64 rows.
                      - Implemented an opt-in row-group dictionary selected-run builder for `INT64 + RLE_DICTIONARY/PLAIN_DICTIONARY + max_def_level 0/1`.
                      - It regressed badly: SF=10 low-selectivity three-key no-order sampled default `~42.9ms` vs dictionary predicate path `~56.4ms`. Profile showed selected predicate/amount time rose to `~136-140ms`, far worse than the default `ColumnReader` path.
                      - Reason: the new path uses Dodam's simple scalar RLE/bit-packed dictionary id decoder over all rows. That is the wrong place for a hand-written scalar decoder; the parquet crate's `ColumnReader` dictionary decode is much better optimized. SIMD comparison cannot help until the dictionary id decode itself is efficient/vectorized.
                      - Improved the hand-written RLE/bit-packed decoder:
                        - Replaced bit-by-bit bit-packed value extraction with word-level load/shift/mask extraction.
                        - Fused dictionary id decode with selected-run construction so the path no longer materializes a full `Vec<i32>` of dictionary ids before building runs.
                        - Added an RLE-run API so non-null dictionary id RLE runs can create selected runs in chunks instead of row-by-row.
                      - These changes helped but did not win. The dictionary predicate path improved from `~56.4ms` to `~48.5ms` in 5-repeat wall time, and selected predicate/amount profile time dropped from `~136-140ms` to roughly `~70-77ms`. The default selected reader remained faster around `~42.5ms` wall time with predicate work in the `~30ms` range.

                        - The path reads Parquet hybrid RLE headers directly, handles RLE runs as chunks, and decodes bit-packed groups from the underlying bytes while building selected runs.
                        - It did not improve further. SF=10 low-selectivity three-key no-order sampled dictionary predicate `~50.2ms`, with selected predicate/amount profile back around `~88ms`.
                        - This suggests the remaining overhead is not just the single-value decoder call. The path still does page-level dictionary-id decoding and branchy membership/run construction in Rust, while the parquet crate's `ColumnReader` remains substantially faster for this column.
                      - Restructured the dictionary-id path into a vectorized boundary:
                        - Added a `DictionaryIdBlock` of 64 ids, block-level membership mask generation, and mask-to-run conversion.
                        - Added `SimpleRleBitpackedDecoder::decode_next_block(...)` so nullable paths can consume decoded ids in blocks instead of directly calling `next_value()` in row code.
                        - Non-null bit-packed runs now decode into `DictionaryIdBlock`, compute a block selected mask, and append selected runs from the mask. The current implementation still scalar-unpacks ids into the block, but the API boundary is now suitable for replacing that inner loop with SIMD unpack/membership kernels.
                        - Performance did not improve yet: SF=10 low-selectivity three-key no-order sampled default `~42.5ms` vs dictionary predicate block path `~50.8ms`, with selected predicate still around `~88-91ms`.
                        - This is an architectural step, not a win. The next performance step is to implement specialized block kernels for common bit widths rather than just wrapping scalar unpack in a block API.
                      - Refined the block path toward a real vector pipeline boundary:
                        - Replaced the non-null bit-packed block helper that recomputed byte/bit offsets per value with a sequential bit-buffer `BitpackedDictionaryIdBlockReader`. This keeps packed-run state and emits 64-id blocks, so future SIMD/unpack kernels can replace one tight block reader instead of scattered row code.
                        - Replaced the fixed bitset membership check with a general `DictionaryIdMatcher` that detects no-match/all-match/small selected-id ranges/bitset. This is a general dictionary-predicate rule, not a query-name fast path. It helps predicates where selected dictionary ids are contiguous or have a few ranges, such as generated decimal dictionaries.
                        - Correctness quick passed after the change. SF=10 focused A/B was mixed: one individual run improved Dodam absolute time from `~43.6ms` to `~41.2ms`, but a two-query pass showed `low_selectivity_decimal_date_group` improving (`~24.8ms -> ~21.7ms`) while `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` regressed (`~34.4ms -> ~41.7ms`) in that run.
                        - Conclusion: the block/range matcher is useful infrastructure but still not safe as a broad default. The planner/storage gate needs to include payload-column cost and selected-run read amplification, not just predicate selectivity. True SIMD should next target the packed dictionary-id block reader for common bit widths, especially non-byte-aligned widths such as 14.
                      - Continued the block/SIMD-oriented structure:
                        - Added aligned chunk decode paths inside `BitpackedDictionaryIdBlockReader`: byte-aligned 8/16/32-bit dictionary ids decode directly from bytes, and 14-bit dictionary ids decode 4 ids from 7 bytes. The generic bit-buffer remains as the correctness fallback for other widths or unaligned tails.
                        - Added a fused `decode_selected_mask_block(...)` path so packed bytes can produce a 64-lane selected mask directly, bypassing the intermediate `DictionaryIdBlock` fill plus second membership pass when the bit width is supported.
                        - Moved matching to a chunk API (`DictionaryIdMatcher::mask_ids`) so the 14-bit path can unpack 4 ids and test them with one matcher dispatch. This keeps the code shaped as `packed bytes -> id chunk -> selected mask -> runs`, which is the right boundary for later AVX2/AVX512 kernels.
                        - Correctness quick passed. SF=10 `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` same-binary A/B sampled default `~43.3ms` vs dictionary selected-run path `~41.3ms`, about `4.7%` Dodam absolute improvement in that run. Profile still showed worker-summed selected predicate around `~90-93ms`, so this is incremental rather than the final page/vector decoder fusion.
                      - Audited other row-at-a-time hot loops and started converting common selection plumbing:
                        - Found many remaining row loops in aggregate consume/update code, projected filter evaluation, join build/probe, and selected payload compaction. The safest common layer to convert first is selection-mask production/consumption because it feeds both scan pushdown and selected readers.
                        - Replaced `append_selected_mask_as_runs(...)` lane-by-lane scanning with bit-run extraction using `trailing_zeros`/`trailing_ones`. This consumes a 64-lane mask as runs directly and is shared by dictionary selected-run generation and decimal SIMD selected-run generation.
                        - Converted scalar decimal selected-run generation to build 64-lane masks before appending runs, instead of updating run state per row. Converted AVX2 decimal selected-run generation to accumulate 16 x 4-lane comparisons into one 64-lane mask before appending runs.
                        - Fixed a release-mode infinite-loop bug in the new mask consumer when all 64 lanes were selected; shifting a `u64` by 64 is not a valid way to clear the word. Correctness quick passed after the fix.
                        - SF=10 focused samples after the fix: `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` default `~43.5ms`, dictionary selected-run path `~41.1ms`; `low_selectivity_decimal_date_group` default `~25.6ms`, dictionary selected-run path `~24.8ms`. The structure is now more vector/block-friendly, but aggregate update and join probe loops still need similar block APIs.
                      - Continued row-at-a-time removal in aggregate state updates:
                        - Added `CountSumGroup::update_raw_i64_non_null_many(...)` and made non-null dense i32 count/sum aggregate consume 64-row blocks with a block-local `(group_id, count, sum)` accumulator. This reduces repeated group state writes when the block has low key cardinality.
                        - Generalized the rule with a data-shape gate instead of an env-only fast path: sample the first rows and use block-local accumulation only when sample unique key count is below `DODAM_DENSE_I32_BLOCK_ACCUMULATE_MAX_SAMPLE_UNIQUE` (default `32`). This avoids the nullable/high-cardinality regression seen during A/B.
