                        - Correctness quick passed. SF=10 individual samples: `non_null_bucket_count_sum` default improved to about `~16.7ms` vs DuckDB `~21.1ms`; `low_selectivity_decimal_date_group` default improved to about `~20.5ms` vs DuckDB `~22.6ms`; forced dictionary selected three-key remained around `~40.8ms` vs DuckDB `~28.1ms`.
                        - Conclusion: block-local aggregate accumulation is a real win for low-cardinality dense integer groups and is general enough to keep. Remaining large gap in three-key selected aggregate is not single-key dense state update; it is still selected dictionary/payload decode plus multi-key/string group update.
                      - Tried extending the same idea to three-key dictionary/string aggregates:
                        - Added a 64-row block-local `(group_id, count, sum)` accumulator for the precomputed string-id path and kept it behind `DODAM_ENABLE_THREE_KEY_BLOCK_ACCUMULATE=1` after testing.
                        - Also tried routing the default lazy dictionary-id path through block helpers, but this regressed the forced dictionary selected generic query. The hot path is sensitive enough that even helper extraction/fallback around dictionary-id -> string-id -> group-id showed measurable overhead.
                        - Final decision: restore the default lazy dictionary path to the original inline row loop, keep only the precomputed string-id block helper as an opt-in experiment, and keep the single-key dense block accumulator as the default useful rule.
                        - SF=10 samples after restoration: default generic `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` around `~34.1ms` vs DuckDB `~28.3ms`; `non_null_bucket_count_sum` around `~18.8ms`; `low_selectivity_decimal_date_group` around `~21.8ms`. Forced dictionary selected path remained poor (`~53ms`) and should not be used as a default.
                        - Conclusion: multi-key/string aggregate update is not helped by naive local group-id accumulation when per-block group uniqueness is high. The next useful three-key work is lower in scan/decode or a dedicated dictionary-id/group-id cache that avoids repeated string/group lookup without adding per-block O(unique) searches.
                      - Follow-up 1-4 pass on the remaining generic three-key gap:
                        - Added finer coalesce aggregate profile counters for typed dictionary paths: `typed_string_id`, `typed_group_id`, and `typed_group_update`. With `DODAM_COALESCE_AGG_PROFILE=1`, SF=10 low-selectivity three-key showed `typed_group_id` dominating the typed update time (`~8-23ms` worker-summed depending on path), while string-id and raw group update were small (`~1-2ms` each).
                        - Extended the existing `(dense leading slot, dictionary id) -> group_id` direct table to the masked selected path. Previously it only applied to compact slice paths, so forced dictionary selected aggregates could not benefit. With `DODAM_ENABLE_COALESCE_GROUP_ID_CACHE=1`, forced dictionary selected low-selectivity three-key improved from about `~50.8ms` to `~40.5ms`.
                        - Promoted the coalesce group-id cache to a default rule with a conservative row gate: default min rows is now `100k`, and it can be disabled with `DODAM_DISABLE_COALESCE_GROUP_ID_CACHE=1`. This lets high-selectivity three-key use the direct table while low-selectivity selected batches avoid the small-regression case. SF=10 `three_key_expression_group_no_order` improved to about `~44.5ms` vs `~47.8ms` with cache disabled; low-selectivity stayed around `~34.9ms`.
                        - Converted projected-view null-free filter mask construction to 64-lane mask production using `BooleanBufferBuilder::append_packed_range(...)`. The raw i32/i64 comparison and IN-list paths now build a word mask per 64 rows instead of appending one boolean at a time. This is a general expression/filter mask rule rather than a query-specific fast path.
                        - Added a conservative null-free block loop to the int32 dense unique join probe path. It avoids per-row Arrow null/value calls for the null-free case and processes probe keys in 64-row blocks before materialization. Generic `join_expression_projection` sampled Dodam `~31.9ms` vs DuckDB `~31.0ms`, so no obvious regression.
                        - Current SF=10 generic samples after this pass: `non_null_bucket_count_sum` `~21.1ms` vs DuckDB `~26.2ms`; `low_selectivity_decimal_date_group` `~22.3ms` vs DuckDB `~22.7ms`; `three_key_expression_group_no_order` `~44.5ms` vs DuckDB `~36.6ms`; `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` `~34.9ms` vs DuckDB `~27.7ms`.
                        - Conclusion: the next largest generic gap is no longer single-key dense aggregation. It is high/low three-key string grouping plus scan/decode/materialization overhead. The direct table helps high-selectivity grouping, but low-selectivity remains dominated by decode/materialization and multi-key/string grouping costs.
                      - Follow-up 1-5 pass:
                        - Added 64-lane packed mask construction to the null-free raw i32/i64 filter paths and covered 63/64/65/129-row tail boundaries with unit tests. This keeps projected-view comparison/IN-list filters on a block-mask boundary instead of row-at-a-time boolean appends.
                        - Added null-free 64-row block probe loops for both dense and hash int32 unique join probe paths. This removes per-row Arrow null/value dispatch in the common non-null int32 join-key case without changing outer/unmatched output semantics.
                        - Promoted the `(dense leading slot, dictionary id) -> group_id` cache as a default guarded rule (`DODAM_DISABLE_COALESCE_GROUP_ID_CACHE=1` disables it, default min rows `100k`). Added masked selected-path support so the cache is not limited to compact selected arrays.
                        - Added typed coalesce aggregate profile counters for string-id lookup, group-id lookup, and group update. Profiling showed `typed_group_id` remains the main typed aggregate update cost; raw state update and string-id lookup are comparatively small.
                        - Added a large grouped coalesce DuckDB differential case with generic column names (`bucket/day/label/amount`) so the default group-id cache path is checked as a general rule rather than a TPC-H column-name path.
                        - Validation: `cargo check -q`, `scripts/check_correctness.sh quick`, targeted mask unit tests, and the new large grouped coalesce differential passed.
                        - SF=10 focused generic sample after the pass: median-sum Dodam `0.151s` vs DuckDB `0.137s` (`1.10x`). Per-query: `join_expression_projection` `31.7ms` vs `30.1ms`; `non_null_bucket_count_sum` `17.3ms` vs `20.1ms`; `three_key_expression_group_no_order` `45.5ms` vs `37.2ms`; `low_selectivity_decimal_date_group` `21.7ms` vs `22.5ms`; `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` `34.9ms` vs `27.5ms`.
                        - Conclusion: filter mask and int32 join probe are now more vector/block-shaped and correctness-covered. The remaining visible generic gap is still the three-key string grouping family, especially group-id lookup plus decode/materialization cost.
                      - Typed group-id follow-up:
                        - Generalized dictionary-key direct table usage to masked three-key view aggregate paths. Previously only unmasked view paths and typed selected dictionary paths had direct `(dense leading slot, dictionary id) -> group_id` lookup. This is a correctness-preserving optimizer/execution rule: it applies whenever leading keys are dense, coalesce input exposes null-free dictionary ids, and selected row count passes the group-id cache gate.
                        - Added a collector-local typed dictionary group-id table cache keyed by dictionary bytes. When row groups reuse the same dictionary order, the `(leading slot, dictionary id) -> group_id` table can be reused across batches instead of being allocated and cold-filled every time. The key includes the dictionary values/order implicitly, and the cached table is accepted only when the slot count matches.
                        - Failed experiment: tried run-flushing consecutive identical direct-table slots so group state updates could be merged with `update_group_id_non_null_sum_many(...)`. This regressed high-selectivity three-key (`~57.7ms` vs the normal `~45ms` range) because the extra branch/flush bookkeeping outweighed any reduction in repeated updates for the generic distribution. Removed the run-flush path and kept immediate row updates.
                        - Gate experiment: lowering `DODAM_COALESCE_GROUP_ID_CACHE_MIN_ROWS` to `32768` reduced typed profile update time for low-selectivity three-key (`typed_group_id` about `14.9ms -> 12.3ms` in one profile), but wall-clock did not improve reliably and sometimes regressed. Default remains `100k`.
                        - Validation: `cargo check -q`, `scripts/check_correctness.sh quick`, and the large grouped coalesce DuckDB differential passed.
                        - Current SF=10 focused sample: median-sum Dodam `0.151s` vs DuckDB `0.144s` (`1.05x`). Remaining slow cases: `three_key_expression_group_no_order` `45.4ms` vs `37.1ms`; `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` `35.0ms` vs `27.5ms`.
                        - Conclusion: direct table reuse is a sound structural improvement but not enough to close the remaining typed group-id gap. `DenseU32GroupIndex` already has small/dense/hash modes, so the next likely win is lower than group-id lookup: reducing selected dictionary/payload decode and RecordBatch/materialization around the three-key aggregate.
                      - Composite hash group-id experiment:
                        - Added an optional 1-depth composite hash index for non-null three-key coalesce grouping: `(first: i64, second: i32, third_string_id: u32) -> group_id`.
                        - The implementation keeps the existing layered `DenseThree + DenseU32GroupIndex` path for ordered output and fallback/null cases. SQL no-order expression aggregate paths now create collectors with composite hash enabled, while ordered output keeps the existing dense ordered finish shortcut.
                        - Added `DODAM_DISABLE_COALESCE_COMPOSITE_HASH=1` for A/B and `DODAM_ENABLE_COALESCE_COMPOSITE_HASH=1` as a force flag for non-SQL/manual paths.
                        - Same-binary SF=10 A/B on the two three-key generic cases: default composite `78.6ms` combined vs disabled `81.9ms` combined. Per-query default: high-selectivity `46.6ms` vs disabled `47.9ms`; low-selectivity `32.0ms` vs disabled `34.0ms`.
                        - Focused SF=10 sample after enabling the no-order rule: median-sum Dodam `0.140s` vs DuckDB `0.144s` (`0.98x`). `three_key_expression_group_no_order` is now near parity (`36.9ms` vs DuckDB `36.6ms`), while `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` remains slower (`32.5ms` vs DuckDB `27.4ms`).
                        - Conclusion: 1-depth composite hash is a useful general rule for no-order multi-key string grouping and should stay. It does not solve the low-selectivity selected path completely; that remaining gap is likely selected dictionary/payload decode and materialization rather than group-id lookup alone.
                      - Conclusion: optimizing the simple decoder moves the needle, but reproducing parquet crate's optimized dictionary reader is still a large task. The next useful SIMD target would be a specialized bit-packed decoder that decodes many dictionary ids per iteration and checks membership without per-value control flow.
                      - Updated SIMD conclusion: SIMD should target either an optimized bit-packed/RLE dictionary id decoder or vector predicate over already-decoded primitive buffers. SIMD copy and scalar dictionary-id decode are both the wrong layer.
                    - Updated conclusion: bypassing `ColumnReader` alone is insufficient. The next layer would need to bypass or specialize below `PageReader`, or fuse scan output directly into aggregate state so the page path is not paying page materialization plus separate payload arrays.
                    - Conclusion: the bottleneck is not the final i64 copy loop. It is the public page-reader path plus nullable def-level proof/extra page processing. SIMD or bulk-copy at the end of this pipeline cannot close the gap; the next useful implementation would need a lower-level fused decoder that reads def levels, selected positions, and values together without the separate all-present proof pass.
                  - Tried an i64-only selected window reader (`DODAM_ENABLE_FUSED_SELECTED_I64_WINDOW_READER=1`) so only the `value`/sum column coalesces selected runs while the other payload columns keep the default selected reader.
                    - Conservative defaults regressed: SF=10 low-selectivity three-key no-order sampled `~41.5ms` vs default `~39.8ms`.
                    - Aggressive settings (`DODAM_FUSED_SELECTED_I64_WINDOW_MAX_GAP=12000`, amplification `100`) reduced selected read calls (`~1800 -> ~1249`) but exploded selected read rows (`~180k -> ~5.63M`) and still regressed (`~41.1ms` vs default `~39.8ms`).
                    - Conclusion: for this sparse periodic predicate, reducing selected read calls is not worth reading millions of extra `i64` values. The current selected-run reader remains the right tradeoff until a true decoder-level selected path exists.
                  - Current conclusion: the next real improvement is not a broader logical page-slice wrapper. It needs a lower-level decoder that can evaluate def levels and selected ranges while decoding value bytes once, ideally feeding an aggregate-specific update loop directly. The current public page reader plus separate def-level proof is too expensive.
                  - UNION ALL generic performance follow-up:
                    - Added generic DuckDB benchmark coverage for `union_all_append` and `union_all_order_limit`, both writing Parquet through `COPY`.
                    - Implemented child top-k pushdown for `UNION ALL ... ORDER BY ... LIMIT N`: each child is evaluated with the same `ORDER BY/LIMIT N`, then the existing global top-k is applied. This is a general set-operation rule, not a column/query-name fast path; it is disabled when top-level `OFFSET` is present.
                    - Added an append-only direct sink path for `COPY (UNION ALL)` when there is no top-level `ORDER BY/LIMIT/OFFSET`, so child outputs are schema-validated and written to the sink sequentially instead of materializing the full union before writing.
                    - Added a same-source disjoint equality-filter shared scan rule: compatible `UNION ALL` operands over the same Parquet path/projection with filters like `column = literal1` and `column = literal2` are executed as one scan with an `IN` filter. In `COPY` direct sink this stream is written directly to the sink, so the full union result is not materialized.
                    - Added a monotonic tail top-k rule for compatible same-source `UNION ALL ... ORDER BY key DESC LIMIT N`: it runs a preserve-order filtered scan, verifies the primitive/date sort key is ascending across emitted batches, keeps only the last `N` rows, and then applies the existing final sort to that small tail. If monotonicity is not proven, it falls back to the existing shared scan + global sort path.
                    - Added a reverse row-group suffix scan before the full preserve-order scan: when row-group statistics prove the sort key is monotonic, scan row groups from the end, apply the shared `IN` filter, keep whole suffix row groups, and stop once at least `LIMIT N` filtered rows are available. It intentionally does not assume row order inside a row group; final `ORDER BY/LIMIT` is still applied to the suffix candidate set.
                    - Failed experiments:
                      - Routing shared `ORDER BY/LIMIT` through the existing ordered scan path did not improve `union_all_order_limit`; it still materialized and sorted the filtered output.
                      - A bounded heap numeric top-k path for single null-free integer/date keys did not beat Arrow `lexsort_to_indices` in this benchmark and was removed.
                    - Validation: `cargo check -q`, targeted `UNION ALL` SQL test, and DuckDB differential `UNION ALL` test passed.
                    - SF=10 focused generic results after the latest change: repeated runs showed `union_all_order_limit` around `~6.3-6.7ms` vs DuckDB `~30.9-32.7ms`, a large win from avoiding the full scan/sort. The benchmark output was checked with DuckDB `EXCEPT ALL` in both directions and matched. `union_all_append` was around `~67-69ms` vs DuckDB `~53-56ms` in the same runs; scan is still shared, and the visible gap is mostly output/write path variance rather than duplicate scan/decode.
                    - Added guard workloads that should not benefit from suffix early-stop:
                      - `union_all_order_limit_full_read` uses the ordered facts file but sets `LIMIT` to all matching rows (`120k * scale`), so every row group must be read. Latest SF=10 result: Dodam `~141.0ms` vs DuckDB `~73.7ms` (`1.92x`), with `EXCEPT ALL` output equivalence confirmed.
                      - `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` uses a new `facts_scrambled.parquet` fixture where `id` is permuted across row groups, so row-group monotonic proof fails and the engine must not use suffix early-stop. Before the streaming heap rule it fell back to full shared scan/sort: Dodam `~89.6ms` vs DuckDB `~33.3ms` (`2.69x`), with `EXCEPT ALL` output equivalence confirmed.
                      - Added a guard on reverse row-group suffix scan (`DODAM_REVERSE_ROW_GROUP_TOPK_MAX_LIMIT_ROWS`, default `65536`) so large LIMIT/full-read cases do not take the row-group-at-a-time suffix path.
                    - Added a generic streaming top-k heap rule for same-source shared scans with a single null-free primitive/date `ORDER BY key DESC LIMIT N` and small `LIMIT` (`DODAM_REVERSE_ROW_GROUP_TOPK_MAX_LIMIT_ROWS`, default `65536`). This avoids materializing and sorting all filtered rows when row-group monotonicity cannot be proven. Latest SF=10 focused result: `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` Dodam `~54.4ms` vs DuckDB `~34.4ms` (`1.58x`), down from `~89.6ms`.
                    - Tried replacing row-group sorted output with reverse `take` materialization when row-group and batch ordering prove ascending data. This regressed full-read (`~140ms -> ~150ms`) because Arrow `take` copies every row and is more expensive than the existing per-row-group sort in this case. The primitive direct-reverse variant and the generic reverse-filter variant also regressed and are disabled by default.
                    - Tried a one-reader bulk row-group scan followed by global sort to reduce repeated reader setup. This was worse (`~139.7ms -> ~174.6ms`) because it gives up the useful per-row-group ordered-sort decomposition.
                    - Tried increasing row-group ordered-sort batch size to `128K` to reduce batch/concat overhead. This was worse (`~141.6ms -> ~154.7ms`), likely because larger filtered/sorted arrays hurt cache behavior more than they save dispatch overhead.
                    - Tried row-group parallel ordered sort (`DODAM_ROW_GROUP_ORDERED_DESC_PARALLEL=1`). This was slightly worse (`~139.8ms -> ~143.7ms`), likely from concurrent Parquet reader/cache contention.
                    - Tried a direct reverse selected-index path for simple integer `IN` filters to avoid Boolean mask creation. This was also worse (`~139ms -> ~148ms`) and is disabled behind `DODAM_ENABLE_REVERSE_IN_LIST_ORDERED_BATCHES=1`.
                    - Added ordered same-source direct sink for `COPY (UNION ALL ... ORDER BY key DESC LIMIT N)`: when row-group statistics prove monotonic order, each row group is scanned, filtered, sorted, projected/renamed, and written directly to the sink. This removes the `execute_sql -> QueryOutput<Vec<RecordBatch>> -> write_output` materialization layer. SF=10 focused result improved `union_all_order_limit_full_read` from `~142.1ms` to `~129.6ms` vs DuckDB `~75.6ms` (`1.71x`). `DODAM_PROFILE_COPY=1` confirms `direct_sink` is used and `materialize/write_output` are `n/a`.
                    - Added `DODAM_PROFILE_ORDERED_SINK=1` instrumentation. Earlier full-read ordered sink profile: total `~118ms`, row-group scan/sort `~89ms`, sink write `~25ms`; row-group detail summed to scan/decode `~72ms` and post-process/filter/sort `~16.6ms`. This means the remaining gap is mostly Parquet scan/decode rather than final sink write.
                    - Tried bypassing Arrow `RecordBatch` scan with a direct primitive ordered sink (`DODAM_ENABLE_ORDERED_PRIMITIVE_SINK=1`) for i32/i64 projection + simple integer `IN` filter. It regressed before and after reducing writer batch count (`~128.9ms -> ~139.0ms`, then `~129.1ms -> ~147.0ms`) because the custom primitive gather path lost more in row copying/schema batching than it saved in decode. It remains disabled by default.
                    - Added chunked ordered direct sink for large LIMITs: row groups are read in small chunks (`DODAM_ORDERED_SINK_ROW_GROUP_CHUNK_SIZE`, default `4` only when `LIMIT > DODAM_REVERSE_ROW_GROUP_TOPK_MAX_LIMIT_ROWS`) and sorted/written per chunk. This reduces reader setup and Parquet writer batch count without hurting small suffix LIMITs. SF=10 focused 7-run result: `union_all_order_limit_full_read` Dodam `~124.9ms` vs DuckDB `~75.4ms` (`1.66x`), while small `union_all_order_limit` stayed fast at `~6.2ms` vs DuckDB `~30.1ms`.
                    - Swept Parquet writer options and changed default `ParquetCopyOptions` from row group `64K` / write batch `32K` to row group `256K` / write batch `64K`. Focused full-read sweep favored `ROW_GROUP_SIZE 262144, WRITE_BATCH_SIZE 65536` (`~117.3ms` in one short run); after making it default, SF=10 7-run focused result was `union_all_order_limit_full_read` Dodam `~122.2ms` vs DuckDB `~75.0ms` (`1.63x`), with `union_all_order_limit` still fast at `~6.8ms`.
                    - Follow-up on the requested 1/2/3 improvements:
                      - Adaptive chunking: `DODAM_ORDERED_SINK_ROW_GROUP_CHUNK_SIZE` now defaults from row-group count and LIMIT: small LIMIT stays `1`, large LIMIT uses `2/4/8` by row-group count. For the SF=10 full-read fixture with 49 row groups, adaptive chooses `4`. Extra sweep confirmed `4` is best in this shape (`3` `~160ms`, `4` `~121.6ms`, `5` `~127.4ms`, `6` `~128.9ms`, `12` `~140.4ms`).
                      - Detailed ordered sink profiling: `DODAM_PROFILE_ORDERED_SINK=1` now prints chunked sink scan/filter/sort/write timings plus `ParquetBatchReader` metadata/planning/next metrics. One full-read profile after the detailed scan metrics: total `~123.5ms`, scan `~73.8ms`, filter `~9.4ms`, sort `~16.6ms`, write `~23.7ms`, chunks `13`, batches `13`, metadata `~0.03ms`, planning `~0.05ms`, reader next `~73.2ms`, `746` next calls, scan rows `6.0M`, output rows `1.2M`. Reader setup is negligible; scan/decode dominates.
                      - Tried ordered sink row-filter pushdown (`DODAM_ORDERED_SINK_ROW_FILTER=1`) to avoid decoding payload for rows failing `bucket IN (...)`. It reduced scan output from `6.0M` rows to `1.2M` rows and reader `next` from about `73ms` to `57ms`, but total focused benchmark regressed (`~123.2ms -> ~128.5ms`) due to Arrow RowFilter predicate/pass overhead. It remains disabled.
                      - Lower-level decoder experiment: added `DODAM_ENABLE_ORDERED_PRIMITIVE_PAGE_READER=1` for the direct primitive ordered sink. The normal primitive path profiled read `~89.3ms`, consume/gather `~41.3ms`, write `~32.7ms`; forcing the page reader reduced read to `~81.4ms` but total worsened to `~221ms`, so this remains disabled. Current lesson: page-level primitive decoding alone is not enough; the selected rows must feed writer-compatible buffers without the current gather/schema batching overhead.
                      - Full generic SF=10 5-run comparison after these changes sampled Dodam `~532.0ms` vs DuckDB `~500.5ms` (`1.06x`). Largest remaining positive gaps: `union_all_order_limit_full_read` `+52.9ms`, `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` `+20.7ms`, `three_key_expression_group` `+6.6ms`, `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` `+5.1ms`.
                    - Conclusion: shared scan is the right structural rule for append-style same-source `UNION ALL`, reverse row-group suffix scan is the right structural rule for monotonic small `DESC LIMIT`, streaming heap top-k is useful for non-monotonic small primitive/date top-k, and direct sink/chunked ordered sink plus larger Parquet writer batches help full-read COPY by removing materialization and amortizing reader/writer setup. Remaining full-read gap is mostly Parquet scan/decode; the next useful step is not more row-level gather, but a lower-level page/vector decoder path that can preserve ordered selection and feed ArrowWriter-compatible column buffers with less copying.
                    - Follow-up on the next 1/2/3 improvements:
                      - Replaced the streaming top-k final materialization from `N` one-row `RecordBatch::slice(...)` chunks plus `concat_batches(...)` with a general selected-row gather helper for primitive/date/bool columns. Unsupported shapes still fall back to the old slice/concat path. This is a generic selected-row materialization rule, not tied to the benchmark query name.
                      - Replaced per-row `ArrayRef` dispatch in the streaming top-k heap update with batch-level typed loops for `Int32/Int64/UInt64/Date32` sort keys. This keeps the hot scan/top-k loop closer to the vectorized pipeline shape by downcasting once per batch instead of once per row.
                      - Added a threshold skip in the bounded heap update: once the heap is full, rows whose key is below the current worst key skip tuple construction and `peek_mut`. This helps non-monotonic small top-k where most scanned rows cannot enter the final heap.
                      - Checked default-off three-key aggregate experiments again. `DODAM_ENABLE_COALESCE_LOCAL_DENSE_ACCUMULATE=1` regressed the focused SF=10 three-key set (`~156ms` vs default `~147ms` in that run), and `DODAM_ENABLE_THREE_KEY_BLOCK_ACCUMULATE=1` was not better than default. Both remain opt-in/disabled.
                      - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_union_all`, release build, focused SF=10 benchmark, and full SF=10 generic benchmark passed.
                      - Focused SF=10 ordered-union result after these retained changes: `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` improved to `~41.8ms` vs DuckDB `~35.1ms` (`1.19x`), down from the prior `~54ms` area; `union_all_order_limit` stayed fast (`~6.3ms` vs `~30.9ms`), and `union_all_order_limit_full_read` remained dominated by scan/decode (`~123.1ms` vs `~75.5ms`).
                      - Full SF=10 generic sample after this pass: Dodam `~509.3ms` vs DuckDB `~500.2ms` (`1.018x`). Largest remaining positive gaps: `union_all_order_limit_full_read` `+47.1ms`, `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` `+7.2ms`, `three_key_expression_group` `+6.4ms`, `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` `+5.3ms`, and `union_all_append` `+1.3ms`.
                    - Additional improvement pass:
                      - Added an ordered chunk rule that detects ascending primitive sort-key runs after filtering and reverses null-free primitive/date batches directly instead of calling full `lexsort_to_indices` for every chunk. This is a general ordered-run rule for primitive batches. It reduced the measured chunk post-processing/sort bucket from the earlier `~20ms` area to about `~15ms`, but total full-read stayed mostly scan/decode/write bound.
                      - Tried fusing raw `IN` filtering with reverse materialization before `filter_batch`, but removed it. It did not reduce filter/sort time and regressed focused full-read (`~125.9ms` vs the normal `~123ms` area), likely because the custom gather path duplicated work that Arrow filter already handles efficiently.
                      - Tested larger scan batch sizes (`32768`, `65536`, `131072`) on the remaining SF=10 gap set. Larger batches regressed three-key aggregation and ordered-union cases, so the benchmark/default batch size should not be raised globally.
                      - Tested forced ordered/no-order composite hash for three-key grouping. It helped some noisy samples but was not better than the current default on the same focused set; disabled composite was clearly worse. Current default remains the better general rule.
                      - Added a primitive-only `ORDER BY` materialization shortcut: after `lexsort_to_indices`, null-free primitive/date batches gather selected rows directly instead of using Arrow's generic `take_record_batch`. This slightly improved focused `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` in one retained run (`~40.8ms` vs DuckDB `~34.2ms`) but does not close the full-read gap.
                      - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_union_all`, release build, focused SF=10 benchmark, and full SF=10 generic benchmark passed.
                      - Latest retained focused sample: `union_all_order_limit_full_read` `~123.4ms` vs DuckDB `~75.6ms`, `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` `~40.8ms` vs DuckDB `~34.2ms`, `union_all_order_limit` `~6.5ms` vs DuckDB `~31.8ms`. Latest full SF=10 generic sample was noisy at Dodam `~505.7ms` vs DuckDB `~489.2ms` (`1.034x`), with the same durable conclusion: the largest real remaining gap is Parquet scan/decode plus writer cost on full-read ordered output, not SQL planning or row-group proof.
                    - Primitive ordered sink follow-up:
                      - Rechecked the direct primitive ordered sink. It still does not beat the default full-read path reliably: read/decode is much lower (`~57-62ms`), but primitive gather/consume (`~30ms`) and Parquet write (`~26ms`) keep total around `~119-124ms`, comparable to the default ordered chunk sink.
                      - Added chunked primitive sink output batching so large LIMITs coalesce several row-group outputs before writing. This reduced writer batches from `49` to `13`, but full-read wall time remained similar because gather/consume and write still dominate after the faster primitive scan.
                      - Promoted the primitive ordered sink only for small LIMITs (`DODAM_ORDERED_PRIMITIVE_SINK_AUTO_MAX_LIMIT_ROWS`, default `16K`) where it is clearly useful and safe: `COPY` profile on `union_all_order_limit` shows direct primitive sink around `~5ms` internally, while full-read remains on the default chunked ordered sink unless `DODAM_ENABLE_ORDERED_PRIMITIVE_SINK=1` forces it.
                      - Tested `DICTIONARY true` for Dodam Parquet output on full-read ordered union; it regressed badly (`~160ms` vs the normal `~120ms` area), so default dictionary remains disabled.
                      - Tested larger scan batch sizes again on the remaining gap set. `65K/128K` regressed three-key and ordered-union cases, so batch-size is not a global fix for the scan/decode gap.
                      - Latest focused sample after the small-LIMIT auto rule: `union_all_order_limit` `~6.2ms` vs DuckDB `~30.9ms`, `union_all_order_limit_full_read` `~121.6ms` vs DuckDB `~75.1ms`, `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` `~41.1ms` vs DuckDB `~34.0ms`. Full SF=10 generic sample was Dodam `~510.1ms` vs DuckDB `~493.8ms` (`1.033x`).
                    - Page/vector decoder follow-up:
                      - Added an opt-in required primitive page decoder for ordered same-source primitive `IN` scans (`DODAM_ENABLE_ORDERED_PRIMITIVE_FUSED_PAGE_DECODER=1`). It can read plain `i32/i64` payload columns directly from Parquet pages and supports dictionary-encoded `i32` filter columns by decoding dictionary ids.
                      - Safety note: the fused decoder now buffers opt-in output before writing so an unsupported page/encoding can fall back without emitting partial COPY output.
                      - Result: the fused page decoder is not enabled by default. On SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_full_read`, it sampled around `~138.5ms` vs `~132.8ms` for the forced primitive sink without it. It reduced some decode layering but still paid a row-at-a-time gather cost, so this is not the right final layer yet.
                      - Retained improvement: generalized the ordered primitive selected materialization for null-free primitive columns into a one-pass filter+gather path. This removes the separate selected-index pass and repeated `BatchView` value dispatch for simple primitive `IN` filters. Focused SF=10 forced primitive sink improved `union_all_order_limit_full_read` from about `~132.8ms` to `~122.9ms`; consume/gather dropped from about `~33ms` to `~16.5ms`.
                      - Current conclusion: the repeated direct-sink/page-reader ideas keep resurfacing because the durable bottleneck has moved lower. The useful retained rule is one-pass null-free primitive materialization. To close the remaining full-read gap, the next real work is either writer-side zero-copy/less-copy primitive output or a lower-level Parquet decoder that emits selected Arrow-compatible buffers in block form, not row-by-row `Vec::push`.
                      - Follow-up: changed the opt-in fused page decoder internals from row-at-a-time output push to selected-position block construction plus column-wise tight gather. This proved the earlier diagnosis: the row-at-a-time gather was the bad layer. However, in the SF=10 fixture the default Parquet encoding still makes the fused page decoder unsupported unless explicitly forced with the primitive sink fallback, so it remains opt-in.
                      - Promoted the now-improved primitive ordered sink to large-LIMIT auto for the same general shape (`same-source UNION ALL`, primitive projection, simple primitive `IN`, monotonic `ORDER BY key DESC LIMIT`). Unsupported shapes still return to the chunked ordered sink. Latest default focused SF=10 sample: `union_all_order_limit_full_read` improved from the chunked default `~122ms` area to `~107.2ms` vs DuckDB `~76.4ms`; `union_all_order_limit` stayed fast at `~6.3ms`; `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` stayed around `~41.8ms`.
                      - Current remaining full-read profile: read/decode `~58ms`, primitive consume/gather `~12-14ms`, Parquet write `~25-26ms`. The next meaningful gap is therefore writer-side copy/encoding and lower-level Parquet decode, not SQL planning or selected-index construction.
                      - Follow-up on the requested 1/2/3/4 pass:
                        - Added nullable-but-all-present vector exposure: `RawNullable` primitive vectors now return a null-free slice when def-levels prove every row is present. This is a general vector rule for optional Parquet columns that are physically null-free.
                        - Made ordered primitive materialization adaptive: sparse/periodic selections use row-wise fused push, while dense selections use selected-position block + contiguous reverse-run gather. In the SF=10 full-read distribution (`bucket IN (1,7)`, about 20% selected), column-wise gather was worse, so the adaptive rule keeps the faster sparse path.
                        - Extended the opt-in page decoder to optional all-present PLAIN payload pages and optional all-present dictionary `i32` filters. It now supports the fixture schema, but forcing it regressed badly (`~226ms` vs default `~109ms`) because public `PageReader` + manual dictionary/page decode is slower than parquet crate's `ColumnReader` for this encoding. It remains opt-in only.
                        - Split COPY Parquet sink profile to include `writer_open` and `writer_close`. Latest profile shows writer open/close are tiny (`~0.1ms/~0.5ms`); the durable writer cost is `ArrowWriter::write()` itself (`~25-27ms`).
                        - Generalized Parquet writer defaults instead of relying on per-benchmark COPY options:
                          - Changed the default SNAPPY writer shape from `WRITE_BATCH_SIZE 64K` / `DATA_PAGE_ROW_COUNT_LIMIT 32K` to `32K` / `16K`, matching the best file-size-preserving option from the focused sweep.
                          - Set COPY Parquet statistics to chunk/row-group level by default. This avoids page-level statistics work while preserving row-group pruning metadata for downstream scans.
                          - Removed the hard-coded `id`/`f.id` delta encoding rule. The writer now inspects the first output batch and enables `DELTA_BINARY_PACKED` for any null-free `Int32`/`Int64`/`Date32` column that is monotonic or heavily adjacent-duplicate. This is a data-shape rule, not a column-name rule.
                          - Confirmed parquet-rs compresses data pages, not individual rows. The remaining cost that looks like "compression/write" is page encoding/compression inside `ArrowWriter::write()`, not per-row compression.
                          - Focused SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_full_read` after the general writer policy sampled Dodam `~103.4ms` vs DuckDB `~73.2ms` (`1.41x`). The profile was read/decode `~55ms`, consume/gather `~12ms`, writer `~25ms`; writer open/close stayed sub-millisecond.
                          - Disabling the new auto-delta rule did not improve the focused case (`~113.8ms` median in a noisy 5-run sample), so encoding selection is not the main remaining gap.
                          - Full SF=10 generic 3-run sample after the change was Dodam `~485.6ms` vs DuckDB `~502.9ms` (`0.97x`). Remaining slower cases were still `union_all_order_limit_full_read`, `union_all_order_limit_scrambled`, and low-selectivity three-key grouping.
                        - Structural primitive sink boundary:
                          - Added a general `PrimitiveBatch` / `PrimitiveColumn` / `PrimitiveColumnValues` sink API below `RecordBatchSink`. Default sinks convert this block to Arrow `RecordBatch`, while Parquet COPY now overrides it and caches the primitive schema.
                          - Changed the ordered primitive direct path to keep selected output as primitive column blocks through projection/alias handling, then pass it to the sink. This removes the SQL-layer dependency on `RecordBatch` as the only direct-sink boundary and is the correct seam for a future lower-level Parquet column writer.
                          - Optimized `PrimitiveBatch::concat` to validate schema first and reserve the final vector capacity before extending source chunks.
                          - Result: this is an architectural step, not a speed win yet. Release SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_full_read` remained noisy around `~123-126ms` in focused profile runs, and full SF=10 generic sampled around Dodam `~0.51-0.52s` vs DuckDB `~0.50-0.52s`. The remaining cost is still the same: Parquet read/decode plus ArrowWriter page encoding. To get a large win, Parquet COPY must stop converting primitive blocks back into `RecordBatch` and write flat primitive column chunks through a lower-level Parquet writer.
                        - Lower-level primitive Parquet writer experiment:
                          - Added an opt-in `DODAM_ENABLE_PRIMITIVE_PARQUET_SINK=1` path that uses parquet-rs `SerializedFileWriter` / row-group writer / typed column writers directly for required flat `Int32`/`Int64`/`Date32` primitive batches. Unsupported schemas still use ArrowWriter.
                          - Ported the data-shape delta encoding rule to this writer so monotonic or adjacent-duplicate primitive columns use `DELTA_BINARY_PACKED`; this fixed the first attempt's file-size regression (`~9.9MB` back to `~5.1MB` on the focused full-read output).
                          - Focused SF=10 full-read samples showed lower-level writer can reduce the measured Parquet write bucket from about `~23-24ms` to `~20-21ms`, but total wall time stayed noisy and did not consistently beat the ArrowWriter fallback. A row-group buffering attempt moved work to close/flush (`~4-6ms`) and regressed, so it was removed.
                          - Decision: keep the lower-level writer as opt-in only. The structure is useful, but it is not ready as the default until it handles row-group buffering/encoding without moving cost to close and proves stable across the generic suite.
                        - Writer/read split follow-up:
                          - Split Parquet COPY sink profiling into `primitive_to_record_batch`, `arrow_write`, and `primitive_write`, and extended ordered primitive profile with `concat`/`slice`. This confirmed the old `write` bucket was not RecordBatch conversion: primitive-to-Arrow conversion was only about `~8-13us` in the focused path, while `ArrowWriter::write()` was about `~22-31ms`.
                          - Baseline focused SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_full_read` with the new profile sampled Dodam around `~116.5ms` vs DuckDB `~52ms`; warm profiles showed read/decode `~55-75ms`, consume/gather `~13-18ms`, concat `<1ms`, and Arrow writer `~22-30ms`.
                          - Added a general parallel row-group scan rule inside the ordered primitive large-LIMIT direct sink. It scans independent row groups within each output chunk in scoped workers and then reassembles batches in the original descending row-group order, so this is a physical execution rule rather than a query-name special case. Focused median improved to about `~95.1ms`; accumulated read/consume metrics are now worker-summed and can exceed wall time.
                          - Re-tested the lower-level primitive Parquet writer after row-group scan parallelism. It became consistently useful on large flat primitive batches, reducing focused median to about `~84-85ms`; `primitive_write` was about `~28-30ms` and close stayed around `~0.05ms`.
                          - Promoted the lower-level primitive writer as an automatic rule only for large flat primitive batches (`DODAM_PRIMITIVE_PARQUET_SINK_AUTO_MIN_ROWS`, default `64K`; disable with `DODAM_DISABLE_PRIMITIVE_PARQUET_SINK_AUTO=1`). This keeps small outputs and unsupported schemas on ArrowWriter while removing the benchmark-specific env requirement.
                          - Default focused SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_full_read` after the auto rule sampled Dodam `~83.4ms` vs DuckDB `~53.0ms` (`~1.57x`) in the 5-run focused profile, and `~67.4ms` vs `~52.5ms` in one full generic 3-run sample. Remaining visible costs are primitive writer `~28-29ms`, concat/copy `~8-10ms`, and the actual Parquet scan/decode work.
                        - Parquet scan/decode parallelism follow-up:
                          - Swept ordered primitive row-group chunk sizes after introducing row-group parallel scan. The old default chunk `4` underused CPU decode parallelism; SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_full_read` sampled roughly `~86ms` at chunk `4`, `~55ms` at `8`, `~41ms` at `12`, and `~40ms` at `16/24`.
                          - Changed only the primitive ordered large-LIMIT direct sink default chunking to use available CPU parallelism capped at `24` row groups (`DODAM_ORDERED_SINK_ROW_GROUP_CHUNK_SIZE` still overrides). This is a general physical rule for large primitive ordered output, while the older RecordBatch chunked sink keeps its conservative default.
                          - Default focused SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_full_read` after the chunk rule sampled Dodam `~49.8ms` vs DuckDB `~55.5ms` (`~0.90x`), and a UNION subset run sampled `~40.8ms` vs DuckDB `~54.0ms`. The profile now shows only `3` writer batches for 49 row groups; read metrics are worker-summed and intentionally exceed wall time.
                          - Tried extending the same primitive direct sink idea to no-order same-source `UNION ALL` append. It reduced writer batches from `367` to `3`, but output size grew from about `~4.6MB` to `~9.9MB` and wall time did not improve (`~48ms` vs the normal `~49-56ms` range, with worse file size). This path is kept opt-in only behind `DODAM_ENABLE_UNION_PRIMITIVE_SINK=1`.
                          - Current retained result: Parquet scan/decode parallelism fixes the large ordered full-read gap without query-name specialization. The next remaining UNION gap is `union_all_append`, but its issue is now Arrow scan output fragmentation/writer encoding quality rather than the monotonic ordered primitive scan/decode path.
                          - Tried coalescing the normal no-order same-source `UNION ALL` Arrow `RecordBatch` stream before Parquet write (`DODAM_UNION_SCAN_WRITE_COALESCE_ROWS`). This also failed as a default: write calls dropped from `367` to `5`, but the output file grew to about `~9.5MB` and total regressed to about `~62.7ms`. Default is therefore `0`/disabled; the experiment remains opt-in because preserving Arrow/dictionary encoding quality matters more than reducing write call count for append.
                          - Profiled `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` after the scan/decode work. It writes a single 5000-row batch and Parquet writer time is only about `~0.1ms`; the remaining gap is inside `materialize` (`~26-31ms`) from non-monotonic streaming top-k scan/update, not COPY output. It should be improved separately from Parquet writer batching.
                          - Added a COPY direct-sink primitive streaming top-k rule for non-monotonic same-source `UNION ALL` with small `ORDER BY primitive DESC LIMIT`. It scans primitive columns in parallel, keeps only compact top-k candidate values in each worker, merges local heaps, and writes one primitive batch to the sink. Unsupported nullable/output shapes fall back to the existing Arrow path.
                          - Focused SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` improved from about `~28.8ms` to `~22.5ms` in profile runs, and a UNION subset sample showed `~19.5ms` vs DuckDB `~18.0ms` (`~1.08x`). The remaining few milliseconds are mainly because this path still decodes payload columns for all scanned rows; a true selected-payload read for final top-k rows would be the next structural step.
                          - Batch-size sweep for this primitive top-k path showed the default `16K` is still best. Larger batches (`32K/64K/128K`) reduced batch count but increased read/cache cost and regressed wall time.
                          - Full SF=10 generic 3-run sample after this pass: Dodam `~287.2ms`, DuckDB `~231.0ms` (`~1.24x`). UNION gaps are no longer the dominant issue; the larger remaining generic gaps are low-selectivity three-key grouping, nullable key grouping, and decimal/date aggregate scans.
                          - Commonization status for the requested 1-5:
                            - `COPY` direct pipeline is retained through `try_execute_set_operation_sql_to_sink` and physical `execute_to_sink`; the UNION-specific materialization bypass now uses the same `RecordBatchSink`/`PrimitiveBatch` sink boundary.
                            - The small top-k improvement is now a physical rule shape (`same-source scan + primitive filter/sort + small DESC LIMIT + sink`), not a query-name rule. It still lives in SQL planning glue and should be moved into a real `TopKExec` once generic selected-payload row identity is available.
                            - Row-group parallel scheduling is now a policy in ordered primitive large-LIMIT and primitive top-k paths, with CPU-based chunking instead of benchmark constants.
                            - Primitive output reaches Parquet through the common `PrimitiveBatch` sink API; lower-level writer auto-selection remains gated by flat primitive schema and row count.
                          - True generic selected-payload late read is not complete yet. The blocker is that `scan_parquet_primitive_columns_parallel_view_fold` exposes `BatchView` but not `(row_group, row_offset)` identity, so top-k cannot re-read only final payload rows without changing the scan API. Next structural API should expose row identity or emit selected row runs per row group.
                          - Generic aggregate follow-up:
                            - Promoted direct late-selection construction for projected-view filters to the default path (`DODAM_DISABLE_DIRECT_LATE_SELECTION=1` disables it). This avoids building a `BooleanArray` mask first when a filter can push selected row ids/runs directly into a `ProjectedViewSelection`. It is a general late-materialization rule, not a query-name rule.
                            - Split no-filter single-key `count(*)/sum(i64)` aggregate update loops by batch-level nullability. Null-free key/sum batches now update from raw primitive slices and nullable key batches avoid rechecking sum validity when the sum input is null-free. This applies to `Int32`, `Int64`, and `UInt64` single-key count/sum groups.
                            - Focused SF=10 sequential samples after this pass: `nullable_key_group` stayed around `~17.7ms` vs DuckDB `~7.0ms`; `three_key_expression_group_no_order` around `~28.9ms` vs `~17.0ms`; `low_selectivity_decimal_date_group` improved in this sample to `~10.6ms` vs `~5.0ms`; `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` around `~27.6ms` vs `~8.0ms`.
                            - Re-tested opt-in `DODAM_ENABLE_DECIMAL_DATE_SIMD_FILTER=1`. It did not win on the SF=10 low-selectivity decimal/date cases (`low_selectivity_decimal_date_group` sampled `~12.2ms` vs the default `~10.6ms` area), so it remains disabled.
                            - Parallel benchmark runs were misleading here: running the four focused cases concurrently pushed some Dodam medians up by CPU/IO contention. For these small generic cases, keep benchmark records from individual/sequential runs.
                            - Current diagnosis: integer count/sum loop cleanup is not enough to close `nullable_key_group`; remaining cost is still scan/decode/materialize/merge overhead around a small dense group result. `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` remains dominated by decimal filter/selection and selected view materialization before the collector, not final Parquet write.
                          - Dense integer count/sum accumulation follow-up:
                            - Added a default batch-local dense accumulator for direct primitive `Int32` key + `sum(i64)` grouped aggregates. For dense key ranges, each batch now accumulates into `counts[]`/`sums[]` arrays and applies one update per touched group, instead of doing group lookup and `CountSumGroup` update per row. This covers non-null raw slices, nullable-key def-level vectors, and raw page-byte inputs.
                            - This is a general low-cardinality integer group rule. It is bounded by `DODAM_DENSE_I32_BATCH_ACCUMULATE_MAX_SLOTS` (default `65536`) and falls back automatically for wide/sparse ranges.
                            - Focused SF=10 sequential profile after the change: `nullable_key_group` improved to `~10.6ms` vs DuckDB `~7.0ms` (previously `~17.5ms` area), and `non_null_bucket_count_sum` improved to `~9.9ms` vs DuckDB `~6.0ms` (previously `~12.8ms` area). The aggregate profile for warm runs is now about `~9-10ms` for 6M rows.
                            - Added `Decimal128VectorView::raw_i64_bytes()` and kept logical len in `RawI64Bytes` so decimal comparison/aggregate code can handle raw i64 decimal bytes without falling back through `raw_values()`. This did not materially move the focused decimal/date benchmarks in the current fixture, but it is the correct vector abstraction fix.

Full SF=10 generic 3-run sample after this pass: Dodam `~299.3ms`, DuckDB `~234.0ms` (`1.279x`), with the main remaining gap still `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` and ordered three-key expression grouping.
                          - Fused selected three-key aggregate follow-up:
                            - Added relaxed decimal min/max metadata reads for cost estimation only. Exact min/max is still used where correctness/pruning needs it, but the fused selected aggregate auto gate can now use available Parquet stats even when `min_is_exact/max_is_exact` are not set.
                            - This allowed `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` to choose the existing fused selected path automatically while high-selectivity `three_key_expression_group_no_order` continues to use late materialization.
                            - Added `DODAM_COALESCE_AGG_PROFILE` output for fused selected scan metrics (`read`, `consume`, predicate, payload, dictionary). The retained diagnosis is that worker-summed payload/dictionary decode dominates the remaining low-selectivity gap; final Parquet write is sub-millisecond.
                            - Swept fused selected worker count. `4` workers was too low (`~47ms`), old default `8` was around the high `20ms`/`30ms` area, `12` was best in a focused run (`~21.7ms`), and `16/24` were not better. Default worker policy now uses available parallelism capped at `12`, still overrideable by `DODAM_FUSED_DICT_SELECTED_AUTO_WORKERS`.
                            - Re-tested opt-in payload variants. `DODAM_FUSED_DICT_SELECTED_PRIMITIVE_PAGE_SLICE=1`, `DODAM_ENABLE_FUSED_SELECTED_I64_AGG_SINK=1`, and `DODAM_ENABLE_DICTIONARY_DECIMAL_SELECTED_RUNS=1` all regressed this fixture, so they remain opt-in/disabled.
                            - Focused SF=10 sample after the auto gate + worker policy: `three_key_expression_group_no_order` `~28.5ms` vs DuckDB `~19.0ms`; `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` `~27.6ms` vs `~9.0ms`. Full SF=10 generic 3-run sample improved to Dodam `~270.6ms`, DuckDB `~224.0ms` (`1.208x`); in that run low-selectivity three-key was `~23.5ms` vs `~8.5ms`.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, release build, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_union_all`, and `copy_parquet` passed.
                          - Cost analyzer first pass:
                            - Started moving scattered fast-path gates into `src/cost.rs` instead of leaving every path with local ad-hoc heuristics.
                            - Added common cost inputs/decisions:
                              - `DecimalRangeSelectivityInput` for metadata-based filter selectivity estimates.
                              - `FusedSelectedAggregateCostInput` / `choose_fused_selected_aggregate()` for fused selected aggregate path choice.
                              - `WorkerCostInput` / `choose_parallel_workers()` for bounded parallel worker selection.
                              - `SelectedPayloadCostInput` / `choose_selected_payload()` for selected-payload vs full-payload decisions.
                            - Wired these into:
                              - fused dictionary selected aggregate auto gate in `sql.rs`;
                              - fused selected worker selection in `sql.rs` and `engine.rs`;
                              - storage selected-payload gate in `storage.rs`.
                            - Kept existing env overrides and thresholds intact. This pass is structural consolidation, not a new benchmark-specific special case.
                            - Added unit tests for selectivity estimation, selected-payload decision reasons, and worker bounding.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q cost::tests`, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_union_all`, `copy_parquet`, and release build passed.

                              - Moved late materialization final accept logic, including selector-shape checks and estimated IO-saving override, into `choose_late_materialization()`.

                              - Moved primitive Parquet sink auto choice into `choose_primitive_parquet_sink()`.
                              - Moved ordered primitive sink auto choice and large-limit row-group chunk policy into `choose_ordered_primitive_sink()` / `choose_ordered_primitive_row_group_chunk()`.
                              - Moved dense `i32` batch/block aggregate gate into `choose_dense_i32_block_accumulate()`.
                              - Existing env knobs still live near their execution feature, but the common “should this physical rule run?” decision now has one policy surface in `src/cost.rs`.
                          - Ordered expression aggregate generalization:
                            - Added `choose_expression_aggregate_late_row_group_chunk()` to the cost analyzer. This is a generic physical aggregate policy: when an expression aggregate produces ordered output with an output limit, the late-materialized row-group chunk defaults larger (`4` instead of `2`) to reduce partial collector count and merge/ordered-finish overhead.
                            - The rule is based on `ordered_output + output_limit`, not on a query or column name. `DODAM_EXPRESSION_AGG_LATE_ROW_GROUP_CHUNK` still overrides it.
                            - SF=10 focused `three_key_expression_group` after the change sampled Dodam `~52.0ms` vs DuckDB `~51.7ms` in a 5-run focused run, and `~37.9ms` vs `~36.8ms` in the full generic 3-run sample. The earlier same-day full sample had this case around `~45.2ms` vs `~36.8ms`.
                            - Full SF=10 generic 3-run after the change sampled Dodam `~475.9ms`, DuckDB `~521.6ms` (`0.912x`). Remaining slower cases in that run: `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` `+4.2ms`, `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` `+3.7ms`, `filter_group_decimal_date` `+4.3ms` (noisy; previously near parity).
                          - Vectorized-pipeline discipline follow-up:
                            - Blocked the nullable/unsupported ordered primitive selected-batch row-at-a-time fallback by default. It is now diagnostic-only behind `DODAM_ENABLE_ROW_AT_TIME_FALLBACK=1`; otherwise unsupported shapes fall back to the higher-level path rather than silently executing row-wise inside the supposed primitive/vector path.
                            - Changed streaming primitive top-k consumption from “scan every row and test/advance one at a time” to a two-step block shape: build selected positions from the null-free primitive filter vector, then run heap updates only for selected positions while preserving row-order tie sequence by deriving it from the batch base offset.
                            - This keeps the vectorized pipeline boundary clearer: scan/filter produces vectors/position blocks, heap update remains per candidate because top-k state is inherently stateful, and payload materialization stays block-based through `PrimitiveBatch`.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, and DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_union_all` passed.
                            - SF=10 result after this structural pass: focused `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` was noisy (`~45-52ms` vs DuckDB `~40-44ms`), while full generic 3-run sampled Dodam `~467.5ms`, DuckDB `~522.6ms` (`0.895x`) and `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` `~37.0ms` vs `~34.6ms` (`+2.4ms`). Profile still shows the remaining top-k gap is read/decode dominated, not Parquet write.
                            - Extended the same discipline to generic expression aggregate physical rules:
                              - Added vector-only projected-view consume methods on `CoalesceKeyCountSumCollector`.
                              - Generic late expression aggregate payload consume now uses vector-only consume by default; the old `try_record_batch() -> consume_batch()` fallback is available only through `DODAM_ENABLE_ROW_AT_TIME_FALLBACK=1`.
                              - Generic projected-view predicate fallback to `RecordBatch` filter is also blocked by default in expression aggregate late/dictionary scan-fold rules.
                              - Correctness fallback APIs remain for non-optimized callers, but optimized physical rules no longer silently drop into row-wise RecordBatch paths.
                            - Inventory after `rg try_record_batch`: many TPC-H-specific paths still call `try_record_batch()` directly. Those are explicit legacy/specialized implementations and should be converted incrementally by query family. The generic expression aggregate and primitive top-k paths are now the first hardened areas.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q cost::tests`, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_union_all`, and `copy_parquet` passed.
                            - SF=10 generic 3-run after blocking these fallbacks sampled Dodam `~458.3ms`, DuckDB `~490.3ms` (`0.935x`). Remaining slower cases in that sample: `join_expression_projection` `+7.2ms`, `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` `+4.4ms`, `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` `+3.9ms`, `union_all_order_limit_full_read` `+2.9ms`, `three_key_expression_group` `+1.3ms`.
                          - Join coalesce late-selection follow-up:
                            - Investigated the remaining `join_expression_projection` gap under SF=10. The default path is the general unique right-key + coalesce group + count/sum aggregate fusion with late materialized left payload; the optional direct primitive path is still disabled by default and remains unsafe for nullable primitive views.
                            - Added tiny integer `IN` representation for direct join-left filters and changed the default late join selection builder to create selected offsets directly instead of using a row callback. This is a general block-selection shape for small integer `IN` filters, not a query-name special case.
                            - Reused the late selected-bucket buffer once payload consumption catches up, avoiding unbounded accumulation of already-consumed row-group selections.
                            - Added an O(1) dense `RawNullable` value fast path when the value and definition-level buffers have the same length. This fixes a general vector access pitfall, although nullable compact vectors still need a better selected-offset reader to avoid row-index recomputation.
                            - Focused SF=10 `join_expression_projection` moved from about `~38.4ms` in the local pre-change focused run to `~36.6ms` after offset-selection/buffer reuse (`DuckDB ~30.5ms`, ratio `~1.20x`). The retained bottleneck is still left scan/decode/materialization, not output writing.
                          - Selection builder vectorization follow-up:
                            - Removed remaining `LateSelectionBuilder::push_selected_rows(...)` call sites. Selection construction now uses explicit runs (`push_repeated`) or selected offset streams (`push_selected_offsets`) instead of hiding row callbacks inside the selection builder.
                            - Generic projected-view filter selection now emits offsets directly for boolean masks, primitive comparisons, decimal/date comparisons, and small integer `IN` predicates. Null-free primitive slices avoid per-row null checks.
                            - Converted Q03/Q04/Q12/Q19 late-selection paths from callback/push-per-row style to selected-offset streams while preserving their side-car selected payload vectors.
                            - This is structural cleanup for the vectorized pipeline: predicates may still scan rows internally when the predicate itself is scalar/string-heavy, but the late-materialization boundary now receives row blocks/offset streams rather than callback-driven row pushes.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q cost::tests`, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_union_all`, `copy_parquet`, and release build passed.
                            - SF=10 generic 3-run after this pass sampled Dodam `~453.8ms`, DuckDB `~497.2ms` (`0.913x`). TPC-H SF=10 focused Q03/Q04/Q12/Q19 sampled Dodam `~781.6ms`, DuckDB `~1001.2ms` (`0.781x`), with all four faster than DuckDB.
                          - Masked aggregate consume follow-up:
                            - Changed `CoalesceKeyCountSumViewReader::update_groups_masked()` to compact the Boolean mask into selected offsets once, then update groups by iterating selected rows only. This removes repeated `if !mask[row] { continue }` checks from each typed aggregate update loop.
                            - Fixed the first implementation to avoid scanning the mask twice for capacity estimation; it now uses Arrow `BooleanArray::true_count()` as the capacity hint.
                            - This keeps the same vectorized pipeline shape: filter/selection produces row blocks, and collector update consumes selected row offsets rather than embedding mask checks in every update path.
                            - Focused SF=10 samples were mixed/noisy: `join_expression_projection` was strong in isolated runs (`~24.7ms` vs DuckDB `~30.7ms`), while `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` remains the main slower generic case (`~32.6ms` vs `~27.5ms`).
                            - Full SF=10 generic 3-run after the capacity fix sampled Dodam `~438.7ms`, DuckDB `~492.8ms` (`0.890x`). Remaining slower cases in that sample: `join_expression_projection` `+4.6ms`, `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` `+4.3ms`, `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` `+4.1ms`.
                            - Follow-up: passed the already-built selected-offset slice into the dictionary group-id cache masked path instead of making that path rescan/count the Boolean mask. This keeps the masked aggregate path as `mask -> offsets -> selected update/cache`, without reintroducing hidden row-at-a-time mask checks in the cache helper.
                            - SF=10 focused after this follow-up: `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` sampled `~32.2ms` vs DuckDB `~28.5ms`; `join_expression_projection` sampled `~26.0ms` vs `~30.3ms`; `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` remained slower at `~41.8ms` vs `~34.4ms`.
                            - Full SF=10 generic 3-run after this follow-up sampled Dodam `~447.9ms`, DuckDB `~493.0ms` (`0.909x`). Main retained structural gap is now selected-payload top-k for non-monotonic UNION and lower-level scan/decode fusion for low-selectivity three-key aggregate.
                          - Primitive top-k block-buffer cleanup:
                            - Reused the selected-position buffer inside `PrimitiveTopkState` instead of allocating a new `Vec<usize>` for each batch. The filter step still emits a block of selected row offsets, and heap/payload updates consume that block.
                            - Removed an unused primitive column spec construction from the same non-monotonic same-source UNION top-k path.
                            - This is a small vectorized-pipeline cleanup, not the full selected-payload solution. Focused SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` sampled `~40.0ms` vs DuckDB `~33.3ms`; full generic 3-run sampled Dodam `~459.6ms`, DuckDB `~495.7ms` (`0.927x`), with this case still around `+6.2ms`.
                            - Retained conclusion: the real fix requires scan APIs to expose row identity `(row_group, row_offset)` so top-k can first read only filter/sort columns and then read payload only for final selected rows. Re-reading whole row groups for selected rows is unlikely to help on scrambled data because final top-k rows can be spread across many row groups.
                          - Direct primitive row identity scaffold:
                            - Added `DirectPrimitiveBatchLocation { row_group, row_offset }` and a location-aware `scan_parquet_primitive_columns_parallel_view_fold_with_location()` engine API.
                            - Wired non-monotonic primitive top-k to consume location-aware batches. It now derives tie/order sequence from physical `(row_group, row_offset)` instead of a worker-local sequence, which is the first required step toward selected-payload second pass.
                            - Existing direct primitive scan APIs are preserved; this is an additive execution API for operators that need row identity.
                            - Focused SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` after this scaffold sampled `~39.4ms` vs DuckDB `~35.3ms`; full generic 3-run sampled Dodam `~455.6ms`, DuckDB `~496.4ms` (`0.918x`).
                            - Next structural step: change `PrimitiveTopkState` slot storage from copied payload values to `(key, row_group, row_offset, filter_value)` plus a second-pass selected payload reader. That requires a selected-row primitive reader capable of reading arbitrary row offsets grouped by row group.
                            - Added optional row-ref slot storage in `PrimitiveTopkState` behind `DODAM_ENABLE_PRIMITIVE_TOPK_ROW_REFS=1`. Default remains payload-copy slots to avoid adding overhead before the second-pass selected-payload reader exists.
                            - SF=10 focused `union_all_order_limit_scrambled`: default row-ref off sampled `~40.7ms` vs DuckDB `~34.6ms`; row-ref opt-in sampled `~38.4ms` vs `~34.2ms` in the same run, but this is noisy and not enough to enable by default. Full generic default sampled Dodam `~452.0ms`, DuckDB `~501.3ms` (`0.902x`).
                          - Row-at-a-time selection API removal + top-k filter block loop:
                            - Removed the unused `LateSelectionBuilder::push_selected_rows(...)` API entirely after converting its call sites to runs/selected-offset streams. This prevents optimized late-materialization paths from quietly reintroducing callback-per-row selection construction.
                            - Changed primitive top-k small integer `IN` filtering to specialized 1/2/3/4-key loops that fill the reusable selected-position buffer in an unrolled block shape. The heap still updates per selected candidate, but filter production is now a block/position-vector stage instead of per-row helper calls.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_union_all`, `copy_parquet`, and release build passed.
                          - Primitive top-k fused filter+threshold experiment:
                            - Tried replacing the separate selected-position generation plus heap reject with a fused `filter && sort_key >= heap_threshold` selected-position builder. This keeps the pipeline blocked (`columns -> selected positions`) and avoids the previous extra `Vec::retain(...)` prune scan.
                            - A/B showed it is not default-worthy for the scrambled top-k fixture. Focused SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_scrambled`: fused threshold auto-on sampled Dodam `~43.7ms` vs DuckDB `~39.6ms`; default/off sampled Dodam `~38.2ms` vs DuckDB `~34.4ms` in the same binary sequence.
                            - Kept the fused filter+threshold code as opt-in only through `DODAM_ENABLE_PRIMITIVE_TOPK_FUSED_FILTER_THRESHOLD=1`. The default path remains cheaper because the heap reject is already inexpensive relative to scanning an extra sort column in the selection builder.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_union_all`, `copy_parquet`, and release build passed.
                            - Default SF=10 generic 3-run after leaving it off sampled Dodam `~442.4ms`, DuckDB `~503.3ms` (`0.879x`). Remaining slower cases in that run: `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` `+5.1ms`, `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` `+3.8ms`, `three_key_expression_group_no_order` `+2.6ms`, and `three_key_expression_group` `+1.2ms`.
                          - Primitive top-k selected-payload base-column reuse:
                            - Fixed the selected-payload experiment so the second pass no longer re-reads columns already produced by the first pass. For `SELECT id, bucket, value ... ORDER BY id`, the first pass keeps `bucket,id`, and the selected payload reader only materializes missing projection columns such as `value`.
                            - This is the correct general rule shape for row-identity payload materialization: `first-pass columns + selected missing columns -> final projection`. It avoids hard-coding the generic benchmark column names.
                            - A/B still did not justify default-on for `union_all_order_limit_scrambled`. Focused SF=10 in one run: default sampled Dodam `~47.6ms` vs DuckDB `~39.5ms`; selected-payload base reuse sampled Dodam `~47.2ms` vs DuckDB `~34.3ms`. Profile with selected payload showed the first pass now reads only two columns, but because final row refs are spread across nearly all row groups, the missing payload column still forces a broad second scan.
                            - Conclusion: selected-payload materialization needs a clustering/spread cost model before defaulting. It is useful only when final row refs are concentrated in fewer row groups/pages or payload columns are much more expensive than the second pass setup.
                            - Re-tested dictionary string-id precompute/cache for three-key aggregates. Both remained default-off: precompute and cache variants were neutral-to-worse overall, while the default/off sample was better for the two focused no-order three-key cases.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_union_all`, and `copy_parquet` passed.
                          - Selected-payload row-group spread cost model:
                            - Added `SelectedPayloadSpreadCostInput` and `choose_selected_payload_by_spread(...)` to the cost analyzer. It rejects selected-payload second pass when final row refs touch too many row groups by absolute count or row-group ratio.
                            - Wired primitive top-k selected-payload tracing to report spread decisions. On SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_scrambled`, the selected 5000 rows touched `49/49` row groups with one missing payload column, so the decision is `row-group-spread`.
                            - Because spread is only known after the first pass, strict fallback would require re-running the full payload-copy path. Measured strict gate was worse (`~68.8ms` vs DuckDB `~33.9ms`) due to double scan, so the gate remains opt-in/diagnostic via `DODAM_ENABLE_PRIMITIVE_TOPK_SELECTED_PAYLOAD_SPREAD_GATE=1`; default selected-payload opt-in still proceeds after tracing.
                            - Selected-payload base reuse without strict gate sampled `~51.3ms` vs DuckDB `~40.0ms`; default payload-copy sampled `~44.4ms` vs DuckDB `~40.8ms`. Conclusion: the useful next structural step is not a post-hoc fallback, but a planner rule that estimates clustering before choosing a selected-payload plan or a page-level reader that can avoid broad second-pass setup.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, selected-payload spread cost test, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_union_all`, `copy_parquet`, and release build passed.
                            - SF=10 focused `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` sampled Dodam `~39.0ms`, DuckDB `~34.7ms` (`1.125x`). Full SF=10 generic 3-run sampled Dodam `~452.5ms`, DuckDB `~494.9ms` (`0.914x`). The remaining gap in this case is not the small IN-list predicate itself; it is mostly scan/decode plus top-k payload materialization/row identity handling.
                          - Blocked filter-position reuse follow-up:
                            - Reused the primitive selected-position generation helpers in the null-free ordered selected-batch path. A first attempt that generated positions in ascending order and then reversed them regressed `union_all_order_limit_scrambled`, so the path now emits descending positions directly with dedicated unrolled loops for the common 1/2-key `i32/i64` filters.
                            - Extended `DirectI64Filter` null-free paths to use raw `i64`/`i32` slices for tiny `IN` and hash-set `IN` filters instead of repeatedly calling `value(row)`. This keeps join late-selection as `column slice -> selected positions -> late RowSelection`.
                            - Removed the now-unused `small_i32_in_list` / `small_i64_in_list` helpers; small-list matching is owned by the block selected-position helpers.
