                            - SF=10 focused samples remained noisy: `join_expression_projection` sampled Dodam `~36.5ms` vs DuckDB `~31.0ms`; `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` sampled `~39.7ms` vs `~34.1ms`. Full SF=10 generic 3-run sampled Dodam `~454.0ms`, DuckDB `~497.3ms` (`0.913x`), with `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` `~36.4ms` vs `~33.1ms` in that run. Remaining gaps are still structural scan/decode + payload materialization, not scalar filter matching.
                          - Mask selection bit-iterator follow-up:
                            - Changed aggregate mask compaction from `mask.value(row)` scanning to Arrow `BooleanBuffer::set_indices()` for null-free masks. This keeps the `mask -> selected offsets -> selected aggregate update` boundary but uses Arrow's word-level bit scan instead of scalar boolean access.
                            - Tried merging consecutive group-id cache updates into run updates for three-key dictionary aggregates. This regressed the low-selectivity three-key fixture because same-group runs are not common enough, so the behavior is now opt-in only through `DODAM_ENABLE_COALESCE_GROUP_ID_CACHE_RUN_MERGE=1`.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_union_all`, `copy_parquet`, and release build passed.
                            - SF=10 focused `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` sampled Dodam `~33.8ms`, DuckDB `~28.1ms` (`1.201x`). Full SF=10 generic 3-run sampled Dodam `~453.1ms`, DuckDB `~506.8ms` (`0.894x`); in that full run `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` was near parity (`~37.5ms` vs `~37.1ms`) while the main remaining slower cases were low-selectivity three-key (`+4.9ms`), `join_expression_projection` (`+3.1ms`), and no-order three-key (`+1.7ms`).
                          - Dictionary selected local accumulator experiment:
                            - Added an opt-in selected dictionary local accumulator for dense-leading three-key aggregates. It accumulates `(dense leading slot, dictionary id) -> count/sum` for selected rows, then resolves dictionary ids to string ids and flushes touched slots. This is a cleaner block/vector shape than updating the global group for every selected row.
                            - Same-binary SF=10 A/B on `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` did not justify default-on: local accumulator on sampled Dodam `~31.8ms`, DuckDB `~28.2ms`; default/off sampled Dodam `~32.0ms`, DuckDB `~28.6ms`. The difference is noise-level, so the path remains opt-in through `DODAM_ENABLE_COALESCE_DICTIONARY_SELECTED_LOCAL_ACCUMULATE=1`.
                            - Full SF=10 generic 3-run with default-off sampled Dodam `~438.2ms`, DuckDB `~503.3ms` (`0.871x`). Remaining slower cases in that run: `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` `+5.2ms` and `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` `+4.7ms`; `three_key_expression_group_no_order` was at parity.
                          - Top-k materialization cleanup:
                            - Tried replacing typed top-k row gather with batch-run `take_record_batch()` materialization. It preserved selected order by grouping only consecutive selected rows from the same source batch, but it regressed `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` (`~44.9ms` vs `~40.7ms` with the path disabled), so the path is default-off via `DODAM_TOPK_TAKE_MATERIALIZATION_MIN_ROWS=usize::MAX`.
                            - Kept the narrower useful piece: typed top-k gather now builds null-free primitive/boolean arrays as `Vec<T>` instead of `Vec<Option<T>>`, avoiding validity construction when all source batches for the column are non-null.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_union_all`, `copy_parquet`, and release build passed.
                            - SF=10 focused `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` sampled Dodam `~39.2ms`, DuckDB `~34.5ms` (`1.136x`). Full SF=10 generic 3-run sampled Dodam `~444.4ms`, DuckDB `~501.6ms` (`0.886x`), with remaining slower cases led by `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` (`+4.7ms`), low-selectivity three-key (`+4.2ms`), and `join_expression_projection` (`+3.4ms`).
                          - Primitive top-k threshold-prune experiment:
                            - Added a block stage that can prune selected positions by the current heap threshold before row candidates enter heap update. This follows the desired pipeline shape (`filter -> selected positions -> threshold prune -> heap`), but in the current fixture the extra selected-position scan did not beat the existing cheap reject inside heap update.
                            - The threshold-prune stage is therefore opt-in only through `DODAM_ENABLE_PRIMITIVE_TOPK_THRESHOLD_PRUNE=1`.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_union_all`, `copy_parquet`, and release build passed.
                            - SF=10 focused default after leaving threshold-prune off sampled `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` Dodam `~36.1ms`, DuckDB `~34.3ms` (`1.053x`). Full SF=10 generic 3-run sampled Dodam `~447.9ms`, DuckDB `~499.0ms` (`0.897x`), with `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` near parity in the full run (`~36.1ms` vs `~34.6ms`).
                          - Primitive top-k selected-payload second pass:
                            - Added an opt-in row-ref based selected payload reader for primitive top-k (`DODAM_ENABLE_PRIMITIVE_TOPK_SELECTED_PAYLOAD=1`). The first pass scans only filter/sort primitive columns, keeps physical `(row_group, row_offset)` refs for heap slots, then a second pass reads projected primitive payload columns only for final selected rows.
                            - The implementation is intentionally a general physical shape rather than query-name special casing: row refs are grouped by row group, workers fill output slots by final output index, and the result preserves top-k order.
                            - A/B showed this is not default-worthy for the current scrambled fixture because final 5000 rows are spread across enough row groups that payload re-scan costs more than avoiding heap-slot payload copies. Focused SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_scrambled`: selected payload on `~60.8ms` vs default `~42.1ms` in the same binary, DuckDB `~34ms`.
                            - Default remains payload-copy slots. Full SF=10 generic 3-run after leaving the feature off sampled Dodam `~452.5ms`, DuckDB `~499.5ms` (`0.906x`); `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` was `~36.5ms` vs DuckDB `~34.7ms`.
                            - Conclusion: second-pass selected payload only helps if the final row refs are clustered or the payload columns are expensive enough. The cost model needs row-group spread / selected-row clustering before enabling it automatically.
                          - Primitive top-k one-pass fused selected page-reader experiment:
                            - Followed up on the second-pass problem by adding an opt-in one-pass fused selected page-reader path (`DODAM_ENABLE_PRIMITIVE_TOPK_FUSED_SELECTED_PAGE_READER=1`). It reads the file once, builds selected positions from the filter page, materializes payload only for filter-selected rows, then runs top-k over those selected batches.
                            - First implementation was sequential across row groups and was very slow (`~201ms`). After restoring row-group parallelism, it improved substantially but still lost to the default path: SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` fused page-reader `~49.3ms` vs default `~37.1ms`, DuckDB `~34ms`.
                            - The useful conclusion is that “read less payload rows” is not enough with the current page cursor implementation. Page-reader setup/raw page handling + selected batch materialization costs more than the default direct primitive path for this fixture. Keep it opt-in until the lower-level page decoder can feed top-k slots directly without building intermediate selected batches.
                          - File cache / read chunk locality sweep:
                            - Tested the hypothesis that larger read chunks improve locality. The file cache is disabled by default (`DODAM_FILE_CACHE_BYTES=0`) and the default chunk size is `512KiB`.
                            - With cache disabled, changing only `DODAM_FILE_CACHE_CHUNK_BYTES` produced mostly noisy results. With a `256MiB` file cache enabled, focused SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` improved as chunk size grew: `512KiB ~47.7ms`, `1MiB ~38.5ms`, `2MiB ~38.7ms`, `4MiB ~35.6ms` vs DuckDB `~32.7ms`.
                            - However full SF=10 generic with `DODAM_FILE_CACHE_BYTES=256MiB DODAM_FILE_CACHE_CHUNK_BYTES=4MiB` regressed badly: Dodam `~495.0ms`, DuckDB `~488.3ms` (`1.014x`). Scan-heavy aggregate cases slowed from cache lookup/lock/copy overhead and read amplification.
                            - Conclusion: larger read chunks can help a narrow repeated/locality-heavy top-k case, but should not be a global default. A future cost rule would need to enable file cache only for repeated reads over the same file/ranges or clustered selected payload reuse, not for broad one-pass scans.
                          - Selected page/top-k and low-selectivity aggregate follow-up:
                            - Added an opt-in selected page-view scanner for primitive `IN (...) ORDER BY primitive DESC LIMIT` top-k. It passes raw selected page views to the top-k state instead of constructing `DirectOrderedPrimitiveBatch` payload vectors first.
                            - Kept this path default-off. Focused SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` regressed in A/B: default sampled `~44.2ms` vs DuckDB `~39.6ms`; selected page-view opt-in sampled `~46.7ms` vs DuckDB `~39.8ms`. The avoided selected-batch allocation did not offset page-reader and local/global top-k merge overhead.
                            - Reworked low-selectivity three-key selected aggregate sum streaming from `Vec<(offset, Vec<i64>)>` into one contiguous `sum_chunk_values` scratch buffer plus `SelectedI64ChunkRange` block metadata. This removes per-page `Vec<i64>` allocations while keeping the operator interface block/range based.
                            - Focused SF=10 `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` improved from the profiled `~40.5ms` vs DuckDB `~33.4ms` to a later default focused sample of `~33.3ms` vs DuckDB `~28.7ms`; full generic 3-run sampled `~34.2ms` vs DuckDB `~28.2ms` for this case.
                            - Tried enabling the existing three-key block accumulator and selected-i64 AVX2 copy as defaults. Both lost on this fixture (`~39.7ms` and `~41.0ms` focused respectively), so they remain opt-in. The default contiguous memcpy path is already the better copy primitive here; remaining cost is page/dictionary read and group-id update.
                            - Added cache/re-read awareness to the common `choose_selected_payload(...)` cost input (`cached_reread`, `cached_min_average_run_len`) without changing the default decision. This gives future file-cache/selected-payload experiments a common cost-rule input instead of another query-local environment check.

                            - Final SF=10 generic 3-run after this pass sampled Dodam `~462.5ms`, DuckDB `~511.6ms` (`0.904x`). Remaining slower generic cases in that sample: `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` `+5.9ms`, `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` `+3.4ms`, `union_all_order_limit_full_read` `+2.8ms`, `non_null_bucket_count_sum` `+1.2ms`, and `three_key_expression_group_no_order` `+1.0ms`.
                          - Generic aggregate fallback boundary cleanup:
                            - Renamed the fallback-allowing masked projected-view aggregate entrypoint to `consume_projected_view_masked_with_record_batch_fallback(...)`, making RecordBatch fallback an explicit call-site choice instead of the default-looking API.
                            - Removed the now-unused `SelectionRuns::for_each_index(...)` callback API and converted masked dictionary aggregate update paths to iterate `SelectionRuns` as run/block ranges. Group updates are still per selected row internally, but the operator boundary is now `runs -> contiguous range -> update` rather than callback-per-row selection expansion.
                            - This is a structural vectorized-pipeline cleanup more than a speed win. Focused SF=10 samples after the change: `three_key_expression_group_no_order` Dodam `~45.0ms` vs DuckDB `~40.4ms`, `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` Dodam `~33.0ms` vs DuckDB `~27.9ms`.
                            - Full SF=10 generic 3-run sampled Dodam `~452.8ms`, DuckDB `~498.0ms` (`0.909x`). Remaining slower cases in that run included `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` `+5.2ms`, `union_all_append` `+3.2ms`, `three_key_expression_group_no_order` `+1.6ms`, and `three_key_expression_group` `+1.4ms`; individual `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` sampled `~43.6ms` vs DuckDB `~37.4ms`.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_union_all`, `copy_parquet`, and release build passed.
                          - Primitive top-k selected-payload precheck:
                            - Moved the row-group spread decision earlier: before choosing selected-payload, the planner now reads primitive Int32/Int64 row-group min/max stats for the `ORDER BY ... DESC LIMIT` key and conservatively estimates how many row groups can contain final top-k rows.
                            - The rule is general for primitive top-k: if projected payload columns are missing from the first pass and the estimated candidate row groups exceed the common spread cost threshold, selected-payload is rejected before any data scan. This avoids the previous strict spread gate's double-scan fallback.
                            - SF=10 focused `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` with `DODAM_ENABLE_PRIMITIVE_TOPK_SELECTED_PAYLOAD=1` now traces `phase=precheck decision=row-group-spread selected_rows=5000 row_groups=49/49 missing_payload_columns=1` and samples Dodam `~44.7ms`, DuckDB `~39.8ms` (`1.124x`). The same default no-env run sampled Dodam `~44.6ms`, DuckDB `~39.0ms` (`1.143x`), so the selected-payload opt-in no longer regresses by running a broad second pass.
                            - Full SF=10 generic 3-run sampled Dodam `~450.4ms`, DuckDB `~507.7ms` (`0.887x`). Remaining slower cases in that run: `filter_group_decimal_date` `+4.3ms`, `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` `+4.2ms`, `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` `+4.2ms`, and `three_key_expression_group_no_order` `+1.0ms`.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, selected-payload spread cost test, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_union_all`, `copy_parquet`, and release build passed.
                          - Low-selectivity three-key selected-payload recheck:
                            - Re-profiled SF=10 `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order`. The aggregate update path is no longer the main cost: selected rows are about `60k/6M`, but selected payload read reports large column-local skip/read work (`selected_skipped_rows` in the tens of millions across payload columns), with dictionary/value payload read dominating.
                            - Rechecked existing opt-in paths before changing defaults. Window payload was not a real win when using the correct env (`DODAM_FUSED_DICT_SELECTED_WINDOW_PAYLOAD=1`) and disabling the page-slice alternative; it sampled worse than default in the focused run. `DODAM_ENABLE_FUSED_SELECTED_I64_PAGE_DECODER=1` improved focused Dodam time from about `~39.7ms` to `~36.6ms`, but full SF=10 generic regressed slightly (`~454.5ms` vs default `~450.4ms`) and worsened the top-k scrambled sample, so it remains opt-in.
                            - Conclusion: the durable improvement should be a costed page/vector selected payload rule, not a blanket enable. The next promising structural work is to make selected payload reading share run/window planning across payload columns and reduce repeated skip/setup without increasing read amplification.
                          - Metadata-only top-k selected-payload stats + selected dictionary cursor:
                            - Changed primitive top-k selected-payload spread precheck to use cached `ArrowReaderMetadata` directly instead of opening the file and constructing a `ParquetRecordBatchReaderBuilder` only to read row-group min/max. Auto selected-payload remains opt-in (`DODAM_ENABLE_PRIMITIVE_TOPK_SELECTED_PAYLOAD_AUTO=1`) because default-on broad-reject stats checks were not free enough for `union_all_order_limit_scrambled`.
                            - Added a small general cleanup to selected byte-array dictionary id decoding: page overlap now advances a run cursor, and dictionary selected-range decode stops once runs pass the current page. This avoids repeatedly scanning irrelevant selected runs in fragmented selected-payload cases. Focused low-selectivity three-key did not show a clear standalone win, confirming the durable cost is page decode/setup rather than run-list traversal alone.
                            - Rechecked SF=10 focused `union_all_order_limit_scrambled`: one 5-run sample after restoring auto-off was Dodam `~43.0ms`, DuckDB `~41.3ms` (`1.04x`, noisy). Full SF=10 generic 3-run sampled Dodam `~433.6ms`, DuckDB `~500.6ms` (`0.866x`); remaining slower cases were `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` `+3.8ms`, `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` `+2.1ms`, `decimal_date_group_no_order` `+1.5ms`, and `three_key_expression_group_no_order` `+0.7ms`.
                          - Pushdown filter audit and generalization:
                            - Checked the remaining scan-pushdown gaps. Simple `FilterExpr` predicates already push through single-table scans and `plan_join_inputs()` for JOIN side filters. The main missing path was mixed expression predicates: when any conjunct required expression evaluation, simple conjuncts could be left outside scan pushdown in the projection-expression executor.
                            - Changed projection-expression execution to split conjuncts into pushable `FilterExpr` predicates and residual expression predicates. Pushable conjuncts now go into `scan_parquet_batches` / ordered scan, while only the residual expression predicates are evaluated after scan. This keeps row-group pruning and scan-side filtering for queries such as `simple_filter AND expression_filter`.
                            - Extended direct JOIN sink planning to allow filters only when the full filter can be pushed into left/right input scans. Mixed-side residuals such as cross-side `OR` still fall back, preserving correctness; side-only `AND` / same-side `OR` filters can now use direct sink with scan filters.
                            - Remaining pushdown work: expression-to-`FilterExpr` rewrites for safe cases (`COALESCE`, simple casts, nested field aliases), common physical set-op operand pushdown beyond same-source rules, and page/vector-level selected predicate application below Arrow `RecordBatch`.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, optimizer tests, join SQL smoke tests, expression-filter aggregate smoke test, and generic benchmark script syntax check passed.
                            - SF=10 generic focused comparison for affected expression/join cases (`repeats=3`, `warmup=1`): Dodam `~56.8ms`, DuckDB `~78.0ms` (`0.73x`). Full generic comparison sampled Dodam `~515.0ms`, DuckDB `~622.5ms` (`0.83x`). Remaining slower cases in that run were `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` (`+6.2ms`) and `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` (`+4.2ms`), not the newly changed expression/join pushdown cases.
                          - Safe expression predicate prefilter pushdown:
                            - Added a common `safe_expression_pushdown_filter(...)` lowering pass. It derives scan-side candidate filters for expression predicates while keeping the original expression residual for final SQL semantics. This avoids false-result risk: pushdown is only used as a prefilter, and unsupported/unsafe shapes simply fall back.
                            - Covered `COALESCE(column, literal) op literal` by lowering to `column op literal` plus `column IS NULL` when the fallback literal can satisfy the predicate. This gives row-group pruning / scan filtering for common `COALESCE` predicates without dropping rows that the residual expression may accept.
                            - Covered simple wrapped column predicates for expression paths: column-like expression `IN`, `LIKE`, `IS NULL`, and comparisons now produce candidate `FilterExpr` when safe. `NOT` and general `CAST(column AS type)` are intentionally not pushed as prefilters because without schema-aware proof they can introduce false negatives.
                            - Applied the same safe prefilter to JOIN expression residuals through `parse_join_filter_plan(...)`; the JOIN keeps the residual expression filter, while `plan_join_inputs()` can now derive left/right scan filters from the candidate filter.
                            - Removed the direct JOIN sink's old broad expression-predicate precheck. Direct sink now relies on parsed query shape and only accepts filters that are fully pushable to join inputs.
                            - Added unit coverage for direct JOIN sink with side filter, `COALESCE` prefilter lowering, and JOIN expression prefilter+residual behavior.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q -p dodam sql::tests`, `executes_aggregate_with_expression_filter_sql`, and `executes_inner_join_sql` passed.
                            - SF=10 focused expression/join comparison (`repeats=3`, `warmup=1`): Dodam `~58.1ms`, DuckDB `~82.3ms` (`0.705x`). Full generic comparison sampled Dodam `~516.7ms`, DuckDB `~623.3ms` (`0.829x`). Remaining slower cases were still `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` (`+3.7ms`) and `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` (`+3.1ms`), not the expression pushdown path.
                          - Standard SQL coverage expansion:
                            - Added `BETWEEN` / `NOT BETWEEN` to expression predicate detection and scalar predicate evaluation. Expression predicates such as `id + 1 BETWEEN 3 AND 6` now take the expression-filter path instead of failing in plain `FilterExpr` parsing.
                            - Added safe `BETWEEN` prefilter lowering for simple column-like expressions. This maps `column BETWEEN low AND high` to `column >= low AND column <= high`, and `NOT BETWEEN` to the corresponding disjunction, enabling scan/row-group pruning where possible.
                            - Added DuckDB differential coverage for expression `BETWEEN`, `NOT BETWEEN`, and expression `LIKE` with `upper/lower(...)`.
                            - Added generic benchmark coverage `standard_between_like_case` for `BETWEEN + LIKE + NOT BETWEEN + ORDER/LIMIT` against DuckDB.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q duckdb_differential_scan_filter_and_nulls --test duckdb_differential`, `cargo test -q -p dodam sql::tests`, and generic benchmark syntax check passed.
                            - SF=10 focused benchmark for `standard_between_like_case`: Dodam `~172.1ms`, DuckDB `~20.9ms` (`8.23x`). Full generic after adding the new case sampled Dodam `~668.5ms`, DuckDB `~652.9ms` (`1.02x`); the new case alone contributed about `+115.6ms` gap in that run. This exposes a real standard-SQL predicate gap: OR + LIKE + ORDER/LIMIT still forces too much scan/filter/sort work compared with DuckDB.
                            - Follow-up pushdown check: `key BETWEEN 10 AND 40` alone already pushed two row-group predicates, but `LIKE` and `NOT BETWEEN` did not. Storage can evaluate OR over comparison predicates conservatively, but `PredicateSet` was dropping OR from pushdown candidates.
                            - Generalized row-group pruning candidate extraction to preserve OR predicates when both sides are comparison-prunable. This lets `NOT BETWEEN` lower to an OR of range comparisons and reach row-group pruning.
                            - `EXPLAIN` for `standard_between_like_case` now reports `pushdown_predicates=1` instead of `0`; focused SF=10 improved from about `~172.1ms` to `~148.6ms` vs DuckDB `~20.9ms` (`7.12x`). The remaining gap is expected for this shape because `label LIKE 'label-2%'` cannot be pruned with min/max stats and the mixed `key BETWEEN ... OR label LIKE ...` predicate still requires broad residual filtering plus `ORDER BY id LIMIT` sorting.
                            - DuckDB-style prefix LIKE / TopN follow-up:
                              - Checked DuckDB's plan for `standard_between_like_case`: DuckDB rewrites `LIKE 'label-2%'` into a prefix predicate, uses `TOP_N`, and pushes an optional dynamic filter on the `ORDER BY id` key into the Parquet scan.
                              - Added conservative row-group pruning for simple ASCII prefix `LIKE 'literal%'` on UTF8 columns. The planner now keeps simple prefix LIKE as a pushdown candidate, and storage uses Parquet UTF8 min/max as the prefix range `[literal, next_ascii_prefix)`. This is a general pruning rule; unsupported patterns, escapes, `_`, non-prefix LIKE, negated LIKE, and non-ASCII prefixes fall back to no pruning.
                              - Added a general monotonic `ORDER BY <single primitive> ASC LIMIT/OFFSET` scan rule. If Parquet row-group stats and a one-column monotonic scan prove the sort key is globally nondecreasing and null-free, Dodam preserves scan order, applies the scan filter, and stops collecting once the LIMIT/OFFSET is satisfied instead of running `SortExec` over all filtered rows.
                              - Focused SF=10 `standard_between_like_case` after this pass: Dodam `~24.0ms`, DuckDB `~10.0ms` (`2.40x`). With `DODAM_DISABLE_MONOTONIC_ORDER_LIMIT_SCAN=1`, the same binary sampled Dodam `~117.5ms`, DuckDB `~9.0ms` (`13.06x`), so the structural win is the monotonic TopN/early-stop rule rather than LIKE matching itself.
                              - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_scan_filter_and_nulls`, and release build passed.
                              - Full SF=10 generic run could not complete because the current generic suite now includes an unsupported `array_length(tags)` case (`UnsupportedSql("expected column or aggregate expression, got array_length(tags)")`). Re-run the full suite after adding that scalar function or splitting unsupported coverage from performance comparison.
                              - Cold-path generalization:
                                - Added an engine-level monotonic column proof cache keyed by file path, length, modified time, and column. This makes exact monotonic verification a physical-property cache instead of repeated column scans in long-lived/query-file processes.
                                - Added a row-group based monotonic order-limit executor for `ORDER BY <single ASC key> LIMIT/OFFSET`. It uses row-group min/max monotonicity as the inter-row-group proof, scans one full row group at a time, verifies the emitted key order inside each scanned row group, and only stops after the current row group is fully checked. If order validation fails, it falls back to the normal full sort path.
                                - This avoids a cold full-column monotonic verification scan while preserving correctness; it is a general rule for ordered Parquet data, not a query-name special case.
                                - SF=10 focused `standard_between_like_case` after row-group early-stop: query mode Dodam `~11.7ms`, DuckDB `~26.2ms` (`0.45x`); disabling the row-group rule sampled Dodam `~59.2ms`, DuckDB `~26.3ms` (`2.25x`). Query-file mode sampled Dodam `~4.35ms`, DuckDB `~9.0ms` (`0.48x`).
                                - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_scan_filter_and_nulls`, and release build passed.
                          - Generic SQL gate + page aggregate default:
                            - Fixed a general execution-order issue where direct join sink planning parsed non-JOIN expression queries with the plain `FilterExpr` parser before the common expression executor could handle them. The direct join sink planner now first checks the SQL AST for explicit JOINs and skips non-JOIN SELECTs without calling `parse_sql(...)`.
                            - Made scalar predicate expression detection more conservative/general: non-AND/OR binary predicates inspect both sides for scalar expressions, and function/cast/subquery-like scalar nodes are explicitly treated as expression-path predicates.
                            - Fixed safe expression prefilter generation so reversed comparison attempts return `None` when the candidate "literal" side is not a literal. This lets residual-only predicates such as `array_length(tags) = 3` run correctly instead of failing while trying to build a scan prefilter.
                            - As a result the full generic suite now includes `nested_projection_filter`; SF=10 sample for that case was Dodam `~2.8ms`, DuckDB `~5.5ms`.
                            - Promoted the direct primitive count/sum page aggregate operator to default-on, with `DODAM_DISABLE_PRIMITIVE_PAGE_AGG_OPERATOR=1` as the escape hatch. This moves single-key primitive `count/sum` aggregation further toward page/block processing instead of Arrow batch/vector fallback. Focused `non_null_bucket_count_sum` improved from roughly `~13-19ms` noisy defaults to `~10-11ms` in repeated samples.
                            - Full SF=10 generic 3-run after this pass completed: Dodam `~313.5ms`, DuckDB `~285.5ms` (`1.10x`). Remaining slower cases are dominated by grouped aggregates and append scans: `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` `+14.2ms`, `union_all_append` `+13.3ms` in that run, `three_key_expression_group` `+8.1ms`, `three_key_expression_group_no_order` `+7.2ms`, `decimal_date_group_no_order` `+6.1ms`, and single-key count/sum/nullable group around `+5ms`.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_nested_struct_field_projection`, `duckdb_differential_scan_filter_and_nulls`, and release build passed.
                          - Row-at-a-time audit + sparse selected-payload spread rule:
                            - Rechecked the current aggregate fallback boundary. `DODAM_ENABLE_ROW_AT_TIME_FALLBACK` is already opt-in, so the remaining slow three-key cases were not caused by the old row-at-a-time fallback being selected by default.
                            - Profiled SF=10 `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order`: the fused selected path processed only about `60k/6M` rows, but touched selected payload across all 49 row groups and reported large payload/dictionary skip/read work. That means the path is vectorized at the operator boundary but loses block/locality benefits because sparse hits are spread across the file.
                            - Generalized the common row-group min/max metadata reader to support Decimal128, Date, Timestamp, and UInt32 stats instead of only Int32/Int64. This lets cost rules use decimal predicate spread without opening an Arrow batch reader.
                            - Added a fused dictionary selected aggregate auto gate using the existing `choose_selected_payload_by_spread(...)` rule. If the decimal predicate may hit too many row groups, the auto path rejects sparse selected payload and falls back to the more contiguous late/vector aggregate path. Forced `DODAM_ENABLE_FUSED_DICTIONARY_SELECTED_AGG=1` still overrides this for experiments.
                            - Focused SF=10 three-key benchmark (`repeats=5`, query-file) improved from Dodam `~81.8ms` / DuckDB `~48.0ms` (`1.70x`) to Dodam `~71.0ms` / DuckDB `~47.5ms` (`1.50x`). The main win was `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order`, down from `~26.1ms` to `~17.1ms`.
                            - Full SF=10 generic 3-run sampled Dodam `~328.2ms`, DuckDB `~304.5ms` (`1.08x`). Remaining gaps are now led by `non_null_bucket_count_sum`, `join_expression_projection`, `three_key_expression_group`, `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order`, and nullable/group aggregate cases.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, release build, focused three-key comparison, and full generic SF=10 comparison passed.
                          - JOIN late pushdown block handoff cleanup:
                            - Rechecked `join_expression_projection` with profiling. The direct all-column join aggregate path remains opt-in for a reason: disabling late left pushdown and forcing direct left aggregate was far slower on SF=10 because it reads join key/group/sum together before applying the low-cardinality `bucket IN (...)` filter.
                            - Kept the late/pushdown path as the default and tightened its operator boundary. The predicate phase now produces selected row offsets and selected group values in the same block pass instead of producing offsets first and rereading selected group values in a second selected-row loop.
                            - This is a general filter-to-payload handoff improvement for integer late materialization: pushdown selection carries the small payload needed by the aggregate, reducing row-at-a-time glue between predicate and payload phases.
                            - A/B checks: default `join_expression_projection` sampled around `~21.9ms`; forced direct left aggregate sampled `~2211ms`; disabling late left sampled `~110.7ms`. Row-group chunk sizes above 1 regressed this query, so the default chunk of 1 remains right for sparse selected payload.
                            - Focused SF=10 after the block handoff (`repeats=5`, query-file): `join_expression_projection` Dodam `~20.6ms`, DuckDB `~14.0ms`; `nullable_key_group` `~12.3ms` vs `~6.0ms`; `non_null_bucket_count_sum` `~10.7ms` vs `~5.5ms`.
                            - Full SF=10 generic 3-run sampled Dodam `~311.4ms`, DuckDB `~285.5ms` (`1.09x`). `join_expression_projection` gap in that run was down to `+1.6ms`; remaining larger gaps are `union_all_append`, three-key grouped aggregates, decimal/date grouped aggregates, and primitive count/sum decode.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, release build, `executes_inner_join_sql`, `duckdb_differential_join`, focused benchmark, and full generic SF=10 comparison passed.
                          - Same-source UNION ALL double-scan removal:
                            - Audited the set-operation path. `union_all_append` itself was already using a same-source single scan for disjoint equality filters, so its remaining gap is not a double-scan issue but Arrow/filter/write path overhead.
                            - Generalized the same-source UNION ALL planner in two ways:
                              - disjoint literal filters now cover both `col = literal` and disjoint `col IN (...)` operands, combining them into one scan-side `IN` filter;
                              - overlapping or otherwise non-disjoint same-source filters now use a single physical scan with an OR prefilter, then apply each operand filter per batch to preserve UNION ALL multiplicity.
                            - This removes the fallback recursive operand-by-operand scan for compatible same-file/same-projection UNION ALL queries. It is a general optimizer rule, not a benchmark-specific rewrite. It intentionally does not combine incompatible projections, JOIN operands, aggregate operands, DISTINCT, or ordered/limited child operands.
                            - Kept `DODAM_ENABLE_UNION_PRIMITIVE_SINK=1` opt-in. It helped one run of `union_all_append` (`~44ms -> ~38ms`) but lost/noised in another, while the Arrow same-source scan already removes the double read. It should only become default after a cost rule proves writer/primitive materialization wins.
                            - Focused SF=10 after this pass: `union_all_append` Dodam `~36.6ms`, DuckDB `~30.0ms`; `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` Dodam `~19.3ms`, DuckDB `~16.0ms`.
                            - Full SF=10 generic 3-run sampled Dodam `~297.9ms`, DuckDB `~281.5ms` (`1.06x`). Remaining slow cases are now mostly grouped aggregate decode/update and JOIN/aggregate path variability, not obvious set-operation double scans.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `same_source_union_all_plan` tests, DuckDB differential `duckdb_differential_union_all`, release build, focused union benchmark, and full generic SF=10 comparison passed.
                          - Late materialization double-read cost gate:
                            - Rechecked the assumption that a second Parquet pass can be more expensive than saved payload decode. Fully disabling expression aggregate late materialization confirmed it is still required for three-key expression aggregates: `three_key_expression_group` and `three_key_expression_group_no_order` regressed to `~120-128ms`, and low-selectivity three-key regressed to `~31ms`.
                            - Instead of removing late materialization, made its cost model more conservative:
                              - expression aggregate late materialization now enables the common IO-cost gate by default (`DODAM_DISABLE_EXPRESSION_AGG_LATE_IO_COST_GATE=1` disables it);
                              - late projection also uses the IO-cost gate by default (`DODAM_DISABLE_LATE_PROJECTION_IO_COST_GATE=1` disables it);
                              - the common minimum estimated IO saving threshold was raised from `5%` to `25%` via `DODAM_LATE_MIN_ESTIMATED_IO_SAVING_RATIO` default.
                            - This keeps late materialization only when the sampled selection and compressed-byte estimate show meaningful IO savings. It directly addresses the double-read risk without breaking cases where late materialization is structurally necessary.
                            - Focused SF=10 late-related sample after the change: `nested_projection_filter` Dodam `~2.3ms` vs DuckDB `~5.0ms`; `coalesce_type_projection` `~4.9ms` vs `~5.0ms`; three-key expression aggregates remained in the `~15-26ms` range instead of falling back to `~120ms`.
                            - Full SF=10 generic 3-run sampled Dodam `~305.6ms`, DuckDB `~281.5ms` (`1.09x`), with a noisy `non_null_bucket_count_sum` outlier. A focused primitive recheck on the same binary sampled `non_null_bucket_count_sum` `~10.4ms` vs DuckDB `~5.5ms` and `nullable_key_group` `~12.1ms` vs `~7.0ms`.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cost::tests`, release build, focused late benchmark, full generic SF=10 comparison, and primitive focused recheck passed.
                          - Dense primitive count/sum accumulator cleanup:
                            - Added an existing-dense-range fast path for i32 count/sum page and vector aggregates. When planner/statistics already initialized `DenseI32GroupIndex`, the accumulator now skips the per-batch min/max discovery pass and accumulates directly into the known dense range, falling back if an out-of-range key appears.
                            - Added reusable dense accumulator scratch buffers to `SingleKeyCountSumVectorState`. This avoids allocating `counts`/`totals` vectors for every dense batch, mainly helping nullable-key dense group shapes with about 1k slots.
                            - Rejected experiments: replacing raw primitive reads with `ptr::read_unaligned` plus bounds asserts regressed sharply on SF=10 primitive aggregates, and replacing the existing `checked_sub`/`get_mut` offset path with a helper also did not improve the hot loop. Both were reverted.
                            - Focused SF=10 primitive comparison (`repeats=9`, query-file): `nullable_key_group + non_null_bucket_count_sum` improved from about `~22.17ms` to `~21.36ms`; `nullable_key_group` sampled `~10.85ms` vs DuckDB `~7.0ms`, and `non_null_bucket_count_sum` sampled `~10.51ms` vs DuckDB `~6.0ms`.
                            - Broader SF=10 sample over primitive + three-key + union cases (`repeats=5`): Dodam `~101.7ms`, DuckDB `~68.5ms` (`1.49x`). Remaining gaps are still dominated by primitive Parquet value decode, three-key selected aggregation, and UNION ALL append/output overhead rather than this accumulator allocation path.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, release build, focused primitive comparison, and broader focused generic comparison passed.
                          - Column decode/read parallelism check:
                            - Added `DODAM_DIRECT_PRIMITIVE_COUNT_SUM_WORKERS` to tune the direct primitive count/sum page aggregate worker count. This is a measurement hook for row-group parallelism and L2/cache behavior before attempting column-level parallel decode.
                            - SF=10 worker sweep over `nullable_key_group + non_null_bucket_count_sum` showed that simply reducing workers to preserve cache locality is not enough: worker counts `2/4/8` were worse than the default row-group parallel path in this environment. `DODAM_ENABLE_BALANCED_DIRECT_PRIMITIVE_PARTITIONS=1` was also mixed/noisy.
                            - Diagnosis: the current path already parallelizes decode/read across row groups. The `column_read` profile is worker-summed, so a large value-column number does not mean single-threaded wall time. For SF=10 with 49 row groups, column-level parallelism inside each row group is likely to increase synchronization and L2 pressure unless it is implemented as a bounded chunk pipeline.
                            - Correct direction if row groups are fewer than cores or one column dominates: a cache-bounded column decode pipeline with at most 1-2 chunks in flight per row group, keeping key/value chunk pairs around the current 16k-row batch size (roughly 64KB i32 key + 128KB i64 value before aggregate scratch) so the aggregate operator consumes paired chunks while they are still cache-hot. Avoid full-column materialization or unbounded per-column queues.
                            - L2 prefetch hint experiment:
                              - Added opt-in `_mm_prefetch(..., _MM_HINT_T1)` for direct i32/i64 page aggregate loops via `DODAM_DIRECT_PRIMITIVE_PREFETCH_ROWS=N`. The default `0` keeps the no-prefetch loop separate so the normal hot path does not pay a per-row branch/call.
                              - SF=10 results did not justify enabling it by default. Prefetch distances `64` and `128` regressed badly. Distance `256/512` sometimes helped `non_null_bucket_count_sum` slightly but consistently hurt `nullable_key_group` and overall sum. Default re-run stayed in the expected range around `~22.5ms` for the two primitive queries.
                              - Diagnosis: these page-byte loops are mostly sequential, so hardware prefetch already handles much of the access. Manual L2 prefetch adds instruction pressure and can pull value bytes too early, evicting dense accumulator/key state. Keep this as an experiment hook only; do not default-enable without a cost rule and hardware-specific evidence.
                          - Bounded/vectorized page pipeline follow-up:
                            - Applied the planned sequence conservatively:
                              - 1 / bounded decode pipeline: confirmed the direct count/sum page reader already emits bounded `batch_size` chunks instead of full-column materialization on the required-page path. Added `DODAM_DIRECT_PRIMITIVE_L2_CHUNK_BYTES` as an opt-in row-width based chunk-size clamp for direct count/sum page fold; default remains the caller batch size because smaller chunks regressed in SF=10 tests.
                              - 2 / value decode + aggregate fusion: kept the direct page path as raw `key bytes + sum bytes -> aggregate` without `RecordBatch`. Added a lower-level nullable page batch variant so compact key bytes + def-levels can be passed directly to the aggregate operator when explicitly enabled.
                              - 3 / nullable key fused path: added `DirectPrimitiveCountSumPageBatch::I32NullableI64`, `NullablePlainPrimitivePageCursor`, and `SingleKeyCountSumVectorState::consume_i32_nullable_i64_page_bytes(...)`. This fuses nullable key def-level scan, compact key decode, required sum byte decode, and dense group update in one aggregate path.
                              - 4 / page-level chunk cost rule: measured 4K/8K/16K chunks on SF=10 primitive aggregates. 4K and 8K were worse, so the L2 chunk rule remains opt-in only.
                              - 5 / apply to gap queries: ran primitive and broader samples including `three_key_expression_group_no_order`, `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order`, and `union_all_append`.
                            - Important result: nullable page cursor is not default-enabled. With `DODAM_ENABLE_NULLABLE_COUNT_SUM_PAGE_CURSOR=1`, `nullable_key_group` and schema-nullable-but-data-non-null shapes regressed because def-level decoding and compact value accounting outweighed the lower-level path. Optional columns that are all-present also regressed when forced through page def-level checks, so default required-page handling remains limited to `max_def_level=0`.
                            - SF=10 measurements were noisy but clear on direction: default primitive focused re-runs stayed around `~24-36ms` for `nullable_key_group + non_null_bucket_count_sum`, while nullable page cursor opt-in and smaller chunks were worse. Broader sample after the follow-up showed Dodam `~153.3ms` vs DuckDB `~103.5ms` (`1.48x`) across primitive, three-key, and union append cases.
                            - Retained as safe defaults: required-page raw-byte aggregate fusion, dense scratch reuse, worker/chunk/prefetch measurement hooks with default-off behavior. Retained as opt-in/research only: nullable page cursor, manual L2 prefetch, and L2 chunk clamp.
                            - Next real improvement needs a page decoder that can avoid expensive def-level decode for all-present optional pages using page metadata/null counts, plus a cost rule that routes nullable columns to the page fused path only when null density and row-group/page shape make it cheaper than the existing vector path.
                          - Optional-page all-present routing attempt:
                            - Added a lower-cost all-present check for Parquet definition levels. `plain_page_all_present(...)` now scans the RLE/bit-packed stream without materializing a `Vec<i16>`, and DataPageV2 uses `num_nulls == 0` to skip full def-level decode in the required-page cursor.
                            - Added opt-in optional all-present routing for direct primitive count/sum via `DODAM_ENABLE_OPTIONAL_ALL_PRESENT_COUNT_SUM_PAGE_CURSOR=1`. The idea is to allow schema-nullable keys to use the required-page raw-byte path when every page is all-present, while falling back on the first null page.
                            - Added DataPageV2 `num_nulls == 0` handling to nullable/general page readers as well. Nullable cursor can fill all-present def-levels without RLE decoding, and the general page reader avoids RLE decode on all-present V2 pages.
                            - Results: the all-present and nullable cursor routes still should not be default. SF=10 focused A/B over `nullable_key_group + non_null_bucket_count_sum`: default sampled around `~32.9ms` in one run, optional all-present `~45.8ms`, nullable cursor `~39.5ms`. Broader SF=10 sample with default routes was Dodam `~104.5ms`, DuckDB `~70.0ms` (`1.49x`) over primitive, three-key, and union append cases.
                            - TPC-H SF=1 spot check on scan/aggregate queries stayed healthy: Q01/Q06/Q14 subset sampled Dodam `~52.1ms`, DuckDB `~61.0ms` (`0.85x`), with only Q06 slightly slower by `~1.7ms`.
                            - Conclusion: the safe retained default is the allocation-free all-present checker and V2 `num_nulls == 0` shortcut where already used by existing paths. Optional all-present count/sum routing and nullable page cursor remain opt-in because page-level checks/def-level accounting still cost more than the existing vector path on the current fixtures.
                          - Union / selected aggregate / primitive re-ranking:
                            - Rechecked `union_all_append` with the same-source primitive sink. One focused run improved from `~56.3ms` to `~48.2ms`, but same-binary broader A/B showed default-off was safer (`union_all_append` around `~64.7ms` with `DODAM_DISABLE_UNION_PRIMITIVE_SINK=1`, while default-on sampled much worse in one run). The primitive sink therefore remains opt-in through `DODAM_ENABLE_UNION_PRIMITIVE_SINK=1`.
                            - Rechecked selected aggregate opt-ins. `DODAM_ENABLE_FUSED_SELECTED_I64_PAGE_DECODER=1`, `DODAM_ENABLE_COALESCE_DICTIONARY_SELECTED_LOCAL_ACCUMULATE=1`, and the combination did not produce a stable enough win for `three_key_expression_group_no_order` / `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order`; they remain opt-in.
                            - Focused SF=10 final sample over representative remaining gaps: Dodam `~130.2ms`, DuckDB `~98.0ms` (`1.33x`). Per-case: `nullable_key_group` `~13.1ms` vs `~8.5ms`, `non_null_bucket_count_sum` `~21.8ms` vs `~7.5ms`, `three_key_expression_group_no_order` `~33.9ms` vs `~22.5ms`, `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` `~17.3ms` vs `~10.0ms`, `union_all_append` `~44.0ms` vs `~49.5ms`.
                            - TPC-H SF=1 structural subsets:
                              - output/materialization-heavy Q03/Q12/Q18: Dodam `~199.1ms`, DuckDB `~88.0ms`; Q12 and Q03 remain large gaps, while Q18 was faster than DuckDB in that sample.
                              - scan aggregate Q01/Q06/Q14: Dodam `~81.9ms`, DuckDB `~77.5ms`; Q06 remained the only meaningful slower case (`+6.0ms`).
                              - selected/join subset Q09/Q21: Q21 was faster than DuckDB; Q09 Dodam samples were empty in the compare script output and need runner/sample collection debugging before using it for performance judgment.
                            - Current priority after this pass: Q12/Q03 structure, generic primitive count/sum decode, and three-key selected aggregate. Repeated default promotion attempts around nullable page cursor, manual prefetch, smaller chunks, selected payload variants, and primitive union sink have been too noisy or regressive to turn on globally.
                          - Q03/Q12 SF=1 focused follow-up:
                            - Reproduced the current SF=1 gap with individual parquet-output comparison: Q03 around `~60.7ms` vs DuckDB `~34.5ms`, Q12 around `~71.4ms` vs `~20.5ms`.
                            - Q12 profiling again showed the dominant cost is the lineitem scan/decode path (`l_orderkey,l_shipmode,l_commitdate,l_receiptdate,l_shipdate`) rather than orders lookup/update. The route reads all 53 lineitem row groups and about `47MB` compressed input for those columns.
                            - Tried promoting Q12 lineitem selection-vector by default. It showed one good run (`~85ms -> ~65ms`) but same-binary A/B later showed default-off was faster (`~64ms` vs default-on `~70ms`), so the selection-vector path remains opt-in through `DODAM_Q12_ENABLE_LINEITEM_SELECTION_VECTOR=1`.
                            - Tried fusing Q12 shipmode matching into the selection-vector construction and checking shipmode before date predicates. This regressed (`~61ms` area vs the prior `~54ms` best sample), likely because raw date comparisons are cheaper and branch/cache behavior is better when dates are checked first. Reverted this experiment.
                            - Retained Q03 `DODAM_Q03_REVENUE_LATE_ROW_GROUP_CHUNK` default from `8` to `4`. Same-binary A/B showed chunk `8` made Q03 much worse (`~38ms` gap vs `~24ms` gap in the focused sample). This is a sparse late-materialization scheduling policy adjustment, not a query-value specialization.
                            - Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, release build, and focused Q03/Q12 SF=1 comparisons passed.
                          - Q03/Q12 row-at-a-time audit:
                            - Added `LateSelectionBuilder::push_selected_u32_offsets(...)` so late-materialization selectors can receive a block-produced selected-offset slice without going through an iterator/closure boundary.
                            - Rewired Q03's null-free late selection paths to build selected offsets and side-car selected order payloads in explicit block loops. This keeps the vectorized boundary while removing a row-at-a-time `filter_map` glue layer from Q03 late selection.
                            - SF=1 focused Q03/Q12 comparison after this pass sampled Q03 around `~43.7ms` vs DuckDB `~24.5ms`, down from the prior final sample around `~47.3ms`; Q12 stayed around `~49ms` because the change targets Q03 late selection.
                            - Tried a Q12 pending run accumulator that locally combines consecutive `l_orderkey` runs before touching the pending `HashMap`. It is retained as an opt-in experiment via `DODAM_Q12_ENABLE_PENDING_RUN_ACCUMULATOR=1`, but not default: one short A/B improved Q12 (`~55.7ms -> ~52.2ms`), while a longer same-binary run was neutral (`~54.1ms` default vs `~54.2ms` opt-in).
                            - Conclusion: Q03 still benefits from removing row-at-a-time glue in late selection. Q12's remaining large gap is still mostly Parquet scan/decode/materialization for the five lineitem columns; small pending-map CPU reductions are not enough without lower-level scan/decode fusion or stronger row/page pruning.
                          - Q12 direct raw scan attempt:
                            - Added a general storage/engine experiment path for `i64 + dictionary byte-array + i32/date32 x3` scans. The path reads dictionary ids and primitive date/key columns directly via Parquet column readers and feeds a block consumer, avoiding Arrow `RecordBatch`/dictionary-array construction when supported.
                            - Wired Q12 lineitem to this path behind `DODAM_Q12_ENABLE_DIRECT_LINEITEM_RAW=1`. It falls back to the existing `dictionary_row_group_map_view` path when the file shape/encoding is unsupported.
                            - Same-binary SF=1 Q12 A/B showed the direct raw path is not default-worthy: direct raw sampled around `~52.6ms`, while the existing Arrow/dictionary row-group map path sampled around `~49.5ms`. This matches earlier direct-column-reader lessons: the public ColumnReader path plus row-group worker setup does not beat Arrow's optimized reader here.
                            - Kept the scanner as opt-in infrastructure because it is a useful lower boundary for future page/vector decoder fusion, but default Q12 remains on the existing row-group map path. Final focused Q03/Q12 sample with direct raw default-off was Q03 `~42.9ms` vs DuckDB `~23.0ms`, Q12 `~47.8ms` vs `~15.0ms`.
                          - Q12 page/dictionary raw follow-up:
                            - Added read/consume profiling for the `i64 + dictionary byte-array + i32/date32 x3` direct raw fold. Profiling confirmed why the prior direct raw path lost: Arrow row-group map lineitem read/consume sampled around `read_next=156ms, consume=24ms`, while direct raw sampled around `read=196ms, consume=22ms`. The consume loop improved slightly, but the lower-level read/decode path paid more.
                            - Added an opt-in page-raw scanner (`DODAM_Q12_ENABLE_DIRECT_LINEITEM_PAGE_RAW=1`) that would pass primitive page bytes directly to the Q12 consumer. It correctly stayed fallback-only on the current TPC-H parquet because the relevant columns are dictionary encoded (`RLE_DICTIONARY`) rather than plain pages, so a plain-page raw-byte cursor cannot apply.
                            - Added an opt-in dictionary-id scanner (`DODAM_Q12_ENABLE_DIRECT_LINEITEM_DICT_RAW=1`) that passes `dictionary ids + dictionary values` for `l_orderkey`, `l_shipmode`, and the three date columns without building Arrow arrays. It was significantly slower: one SF=1 profile sampled lineitem `read=494.9ms, consume=46.3ms`, and focused Q12 repeated runs were worse than default.
                            - Conclusion: simply bypassing `RecordBatch` is not enough. The remaining Q12 gap needs a real Parquet dictionary page/vector decoder that can apply page/selection pruning while decoding, not a row-group-wide dictionary-id decode followed by a Rust consumer. Keep both new routes opt-in only; default remains Arrow row-group map.
                          - Q12 selected-payload experiment:
                            - Added an opt-in selected-payload route (`DODAM_Q12_ENABLE_DIRECT_LINEITEM_SELECTED_PAYLOAD=1`) for the general shape `i64 payload + byte-array dictionary payload selected by i32 dictionary x3 predicate`. It reads the three date predicate columns, builds selected runs, then reads/compacts only selected `orderkey/shipmode` payload into the Q12 pending map.
                            - The first version fell back because `ColumnReader::skip_records/read_records` selected key reads failed on the current dictionary-encoded nullable `l_orderkey` shape. Added an i64 dictionary-id fallback and selected-run compaction so the route executes.
                            - Result was still worse than default. SF=1 selected-payload profile sampled lineitem `read=452.9ms, consume=3.2ms`, and focused Q12 repeated runs were around `~79-106ms` versus default `~50-82ms` in the same binary. Payload consume became tiny, but the custom dictionary predicate/key decode dominated.
                            - Diagnosis: this confirms the important boundary. Reducing rows after row-group-wide custom dictionary decode is too late. The needed improvement is page/vector-level dictionary decode with selection applied while decoding, ideally reusing or matching Arrow/Parquet's optimized dictionary decoder rather than re-decoding full row groups in Rust helper code.
                            - Follow-up: added selected-run i64 dictionary value decoding for `l_orderkey`, avoiding full key dictionary-id decode before compaction. This reduced the selected-payload lineitem profile from `read=452.9ms` to `read=418.9ms`, so selected payload/key pruning helps, but not nearly enough to beat the default Arrow row-group map path. The remaining cost is dominated by the three predicate date dictionary columns being fully decoded by the custom RLE/dictionary helper.
                            - Tried replacing the selected-run predicate builder's row loop with an 8-row mask/block loop and explicit bounds checks. This regressed the selected-payload lineitem profile (`read=418.9ms -> 436.9ms`) and focused Q12 runs, likely because the extra mask/run helper calls and less favorable branch shape outweighed the removed `Option::zip` chain. Reverted the block-loop rewrite; the next useful step is not this row-level glue but a true page-level dictionary predicate cursor.
                            - Added the first real page-level predicate cursor for the selected-payload experiment. The three i32 dictionary predicate columns now advance page-by-page instead of materializing row-group-sized id vectors before building selected runs. This reduced the selected-payload lineitem profile from `read=418.9ms` to `read=360.1ms`. It is still slower than the default Arrow row-group map path, but it confirms the page/vector direction is the right layer. Next bottleneck is the custom RLE/dictionary id page decoder hot loop itself.
                            - Tried changing the predicate page cursor's dictionary id buffer from `Vec<i32>` to `Vec<u32>` with a dedicated decoder to remove negative-id checks/casts. This regressed (`read=360.1ms -> 378.3ms`) and focused Q12 also worsened, so it was reverted. The existing `i32` decoder shape is not the main issue; the bigger cost is still RLE/bitpack decode and page cursor orchestration.
                            - Reworked `decode_rle_i32_values(...)` to use a batch decoder API instead of calling `next_value()` once per value. RLE runs now append repeated values in one chunk and bitpacked runs are consumed run-by-run. This is a general dictionary-id decode improvement, not Q12-specific. The selected-payload lineitem profile improved again from `read=360.1ms` to `read=321.4ms`, confirming that the decoder hot loop is now the right target.
                            - Added byte-aligned bitpacked fast paths for dictionary id decode (`bit_width` 0/8/16/32). The decoder now copies/converts those runs in a batch instead of calling `read_bitpacked_value()` for every id. Q12 selected-payload lineitem improved from `read=321.4ms` to `read=307.7ms`. The path is still slower than Arrow default overall, but the cumulative page/vector decoder work is moving the right part of the profile.
                            - Traced the actual TPC-H Q12 lineitem date dictionary id width and confirmed it is `bit_width=12`. Added a dedicated 12-bit unpack fast path (`2 ids / 3 bytes`) to the batch decoder. This produced the first large decoder win: selected-payload lineitem profile improved from `read=307.7ms` to `read=194.7ms`, and focused Q12 selected-payload runs are now close to the default Arrow row-group map runs. Keep opt-in until repeated SF=1/SF=10 checks show it is consistently faster, but the gap is now mainly down to orchestration/noise rather than an obviously slower decoder.
                            - Tried fusing dictionary value lookup into the i32 dictionary page cursor so it would store decoded date values instead of dictionary ids. This regressed badly (`read=194.7ms -> 398.6ms`) and focused Q12 worsened. Reverted it. Keeping compact id decode plus lookup in the predicate loop is better; the next useful fusion should build the predicate mask/runs directly from decoded id blocks rather than materializing value vectors.
                            - Tried exposing cursor id slices/dictionaries and building selected runs through a slice-view helper to remove `value()` method calls and `Option::zip`. This also regressed (`read=194.7ms -> 221.9ms`) and did not beat default in repeated Q12 runs. Reverted it. The compiler/locality behavior of the compact `value()` loop is better than the extra view/helper abstraction.
                            - Re-applied the selected-run loop cleanup without the extra helper abstraction: pagewise predicate selection now reads cursor id slices directly inside the existing function, and dictionary ids are validated as hard errors during decode. This removes per-row `Option::zip`/validity branches while keeping the page cursor shape. The improvement is modest but stable enough to keep as part of the opt-in selected-payload path.
                            - Added selected-payload breakdown metrics for this direct path: `selected_predicate`, `selected_payload`, `selected_rows`, `selected_ratio`, and `selected_runs`. Q12 SF=1 showed the true split: about `108k` selected rows from `6.0M` lineitem rows, but about `98k` selected runs, so payload reads are extremely fragmented. This is why selected ratio alone is not a sufficient cost rule.
                            - Added a conservative fragmented-payload strategy: when selected runs are numerous and average run length is <= 2, try full dictionary-id decode for payload columns and compact selected rows instead of issuing many selected skip/read ranges. On Q12 SF=1 this was roughly neutral/slightly noisy (`selected_payload` stayed around `34-39ms` warm), so it is retained only behind the selected-payload opt-in path and should not be promoted without broader evidence.
                            - Added unchecked dictionary value loads in the hot i32x3 predicate loop after making dictionary-id decode validate ranges. Q12 SF=1 selected-payload warm runs sampled around `~48-52ms`, roughly matching the default Arrow row-group map path in the same environment. The dominant remaining selected path cost is still predicate column decode/build (`selected_predicate` around `78-83ms` warm), so the next meaningful step is true fused page/block predicate construction rather than more row-loop cleanup.
                            - Rejected follow-up after commit `e3fb51a`: unrolling the 12-bit dictionary id decoder from `2 ids / 3 bytes` to `8 ids / 12 bytes` did not materially reduce `selected_predicate`, and changing the page cursor id buffer from `Vec<i32>` to `Vec<u16>` regressed Q12 selected-payload warm runs (`~55-64ms`, `selected_predicate` often `80-87ms`). Both were reverted. The likely reason is that extra conversion/specialized decode code did not beat the existing compact i32 path; the useful next layer remains fused page/block predicate construction that avoids building three decoded id buffers independently.
                            - Kept a small general run-heavy selected-path cleanup: selected run vectors now reserve a conservative row-count-based capacity in the Q12 selected-payload path, and i32x3 selected-run builders use `push_disjoint_run(...)` when they know emitted runs cannot be adjacent. This avoids repeated capacity growth and the generic adjacent-run merge check for highly fragmented selections. SF=1 Q12 selected-payload warm runs stayed around `~48ms`, with `selected_predicate` mostly `~78-80ms`; this is a small stability/overhead cleanup, not the structural fix.
                            - Started selected offsets representation for run-fragmented payloads. When the fragmented full-decode payload strategy is chosen, selected runs are materialized once into `u32` offsets and reused to compact both `l_orderkey` dictionary values and `l_shipmode` dictionary ids. SF=1 Q12 selected-payload stayed healthy (`~47.5-48.9ms` warm, `selected_payload` as low as `~33.6ms`). This confirms offsets are viable for highly fragmented selections, but the current implementation still derives offsets from runs after predicate evaluation; the stronger version should let the predicate builder emit offsets/bitmap directly.
                            - Rejected predicate trait/spec lowering attempt. Replacing the opaque closure with an `I32x3PredicateEval` trait and an ordered-range predicate struct made Q12 selected-payload worse (`selected_predicate` often `~92-100ms` warm). It was reverted. The next real fused step should not add a trait-call layer; it should stage the predicate physically: build candidate rows from the range column first, then selected-decode/evaluate the dependent columns only for those candidates.
                            - Rejected staged `receiptdate range -> selected commit/ship/receipt decode` implementation. It reduced final run count slightly (`~98.8k -> ~95.3k`) but made predicate time much worse (`selected_predicate` around `~114-124ms` warm) because the receipt range did not cut candidates enough to pay for extra selected decode passes. Reverted the staged implementation. If revisited, it needs page-level fusion that reuses decoded receipt ids/values and avoids rereading the range column, not a multi-pass selected decode pipeline.
                            - Rejected direct-offset emission from the current pagewise predicate loop. Having the predicate builder push `u32` offsets while also building runs regressed Q12 selected-payload (`selected_predicate` around `~83-87ms` warm, wall time often `~56-62ms`) because the extra push in the hot predicate loop outweighed avoiding the later run-to-offset materialization. Keep offsets materialized only inside the fragmented payload path for now. Direct offsets should be revisited only when a fused page decoder can emit offsets without an additional per-selected-row side effect in the existing hot loop.
                            - Generalized the fragmented selected-payload full-decode rule into `cost.rs` as `choose_fragmented_selected_payload_full_decode(...)` with tests. The Q12 path now calls this shared rule instead of embedding the fixed `runs >= 4096 && avg_run_len <= 2` condition locally. SF=1 Q12 selected-payload remained in the expected range (`~49-51ms` warm). This does not change the hot path, but it makes the selected payload strategy easier to reuse for Q03/top-k/selected aggregate paths.
                            - Q03 late materialization selection cleanup: the null-free Q03 revenue late-selection paths now keep a reusable `Vec<u32>` scratch in the state instead of allocating `selected_offsets` for every batch. This preserves the block/vector selection structure and removes small allocation churn. SF=1 Q03 focused run stayed healthy but not materially faster (`~55-63ms` warm in the latest focused run); the remaining Q03 cost is still scan/decode and payload aggregation rather than this selection scratch.
                            - Nullable selected i64 decoder cleanup: the page decoder no longer recomputes `def_levels[..local_start]` for every selected run. It now builds a reusable page-level present-value prefix once and computes each run's value offset in O(1). This is a general block/vector selected-decode improvement for nullable INT64 payloads; it keeps the unsupported/null-in-selected-run fallback behavior unchanged. Generic `nullable_key_group` remained healthy (`Dodam=7.616ms`, `DuckDB=13.921ms`, `0.547x`) and Q12 was unaffected as expected because that path is not nullable-i64 selected-payload bound.
                            - Added batch-local dense accumulate for non-null `i64` key + `i64` sum count/sum aggregation. This is not a TPC-H special case: if a batch has at least 1024 rows, a small enough key range, and reasonable density (`slot_count <= rows * 4`), Dodam accumulates count/sum by dense slot and updates the hash/group index once per populated slot instead of once per row. Sparse or wide i64 ranges keep the existing hash path. A direct BIGINT small-group fixture (`1M` rows, `small64 = i % 100`) measured `Dodam=9.440ms`, `DuckDB=18.342ms`, `0.515x`; existing generic `non_null_bucket_count_sum` and `nullable_key_group` stayed healthy (`~0.52x`).
                            - Extended the dense i64 count/sum rule to nullable BIGINT keys with non-null i64 sums and wired the direct page-byte i64 count/sum path into the same dense accumulate helper. Nullable keys now accumulate null rows separately and update non-null groups by dense slot, preserving the hash fallback for sparse/wide ranges. Direct nullable BIGINT fixture improved from about `0.524x` to `0.497x` vs DuckDB (`Dodam=9.272ms`, `DuckDB=18.651ms`); non-null BIGINT stayed healthy (`Dodam=9.599ms`, `DuckDB=18.761ms`, `0.512x`). Full generic suite stayed ahead overall (`Dodam=0.215s`, `DuckDB=0.376s`, `0.572x`), with the weakest remaining generic cases being three-key expression grouping (`~0.89-0.95x`).
                            - Generalized three-key dictionary grouping local slot accumulation. When two leading keys form a dense range and the dictionary dimension keeps `slot_count` bounded, Dodam now accumulates count/sum in a dense slot vector and merges only touched slots into the group table instead of updating the group per row. The rule is guarded by `coalesce_dictionary_selected_local_accumulate_max_slots()` and can be disabled with `DODAM_DISABLE_COALESCE_DICTIONARY_SELECTED_LOCAL_ACCUMULATE=1`. Full generic suite improved from about `Dodam=0.2195s` to `0.2105s`; three-key no-order improved (`~19.1ms -> ~17.4ms` in the full suite), while ordered three-key remains noisy and is still the weakest generic case (`~1.02x` vs DuckDB in the latest full run).
                            - Reused the three-key dictionary local-accumulate scratch buffers in `CoalesceKeyCountSumCollector` instead of allocating the dense slot vector and touched-slot vector for every batch. Touched slots are cleared after merge, keeping the block/vector update shape without full re-zeroing. The latest full generic suite remained improved vs the pre-local baseline (`Dodam=0.212s`, `DuckDB=0.367s`, `0.578x`), and ordered three-key improved in the full run (`~19.7ms`, `0.953x`), but weak-only focused runs are still noisy and can report ordered three-key above DuckDB.
                            - Tried a dense matrix accumulator for join coalesce count/sum fusion when the left group filter is tiny `IN` and right class ids are bounded. It did not improve the real generic join case: focused `join_expression_projection` stayed around `~15.9ms` and the full generic suite regressed slightly (`~0.214s`). The likely reason is that the late-left path is scan/decode dominated (`left_scan ~7.3-7.8ms`) and the existing small accumulator only has six groups, so removing the tiny linear lookup does not move the needle. Reverted the code change and kept the note as a rejected experiment.
                            - Rechecked the three-key dictionary local slot accumulation default after scratch reuse. Latest A/B showed default-on was not stable: `three_key_expression_group_no_order` focused run was slower (`22.4ms` on vs `20.8ms` off), and full generic was better with it disabled (`Dodam=0.207s`, `DuckDB=0.356s`, `0.581x`). Reverted the rule to opt-in via `DODAM_ENABLE_COALESCE_DICTIONARY_SELECTED_LOCAL_ACCUMULATE=1`; keep the implementation and scratch reuse for future cost-model gating, but do not enable it by default.
                            - Rechecked SF=10 low-selectivity three-key with `DODAM_ENABLE_FUSED_SELECTED_I64_PAGE_DECODER=1` plus `DODAM_ENABLE_COALESCE_DICTIONARY_SELECTED_LOCAL_ACCUMULATE=1`. Focused runs can reach near parity (`Dodam ~27.4ms`, DuckDB `~27.1ms`), but the full SF=10 generic suite did not retain the win (`low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` still around `~38ms` in that run). This points to cache/IO/order sensitivity, so both knobs remain opt-in until the selected payload path has a real cost model that accounts for row-group spread and cross-query cache effects.
                            - Rechecked SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_scrambled`. The default primitive streaming top-k path was better than the opt-ins in this run (`~43ms` default vs `~62ms` with fused selected page reader and `~60ms` with selected payload). The profile shows all 49 row groups and 6M rows are scanned; selected payload rereads scattered rows and is not a win for scrambled input. Keep selected-payload/fused-page top-k paths opt-in until the cost model can recognize scattered top-k winners.
                            - Forced top-k selected payload to accept full row-group spread on SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` by raising the spread thresholds. It still regressed (`~43.1ms` vs DuckDB `~32.7ms`) even though the first pass read only key/filter columns (`column_read_ms` around `~88ms + ~160ms`). This confirms that for scrambled top-k the second payload pass is not a structural win; the default full-projection streaming top-k remains the better general rule.
                            - Added generic late-materialization policy profiling plus fused selected aggregate auto decision profiling. On SF=10 `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order`, fused selected is rejected because the qualifying rows span all row groups (`row_groups=49/49`, estimated selectivity `~1%`), while the generic late-materialization sample accepts because estimated IO saving is high (`~76%`). This explains why selected second-pass ideas remain unstable: the selected rows are sparse but spread across the full file.
                            - Rechecked `DODAM_ENABLE_COALESCE_DICTIONARY_SELECTED_LOCAL_ACCUMULATE=1` on SF=10 after the profiling change. Focused `three_key_expression_group_no_order` can improve a lot in one run (`~39.5ms` vs default runs around `~47-52ms`), but the full generic suite stayed essentially flat/slightly worse (`~0.644s` opt-in vs `~0.634s` default in this run). Keep it opt-in; the next general rule needs to account for selectivity, group cardinality, and cross-query cache effects rather than blindly enabling local accumulation.
                            - Generalized dictionary selected local accumulation with an automatic density gate instead of keeping it purely query/env opt-in. The rule accepts only when selected rows are large enough and rows-per-slot is high enough, so sparse selected rows spread across many slots avoid the local scratch-table cost. SF=10 generic improved in this run from `Dodam=0.652s` with the auto gate disabled to `Dodam=0.628s` with the gate enabled (`DuckDB ~0.74-0.76s` in both runs). Focused `three_key_expression_group_no_order` also improved slightly versus the disabled gate (`~49.5ms` vs `~50.9ms`), while low-selectivity three-key stayed acceptable in this noisy run.
                            - Made primitive top-k selected-payload auto selection default-on, but guarded it with default row-group spread gating and a minimum missing-payload-column threshold. This keeps the "avoid payload reads" direction without paying second-pass/precheck overhead for cases with only one omitted payload column. On SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_scrambled`, the auto path now rejects immediately with `decision=payload-columns` for one missing payload column; full generic stayed favorable in this run (`Dodam=0.533s`, DuckDB `0.639s`, `0.833x`). Also made the primitive top-k fused threshold filter default-on so heap candidates are filtered at the block scan stage after the heap is full.
                            - Relaxed fused dictionary selected aggregate auto gating for very low estimated selectivity. The old rule reused selected-payload row-group spread rejection, but fused aggregate is a one-pass typed selected fold, not a scattered second payload pass, so low-selectivity predicates can still win even when every row group may match. Added a `spread-override` threshold (`2%` default). SF=10 full generic improved in this run from `Dodam=0.654s` with the override disabled to `0.620s` enabled; focused `low_selectivity_three_key_expression_group_no_order` improved from about `40.5ms` disabled to `32.5ms` enabled in the repeated run.
                            - Revisited SF=10 `union_all_order_limit_scrambled` for payload avoidance. Fixed the primitive top-k selected-payload auto path so accepted auto decisions actually retain row references for the final payload read; previously row refs were only allocated for explicit env-enabled selected payload. Added an i32-filter/i64-sort block-max skip for thresholded top-k and made it default-on to keep the top-k path moving toward block/vector execution. The latest focused run improved versus disabling it (`~56.1ms` default-on vs `~58.4ms` disabled), and full generic stayed favorable (`Dodam=0.554s`, DuckDB `0.670s`, `0.826x`).
