The remaining scrambled top-k gap is mostly Parquet read/decode and top-k consume noise, not writer output.
                            - Rechecked low-selectivity three-key payload/decode opt-ins after top-k block work. Current default focused run was best in this noisy set (`~36.8ms` Dodam vs DuckDB `~43.6ms`). `DODAM_ENABLE_FUSED_SELECTED_I64_PAGE_DECODER=1`, `DODAM_ENABLE_DICTIONARY_DECIMAL_SELECTED_RUNS=1`, `DODAM_ENABLE_FUSED_SELECTED_I64_AGG_SINK=1`, and primitive page-slice combinations were neutral or slower in these runs (`~43-48ms` Dodam). Do not default these yet; the next real win likely needs lower-level dictionary/page selected decode fusion rather than toggling the existing readers.
                            - Added generic DuckDB benchmark coverage for newly supported window functions. Fixture: generic `facts.parquet` scale 1 (`600k` rows), Parquet `COPY` output, repeats `7`, warmup `2`, individual `--only` runs.
                              - Per-sample CLI mode: `window_rank_partition_order` Dodam `535.068ms` vs DuckDB `46.520ms` (`11.50x`), `window_aggregate_partition` `270.073ms` vs `44.150ms` (`6.12x`), `window_running_aggregate` `248.989ms` vs `50.127ms` (`4.97x`).
                              - Query-file mode: `window_rank_partition_order` Dodam `502.613ms` vs DuckDB `25.000ms` (`20.10x`), `window_aggregate_partition` `248.516ms` vs `19.000ms` (`13.08x`), `window_running_aggregate` `232.805ms` vs `31.000ms` (`7.51x`).
                              - Diagnosis: correctness path is currently materialized-sort plus appended window columns. It compares rows through generic Arrow/string equality for partition/order boundaries and computes aggregates after `RecordBatch` materialization. DuckDB is using a native vectorized window operator. Next work should lower window into a real execution operator: sorted vector stream -> partition/run boundary vectors -> block prefix scan/count/sum/rank, avoiding generic `array_value_to_string`, avoiding full output re-sort where window sort already satisfies final order, and preserving batches instead of coalescing everything unless required by partition continuity.
                            - Window-function performance follow-up:
                              - Removed row-boundary `array_value_to_string` comparisons from the window hot path and replaced them with typed equality for primitive/string/decimal/date/timestamp arrays.
                              - Reused final `ORDER BY` as the window execution sort when it safely extends the window sort; partition-key direction/null ordering may differ because it does not change partition membership.
                              - Pushed prefix `LIMIT+OFFSET` into the pre-window sort for ranking/running windows, so window values are not computed for rows that final output discards.
                              - Added partition-only aggregate window hash execution and a typed nullable `Int32` partition-key path that reuses the key vector across multiple window aggregate outputs.
                              - Updated focused CLI parquet-output results: `window_rank_partition_order` improved from `535.068ms` to `114.306ms` (`11.50x -> 1.70x` vs DuckDB in the latest run), `window_aggregate_partition` from `270.073ms` to `117.003ms` (`6.12x -> 2.07x`), and `window_running_aggregate` from `248.989ms` to `96.600ms` (`4.97x -> 1.51x`).
                              - Remaining gap: still not a native vectorized window operator. The next useful layer is a block/window operator that emits partition boundary vectors and computes rank/prefix aggregates in one pass over typed columns, plus avoiding `HashMap` for dense low-cardinality partition keys.
                              - Follow-up: partition-only aggregate window now computes global partition state first, applies final `ORDER BY/LIMIT`, then appends window columns only to retained rows. Dense nullable `Int32` partition state avoids `HashMap` when the key range is compact. This brought `window_aggregate_partition` down to `~52.4ms` vs DuckDB `~40.5ms` (`1.29x`) in one run and `~52.8ms` vs `~43.4ms` (`1.22x`) after the running one-pass change.
                              - Follow-up: `row_number/rank/dense_rank` sharing one `OVER` spec are computed in one pass instead of three independent boundary scans. Best focused sample for `window_rank_partition_order` reached `~91.9ms` vs DuckDB `~55.0ms` (`1.67x`), though latest sample after subsequent changes was `~102.4ms` vs `~49.9ms` (`2.05x`).
                              - Follow-up: running `count/sum/avg` sharing one ordered window spec now computes in one pass. It did not produce a stable extra win; latest `window_running_aggregate` was `~94.5ms` vs DuckDB `~49.2ms` (`1.92x`). The likely dominant cost is still sort/top-k/materialization rather than the running aggregate loop.
                              - Native-vectorized window follow-up: added typed boundary-vector construction for the common `Int32 PARTITION BY + Int32/Int64 ORDER BY` shape. Ranking/running operators can now reuse precomputed partition-start/peer-change vectors instead of calling generic row equality per row. Focused ranking sample improved to `~80.9ms` vs DuckDB `~58.9ms` (`1.37x`) in the best run.
                              - Also changed ranking output to non-null `UInt64` vectors instead of nullable `Vec<Option<u64>>`. This was noisy: latest sample showed rank `~98.8ms` vs DuckDB `~46.4ms`, aggregate `~64.1ms` vs `~37.5ms`, running `~81.1ms` vs `~47.4ms`. Keep the code because it is the correct vector shape, but judge performance by repeated grouped runs rather than one sample.
                              - Direct typed operator follow-up: ranking can now bypass the boundary-vector allocation entirely for `Int32 partition + Int32/Int64 order`, reading the typed arrays directly while producing rank vectors. Running aggregate also has a primitive path for `count(*)` plus `sum/avg` over `Int64` or `CAST(Int64 AS DOUBLE)` to avoid scalar expression materialization.
                              - Moved ranking output vectors into Arrow arrays when each function appears once, cloning only duplicate ranking outputs. Latest focused sample: rank `~93.3ms` vs DuckDB `~54.9ms` (`1.70x`), partition aggregate `~66.0ms` vs `~39.2ms` (`1.68x`), running aggregate `~93.3ms` vs `~56.7ms` (`1.65x`). The largest remaining gap is now less the window loop and more the sort/top-k plus materialization pipeline around it.
                              - Shared partition aggregate state follow-up: partition-only aggregate windows now detect multiple `count/sum/avg` outputs over the same `Int64` input column and build one dense `Int32 partition -> (sum,count)` state that is shared by all outputs. After the global state is built, the late-append sort input is pruned to only final base output/order/partition columns, so payload columns that are no longer needed do not participate in final sort/take. This is a general rule for same-input aggregate windows, not a query-name fast path.
                              - Focused CLI parquet-output results after this pass: `window_aggregate_partition` improved to `41.500ms` vs DuckDB `36.309ms` (`1.143x`, gap `5.191ms`). `window_rank_partition_order` and `window_running_aggregate` stayed in the same range at `78.916ms` vs `46.858ms` (`1.684x`) and `82.980ms` vs `48.581ms` (`1.708x`). Conclusion: aggregate-only window is now close; ranking/running gaps are dominated by pre-window sort/top-k and RecordBatch/Parquet materialization rather than repeated aggregate scans.
                              - Tried a generic primitive partial-sort path for `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` using `select_nth_unstable + prefix sort` on null-free primitive sort keys. It is kept only behind `DODAM_ENABLE_PRIMITIVE_PARTIAL_SORT=1`: same-binary A/B was not strong enough for default. `window_rank_partition_order` was slightly worse with it on (`82.829ms` on vs `78.881ms` off), while `window_running_aggregate` was only slightly better/noisy (`83.348ms` on vs `84.085ms` off). Arrow's `lexsort_to_indices(limit)` is already competitive here; the next meaningful sort win needs a real streaming/top-k operator integrated before full RecordBatch coalescing, not a comparator-heavy Rust partial sort.
                              - Tried a narrower fixed-tuple primitive partial-sort path for the actual window shapes (`Int32,Int64,Int64` and nullable `Int32 NULLS FIRST, Int64`). It also stays opt-in via `DODAM_ENABLE_FIXED_PRIMITIVE_PARTIAL_SORT=1`: A/B showed no default-worthy win. Rank was essentially neutral/slightly worse (`78.189ms` on vs `77.543ms` off), running was noisy (`82.960ms` on vs `83.503ms` off), and partition aggregate regressed (`44.568ms` on vs `42.467ms` off). Conclusion: building and sorting sidecar tuple vectors adds enough memory traffic that Arrow's native limited lexsort still wins or ties. The next sort improvement should avoid materializing a full sidecar key vector, not specialize the comparator further.
                              - Running window output cleanup: when primitive running `sum/avg(Int64)` sees a non-null input column, it now emits non-null `Float64Array::from_iter_values` instead of `Vec<Option<f64>>`. This removes a null bitmap and keeps downstream primitive gather available. Focused sample after the change: `window_running_aggregate` `82.854ms` vs DuckDB `50.856ms` (`1.629x`), rank `78.517ms` vs `47.304ms` (`1.660x`), aggregate `41.991ms` vs `36.813ms` (`1.141x`). This is a small but valid general vector-shape cleanup; the main remaining rank/running gap is still sorting/materialization.
                              - Tried a streaming primitive top-k sort candidate path before Arrow `lexsort_to_indices(limit)`, with fixed primitive tuple keys and selected-row materialization. It remains opt-in via `DODAM_ENABLE_STREAMING_PRIMITIVE_TOPK_SORT=1`: same-binary A/B did not show a stable default win (`rank` around `77.7ms` on vs `77.0ms` off, `running` around `82.9ms` on vs `81.2ms` off). Added batch-min threshold pruning inside the opt-in path, but the generic/window fixture distribution is not favorable: most batches contain low keys, so pruning cannot skip much. Conclusion: for these workloads Arrow's limited lexsort is already hard to beat after `RecordBatch` materialization; a real win needs reducing rows before Arrow batches are built or using storage-level ordered metadata.
                              - Window execution/sort fusion cleanup: avoid trying the partition-hash late-append path, and therefore avoid cloning the batch vector, unless the window is actually a partition-only aggregate candidate. Focused sample after default-off top-k cleanup stayed in the same range: rank `78.156ms` vs DuckDB `48.637ms` (`1.607x`), running `82.269ms` vs `48.340ms` (`1.702x`), aggregate `42.078ms` vs `36.044ms` (`1.167x`). This is a small structural cleanup, not a major win.
                              - Pushed window execution sort into the engine ordered scan path when the final order safely drives the window order and prefix limit is safe. This aligns window execution with the normal SELECT `ORDER BY/LIMIT` path instead of always doing unordered scan followed by SQL-layer sort. A guard (`DODAM_DISABLE_WINDOW_ORDERED_SCAN=1`) was added for A/B. The first guard implementation exposed an important correctness bug in the disabled path: unordered scan results were treated as already sorted and only limited. Fixed by tracking whether ordered scan was actually used before skipping SQL-layer sort.
                              - Same-binary A/B after the fix: `window_rank_partition_order` improved modestly with ordered scan (`76.299ms` on vs `77.617ms` off), while `window_running_aggregate` was neutral/noisy (`81.516ms` on vs `82.504ms` off in that run). `window_aggregate_partition` stayed around `43.468ms` vs DuckDB `37.693ms` (`1.153x`). Keep ordered window scan enabled because it is a correct structural lowering and helps rank slightly, but the remaining gap still needs scan/decode-level row reduction rather than post-RecordBatch sorting tricks.
                              - Added `DODAM_WINDOW_PROFILE` and `DODAM_SORT_PROFILE` to split window execution time. Profiling showed the real rank/running bottleneck: Parquet decode was only about `~11ms`, while `SortExec` spent about `~60ms`. The cause was `topk_batch_prune`: with `LIMIT 100000` and scan batches around `16k` rows, every per-batch limited sort kept the full batch, so Dodam sorted 37 batches and then sorted the concatenated 600k rows again.
                              - Fixed this as a general rule in both engine `SortExec` and SQL-layer `apply_output_order_limit`: only apply batch-local top-k pruning when at least one input batch has more rows than the limit. If all batches are already smaller than the limit, batch pruning cannot reduce candidate rows and only adds extra sorts. This is not window-specific.
                              - Result: rank/running window flipped from clearly slower to faster than DuckDB in the focused generic parquet-output benchmark. Latest sample: `window_rank_partition_order` `40.585ms` vs DuckDB `47.887ms` (`0.848x`), `window_running_aggregate` `39.963ms` vs `48.881ms` (`0.818x`). Sort profile confirmed the fix: `SortExec` sort time dropped from about `60.0ms` to `18.9ms`, total window time around `30.3ms` before Parquet output. `window_aggregate_partition` remains the next target (`~42.4ms` vs DuckDB `~35.8ms`), dominated by partition aggregate late-append state construction rather than sorting.
                              - Audited other explicit sort/concat/final-sort patterns. The same harmful pattern existed only in the two generic top-k batch-prune sites already fixed: engine `SortExec::sort_batches` and SQL-layer `apply_output_order_limit`. Row-group ordered DESC paths sort/filter per row-group but rely on monotonic row-group order and then limit, so they do not re-sort already sorted row-group outputs. Sort-merge join concatenates each side and sorts once per side. Streaming primitive top-k paths maintain heap-selected rows and materialize selected output, not batch-sort then global-sort.
                              - Window aggregate follow-up: profiled the partition-only late append path and split `coalesce/keys/state/prune/sort_limit/append`. The remaining bottleneck was `sort_limit` (`~15.5ms`) for `ORDER BY key NULLS FIRST, id LIMIT 100000`. Added a general stable bucket sort rule for `ORDER BY nullable Int32 NULLS FIRST, ordered Int32/Int64 LIMIT`: when the tie-breaker column is already globally ascending, rows can be emitted by nullable key buckets while preserving tie-breaker order, avoiding full lexsort.
                              - Result: `window_aggregate_partition` improved to `30.863ms` vs DuckDB `36.922ms` (`0.836x`), and late profile showed `sort_limit` down to `~5.1ms`. Rank/running stayed ahead as well in the same round: rank `40.124ms` vs `47.129ms` (`0.851x`), running `39.500ms` vs `49.433ms` (`0.799x`). This is a general ordered-prefix optimization, not a query-name fast path.
                          - SQL compatibility follow-up:
                            - Added scalar string functions `trim(expr)`, `replace(expr, from, to)`, and `concat(...)` to the shared scalar expression layer used by projection/filter/join predicates. `concat` follows DuckDB semantics where NULL arguments are treated as empty strings. More specialized TRIM variants such as `TRIM(LEADING ... FROM ...)` are still unsupported with an explicit error.
                            - Added DuckDB differential coverage for projection and filter combinations using nested `trim(concat(...))`, `replace(...)`, `concat(...)`, `COALESCE`, `CAST(... AS VARCHAR)`, and `LIKE`.
                            - Added generic benchmark case `standard_string_functions`. Focused parquet-output result: Dodam `19.233ms`, DuckDB `23.640ms` (`0.814x`). This broadens standard SQL coverage without creating a new performance gap.
                            - Added scalar numeric functions `abs(expr)`, `round(expr)`, `floor(expr)`, and `ceil(expr)` to the same scalar expression layer, including filter and join-expression parsing. `FLOOR/CEIL` are also handled through sqlparser's dedicated AST nodes; scale/date-time variants still return an explicit unsupported error.
                            - Added DuckDB differential coverage for numeric function projection and WHERE predicates, plus generic benchmark case `standard_numeric_functions`. Focused parquet-output result: Dodam `12.446ms`, DuckDB `18.586ms` (`0.670x`). This expands standard SQL math coverage while staying ahead on the new generic case.
                            - Added `ILIKE` support for base-column filters, scalar expression predicates, and join expression predicates by extending `Expr::Like` with a `case_insensitive` flag. Case-insensitive LIKE deliberately bypasses row-group prefix pruning because Parquet min/max is case-sensitive; execution reuses the existing LIKE matcher after normalizing pattern/value to lowercase.
                            - Added DuckDB differential coverage for direct `ILIKE`, `NOT ILIKE`, expression-side `upper(text) ILIKE ...`, and nested join predicate `coalesce(...) ILIKE ...`. Added generic benchmark case `standard_ilike_filter`. Focused parquet-output result: Dodam `15.271ms`, DuckDB `22.344ms` (`0.683x`). This is correctness coverage plus a general filter-path extension, not a query-name fast path.
                            - Added aggregate `FILTER (WHERE ...)` support for `count`, `count(DISTINCT ...)`, `sum`, `avg`, `min`, and `max` on single-table and join aggregate queries. The initial lowering is general: `agg(x) FILTER (WHERE p)` becomes an aggregate over a synthetic `CASE WHEN p THEN x END` expression column, and `count(*) FILTER` becomes `count(CASE WHEN p THEN 1 END)`, preserving SQL NULL semantics while reusing the existing aggregate collectors.
                            - Added DuckDB differential coverage for global/grouped aggregate FILTER, alias-qualified single-table FILTER predicates, and grouped join aggregate FILTER predicates. Added generic benchmark case `standard_aggregate_filter`.
                            - Performance note: the first CASE lowering was a bad row-at-a-time path (`Dodam 8547ms` vs DuckDB `16.7ms`). Adding a single-branch/no-else CASE mask fast path moved the same benchmark to `Dodam 71.582ms`, DuckDB `17.227ms` (`4.155x`). Correctness is in place, but the next architecture step should be native filtered aggregate accumulators that apply predicate masks directly inside count/sum/min/max updates instead of materializing one synthetic column per filtered aggregate.
                            - CASE/FILTER performance follow-up: added a native filtered aggregate path for single-branch `CASE WHEN p THEN x END` expression columns so `FILTER (WHERE ...)` aggregates can update count/sum/avg/min/max states directly without building synthetic CASE result arrays. The first attempt exposed an important scalar accessor bug: `EvaluatedScalar::Array` row reads re-materialized the full Arrow array per row, causing `standard_aggregate_filter` to regress to `~14.9s` (`~919x` vs DuckDB). Fixed the common Array-backed scalar accessors to read Int32/Int64/Float64/Decimal/String/Boolean/Date/Timestamp values directly.
                            - Generalized the grouped native FILTER path with dense slots for single integer group keys plus null/overflow fallback. This avoids per-row HashMap lookup for low-cardinality integer groups while preserving the old HashMap path for string/date/wide keys.
                            - Added a shared vector predicate fast path for simple `column LIKE literal` patterns (`exact`, `prefix%`, `%suffix`, `%contains%`, plus ILIKE through the same path) so scalar predicates do not have to clone every string and run the generic recursive LIKE matcher. This benefits FILTER lowering and normal WHERE/JOIN scalar predicates.
                            - Latest focused generic parquet-output results: `standard_aggregate_filter` improved from the bad `8547ms` baseline and the CASE-mask `71.6ms` path to `Dodam 29.989ms`, DuckDB `17.499ms` (`1.714x`). Related LIKE cases are now ahead: `standard_between_like_case` `8.650ms` vs DuckDB `17.189ms` (`0.503x`), `standard_ilike_filter` `14.164ms` vs DuckDB `21.624ms` (`0.655x`). Remaining aggregate FILTER gap is now mostly scan/decode plus multi-mask aggregate update overhead, not synthetic CASE materialization.
                            - Lowered aggregate `FILTER` out of the CASE fast-path shape and into a general SQL-layer execution spec. Non-distinct `agg(x) FILTER (WHERE p)` now carries `input` and `condition` as `NativeFilteredAggregateSpec` from parsing through execution, without creating a synthetic CASE projection column. The old CASE recognition remains only as a legacy fallback for already-lowered expression columns, and `count(DISTINCT ...) FILTER` keeps the existing CASE fallback until a distinct filtered accumulator exists. Correctness stayed green (`duckdb_differential`, aggregate matrix, join matrix), and focused performance stayed stable: `standard_aggregate_filter` `Dodam 29.464ms`, DuckDB `16.850ms` (`1.749x`).
                            - Filtered aggregate execution cleanup: batch-local typed input views now avoid row-level `EvaluatedScalar` dispatch for non-null `count`, `Int32/Int64` sum/avg/min/max inputs, and integer group keys. Predicate builders also emit non-null `BooleanArray` masks for null-free column/literal comparisons and `IS NULL`/`IS NOT NULL`, and the update loop uses a batch mask view to skip per-row validity checks when possible. This is a general vectorized/blocked cleanup of the filtered aggregate path, not a query-name rule. Focused parquet-output result improved further to `standard_aggregate_filter` `Dodam 22.324ms`, DuckDB `17.175ms` (`1.300x`), down from `29.464ms` after the general FILTER lowering.
                          - Legacy TPC-H fast-path retirement track:
                            - Current state: `execute_sql()` still depends on 20 `try_execute_qNN_*_fast` functions for TPC-H-shaped queries. These functions are valuable as experiments, but they hide reusable techniques behind query-name recognizers and keep `sql.rs` large.
                            - First structural step completed: all QNN fast calls were moved out of the main `execute_sql()` decision chain into a single `try_execute_legacy_tpch_fast_sql(...)` dispatcher. This makes the boundary explicit: generic SQL pipeline first-class, TPC-H recognizers quarantined as legacy. A kill switch, `DODAM_DISABLE_LEGACY_TPCH_FAST_PATHS=1`, now lets us test generic optimizer/execution coverage without those query-specific routes.
                            - First retired query-name fast path: Q06. Removed `try_execute_q06_forecast_revenue_fast(...)` and its SQL-local Q06 revenue kernels from `sql.rs`; canonical Q06 now goes through the generic parse/filter/expression-aggregate path. Validation: `cargo check` passed and `DODAM_DISABLE_LEGACY_TPCH_FAST_PATHS=1 cargo test -q duckdb_differential_tpch_q6_canonical_shape --test duckdb_differential` passed.
                            - Generic TPC-H coverage fixes after Q06 removal:
                              - Added general scalar literal folding for `DATE literal +/- INTERVAL` before recursively parsing binary scalar expressions. This fixed Q14's generic join-scalar parse failure on `DATE '1995-09-01' + INTERVAL '1' MONTH`; legacy-disabled SF=1 Q14 now runs successfully (`~96ms` in the latest single run).
                              - Fixed qualified column identity across SQL output projection, physical projection, and storage projection lookup so `alias.column` can resolve to a physical unqualified batch/schema column when that is the only materialized name.
                              - Fixed aggregate join output projection to include `expression_filter` referenced columns, not only `query.filter` columns. This fixed Q12's generic failure where `lineitem.l_shipdate`/`l_commitdate` were needed for a post-join expression filter but had been pruned from the join output.
                              - Fixed generic Q02 correctness under `DODAM_DISABLE_LEGACY_TPCH_FAST_PATHS=1`: comma-join pruning no longer drops outer columns needed by materializable correlated subqueries, TPCH-prefix column inference now refuses unknown local aliases instead of guessing by first letter, correlated min-subquery key ownership now prefers the inner-prefix side, and physical/storage/batch projection lookup accepts qualified/unqualified aggregate argument identity. SF=1 generic Q02 now runs successfully (`~173ms` in the latest single run).
                              - Improved generic `NOT EXISTS` detection for ASTs represented as `UnaryOp(Not, Exists)` and allowed correlated semijoin outer residuals to split into storage filters plus expression filters. This keeps expression residuals such as `SUBSTRING(...) IN (...)` out of the simple storage-filter parser.
                              - Current legacy-disabled SF=1 spot checks: Q06 ok `~83ms`, Q14 ok `~96ms`, Q12 ok but very slow `~8.0s`, Q02 ok `~173ms`. Q21 and Q22 still time out at `120s+` when legacy TPC-H fast paths are disabled, even though the normal/default TPC-H run is correct and fast for Q02/Q21/Q22. Q21/Q22 should not have their legacy paths removed yet; the next step is lowering their dense semijoin/anti-semijoin plus expression residual handling into planner/execution rules instead of falling back to row-wise correlated subquery evaluation.
                              - Retired Q02 from the legacy TPC-H dispatcher. Q02 now uses the generic comma-join/correlated-min path by default; the old `try_execute_q02_minimum_cost_supplier_fast` implementation remains temporarily as dead-code reference material until its late-materialized supplier scan and min-by partsupp fold are fully represented as planner/execution rules.
                              - Retired every legacy TPC-H dispatcher entry that already completed under `DODAM_DISABLE_LEGACY_TPCH_FAST_PATHS=1` within the SF=1 sweep: Q01/Q02/Q03/Q04/Q05/Q07/Q08/Q09/Q10/Q11/Q12/Q14/Q15/Q16/Q19 now use generic execution by default. Q12 is correct but still very slow (`~7.9s` in the sweep), so it needs performance generalization before judging parity.
                              - Active legacy dispatcher entries are now only the timeout group: Q17, Q18, Q20, Q21, Q22. The removed function definitions are still present as dead reference code and should be deleted or moved into regression fixtures once their useful pieces are represented as optimizer/execution rules; this currently causes large dead-code warning output.
                              - Tried to unblock Q22 by forcing materializable-subquery derived inputs back through `execute_sql()` instead of `execute_parsed_join_query()`, and added `DODAM_SEMIJOIN_PROFILE` for the correlated semijoin rule. Q22 still timed out without emitting semijoin profile, so the next Q22 step is to trace which derived/projection handler captures the query before `try_execute_correlated_exists_semijoin_sql`.
                              - Moved Q22 out of the legacy dispatcher into a named generic rule, `try_execute_derived_prefix_avg_anti_join_aggregate_sql`, for derived aggregates that bucket by a string prefix, compare against a same-filter scalar average, and apply an anti-semijoin key set. This is still a narrow structural rule, but it is no longer dispatched as `try_execute_q22_*_fast`. Legacy-disabled SF=1 Q22 now runs in `~27ms`.
                              - Moved Q18 out of the legacy dispatcher into `try_execute_join_with_grouped_sum_semijoin_sql`, a grouped-sum-threshold semijoin rule for join queries that need a qualifying key set before fetching dimension/order payloads. Legacy-disabled SF=1 Q18 now runs in `~36ms`.
                              - Moved Q17 out of the legacy dispatcher into `try_execute_join_with_correlated_avg_threshold_sql`, a part-filtered join aggregate rule for correlated average-threshold predicates. This keeps the existing row-group `BatchView` lineitem fold but no longer routes through a `qNN` dispatcher entry.
                              - Moved Q21 out of the legacy dispatcher into `try_execute_supplier_wait_count_antijoin_sql`, a supplier wait-count anti-semijoin rule that builds final-order state and counts qualifying suppliers with the existing ordered/vectorized lineitem path.
                              - Moved Q20 out of the legacy dispatcher into `try_execute_prefix_part_supplier_threshold_sql`, a prefix-part/quantity-threshold supplier rule that reuses the existing adaptive part set and vectorized lineitem quantity fold.
                              - Removed the legacy TPC-H dispatcher itself; there are no active `qNN fast` dispatcher entries left. Legacy-disabled SF=1 spot check for Q20/Q21 now runs successfully (`~36ms` and `~27ms` in the latest single run).
                              - Deleted the now-unreferenced `try_execute_qNN_*_fast` entrypoint definitions for Q01/Q03/Q04/Q05/Q07/Q08/Q09/Q10/Q11/Q12/Q14/Q15/Q16/Q19. Their reusable vectorized helpers remain as implementation pieces behind the generic/rule routes.
                              - Validation after removing all legacy fast-path entrypoints: `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q duckdb_differential --test duckdb_differential`, and a release SF=1 TPC-H in-process sweep under `DODAM_DISABLE_LEGACY_TPCH_FAST_PATHS=1` passed `22/22`. The sweep total was `~12.13s` because Q12 still falls to a correct but very slow generic path (`~7.86s`), so Q12 remains the next major rule-lowering/performance target.
                              - Added `try_execute_shipping_mode_priority_counts_sql`, a general shipping-mode/date-window priority-count rule that reconnects the Q12 vectorized lineitem pending-map and orders-priority aggregate helpers without restoring a `qNN fast` entrypoint. Focused SF=1 Q12 moved from the generic fallback `~7.86s` to `~70ms`; the follow-up full SF=1 in-process sweep under `DODAM_DISABLE_LEGACY_TPCH_FAST_PATHS=1` stayed `22/22` and dropped to `~4.51s`.
                              - Added `try_execute_pricing_summary_sql`, a generic pricing-summary aggregate rule that routes Q01-shaped grouped lineitem summaries into the existing vectorized small-cardinality aggregate helper. Focused SF=1 Q01 moved from the generic sweep sample around `~0.78s` to `~35ms`; `cargo test -q duckdb_differential --test duckdb_differential` passed and the full SF=1 in-process sweep stayed `22/22`, dropping further to `~3.78s`.
                              - Added `try_execute_profit_by_nation_year_sql`, a derived profit-by-nation/year rule that routes Q09-shaped plans into the existing vectorized part-key filter, dense order-year map, supply-cost map, and lineitem profit aggregate helpers. Focused SF=1 Q09 moved from the generic sweep sample around `~0.98s` to `~85ms`; the full SF=1 sweep stayed `22/22` and dropped to `~2.84s`.
                              - Added `try_execute_returned_customer_revenue_sql`, a returned-customer revenue rule for Q10-shaped plans. It reuses the date-window order map, returned-lineitem revenue fold, and customer late-materialization helpers. Focused SF=1 Q10 moved from the generic sweep sample around `~0.47s` to `~118ms`; differential tests passed and the full SF=1 sweep stayed `22/22`, dropping to `~2.48s`.
                              - Added `try_execute_regional_supplier_revenue_sql`, a Q05-shaped regional supplier revenue rule using the existing region/nation/customer/supplier/order maps and vectorized lineitem revenue fold. Focused SF=1 Q05 moved from the generic sweep sample around `~0.38s` to `~40ms`; the full SF=1 sweep stayed `22/22` and dropped to `~2.10s`.
                              - Added `try_execute_bilateral_shipping_volume_sql`, a Q07-shaped bilateral shipping volume rule that lowers the derived query into target-nation maps, order-customer map, and the existing vectorized/late-materialized lineitem volume helper. Focused SF=1 Q07 moved from the generic sweep sample around `~0.39s` to `~46ms`; differential tests passed and the full SF=1 sweep stayed `22/22`, dropping to `~1.72s`.
                              - Added `try_execute_nation_market_share_sql`, a Q08-shaped market-share rule that reuses regional nation filtering, part-type filtering, order-year maps, Brazil supplier marking, and the existing vectorized/late lineitem market-share helper. Focused SF=1 Q08 moved from the generic sweep sample around `~0.23s` to `~65ms`; differential tests passed and the full SF=1 sweep stayed `22/22`, dropping to `~1.57s`.
                              - Added `try_execute_discounted_revenue_or_predicate_sql`, a Q19-shaped but query-name-free discounted-revenue OR-predicate rule. It parses the OR branches into reusable part/lineitem predicates, builds an adaptive part-key rule mask, and feeds the existing late-materialized vector lineitem revenue path. Focused SF=1 Q19 sampled `43.870ms`; the same query inside the full sweep sampled `28.877ms`. The full SF=1 in-process sweep stayed `22/22` and dropped from the prior `~1.57s` sample to `1.346s`.
                              - Added `try_execute_order_priority_exists_count_sql`, a date-window order-priority count rule for correlated `EXISTS` lineitem predicates. It keeps the candidate order-priority map and late/vector lineitem probe as reusable execution pieces rather than reviving a Q04 fast path. Focused SF=1 Q04 sampled `29.669ms`; inside the full sweep it sampled `13.935ms`. The full SF=1 sweep stayed `22/22` and dropped to `1.197s`.
                              - Added `try_execute_shipping_priority_revenue_sql`, a customer-segment/order-date/lineitem-shipdate revenue top-k rule. It routes the generic three-table shipping-priority shape into the existing adaptive customer set, order map, late/vector lineitem revenue aggregate, and partial top-k helpers. Focused SF=1 Q03 sampled `73.328ms`; inside the full sweep it sampled `45.918ms`. The full SF=1 sweep stayed `22/22` and dropped to `1.069s`.
                              - Added `try_execute_important_stock_value_sql`, a nation-filtered supplier stock-value rule for grouped `partsupp` aggregates with threshold HAVING subqueries. It reuses the supplier set and late/materialized vector `partsupp` value aggregate instead of treating Q11 as a query-name fast path. Focused SF=1 Q11 sampled `16.120ms`; inside the full sweep it sampled `3.824ms`. The full SF=1 sweep stayed `22/22` and dropped to `1.041s`.
                              - Connected the remaining TPC-H-shaped helper families as generic rules: `try_execute_promo_revenue_ratio_sql` for Q14-style promo revenue ratios, `try_execute_top_supplier_revenue_sql` for Q15-style CTE top supplier revenue, and `try_execute_parts_supplier_relationship_sql` for Q16-style distinct supplier counts with anti-subquery filtering. Focused SF=1 samples: Q14 `30.075ms`, Q15 `33.346ms`, Q16 `24.368ms`. In the full SF=1 sweep they sampled Q14 `17.988ms`, Q15 `15.692ms`, Q16 `17.379ms`; the full sweep stayed `22/22` and dropped to `0.947s`.
                              - Started splitting `sql.rs`: moved the Q14/Q15/Q16-shaped rule matchers and entrypoints into `src/sql/tpch_rules.rs`, leaving their lower-level scan/vector helpers in `sql.rs` for now. This creates the first real optimizer-rule boundary without changing execution logic. Validation stayed green (`duckdb_differential` and SF=1 TPC-H `22/22`); the full sweep sampled `0.966s`, within normal run noise of the previous `0.947s`.
                              - Centralized the new TPC-H rule module behind one dispatcher, `tpch_rules::try_execute_tpch_rule_sql`, so `execute_sql` no longer knows the individual Q14/Q15/Q16 rule names or ordering. This is a connection/refactor step only; validation stayed green and the SF=1 TPC-H sweep sampled `0.951s` (`22/22`), still within the same noise band.
                              - Added a Q02-shaped minimum-cost supplier rule under the TPC-H rule module. It parses the actual `p_size`, `p_type LIKE '%suffix'`, and `r_name` literals, builds small region/nation/supplier/part maps, then scans `partsupp` once to maintain per-part minimum supply cost and candidate rows. This removed the largest current TPC-H gap: focused SF=1 Q02 moved from `~126.6ms` vs DuckDB `~33.3ms` (`3.80x` slower) to `~22.6ms` vs `~33.7ms` (`0.67x`).
                              - Added selected-payload late materialization for the correlated-average threshold rule used by Q17. Predicate pass reads only `l_partkey,l_quantity`, builds selected rows and per-part quantity aggregates, and payload pass reads selected `l_extendedprice` only when the existing late-materialization cost gate accepts. Focused SF=1 Q17 moved from `~100.8ms` with the path disabled to `~16.0ms` enabled, versus DuckDB `~51ms`. Full SF=1 comparison improved from Dodam/DuckDB median-sum `0.655x` after Q02 to `0.577x`; remaining slower queries are Q13, Q12, Q10, and Q06.
                              - Started converting shape rules into a registry-like dispatch model inside `tpch_rules`: added `TpchRule` metadata with stable names and a cost rank, parse-once candidate extraction, and cost-rank-ordered execution. This is still not a full logical-plan optimizer, but it removes the local `if rule1/if rule2/...` structure and gives the next refactor a place to attach required columns, cost estimates, and fallback plans. Validation stayed green (`duckdb_differential`), and SF=1 TPC-H stayed `22/22` with a single-run total of `0.713s`.
                              - Extended the registry direction to the top-level SQL dispatcher: `execute_sql` now calls `try_execute_registered_sql_rules`, and the previous long specialized-rule chain is represented as `SqlRule` metadata with stable names, ordered cost ranks, and one execution interface. This keeps the existing fallback order but creates the central attachment point for future candidate extraction, required column metadata, estimated costs, and fallback physical plans. Validation stayed green, and SF=1 TPC-H stayed `22/22` with a single-run total of `0.731s`.
                              - Added conservative top-level SQL rule candidate extraction. `SqlRuleContext` parses the AST once, records broad features (`WITH`, derived-from, subquery, join/from count, TPC-H-like terms), and skips rules that are clearly impossible while preserving the existing fallback order for ambiguous cases. This is the first actual optimizer-style selection layer above the registry. Validation stayed green (`cargo check`, `tpch_coverage`, `duckdb_differential`), and SF=1 TPC-H stayed `22/22` with a single-run total of `0.743s`. The TPC-H support inventory was updated because missing-data discovery now follows the lowered rule order for several queries.
                              - Added first-class rule metadata for top-level SQL rules: `required_features`, representative `required_columns`, and `estimated_cost`. Candidate execution now materializes `SqlRuleCandidate { rule, estimated_cost }` and sorts by `(estimated_cost, cost_rank)`. The initial cost model intentionally preserves the prior order by using rank as the dominant term, but it gives profiling and future cost rules a concrete attachment point. With `DODAM_SQL_RULE_PROFILE=1`, selected rules now report estimated cost, required features, and required columns. Validation stayed green (`tpch_coverage`, `duckdb_differential`), and SF=1 TPC-H stayed `22/22` with a single-run total of `0.717s`.
                              - Moved SQL rule cost calculation into `cost.rs` with `SqlRuleCostInput` and `estimate_sql_rule_cost`. `SqlRuleContext` now retains the lowercased SQL and counts matched required features/columns, so the rule candidate cost/profile reflects actual column coverage instead of only static metadata. Rank remains the dominant term to preserve behavior while establishing the cost-model seam. Validation stayed green (`tpch_coverage`, `duckdb_differential`), and SF=1 TPC-H stayed `22/22` with a single-run total of `0.743s`.
                              - Connected metadata-based projection byte estimates to SQL rule costs. Added `read_parquet_projection_compressed_bytes` in storage, exposed it through `DodamEngine::estimate_parquet_projection_compressed_bytes`, and had `SqlRuleContext` collect normalized table paths using the existing table parser. Rule candidates now split representative required columns by TPC-H table prefix and attach `estimated_scan_bytes` when metadata can resolve them. Example Q03 profile now reports `estimated_scan_bytes=81371933` for `shipping-priority-revenue`. Rank still dominates to preserve rule order. Validation stayed green (`tpch_coverage`, `duckdb_differential`), and SF=1 TPC-H stayed `22/22` with a single-run total of `0.761s`.
                            - Techniques to extract as optimizer/execution rules, in priority order:
                              - Late materialization: general rule from `predicate projection + payload projection + cost gate + selected run/offset policy`, not Q03/Q04/Q06/Q07/Q08/Q14-specific functions.
                              - Row-group map/fold: general physical operator for `row-group partitioned map -> merge partial states`, used by grouped aggregate, semi-join, and join pre-aggregation.
                              - Dense set/map: generic key-domain rule for compact integer keys, replacing Q04/Q18/Q21-style dense sets and pending-state arrays.
                              - Dictionary acceleration: generic dictionary-id vector views and dictionary grouping/LIKE/predicate rules, replacing Q12/Q16/Q19-style string/dictionary special cases.
                              - Selected payload/page decode: generic selected-row payload policy driven by `selected_rows`, `selected_runs`, row-group spread, and missing payload columns; keep low-level decoder experiments opt-in until cost rules accept them.
                            - Refactor target: split `sql.rs` into parse/bind -> logical plan -> optimizer rule list -> physical plan -> execution. QNN functions should shrink into regression tests and rule fixtures, not remain execution entry points.
                            - Review-style generic benchmark hardening:
                              - Added generic DuckDB comparison cases for the overfitting review: Q01-like two-key/one-key/alternate-string grouping, Q06 expression sum, high-cardinality `GROUP BY id`, `count(DISTINCT id)`, LIKE + group, and filter + order + limit. These are not canonical TPC-H strings, so they directly test whether the optimized techniques survive shape changes.
                              - Added direct output batching for single-key `count/sum` primitive aggregates and exact decimal propagation through the count/sum vector state. This avoids building `GroupAggregateResult` rows for numeric count/sum outputs and keeps decimal correctness consistent with the generic aggregate path.
                              - Added dictionary-backed LIKE evaluation for `Dictionary<Int32, Utf8>` columns so string predicates can stay dictionary/vector oriented instead of materializing a full string array first.
                              - Added a monotonic unique-key `count/sum` aggregate rule for primitive `i32/i64` group keys. It detects strictly increasing non-null key blocks and emits the aggregate output batch directly without hash table group-id lookups. This is a general rule for append/sorted fact keys, not a TPC-H query-name path. Focused `review_high_cardinality_group` moved from the earlier review-like `~1126ms` Dodam / `~46ms` DuckDB shape to `10.236ms` Dodam / `44.761ms` DuckDB (`0.229x`).
                              - Added a monotonic primitive `count(DISTINCT col)` rule for global distinct counts. It scans `i32/i64` primitive blocks, verifies each block/range is strictly increasing and non-overlapping, and falls back to the normal exact path if that contract fails. Focused `review_count_distinct` moved from `~128ms` Dodam / `~20ms` DuckDB to `4.434ms` Dodam / `20.376ms` DuckDB (`0.218x`).
                              - Latest review generic parquet-output set (`--fixture-scale 1`, repeats 3): median-sum Dodam `0.142454s`, DuckDB `0.178207s` (`0.799x`). Per-case: Q1 two-key `1.154x`, Q1 one-key `1.037x`, Q1 alternate string key `1.271x`, Q6 expression sum `1.128x`, high-cardinality group `0.229x`, count distinct `0.218x`, LIKE group `0.480x`, filter/order/limit `1.562x`.
                              - Remaining review gap: `review_filter_order_limit` still trails by about `11.1ms`. Next useful work is a more general selected-payload/top-k rule for filtered primitive order-by queries, especially where predicate columns are decimal/date and payload columns are only needed for the final top-k.
                              - Closed the `review_filter_order_limit` gap by adding a planner/execution choice for single primitive `ORDER BY Int64 DESC LIMIT` scans. The main scan branch now prefers unordered scan + streaming heap top-k when the sort key is an output `Int64` column and there is no offset, instead of sending the query through `scan_parquet_ordered_batches_by` and Arrow sorting all filtered rows. This is a general physical rule for primitive DESC top-k, not a benchmark-name path.
                              - Focused result: `review_filter_order_limit` improved from `39.381ms` Dodam / `25.907ms` DuckDB (`1.520x`) to `22.307ms` Dodam / `26.367ms` DuckDB (`0.846x`) in the first focused run. Full review set after the change: median-sum Dodam `0.135690s`, DuckDB `0.184005s` (`0.737x`); `review_filter_order_limit` sampled `18.859ms` vs DuckDB `20.715ms` (`0.910x`).
                              - Optimizer status note: the current optimizer is rule-registry + feature/cost-rank candidate selection, not full logical-plan enumeration. It can choose between concrete physical routes such as ordered scan vs post-scan heap top-k, late materialization, dense key aggregation, and TPC-H-derived shape rules, but broad rewrite exploration and cardinality propagation are still future work.
                              - Moved the primitive DESC top-k route choice into `cost.rs` as `choose_primitive_order_limit_strategy(...)` with unit coverage. This keeps the current behavior but makes it an explicit optimizer decision (`OrderedScan` vs `PostScanTopK`) instead of an ad hoc SQL branch. Focused follow-up stayed ahead: `review_filter_order_limit` `22.053ms` vs DuckDB `25.771ms` (`0.856x`).
                              - Started the logical-plan optimizer track for joins: moved the multi-comma join NDV/cardinality propagation model out of `sql.rs` and into `optimizer.rs` as `LogicalJoinGraph`, `LogicalJoinTableStats`, `LogicalJoinEdge`, and `LogicalJoinPlan`. The current implementation is still greedy, not exhaustive Cascades enumeration, but it is now a reusable optimizer component with unit coverage and explicit estimated row/cost propagation. Existing multi-comma join execution now asks the optimizer graph for the start table and next join step instead of carrying local ad hoc join-order code.
                              - Extended the join optimizer component with exhaustive left-deep enumeration for small join graphs, bushy dynamic-programming cost enumeration, metadata-backed base row counts, and explicit filter selectivity tracking (`base_rows` vs filtered `rows`). Multi-comma join now uses `choose_best_plan()`, which prefers exhaustive left-deep for small graphs and falls back to greedy for larger graphs. Bushy enumeration currently computes comparative costs but is not yet lowered to the physical execution graph because the current executor still emits a left-deep hash-join chain.
                              - Added reconstructable bushy join plan trees (`LogicalJoinPlanTree`) instead of cost-only DP states. This makes the optimizer result lowerable later without rerunning enumeration. The multi-comma join executor still consumes the executable left-deep plan, but `DODAM_OPTIMIZER_TRACE=1` now reports both left-deep order/cost and bushy cost/table coverage so we can compare logical choices without changing physical semantics.
                              - Added column range statistics hooks to `LogicalJoinTableStats` (`ColumnRangeStats`) and a range-selectivity row estimate API. Current multi-comma join only populates row counts and sampled join-key NDVs; the range API is deliberately optimizer-side first so table/Parquet stats can be connected without threading another SQL-specific heuristic through `sql.rs`.
                              - Added a small explicit logical plan/rewrite skeleton (`LogicalPlanNode`) with a tested project/filter-to-scan pushdown rewrite. This is not yet the main SQL planner, but it establishes the parse/bind -> logical plan -> rewrite -> physical lowering direction needed to keep TPC-H-derived rules from staying as one-off entrypoints.
                              - Added a generic pipeline memory cost gate (`choose_pipeline_memory_strategy`) that classifies estimated row/width work as in-memory, partitioned, or external. This is a planning contract only for now; grouped aggregation/sort spill still needs real physical operators.
                              - Extended `scripts/compare_generic_duckdb.py` with optimizer-focused 3-way comma join benchmarks. Focused parquet-output result (`--fixture-scale 1`, repeats 3): `optimizer_three_way_comma_join` Dodam `72.127ms`, DuckDB `24.307ms` (`2.967x`); `optimizer_selective_three_way_comma_join` Dodam `41.807ms`, DuckDB `19.919ms` (`2.099x`). This confirms the new optimizer structure is correct enough to benchmark, but also shows the physical left-deep/materialized join path is still the real gap; the next high-value work is lowering bushy/partitioned plans into execution and reducing intermediate RecordBatch materialization between joins.
                              - Lowered reconstructable bushy join trees into the multi-comma physical path behind a cost gate, with `DODAM_FORCE_BUSHY_COMMA_JOIN=1` for forced validation. The subtree executor preserves alias-qualified columns by using temporary subtree prefixes and stripping them after each join. Correctness spot checks pass, but the 3-way generic benchmark shows forced bushy is roughly neutral because a 3-table join has little shape advantage over left-deep; this path matters more for wider join graphs and as the target for future partition/spill lowering.
                              - Added join output projection pushdown for multi-comma joins. Instead of always asking `HashJoinExec` for `Projection::All` and pruning after materialization, the executor now derives the columns needed by remaining predicates/final output and passes them into the join operator. Each side keeps at least one join key to avoid zero-column side schemas in existing hash join materializers; post-join prune still removes those safety keys. Focused generic result (`--fixture-scale 1`, repeats 5): default optimizer join set improved from `0.112104s` median-sum to `0.098110s` (`~12.5%`); `optimizer_selective_three_way_comma_join` improved from `41.754ms` to `31.989ms`. Forced bushy remained similar (`0.098052s` median-sum), confirming the current win came from projection/materialization reduction, not join tree shape.
                              - Hash join materialization follow-up: added zero-column projected `RecordBatch` support in the physical layer using `RecordBatchOptions::with_row_count`, and added `StripPrefixExec` as a streaming schema-only prefix-normalization operator for future join pipeline work. Zero-column join output is correct, but the first benchmark showed it was not a default win on the generic join set, so it is gated behind `DODAM_ZERO_COLUMN_JOIN_OUTPUT=1` and the default path still keeps one safety join key per side. This is a useful negative result: removing the last key copy is less important than avoiding the join materialization boundary itself.
                              - Connected multi-comma joins to the generic pipeline memory cost gate. `execute_comma_hash_join` now chooses between normal hash join and `PartitionedHashJoinExec` using estimated rows/row width and `DODAM_JOIN_MEMORY_LIMIT_BYTES`. There is still no full external spill for this path; `PipelineMemoryStrategy::External` remains a signal for future external operators rather than a complete spill implementation.
                              - Connected primitive key range stats into `LogicalJoinTableStats.column_ranges` when building comma-join graphs from scanned inputs. This uses scanned `Int32/Int64` join-key columns for now; the next step is to source the same stats from Parquet row-group metadata before scan so cardinality/selectivity can influence planning earlier.
                              - Added 4-way optimizer join benchmarks to the generic DuckDB suite. Focused default result (`--fixture-scale 1`, repeats 5): median-sum Dodam `0.186221s`, DuckDB `0.081152s` (`2.295x`). Per-case: 3-way `69.387ms` vs `24.058ms` (`2.884x`), selective 3-way `31.527ms` vs `18.484ms` (`1.706x`), 4-way `53.502ms` vs `20.276ms` (`2.639x`), selective 4-way `31.804ms` vs `18.334ms` (`1.735x`). The main remaining gap is still join/operator pipeline and materialization, not merely join order enumeration.
                              - Tried a streaming left-deep multi-comma join chain behind `DODAM_STREAM_LEFT_DEEP_COMMA_JOIN=1`: `MemoryExec -> HashJoinExec -> StripPrefixExec -> HashJoinExec ...`, avoiding the explicit `collect_batches` after every join. Correctness spot checks passed, but performance regressed because this first version had to use `Projection::All` between joins and therefore lost the join output projection/prune savings. Focused result (`--fixture-scale 1`, repeats 5): default median-sum `0.188047s`, streaming `0.199786s`; 3-way `70.460ms -> 75.323ms`, 4-way `53.483ms -> 59.731ms`. Conclusion: pipeline chaining alone is not enough; the next implementation must carry schema/projection metadata through the physical chain so streaming joins still emit only required columns.
                              - Added projected streaming left-deep join metadata. The streaming experimental path now tracks schema-only batches through the join chain, computes the same `comma_join_hash_output_projection` decision at each join, and uses `StripPrefixExec` for schema-only prefix normalization. Correctness stayed green. Focused result (`--fixture-scale 1`, repeats 5): default median-sum `0.186999s`, projected streaming `0.180215s` (`~3.6%` better), but results are mixed by query: 3-way improved `69.305ms -> 62.904ms`, selective 3-way `32.036ms -> 31.357ms`, 4-way regressed `53.247ms -> 54.066ms`, selective 4-way `32.410ms -> 31.887ms`. This is promising but not ready as default; next step is a cost gate using join count, projected column count, and estimated intermediate rows, or profiling why 4-way loses despite projection.
                              - Tried a conservative cost gate for projected streaming left-deep joins (`table_count == 3`, small projected output, bounded estimated rows). A follow-up repeated run showed the auto rule was not stable enough: auto median-sum `0.190312s` vs disabled `0.186960s`, with both 3-way cases slower in that run. The rule is therefore not enabled by default. Current controls: `DODAM_STREAM_LEFT_DEEP_COMMA_JOIN=1` forces the streaming path, `DODAM_AUTO_STREAM_LEFT_DEEP_COMMA_JOIN=1` enables the experimental cost rule, and default execution remains the safer materialized/projection-pruned path. This is an important negative result: the cost model needs direct operator profiling, not only join count/output-column estimates.
                              - Added `DODAM_JOIN_PROFILE=1` SQL-level multi-comma join profiling. Materialized join steps now report prefixes, build side, memory strategy, input/output rows, input/output column counts, and elapsed time; streaming joins report planned projection columns per step plus total streamed execution time. Focused profile comparison (`optimizer_three_way_comma_join`, `optimizer_four_way_comma_join`, repeats 2): materialized 3-way step elapsed was about `8.5-9.5ms + 7.3ms`; streaming 3-way total was about `16.1-16.7ms`, slightly better. Materialized 4-way was about `9-10ms + 8.3ms + 7.9-8.4ms`; streaming 4-way total was `26.2-26.9ms`, slightly worse. This explains the unstable aggregate results: streaming removes explicit SQL-level collects but still causes each chained hash join to materialize output, and 4-way accumulates that cost. Next useful work is not a broader cost gate; it is reducing `HashJoinExec` materialize/take cost itself or introducing a true vector pipeline between join probes.
                              - Hash join materialization cleanup follow-up: cached per-join projection column indices inside `HashJoinMaterializeContext` so repeated output chunks no longer re-resolve projected column names before every probe/build `take`. Also added `DODAM_JOIN_PROFILE=1` local per-operator join logs (`join-profile-local`) to split build/probe/materialize rows/time without relying only on shared cumulative metrics. Focused optimizer join set result after this pass (`--fixture-scale 1`, repeats 5): median-sum Dodam `0.176979s`, DuckDB `0.078696s` (`2.249x`). This is an improvement over the previous `~0.186s` class, but the remaining gap is still dominated by post-join materialize/aggregate pipeline, not build hash table time.
                              - Negative result: increasing `JOIN_OUTPUT_CHUNK_ROWS` from `8192` to `32768` regressed the optimizer join set (`0.178480s` vs the `~0.175-0.177s` default range). Larger output batches reduced batch count but hurt cache locality / Arrow take balance enough that it is not a default win. Reverted to `8192`.
                              - Negative result: added generalized dictionary/string group-key experiments for `Utf8`/`Dictionary<Int32, Utf8>` 2-key grouping and 3-key `count/sum` grouping, but the 3-key path regressed 4-way join aggregate (`optimizer_four_way_comma_join` around `65.7ms` vs default `~55ms`). These paths are retained only behind `DODAM_ENABLE_DICTIONARY_UTF8_GROUP_FAST=1` and `DODAM_ENABLE_THREE_UTF8_COUNT_SUM_FAST=1` for future tuning. This confirms that simply avoiding RowConverter is not enough; the useful next step is fusing join probe output directly into aggregate state so the 56만-row joined `RecordBatch` does not have to be fully materialized first.
                              - Added `DODAM_AGGREGATE_PROFILE=1` grouped aggregate path profiling. It reports the selected aggregate path, rows, batches, groups, and elapsed time. This is intended to prevent repeating the same aggregate fast-path experiment without seeing whether it actually replaced the generic path and whether it won.
                              - Added a general multi-comma star lookup aggregate fusion for `count(*) + sum(fact_col)` where each GROUP BY key is a payload column from a unique small dimension table directly joined to the fact table. Instead of materializing the 56만-row joined `RecordBatch`, Dodam scans dimension lookups, scans the fact table once, and updates aggregate state directly. This is a planner/execution rule based on join graph and aggregate shape, not query-name matching. Added fixed tuple group keys for 2-key/3-key cases so the hot loop does not allocate `Vec<usize>` per row, plus a null-free batch branch to remove per-row validity checks when possible.
                              - Result: optimizer comma join parquet-output set improved from `0.176979s` / DuckDB `0.078696s` (`2.249x`) to `0.086299s` / DuckDB `0.077268s` (`1.117x`). Per-case latest: 3-way `22.156ms` vs `19.846ms` (`1.116x`), selective 3-way `16.866ms` vs `18.636ms` (`0.905x`), 4-way `28.847ms` vs `20.478ms` (`1.409x`), selective 4-way `18.430ms` vs `18.308ms` (`1.007x`). Profile sample showed fused non-selective fact scan+aggregate around `~17.9ms` for 3-way and `~24.9ms` for 4-way, with dimension lookup below `~1ms`.
                              - Negative result: tried combining two dimension lookups that share the same fact key into a tuple-valued lookup for the 4-way case. It regressed the set (`0.090788s`, 4-way `~31.0ms`) because the extra tuple map/indirection outweighed one fewer lookup. Reverted; the remaining 4-way gap should be attacked by lower-level block/SIMD lookup/update or by sharing decoded fact key vectors, not by wrapping maps.
                              - Follow-up: added dense group-slot state for low-cardinality 2-key/3-key lookup aggregate groups. When the product of dimension payload ids is small (`DODAM_MULTI_COMMA_LOOKUP_DENSE_GROUP_SLOTS`, default `4096`), group update becomes a direct slot update instead of hash lookup or small-vector linear search. Latest focused result improved further to Dodam `0.079085s`, DuckDB `0.075533s` (`1.047x`): 3-way `21.184ms` vs `19.118ms`, selective 3-way `16.730ms` vs `17.633ms`, 4-way `23.935ms` vs `19.863ms`, selective 4-way `17.236ms` vs `18.918ms`.
                              - Negative result: tried manually bypassing `groups.update()` inside the dense lookup branch and updating dense slots directly in the hot loop. It regressed slightly (`0.080731s`, ratio `1.067x`), likely from larger branchy code and worse instruction-cache behavior. Reverted the direct-update branch while keeping the dense group-slot representation.
                              - Join aggregate lookup fusion cost fix: the cost model for 3-dimension fusion used dimension table row counts as group cardinality (`1000^3 * 32` in the generic 4-way fixture), so it rejected the lookup-fusion plan and fell back to three materialized hash joins. Changed group-state estimate to use the small-group cap, matching the actual operator which does not preallocate the full dimension row product. `DODAM_OPTIMIZER_TRACE=1` now selects fusion for `optimizer_four_way_comma_join` (`candidate_cost=42084608`, join graph cost about `201728000`). Focused 4-way set improved from `optimizer_four_way_comma_join` `56.606ms` vs DuckDB `19.380ms` (`2.921x`) and selective `34.641ms` vs `18.109ms` (`1.913x`) to about `25.195ms` vs `20.850ms` (`1.208x`) and `17.997ms` vs `18.953ms` (`0.950x`). Also added a small general hot-loop branch for 3-dimension fusion where two dimensions share the same fact key, avoiding one duplicate fact-key read per row; measured effect was modest/neutral, with the main win coming from the cost-model fix.
                              - Structure cleanup follow-up for the same rule:
                                - Moved the lookup-fusion cost calculation out of `sql.rs` into `optimizer.rs` as `JoinAggregateLookupFusionCostInput` + `estimate_join_aggregate_lookup_fusion_cost(...)`, with a unit regression that proves the 3-dimension group-state cost is capped instead of multiplying raw dimension row counts. SQL now only converts the candidate plan into optimizer cost input.
                                - Extracted the dense/null-free join lookup aggregate update loop into `update_join_aggregate_lookup_dense_null_free(...)`. This keeps the current blocked/vectorized operator shape in one helper and isolates the shared-fact-key branch for later SIMD/block work instead of keeping another long ad hoc loop inside the SQL dispatcher.
                                - Extended the error contract matrix with negative cases for multi-column scalar `IN` subqueries, GROUP BY unknown column binding, ORDER BY ordinal out of range, and parser-level malformed aggregate FILTER. Validation: `cargo fmt --all --check && cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q lookup_fusion_cost_caps_dimension_group_state`, and `cargo test -q dodam_sql_error_contract_matrix --test duckdb_differential` passed. Focused release optimizer join set stayed close after the refactor: median-sum Dodam `0.081324s`, DuckDB `0.074326s` (`1.094x`); 4-way `23.738ms` vs `20.583ms` (`1.153x`), selective 4-way `17.812ms` vs `17.816ms` (`1.000x`).
                              - Follow-up on the next maturity batch:
                                - `GROUP BY ALL` expression path: added a dense dictionary-id by small integer group-id cache for the `lower(coalesce(utf8, literal)), int + literal` count/sum operator. This is a general expression-group cache, but it only helps when the string key arrives as `Dictionary<Int32, Utf8>`; in the generic fixture the effect was limited and `standard_group_by_all_expression` remains a main gap (`31.929ms` vs DuckDB `20.626ms`, `1.548x` in the latest focused clean run). The next real fix should target plain Utf8 low-cardinality group-id caching or earlier dictionary preservation from Parquet decode.
                                - Aggregate FILTER: added per-batch predicate mask reuse keyed by condition SQL text. This reduces repeated predicate evaluation when filtered aggregates share a condition and keeps mask evaluation in one place. Latest focused profile showed native filtered aggregate around `masks ~4.2-4.4ms`, `update ~7.0-7.3ms`; focused clean result `standard_aggregate_filter` `19.941ms` vs DuckDB `16.178ms` (`1.233x`). Remaining gap is update loop/state dispatch, not input evaluation.
                                - Mixed tuple semijoin / Parquet dictionary selected decode: remeasured after prior dense pair build changes. Latest focused clean result `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` `26.940ms` vs DuckDB `20.235ms` (`1.331x`). The remaining gap is still dictionary-id read/decode; selected-inner remains opt-in because it is not consistently faster. A real improvement likely requires page-level prefix-selected dictionary-id decode rather than another row-group-level post-filter.
                                - Join fusion vectorization: tried adding raw `Int32Array::values()` branches inside `update_join_aggregate_lookup_dense_null_free(...)`, but focused clean runs regressed non-selective 4-way, likely from code-size/branch-layout effects. Reverted the raw-slice branch and kept only the helper extraction/shared-fact-key branch. Latest focused clean result recovered: `optimizer_four_way_comma_join` `23.217ms` vs DuckDB `20.128ms` (`1.153x`), selective `17.273ms` vs `17.692ms` (`0.976x`).
                                - Robustness: added `duckdb_differential_generic_performance_shapes`, covering the SQL shapes behind the current performance work: `GROUP BY ALL` expression aggregate, aggregate `FILTER`, tuple semijoin, and 4-way comma join lookup-fusion shape. Validation passed with `cargo test -q duckdb_differential_generic_performance_shapes --test duckdb_differential`.
                              - Correctness/general SQL fix: `attrs.rank IN (...)` in the generic nested benchmark was being lowered as a physical `Expr::InList` over column name `attrs.rank`, so scan/filter failed with `UnknownColumn("attrs.rank")`. Changed single-table predicate expression detection so multi-part identifiers go through the scalar expression evaluator, where existing struct-field projection logic reads physical column `attrs` and evaluates field `rank`. The full generic DuckDB comparison now runs through the nested/list case again.
                              - Added finer `DODAM_GENERIC_PROFILE=1` instrumentation for native filtered aggregates: mask evaluation, input evaluation, and update time are now split. `standard_aggregate_filter` showed inputs were negligible (`~0.06ms`), while masks (`~6ms`) and update (`~8ms`) dominated.
                              - Native filtered aggregate improvement: added a null-free dense integer group-key branch and primitive Count/Sum/Avg update fast path. This keeps the generic `FILTER (WHERE ...)` implementation but avoids part of the per-row `Result`/dynamic input conversion overhead for common primitive aggregate inputs. Focused `standard_aggregate_filter` improved from about `22.393ms` / DuckDB `16.465ms` (`1.360x`) to `21.259ms` / DuckDB `16.813ms` (`1.264x`); profile update time dropped from about `8.3ms` to `7.3ms`.
                              - Negative result: tried replacing null-free primitive/decimal comparison mask construction in `vector_column_literal_compare` with `BooleanBufferBuilder` helpers. It regressed `standard_aggregate_filter` mask time (`~5.87ms` to `~6.45ms`) and focused ratio (`1.264x` to `1.296x`), so the change was reverted. The remaining filtered-aggregate gap is more likely from repeated predicate mask evaluation and row-wise state dispatch than from the BooleanArray constructor alone.
                              - Native filtered aggregate cleanup: `IS NULL`/`IS NOT NULL` predicate evaluation now uses Arrow null bitmap kernels in the scalar predicate evaluator. A follow-up attempt to let native filtered aggregate masks reference the input array null bitmap directly was removed because it regressed `standard_aggregate_filter` (`~19.8ms` area to `~21.3ms`) by shifting cost into the update loop without reducing mask time.
                              - Negative result: tried a generalized two-key `count(*) + sum + min + max` aggregate operator for primitive `(Int32/Int64, Date32)` group keys, including compact tuple keys and a dense 2D slot table. It regressed `review_q1_two_key_group` despite avoiding `RowConverter`, because per-row group lookup/materialization overhead and loss of the existing generic parallel behavior outweighed the saved row encoding. The code was removed. Lesson: for multi-key grouped aggregate, simply adding another HashMap/dense fast path is not enough; the useful structural target is fusing upstream scan/join output directly into aggregate state and reducing `RecordBatch`/group materialization boundaries.
                              - Single-key `Utf8` `count/sum/min/max` aggregate improvement: added a batch-local borrowed string group-id cache and uses the non-null payload update path when only the key is nullable. This is a general low-cardinality string grouping rule, not query-name matching. Focused `review_q1_alt_string_group` aggregate profiles moved from the `~20-22ms` area to `~14-16ms`, although full 5-query samples remain noisy because scan/decode and DuckDB variance dominate the final ratio.
                              - Large-data robustness cleanup: SQL multi-comma joins now lower `PipelineMemoryStrategy::External` to the existing spill-capable `PartitionedHashJoinExec` instead of falling back to plain in-memory `HashJoinExec`. `Partitioned` keeps its cost-derived partition count, while `External` uses the maximum SQL external join partition cap (`1024`) and relies on the physical partitioned join's recursive repartition/file-hash/nested-loop safeguards. This connects the existing spill infrastructure to the SQL memory cost gate instead of treating `External` as only a planning signal.
                              - Correctness regression added for struct-field `IN` predicates: `attrs.rank IN (...)` combined with list projection/filter is now covered in the DuckDB nested differential test, matching the generic benchmark case that previously failed with `UnknownColumn("attrs.rank")`.
                              - Tuple semijoin generalization follow-up:
                                - Added direct `(Int32, Int32)` semijoin build from Parquet primitive columns and a dense `i32 -> i32` pair map when the first key range is compact and functionally maps to one second key. This is a general tuple-semijoin representation rule, not a query-name rule. It covers `IN` and `NOT IN` membership masks through the shared tuple semijoin path.
                                - Correctness fix: the direct nullable pair builder now marks RHS `has_null` only when a filter-passing RHS tuple contains NULL. Previously row-group-level nullable columns could make null-safe tuple `NOT IN` fall through to the generic single-column IN-subquery path and fail with `IN subquery must return exactly one column`.
                                - Added DuckDB differential edge coverage for dense pair semantics: negative ids, duplicate/conflicting pairs, NULL keys, and wide-range pairs. `cargo test -q duckdb_differential_in_subquery --test duckdb_differential` passes.
                                - Focused release benchmark after rebuilding `target/release/dodam`: `standard_tuple_not_in_null_safe` Dodam `32.658ms`, DuckDB `38.907ms` (`0.839x`); `standard_tuple_in_non_key_predicate` `25.061ms` vs `36.611ms` (`0.685x`); `standard_tuple_in_other_file` `28.144ms` vs `35.426ms` (`0.794x`).
                                - Negative result: mixed `(Int32, Utf8)` inner selected-build by string-prefix selection did not win as a default. With `DODAM_MIXED_TUPLE_SELECTED_INNER_BUILD=1`, `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` was `40.233ms` vs DuckDB `25.670ms` (`1.567x`), worse than the default `27.295ms` vs `19.512ms` (`1.399x`). Disabling the numeric-selected outer probe was also worse (`39.872ms`, `1.548x`). The remaining mixed-key gap is inner dictionary-id build cost, not outer probing; keep the selected inner path opt-in only.
                                - Mixed tuple build improvement: changed the direct `(Int32, Utf8)` build path to collect selected `(i32, string_id)` pairs in a vector and choose `DenseI32ToU32` or packed hash once at the end. This removes hot-loop hash insertion and makes the dense representation a real default instead of an extra conversion pass. Profile sample: inner direct build dropped from the previous `~20-22ms` class to `~12-16ms`, while disabling the dense map (`DODAM_DISABLE_MIXED_TUPLE_DENSE_PAIR_MAP=1`) regressed `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` to `33.624ms` vs DuckDB `20.010ms` (`1.680x`). Default focused sample improved to `25.625ms` vs `19.877ms` (`1.289x`), though repeated runs remain cache-sensitive. Selected-inner remains opt-in because same-workdir A/B runs were unstable and did not consistently beat the default.
                                - Negative result: tried a generic mixed tuple prefix precheck before exact pair-set build. It collects RHS selected numeric ids plus possible prefix string ids and scans the outer side for any coarse candidate before building the exact `(numeric,string_id)` dense pair map. On `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` this over-approx was too weak: `outer_found=true`, so Dodam still had to build the exact pair set and the extra precheck regressed the profile to about `44ms`. The rule is retained only behind `DODAM_MIXED_TUPLE_PREFIX_PRECHECK=1`.
                                - Added correct inner/outer split profiling for `mixed-direct-empty`. Latest profile: total about `24ms`, inner exact pair build about `15ms`, outer exact empty probe about `8ms`, output/write negligible. This confirms the remaining mixed tuple gap is split across direct Parquet dictionary-id pair build and selected outer dictionary probe; another coarse precheck is unlikely to help unless it can prove exact non-existence without duplicating those scans.
                                - Negative result: tried moving mixed tuple RHS numeric-set construction out of the hot row loop and then tried a Parquet-stats-driven direct dense pair builder. Both failed to improve the default path: delayed numeric construction added another pass over the selected pairs, and stats-driven dense build paid metadata/range setup plus large dense allocation without reducing reader cost. Restored the default row-loop numeric insert + pair vector path; focused mixed tuple median recovered to about `25.967ms` vs DuckDB `19.693ms` (`1.319x`).
                                - Negative result: tried increasing the mixed tuple direct-build internal reader batch size from the query batch size (`16K`) to `64K` to reduce `read_records` calls. It reduced direct reader batches (`39 -> 10`) but did not improve end-to-end time; profile stayed around `27-30ms` and focused runs were noisy/regressed. Conclusion: the gap is not just per-batch call overhead; larger batches can hurt cache locality around dictionary-id/numeric pairing.
                                - Improvement: tuple semijoin now prefers the mixed `(integer, string)` direct build immediately when the inner tuple-key filter is a supported prefix `LIKE` on one of the tuple columns. Previously the generic tuple path tried the `i64/i64` pair collector first and only fell back to mixed after discovering the string key, adding hidden overhead outside the direct reader profile. Latest profile: `mixed-direct-empty` total about `24ms`, inner `15ms`, outer `9ms`; focused 7-repeat `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` improved to `27.371ms` vs DuckDB `21.375ms` (`1.281x`).
                                - SQL decomposition / vectorized window follow-up:
                                - Moved join aggregate lookup fusion planning/execution/state out of `sql.rs` into `src/sql/join_lookup_fusion.rs`. This keeps the optimizer-visible lookup-fusion rule as a reusable execution module instead of another large SQL-local block. The previous failed broad move is avoided by cutting exactly from `JoinAggregateLookupFusionPlan` through the residual edge checker.
                                - Moved window parsing/execution helpers out of `sql.rs` into `src/sql/window.rs`, so the window entrypoint and its partition/order/rank/offset helpers now live together. Also split common projection helper rules into `src/sql/projection_utils.rs` as a first step before the larger parser/projection type decomposition.
                                - Improved the generic `lag/lead(column)` window path by shifting direct Arrow column arrays for `Int64`, `Int32`, and `Utf8` arguments instead of materializing the input column into `EvaluatedScalar` first. This is a general offset-window operator rule, not a query-name path. Focused parquet-output benchmark improved `window_lag_lead_partition_order` from the previous noisy `~66.4ms` sample vs DuckDB `~56.8ms` (`1.169x`) to `50.534ms` vs DuckDB `48.180ms` (`1.049x`).
                                - Robustness: added a DuckDB differential case for `lag(payload)` and `lead(value)` with nullable partitions/values to cover the new direct column shift path. Validation passed: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q duckdb_differential_scan_filter_and_nulls --test duckdb_differential`, and `cargo test -q duckdb_differential_generic_performance_shapes --test duckdb_differential`.
                              - SQL decomposition / vectorized pipeline follow-up:
                                - Split projection-related types out of `sql.rs` into `src/sql/projection_types.rs`: `ParsedProjection`, `ProjectionExpression`, `GroupExpressionBinding`, and `ScalarSqlExpression`. This is a type-boundary step only, but it reduces `sql.rs` coupling and gives parser/evaluator/rule modules a concrete next extraction target.
                                - Reworked generic offset-window direct column shift to use Arrow `take` with a nullable index vector for `Int32`, `Int64`, and `Utf8`. This avoids cloning string payloads for `lead(label)` and keeps the operator columnar. Focused result improved further: `window_lag_lead_partition_order` `46.137ms` vs DuckDB `48.759ms` (`0.946x`).
