                                - Improved plain-Utf8 two-expression grouped aggregate (`lower(coalesce(utf8, literal)), int + literal`) with a batch-local borrowed string-id map plus dense `(string_id, int)` group slots. The first attempt still cloned a `String` per row and only reduced the case to the `~36.9ms` class; changing it to borrowed `&str` keys for already-lowercase batches was the real win. Focused `standard_group_by_all_expression` moved to `24.604ms` vs DuckDB `21.360ms` (`1.152x`), down from the previous `~41.5ms` sample.
                                - Tightened SQL rule registry candidate selection with a logical-shape summary. `ProjectionExpression` is now considered only for projection expressions or expression predicates, preserving `WHERE id + 1 ...` correctness while avoiding unrelated rule attempts. Also narrowed TPC-H-like detection to table names instead of raw prefixes such as `p_`, which had falsely triggered TPC-H candidates for aliases like `id_plus_one`. `DODAM_SQL_RULE_PROFILE=1` now reports shapes such as `project-expr+order-by+limit` and the generic projection query only lists the `projection-expression` candidate.
                                - Validation: `cargo fmt --all`, `cargo check -q`, `cargo test -q duckdb_differential_generic_performance_shapes --test duckdb_differential`, and `cargo test -q duckdb_differential_scan_filter_and_nulls --test duckdb_differential` passed. Focused 3-query parquet-output comparison: median-sum Dodam `0.091682s`, DuckDB `0.088558s` (`1.035x`); remaining gaps are `standard_aggregate_filter` (`1.136x`) and `standard_group_by_all_expression` (`1.152x`), while window lag/lead is ahead (`0.946x`).
                              - Native filtered aggregate follow-up:
                                - Added batch-local input caching for native filtered aggregate inputs. The existing mask cache avoided repeated predicate evaluation, but repeated inputs such as `value` were still evaluated once per aggregate. This is now cached by scalar-expression/input-kind key. Focused `standard_aggregate_filter` single-query sample improved from the `~25.4ms` class to about `20-22ms` depending on run noise, and global filtered aggregate profile showed input evaluation was no longer material.
                                - Added global native-filtered profile splits (`masks`, `inputs`, `update`) and found the first attempted spec-wise global update with eager `selected_count` was a negative result: it reduced typed update dispatch but forced every mask to be scanned up front, regressing the benchmark. That selected-count path was removed.
                                - Replaced the global row/spec dispatch with typed per-aggregate update loops without eager selected-count scanning. Global profile improved update time from about `9.1ms` to `7.1ms` and aggregate materialization from about `21.1ms` to `18.9ms` on the global filtered test.
                                - For grouped native filtered aggregate, enabled sparse selected-row lists only on the grouped path. Profile tradeoff: masks increased from about `6.1ms` to `9.9ms`, but update dropped from about `10.0ms` to `5.3ms`, slightly improving total profile (`20.4ms` to `19.8ms`). The latest focused 5-repeat parquet-output run remains noisy: `standard_aggregate_filter` Dodam `22.386ms`, DuckDB `16.614ms` (`1.347x`), `standard_group_by_all_expression` `25.358ms` vs `20.604ms` (`1.231x`), `window_lag_lead_partition_order` `46.052ms` vs `48.017ms` (`0.959x`). The remaining filtered-aggregate gap is still mostly predicate mask construction plus grouped update; the next useful step is cheaper mask construction for decimal/string LIKE predicates or fusing mask evaluation with grouped updates.
                                - Added direct predicate evaluation for grouped native filtered aggregates when the group key is a null-free integer. Simple `IS NOT NULL`, primitive/decimal column-literal comparisons, and prefix `LIKE` predicates can now skip `BooleanArray` mask materialization. A first direct path was still spec-major and repeatedly looked up group state per aggregate; changing it to row-major reduced only a small amount (`update ~12.08ms -> ~11.78ms`) because the remaining cost is predicate dispatch plus state update. Correctness is gated so nullable group keys still use the mask path.
                                - Added profile output for the two-expression `GROUP BY ALL` count/sum operator and removed one unnecessary row pass in the plain-Utf8 lower/coalesce + integer-add path. The failed variant removed the `string_ids` vector and did a hash lookup again during the update pass, which made the loop shape worse. The retained version fuses lowercase validation with string-id creation, keeps a compact `string_ids` vector, and lets the update pass do direct dense-slot lookup. Profile improved `two-expression-count-sum` update from `13.938ms` to `9.731ms` and materialize from `27.073ms` to `24.174ms`; focused 9-repeat `standard_group_by_all_expression` was `30.931ms` vs DuckDB `27.173ms` (`1.138x`). The 3-query comparison remains noisy (`standard_aggregate_filter` `1.171x`, `standard_group_by_all_expression` `1.460x`, window `1.001x` in one run), so future work should use per-case runs plus profile splits before drawing conclusions.
                                - Join aggregate lookup fusion follow-up: added a direct dense-slot update helper for the null-free dense lookup branch so 2-key/3-key dense groups can update the target slot without constructing a temporary key slice and re-entering `groups.update(...)`. Focused measurements are noisy (`optimizer_four_way_comma_join` sampled around `23-31ms` vs DuckDB `20-27ms`), but this keeps the blocked operator shape explicit and isolates the dense hot loop for future SIMD/block work.
                                - Negative result: tried combining two shared-fact-key dense lookups into one `dense_usize_lookup_pair(...)`. It regressed the 4-way focused run because the extra combined helper branches were not cheaper than two simple dense lookups. Removed it.
                                - Negative result: tried changing dense lookup aggregate group state from `Vec<Option<Group>>` to three columnar arrays (`counts/sums/sum_counts`). It regressed `optimizer_four_way_comma_join` (`~34.3ms` vs DuckDB `~25.7ms` in one repeated run), likely because this tiny-group workload benefits from updating one compact struct slot rather than three separate arrays. Reverted; the useful next target is lower-level fact scan/decode or a genuinely vectorized lookup/update block, not splitting this state.
                                - Mixed tuple semijoin re-profile: `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` currently spends about `17ms` in inner exact `(id,label)` pair build and `11ms` in the outer empty probe (`mixed-direct-empty`, output rows 0). Since the coarse prefix precheck already failed to prove emptiness, the remaining general improvement likely needs page-level dictionary-id/prefix-selected decode or a stronger exact relationship index; another extra scan/precheck is unlikely to win.
                                - Native filtered aggregate follow-up: tried spec-major direct predicate updates for `FILTER` aggregates. It did not improve `standard_aggregate_filter`; repeating group-state lookup once per selected aggregate outweighed fewer predicate checks. Removed the experiment.
                                - Native filtered aggregate negative result: tried a dense-range prepass for non-null integer group keys so the update loop could index `dense[key]` directly. It regressed because the extra key-range scan cost more than the saved dense/hash branch on the current low-cardinality grouped filter workload. Removed the experiment.
                                - Native filtered aggregate retained cleanup: direct predicate update now uses an infallible primitive Count/Sum/Avg update helper before falling back to the generic `Result` path. This is a small general hot-loop cleanup for primitive filtered aggregates; focused `standard_aggregate_filter` remains noisy around `~25-26ms` vs DuckDB `~21-22ms`, so the larger remaining gap is still the row-major predicate/state dispatch structure rather than input/mask materialization.
                                - Mixed tuple semijoin direct-reader profile: with `DODAM_DIRECT_PRIMITIVE_PROFILE=1`, `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` shows inner `facts_scrambled(id,label)` dictionary-id build dominated by read/decode (`~12-14ms read`, `~2.5-3ms consume`) and outer empty probe dominated by selected byte-array read (`~10-11ms read`, `<1ms consume`). Re-tested `DODAM_MIXED_TUPLE_SELECTED_INNER_BUILD=1`; it still regressed because selected byte-array prefix read was about `~18ms` before consume. This confirms the useful 1/4 direction is page-level dictionary-id/prefix fusion or lower read/decode cost, not another row-level set/precheck.
                                - Join aggregate lookup fusion: changed dense 2-key/3-key group state from `Vec<Option<Group>>` to preinitialized `Vec<Group>` and filters `count == 0` at finish. This removes the per-row `Option::get_or_insert` branch while preserving the compact struct state that previously beat the failed three-array columnar state. Focused optimizer join set sampled Dodam `0.090641s` vs DuckDB `0.091836s` (`0.987x`); 4-way was `22.643ms` vs `20.871ms` (`1.085x`) and selective 4-way was ahead (`0.763x`).
                                - Parquet/Arrow decode fusion step: enabled a conservative automatic required/plain primitive page-stream attempt in the shared `scan_parquet_primitive_columns_with_store` path. The auto path only tries the zero-repetition/zero-definition page-stream reader and falls straight back to the existing ColumnReader path if unsupported, instead of forcing the broader generic page reader. This moves more primitive operators toward page/vector views without requiring query-specific flags. Full generic one-shot comparison after this and the dense group-state change sampled Dodam `1.001s` vs DuckDB `1.460s` (`0.686x`); remaining slower cases are now narrow (`review_q6_expression_sum`, low-cardinality string group, mixed tuple, a few window shapes), while `standard_aggregate_filter` was close (`19.541ms` vs `18.981ms`).
                                - Parquet dictionary page-fusion experiment: added narrow page-level readers for `(i32, byte-array dictionary id)` and selected byte-array dictionary paths so storage can try a page/vector loop before falling back to ColumnReader. This is structurally closer to decoder/operator fusion, but the mixed tuple benchmark did not improve: default `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` remained around `33.6ms` vs DuckDB `25.3-26.1ms` (`~1.29-1.35x`), and forcing selected-inner stayed worse at `~38.6ms` (`~1.5x`). Follow-up lazy dictionary-id decode removed immediate `Vec<i32>` materialization for non-null byte-array dictionary pages and emits selected prefix rows through `DictionaryIdBlock`, but read time still stayed around `18ms` in selected-inner. The reason is clearer now: the dominant cost is page load plus RLE/bitpack traversal/payload strategy, not just the final id Vec. The next useful target is a lower-level specialized RLE/bitpack kernel with direct build/update and better numeric/payload access, not another wrapper around the current page cursor.
                                - Parquet dictionary selected emit follow-up: rewrote the selected prefix emit path to parse hybrid RLE/bitpack headers directly. RLE runs now emit selected rows without expanding to `DictionaryIdBlock`; bitpacked runs still decode in 64-id blocks. This also did not materially move `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` (`~33.9ms` default, forced selected-inner still `~38.9ms`). This suggests the data is dominated by bitpacked dictionary-id traversal and page access rather than long RLE runs or id Vec allocation.
                                - Filtered aggregate follow-up: profiled `standard_aggregate_filter` with `DODAM_GENERIC_PROFILE=1`; masks/input are ~0.1ms total while update is ~11-15ms for 397k rows and 10 groups. A generic heuristic to avoid direct predicates for more than 4 specs did not improve this query because it has exactly 4 filtered aggregates and direct row-major remains selected. The next useful improvement is a compiled/columnar direct grouped aggregate operator for primitive predicate/input/state combinations, not direct-vs-mask routing.
                                - Retained cleanup/perf wins from the next pass: moved single-key UTF8 count/sum/min/max group cache from batch-local to stream-local, which reduced `review_q1_alt_string_group` aggregate work from roughly `~20-22ms` to `~15-16ms`; added 4-key dense join aggregate slots so non-selective 4-way comma join no longer falls back to generic `Vec<usize>` hash state (`optimizer_four_way_comma_join` sampled `~31ms -> ~23.6ms`); added 7-bit parquet dictionary id unpacking for the generic RLE/bitpacked decoder and dictionary-id block reader, reducing mixed tuple dictionary-id read from `~12.5ms` to `~9.3ms` and outer selected dictionary read from `~10.5ms` to `~8ms`.
                                - Cleanup from the same pass: removed the compiled direct-updater experiment for `FILTER` grouped aggregate after measurement showed no meaningful improvement over the existing row-major direct predicate path. Keep this as a failed experiment; the next real fix still needs a columnar grouped filtered aggregate operator, not another enum wrapper.
                                - Filtered aggregate dense-group follow-up: added an eager dense initialization path for non-null small integer grouped `FILTER` aggregates. It avoids calling the generic dense/hash group-state resolver on every row when the key range is small (`bucket`-style groups). Focused `standard_aggregate_filter` moved only modestly/noisily (`~20.6ms` full generic before, `~19.8-20.0ms` focused after). Also tested disabling direct predicates in favor of sparse masks and a null-free predicate branch; sparse masks were clearly worse (`~26ms`), and the null-free branch did not improve, so only the eager dense path and diagnostic direct-predicate disable flag were retained.
                                - Filtered aggregate columnar experiment: tried a per-batch columnar dense state for small integer grouped `FILTER` aggregates, including a spec-major loop. Profiling sometimes showed a modest update-loop improvement (`~19.7ms` focused), but no-profile repeated comparisons were not stable and could match or slightly lose to the existing eager dense/direct path (`~26.6ms` enabled vs `~26.1ms` disabled in one run). Kept the code behind opt-in `DODAM_ENABLE_NATIVE_FILTERED_COLUMNAR_DIRECT=1` for future profiling, but it is not default. The real fix still needs a broader operator rewrite that avoids predicate enum dispatch and string LIKE checks in the hot loop.
                                - Filtered aggregate retained win: precompute direct predicate selections once per batch (`NativeFilteredDirectPredicate::Precomputed`) before grouped update. This moves decimal comparisons, `IS NOT NULL`, and prefix LIKE checks out of the hot update loop while preserving the direct dense update path. Focused `standard_aggregate_filter` update time dropped from the previous direct path's `~16ms` range when precompute is disabled to `~12ms`, and the focused comparison sampled `19.970ms` vs DuckDB `17.210ms` (`1.160x`). Disable with `DODAM_DISABLE_NATIVE_FILTERED_PRECOMPUTED_DIRECT_PREDICATES=1` for A/B testing.
                                - Retained vectorized/block update wins: changed single-key count/sum/min/max aggregate for non-null inputs to batch-local partial slots followed by touched-group merge. This removes repeated final group-state mutation from the row hot loop and applies to Int32/Int64/UInt64/Utf8 keys. Focused profile for `review_q1_one_key_group` aggregate dropped to the `~10-12ms` class, and `review_q1_alt_string_group` aggregate stayed around `~15-18ms`; focused wall-clock sample after validation was `review_q1_one_key_group` `19.455ms` vs DuckDB `19.536ms` (`0.996x`) and `review_q1_alt_string_group` `25.805ms` vs `21.694ms` (`1.190x`, still partly scan/sort/output noise).
                                - Join aggregate lookup fusion retained cleanup: direct dense-slot update avoids constructing a temporary key slice and re-entering `groups.update(...)` for 2/3/4-key dense groups. Negative follow-up: tried per-batch dense scratch accumulation, first heap-backed and then stack-backed for <=64 slots. Repeated profile was not stable and eventually showed `optimizer_four_way_comma_join` fact update back around `~13ms`, so the scratch path was removed. The compact direct slot update is the retained path; further improvement should target dense lookup/key decode, not another local group scratch.
                                - Limited/neutral semijoin cleanup: changed mixed tuple semijoin dictionary string-id cache from `Option<u32>` lookups to dense `u32::MAX` sentinel ids in the direct `(i32, utf8)` pair build. This removes row-level option matching in consume, but the benchmark remains read/decode dominated: `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` profile still shows inner dictionary-id read around `~12ms`, consume around `~2.5ms`, and outer selected read around `~10-11ms`; focused comparison sampled `25.876ms` vs DuckDB `20.675ms` (`1.252x`). Keep the cleanup, but the next real improvement is lower-level page/vector decode fusion rather than more consume-side row logic.
                                - Retained aggregate rule: added a generic two-key `count(*)/sum/min/max` path for primitive integer + `Date32` grouping keys. This moves `review_q1_two_key_group` out of the broader generic aggregate path and uses the same batch-local partial merge pattern as single-key `count/sum/min/max`. Focused profile after the rule showed aggregate work around `~12.6-14.6ms`, down from the previous `~17-19ms` class; focused wall-clock sampled `18.217ms` vs DuckDB `15.376ms` (`1.185x`).
                                - Negative result: tried an additional dense per-batch `(int,date)` accumulator for the two-key `count/sum/min/max` rule. It regressed aggregate work back to the `~17-18ms` class because per-batch range scans plus dense slot initialization cost more than the saved global group-index lookups on this dataset. Removed it; a future dense variant needs persistent slot state or a cost model that accounts for initialization cost.
                                - Retained filtered aggregate cleanup: compressed precomputed direct predicates into per-row `u8` bitmasks for small grouped `FILTER` aggregate batches. The grouped update loop now iterates set bits instead of calling `predicate.selected(row)` for every predicate. Focused `standard_aggregate_filter` update dropped to about `~11.5-12.3ms`, with wall-clock sample `19.454ms` vs DuckDB `16.506ms` (`1.179x`).
                                - Negative result: tried a direct-mapped UTF8 group-id cache before the stream-level `FastHashMap<String, usize>` for single-key string `count/sum/min/max`. A/B showed it was worse: enabled `review_q1_alt_string_group` sampled `27.676ms` with aggregate `~20-23ms`, while disabled sampled `20.432ms` with aggregate `~15-17ms`. The extra FNV hash plus group-key verification cost more than the existing fast hash lookup. Removed it.
                                - Negative result: tried selected-row dictionary-id decode for `scan_parquet_i32_byte_array_selected_by_i32` so the outer mixed tuple empty probe would not materialize all dictionary ids before applying the numeric predicate. It regressed/noised the mixed tuple case (`standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` sampled around `~27.9-34.1ms` vs DuckDB `~21-25ms`) because the current full-id decoder is already efficient and the new closure/block traversal added overhead. Removed it.
                                - Retained scratch cleanup: reused batch-local `partials/touched` buffers for single-key and two-key `count/sum/min/max` aggregate partial merge instead of allocating them each batch. Focused aggregate profile after this cleanup sampled `review_q1_one_key_group` aggregate around `~10-11ms` and `review_q1_alt_string_group` around `~15-17ms`; wall-clock sample was one-key `14.968ms` vs DuckDB `15.067ms` (`0.993x`) and alt-string `20.130ms` vs `16.779ms` (`1.200x`). Two-key remains around `1.2x` and needs a better key lookup/update strategy rather than scratch allocation tweaks.
                                - Retained mixed tuple page/vector cleanup: added a raw required/plain INT32 + byte-array dictionary-id page scan path and connected the `(Int32, Utf8)` tuple semijoin build/probe to it. Storage no longer forces numeric column materialization into `Vec<i32>` for this page-reader path; consumers receive the numeric block bytes and read selected rows in the blocked loop. This is not query-name specialization: it is a lower-level scan API for required/plain INT32 paired with dictionary ids, with the existing ColumnReader fallback preserved. Focused rebuilt release result for `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` improved from the previous `33.781ms` vs DuckDB `26.199ms` (`1.289x`) sample to `30.541ms` vs `26.219ms` (`1.165x`), and the full generic run sampled it at `24.787ms` vs `20.344ms` (`1.218x`). The remaining gap is still read/decode dominated, especially outer selected byte-array read, so the next useful work is deeper dictionary/page decode fusion rather than more consume-side row checks.
                                - Negative result: re-tried numeric-selected dictionary-id decode for the outer mixed tuple probe after the 7-bit dictionary unpack and raw numeric block changes. It still did not beat the default full-id decode path: default focused sample was `30.314ms` vs DuckDB `26.433ms` (`1.147x`), while the opt-in selected decode sample was `30.632ms` vs `26.207ms` (`1.169x`). The reason is the same as earlier: bitpacked dictionary traversal still has to walk/decode the stream, and the extra selected emit branches do not pay for themselves at this selectivity. Removed the experiment again.
                                - Negative result: tried changing native filtered aggregate precomputed predicate masks from `Vec<bool>` per predicate to a bitset representation and building row masks by visiting set bits. It did not improve `standard_aggregate_filter`: mask time stayed around `~11-12ms` and focused wall-clock sampled `26.568ms` vs DuckDB `22.022ms` (`1.206x`), slightly worse than the existing precomputed bool path. Reverted. The next useful filtered-aggregate improvement should fuse predicate evaluation with grouped update or combine same-column comparisons in one scan, not just repack the mask storage.
                                - Negative result: tried fusing same-column decimal filtered predicates (`amount >= ...` and `amount < ...`) into one precompute scan. It also regressed `standard_aggregate_filter` (`26.792ms` vs DuckDB `21.208ms`, `1.263x`) because two output `Vec<bool>` writes plus extra inner branches were more expensive than two simple tight scans. Removed it. This reinforces that filtered aggregate needs a direct grouped operator that evaluates predicate and updates state in one pass, not another standalone mask precompute variant.
                                - Limited retained cleanup: added a batch-local borrowed `&str -> group_id` cache for the single-key Utf8 `count/sum/min/max` path. It avoids repeatedly going through the stream-global `HashMap<String, usize>` for duplicate strings within one batch. Focused `review_q1_alt_string_group` remained noisy but sampled around `26.384ms` vs DuckDB `21.390ms` (`1.233x`) after rebuild, better than some earlier `~27ms+` samples but not a structural fix. Keep as a low-risk general cache.
                                - Negative result: tried a stream-persistent dense slot index for null-free two-key `(integer, Date32)` `count/sum/min/max`. It regressed/noised `review_q1_two_key_group`; disabling the dense slots with the same binary sampled `22.820ms` vs DuckDB `19.930ms` (`1.145x`), while the default dense attempt sampled `23.809ms` vs `19.804ms` (`1.202x`). The rebuild/range maintenance cost and extra code path did not beat the compact tuple HashMap on this 200-group workload. Removed it.
                                - Retained generic direct-fold improvement: extended `DirectPrimitiveFoldExec` for single primitive-key `count(*)/sum(i64)/min(decimal)/max(decimal)` with a separate Date32 filter column. Previously this direct/vectorized path only handled `max(Date32)`, so Q1-like variants with `max(amount)` fell back to the slower generic aggregate path. This is a shape rule, not a query-name fast path: it applies when the aggregate min/max decimal column is the same and the filter references a Date32 column. Focused rebuild result: `review_q1_one_key_group` improved to `8.527ms` vs DuckDB `16.311ms` (`0.523x`), while `review_q1_two_key_group` and `review_q1_alt_string_group` remain separate gaps because they use two-key and Utf8 grouping.
                                - Retained filtered aggregate improvement: grouped native `FILTER` aggregate no longer precomputes `Vec<bool>` direct predicate masks by default. Current profile showed precompute spent about `12.3ms` in mask construction and `5.0ms` in update, while evaluating direct predicates in the grouped update loop spent about `0.1ms` in masks and `15.4ms` in update, reducing wall time. Focused rebuilt result improved `standard_aggregate_filter` from `27.389ms` to `19.982ms` in one A/B run; latest focused sample was `19.006ms` vs DuckDB `16.112ms` (`1.180x`). This is a grouped-policy change, not a query-specific path.
                                - Retained filtered aggregate follow-up: promoted the grouped native filtered columnar-direct path to default after the precompute policy change made it beneficial again. The columnar path updates per-aggregate dense group arrays and merges them back, avoiding repeated per-row state lookup for small integer group keys. Also removed its temporary `keys: Vec<usize>` materialization and reads keys directly from the `Int32Array`. A/B: disabling the columnar path sampled `standard_aggregate_filter` at `24.403ms` vs DuckDB `21.723ms`; default sampled `16.616ms` vs `15.898ms` in focused 7-repeat, and profile showed update around `12.0ms`. Disable with `DODAM_DISABLE_NATIVE_FILTERED_COLUMNAR_DIRECT=1`.
                                - Negative result: tried making the `GROUP BY ALL` two-expression `Utf8 + int` dense group cache stream-persistent instead of rebuilding per batch. It regressed `standard_group_by_all_expression` (`~22-25ms` class to `28.432ms`) because the extra pre-scan/string ownership work outweighed saved per-batch slot rebuild. Reverted. The remaining gap likely needs a lower-level string dictionary preservation or a fused expression-key reader, not another HashMap cache layer.
                                - Retained correctness/robustness cleanup: propagated `SqlExecutionOptions` through the registered SQL rule dispatcher into derived joins, materialized subquery rewrites, CTE subqueries, explicit parsed joins, and multi-comma join execution. These paths no longer silently fall back to `default_join_memory_limit_bytes()` when an outer caller sets a low join memory limit; materialized comma joins now pass the chosen memory limit into the shared pipeline memory strategy and `PartitionedHashJoinExec` options. This is not a query-specific speed path, but it tightens the engine/CLI boundary and makes spill/memory behavior more production-like across nested SQL execution.
                                - Retained direct-fold safety net: added a physical test for `DirectPrimitiveFoldExec` single-key `count(*)/sum(i64)/min(decimal)/max(decimal)` with a separate Date32 filter column, covering the generic Q1-like decimal-max shape that was recently lowered into the direct/vectorized primitive fold rule.
                                - Focused validation after the cleanup: `cargo check -q`, direct primitive fold min/max tests, `duckdb_differential_join_low_memory_limit`, `duckdb_differential_generic_performance_shapes`, and `duckdb_differential_in_subquery` passed. Focused generic parquet-output comparison sampled median-sum Dodam `0.105471s` vs DuckDB `0.100333s` (`1.051x`): `review_q1_one_key_group` remained faster (`0.567x`), while the remaining slower cases were `review_q1_alt_string_group` (`1.195x`), `standard_aggregate_filter` (`1.210x`), `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` (`1.140x`), and `review_q1_two_key_group` (`1.127x`). These are the next useful targets for general vector/block operators rather than query-name fast paths.
                                - Retained filtered aggregate cleanup: specialized the grouped native filtered columnar-direct update loop by aggregate/input shape instead of calling a generic `update()` enum dispatch for every selected row. This keeps the existing vector/block operator shape (`Int32` dense group key + direct predicates + primitive inputs) and reduces row-at-a-time dispatch without adding a query-name path. Focused rebuilt samples for `standard_aggregate_filter` moved around `1.05-1.12x` vs DuckDB, with one short run ahead at `0.957x`; retained because it is a general hot-loop simplification and passed differential coverage.
                                - Retained mixed tuple semijoin cleanup: changed integer/string tuple semijoin mask materialization from `Vec<Option<bool>> -> BooleanArray` to direct `BooleanBufferBuilder` construction for both dictionary and plain Utf8 paths. The output mask has no nulls, so this avoids per-row `Option` allocation/collection while preserving semantics. Final focused rebuilt sample for `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` was `24.472ms` vs DuckDB `20.373ms` (`1.201x`); still slower, but the change is a general mask-materialization cleanup.
                                - Negative result: tried splitting `TwoKeyCountSumMinMaxIndex` into a non-null `(i64, Date32)` hash map plus nullable fallback map to avoid `Option` tuple keys on `review_q1_two_key_group`. Repeated focused measurement did not improve and sometimes regressed (`~1.25x`), likely because the extra map/branch/code-shape cost outweighed removing `Option` wrapping. Reverted. The remaining two-key gap is more likely group lookup/update structure or scan/decode/materialization, not the `Option` key alone.
                                - Current focused four-case checkpoint after retaining only the useful changes: median-sum Dodam `0.082548s`, DuckDB `0.068866s` (`1.199x`); `review_q1_two_key_group` `1.166x`, `review_q1_alt_string_group` `1.301x`, `standard_aggregate_filter` `1.125x`, `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` `1.201x`. Next useful work: lower alt-string/two-key count/sum/min/max further into scan/vector fold where possible, and reduce mixed tuple dictionary read/decode rather than adding more consume-side checks.
                                - Retained follow-up across the five current generic gaps:
                                  - `alt_string_group`: split the single-UTF8-key `count/sum/min/max` loop into null-free and nullable branches. This removes the per-row key-null branch for null-free string batches while keeping the same generic `GROUP BY Utf8 count/sum/min/max` rule. Focused rebuilt sample improved to `review_q1_alt_string_group` `19.734ms` vs DuckDB `16.090ms` (`1.226x`) from the prior `~1.30x` class.
                                  - `FILTER aggregate`: tried selected-row blocks for grouped columnar-direct predicates, so predicate dispatch happens once per predicate and aggregate update consumes selected row indexes. This was mostly neutral-to-small-positive in focused runs; latest sample had `standard_aggregate_filter` `15.990ms` vs DuckDB `16.380ms` (`0.976x`). Keep for now because it preserves the block/vector direction and passed differential tests, but watch for regressions since it adds selected-index writes.
                                  - `mixed tuple semijoin`: split integer/string pair mask generation by numeric key type (`Int32` vs `Int64`) so the outer mask loop no longer calls the numeric enum helper per row. Latest sample was still slower (`24.717ms` vs `20.570ms`, `1.202x`), confirming again that the dominant gap is dictionary/page read-decode rather than mask materialization.
                                  - `two-key grouping`: no new retained code beyond measurement; the previous non-null map split was reverted. Latest sample was `17.835ms` vs DuckDB `15.243ms` (`1.170x`). The next real improvement should be a better group-id block/direct scan fold, not another HashMap key-shape tweak.
                                  - `RecordBatch` boundary: no safe broad cut was added in this pass. The useful direction remains direct scan/vector fold for these aggregate shapes, especially string dictionary preservation and two-key direct fold. Current focused four-case median-sum: Dodam `0.078275s`, DuckDB `0.068283s` (`1.146x`). Validation passed with `cargo check -q`, `duckdb_differential_generic_performance_shapes`, and `duckdb_differential_in_subquery`.
                                - Follow-up pass over the same 1-5 items:
                                  - `FILTER aggregate`: added a selectivity guard around selected-row blocks. This is a general policy for grouped native filtered columnar-direct, not tied to one query.
                                  - `alt_string_group`: kept the null-free UTF8 branch split and added batch-local group-id cache reservation. Latest focused sample was `review_q1_alt_string_group` `19.580ms` vs DuckDB `16.420ms` (`1.192x`), better than the prior `~1.22-1.30x` class but still not DuckDB-level.
                                  - `mixed tuple semijoin`: dictionary prefix refresh now returns selected dictionary count and skips pair construction when a page/dictionary has zero prefix matches; mask generation remains type-split by numeric key.                                   - `two-key grouping`: tried reserving the two-key group map/vector based on batch size, but it regressed (`review_q1_two_key_group` around `1.22x`), likely because reserve growth/branch cost exceeded allocation savings for the small group count.                                   - `RecordBatch` boundary: no broad removal yet. Direct scan/vector fold remains the next structural target.                                   - `two-key direct fold`: inspected the direct primitive fold stack and confirmed the current `DirectPrimitiveFoldMode` / `DirectCountSumMinMaxShape` is single-key-only.                                   - `mixed tuple semijoin`: retained dictionary-prefix selected-count propagation. When a dictionary/page has zero prefix matches, pair construction is skipped immediately. This is a general prefix-dictionary guard for mixed tuple semijoin build paths.
                                  - `alt_string_group`: removed the failed batch-local cache reserve after repeated runs showed it regressed string grouping. Kept only the earlier null-free branch split. Latest focused sample: `review_q1_alt_string_group` `19.863ms` vs DuckDB `16.404ms` (`1.211x`).
                                  - `FILTER aggregate`: added benchmark and differential guard coverage for sparse/dense filtered aggregate selectivity.                                 - Join aggregate lookup fusion shared-key follow-up:
                                  - Added a general shared fact-key pair dense group state for 3-dimension lookup aggregates. When two dimension payloads come from the same fact key, the operator now builds a dense `fact_key -> (payload_id_left,payload_id_right) pair_id` table and groups by `(first_payload_id,pair_id)` instead of falling back to tuple HashMap just because one payload column has high cardinality. This is a rule-level shape optimization, not a query-name fast path.
                                  - Added a typed blocked loop for the common `Int32 first key + Int32 shared key + Int64 sum` case so enum dispatch is moved out of the hot row loop. This retained the vector/block direction and was the part that produced the measurable win.
                                  - Measurement: focused `optimizer_four_way_comma_join` improved from the immediately prior repeated sample around `29.190ms` vs DuckDB `20.603ms` (`1.417x`) to `25.716ms` vs `20.860ms` (`1.233x`) in a 7-repeat run. Full generic 3-repeat remained healthy overall: median-sum Dodam `0.895s`, DuckDB `1.396s` (`0.641x`), with `optimizer_four_way_comma_join` still slower at `26.541ms` vs `20.507ms` (`1.294x`).
                                  - Remaining gap: join-fusion profile still shows fact update around `~15.6ms` for 600k rows, while fact decode is about `~11.5ms`. The next useful work is a lower-level fact scan/vector operator that can consume dictionary/int key blocks directly, or broader typed lookup loops for additional key/sum type combinations. More group-state reshaping alone has repeatedly been neutral or negative.
                                - Join aggregate lookup fusion null-key pushdown:
                                  - Added fact-side `IS NOT NULL` filter synthesis for lookup-fusion inner joins. Equality joins reject NULL keys, so the fused fact scan can safely AND `fact_key IS NOT NULL` for each required fact join key before aggregation. This is a semantic optimizer rule, not a query-name fast path.
                                  - Added a nullable dense direct fallback for shared-key pair groups, but the larger retained win came from pushing NULL rejection into the scan: the filtered fact stream becomes effectively null-free and can use the existing dense direct update path.
                                  - Important measurement note: `scripts/compare_generic_duckdb.py` defaults to `target/release/dodam`; after code edits, `cargo build --release -q` is required before trusting benchmark numbers. Earlier post-edit samples taken after only `cargo check` were stale release-binary measurements.
                                  - Release measurement after rebuilding: `optimizer_four_way_comma_join` focused 7-repeat moved to `16.233ms` vs DuckDB `20.139ms` (`0.806x`), and `optimizer_selective_four_way_comma_join` was `16.178ms` vs `18.619ms` (`0.869x`). Profile showed fact rows reduced from `600000` to `564705`, scan filter cost about `1.8ms`, and fact update dropped from the previous `~15.6ms` class to about `~1.9ms`.
                                  - Full generic 3-repeat after release rebuild: median-sum Dodam `0.893549s`, DuckDB `1.400176s` (`0.638x`). Remaining slower cases are now mostly `review_q1_alt_string_group` (`1.31x` in full run, noisy focused `~1.16-1.22x`), `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` (`1.24x`), and smaller expression/filtered aggregate gaps.
                                - UTF8 dictionary aggregate cleanup:
                                  - Changed the single-key dictionary UTF8 `count/sum/min/max` group-id cache from `Vec<Option<usize>>` to a `usize::MAX` sentinel vector in the min/max aggregate path. This removes an Option flatten from the row loop while preserving the same dictionary-id based group-id cache.
                                  - Focused release sample for string grouping remained noisy but acceptable: `review_q1_alt_string_group` sampled `25.180ms` vs DuckDB `21.712ms` (`1.160x`) alongside `review_like_group` `0.501x` and `string_low_cardinality_group` `0.514x`. The remaining alt-string gap is still mostly scan/decode/filter plus aggregate stream consumption, not a simple cache representation issue.
                                - Semijoin/expression aggregate cleanup pass:
                                  - Changed mixed tuple semijoin selected-dictionary probe to use `refresh_existing_dense()` and a `u32::MAX` sentinel dictionary-id map in the selected outer probe. This removes per-row `Option<u32>` flattening in the direct selected dictionary probe. Focused repeated run remained mostly read/decode dominated: `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` sampled `24.510ms` vs DuckDB `19.992ms` (`1.226x`), while related tuple cases were faster than DuckDB. Keep as a small general cleanup; it is not the structural fix.
                                  - Re-tested enabling `DODAM_MIXED_TUPLE_PREFIX_PRECHECK=1`; it regressed badly because the prefix precheck produced a false positive (`outer_found=true`) and caused an extra outer scan before the final pair probe. Keep prefix precheck opt-in only. The useful future direction is sharing/continuing the precheck scan into the final probe or stronger metadata pruning, not enabling it globally.
                                  - Changed `DictionaryIntGroupCache` for two-expression `GROUP BY ALL` count/sum from `Vec<Option<usize>>` to `usize::MAX` sentinel slots. This simplifies the dense dictionary-id + integer-key row loop but is a small/neutral cleanup. Focused repeated run: `standard_group_by_all_expression` `24.566ms` vs DuckDB `21.925ms` (`1.120x`); expression group siblings were already faster (`three_key_expression_group` `0.943x`, no-order `0.937x`). Remaining gap is split between Parquet decode (`~13.6ms`) and update (`~12.2ms`), so a real fix likely needs a lower-level dictionary/int block aggregate rather than more cache representation tweaks.
                                  - Negative result: tried reusing the outer `batch_partials` / `batch_touched` buffers inside the dictionary UTF8 `count/sum/min/max` helper. It did not improve `review_q1_alt_string_group` and produced worse/noisy focused samples (`~1.31x-1.44x` vs DuckDB). Reverted to the helper-local partial buffers; the remaining gap is not allocation churn in that helper.
                                - Current cleanup pass:
                                  - Added a generic direct global aggregate rule for `SUM(int_col * int_col)` expression aggregates. The planner/execution path now detects the expression shape before materializing an appended expression column, then folds primitive Arrow buffers directly. A follow-up split null-free primitive arrays into buffer loops instead of per-row `Option` dispatch. Focused rebuilt sample for `review_q6_expression_sum` moved into the DuckDB-parity/ahead range (`17.091ms` vs DuckDB `18.808ms` in 9-repeat focused; a profiled one-shot sampled `18.158ms` vs `18.697ms`). This is a shape rule, not a query-name fast path.
                                  - Added a null-free dictionary-key branch for single-key UTF8 `count/sum/min/max` aggregate so dictionary batches with no key nulls skip the per-row `is_null` branch. Focused `review_q1_alt_string_group` remains noisy but sampled around `26.145ms` vs DuckDB `21.508ms` (`1.216x`) in the retained binary; the improvement is small because scan/decode/filter still dominates.
                                  - Tightened mixed tuple semijoin early-empty probing with a pair-count cost gate (`DODAM_MIXED_TUPLE_EARLY_EMPTY_MAX_PAIRS`, default `4096`). The previous numeric-ratio-only gate could run an extra outer probe on large pair sets where output was likely not cheaply provable. In the current `standard_tuple_in_mixed_key_other_file` shape the gate disables that extra selected probe (`pairs=95192`, accepted=false); focused medians remain around `24-25ms` vs DuckDB `20-21ms`, so the retained value is avoiding bad extra scans rather than solving the lower-level dictionary read/decode gap.
                                - Parquet/RecordBatch materialization reduction:
                                  - Lowered the generic `Lower(Coalesce(utf8, literal)) + Int64AddLiteral` two-expression `count(*)/sum(i64)` aggregate shape into a direct Parquet dictionary-id scan-fold. This bypasses Arrow `RecordBatch` expression materialization for `GROUP BY ALL`-style expression grouping and consumes `(i32 add-key, i64 sum, utf8 dictionary id)` blocks directly.
                                  - First attempt used a per-row `(String, i64)` HashMap key and regressed badly (`standard_group_by_all_expression` consume around `25.5ms`, wall `~36-58ms`). Retained fix adds batch-local dense group-id slots keyed by `(dictionary_id, add-key)` plus fallback slots, so rows update counters through slot lookup and only miss paths touch the global string HashMap.
                                  - Focused rebuilt result: `standard_group_by_all_expression` improved to `19.164ms` vs DuckDB `21.799ms` (`0.879x`) in a 9-repeat run. Full generic 3-repeat sampled the same case at `18.797ms` vs `21.931ms` (`0.857x`) and total median remained strong (`0.626x`). This is a general expression-shape rule, not a query-name fast path. Remaining string/dictionary gaps are `review_q1_alt_string_group` and occasional `three_key_expression_group` noise.
